```
   147→    }),
```

```
     1→---
     2→title: Platform Architecture
     3→sidebar_label: Overview
     4→sidebar_position: 1
     5→description: Technical architecture overview of the KIX Platform on Google Cloud
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Platform Architecture
     9→
    10→This section provides the complete technical architecture of the **KIX Platform** (Konecta Intelligence eXchange) — the orchestration platform built on Google Cloud that enables Konecta to deliver orchestrated outcomes through Human Experts, AI Copilots, and Autonomous Agents.
    11→
    12→## Architecture Diagram
    13→
    14→![KIX Platform Architecture](/img/diagrams/kix-platform-combined.png)
    15→
    16→## Architecture Sections
    17→
    18→| Section | Description |
    19→|---------|-------------|
    20→| [Control Plane](/docs/architecture/control-plane) | The orchestration engine, classification layer, routing logic, and three execution engines |
    21→| [Platform Layers](/docs/architecture/platform-layers) | The five platform layers — ENGAGE, KNOW, GOVERN, CONNECT, ACT |
    22→| [Infrastructure](/docs/architecture/infrastructure) | GCP infrastructure architecture including VPC, networking, and compute resources |
    23→| [GKE Cluster](/docs/architecture/gke-cluster) | GKE cluster architecture, node pools, Gateway API, and configuration |
    24→| [Namespaces](/docs/architecture/namespaces) | Kubernetes namespace design, RBAC, resource quotas, and network policies |
    25→| [Network Flows](/docs/architecture/network-flows) | Network traffic flows between platform components and external services |
    26→| [Decision Records](/docs/architecture/decision-records) | Summary of key architectural decisions and their rationale |
    27→
    28→## Key Architecture Principles
    29→
    30→The platform architecture is governed by the following principles:
    31→
    32→### Core Services Model
    33→
    34→Use case developers consume standardised platform services (LLM gateway, API gateway, databases, caching, event bus) without managing infrastructure. The platform team provisions and operates all shared services, and each use case receives an isolated namespace with pre-configured access.
    35→
    36→### Security-First
    37→
    38→Every layer enforces a zero-trust posture. There are no public IP addresses, no service account keys, and no secrets in Git. Workload Identity provides automatic GCP authentication, and network policies enforce pod-level isolation. All data resides in EU regions only.
    39→
    40→### Multi-Tenant Isolation
    41→
    42→Each use case operates in its own Kubernetes namespace with dedicated resource quotas, network policies, GCP service accounts, and data services. Platform services (LiteLLM, Kong, monitoring) are shared but access-controlled.
    43→
    44→### GitOps-Driven
    45→
    46→ArgoCD on a shared platform cluster manages continuous delivery across all environment clusters. Infrastructure is defined in Terraform, application deployments are defined in Helm charts, and Git is the single source of truth.
    47→
    48→### Observable by Default
    49→
    50→All services emit metrics, logs, and traces automatically. Cloud Monitoring, Cloud Logging, and Cloud Trace (with OpenTelemetry) provide full visibility. Custom dashboards in Grafana surface use-case-specific KPIs.
    51→
    52→## Technology Stack Summary
    53→
    54→The stack is built on three foundational constraints:
    55→
    56→| Constraint | Technology | Rationale |
    57→|------------|------------|-----------|
    58→| **Cloud Platform** | Google Cloud Platform (GCP) | Enterprise standard, Konecta relationship |
    59→| **Agent Framework** | CrewAI | Multi-agent orchestration, production-ready |
    60→| **Voice AI** | ElevenLabs | Best-in-class voice synthesis and STT |
    61→
    62→### Core Technology Choices
    63→
    64→| Layer | Capability | Primary Technology |
    65→|-------|------------|-------------------|
    66→| **ENGAGE** | Voice AI | ElevenLabs |
    67→| **ENGAGE** | Conversational AI | CrewAI + LiteLLM |
    68→| **KNOW** | Journey Analytics | Third-party solution (Q2 selection) |
    69→| **KNOW** | Customer Profile | Cloud SQL + Redis (LaaS in Phase 2) |
    70→| **GOVERN** | AI Governance | LiteLLM + Custom |
    71→| **GOVERN** | Compliance | Cloud Audit Logs |
    72→| **CONNECT** | Omnichannel | Channel Hub + Kong |
    73→| **ACT** | Agent Runtime | CrewAI on GKE |
    74→| **ACT** | LLM Proxy | LiteLLM Proxy |
    75→| **ACT** | Tool Integration | MCP Gateway |
    76→| **ACT** | API Gateway | Kong API Gateway |
    77→| **ACT** | Event Bus | Cloud Pub/Sub |
    78→| **ACT** | Vector DB | Qdrant |
    79→| **ACT** | Service Catalogue | Backstage |
    80→
    81→### Infrastructure Stack
    82→
    83→| Component | Technology | Configuration |
    84→|-----------|------------|---------------|
    85→| **Kubernetes** | GKE Autopilot | Auto-scaling, managed control plane |
    86→| **Relational DB** | Cloud SQL PostgreSQL | HA with failover replica (LaaS in Phase 2) |
    87→| **Cache** | Cloud Memorystore Redis | Standard tier |
    88→| **Object Storage** | Google Cloud Storage | Regional, lifecycle policies |
    89→| **Vector DB** | Qdrant (on GKE) | 3 replicas, persistent storage |
    90→| **Data Lake** | Databricks LaaS | Phase 2 - analytics, ML integration |
    91→| **Service Catalogue** | Backstage | Internal service discovery |
    92→| **Identity** | Workload Identity | No service account keys |
    93→| **Secrets** | Secret Manager + CSI Driver | Auto-rotation |
    94→| **Encryption** | Cloud KMS (CMEK for Prod) | AES-256 |
    95→| **Network** | VPC with Private Google Access | No public IPs |
    96→| **WAF** | Cloud Armor | DDoS protection |
    97→| **Metrics** | Cloud Monitoring | Custom dashboards |
    98→| **Logging** | Cloud Logging | 30-day hot, 7-year archive |
    99→| **Tracing** | Cloud Trace + OpenTelemetry | Distributed tracing |
   100→| **GitOps** | ArgoCD | Multi-cluster CD, App-of-Apps, Image Updater |
   101→| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Continuous integration and deployment pipelines |
   102→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>
```

     1→---
     2→title: CI/CD Runbook
     3→sidebar_label: CI/CD Runbook
     4→sidebar_position: 4
     5→description: CI/CD pipeline operations, workflow reference, and troubleshooting for KIX Platform
     6→---
     7→
     8→# CI/CD Runbook
     9→
    10→This runbook provides operational guidance for the KIX Platform CI/CD pipelines.
    11→
    12→## Pipeline Overview
    13→
    14→The KIX Platform uses a **task-based CI/CD architecture** with reusable workflows and manual deployment control.
    15→
    16→### Architecture Diagram
    17→
    18→```
    19→+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+
    20→|                    REUSABLE WORKFLOWS (Building Blocks)                |
    21→+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+
    22→|                                                                       |
    23→|  terraform-validate.yml    Format, lint, init, validate, tfsec        |
    24→|  terraform-plan.yml        Generate plan + summary/comment            |
    25→|  terraform-apply.yml       Apply with approval gate                   |
    26→|  terraform-test.yml        Run Terratest (fast or full suite)         |
    27→|  terraform-drift.yml       Detect drift from state                    |
    28→|                                                                       |
    29→+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+
    30→                                    ^
    31→                                    | calls
    32→                                    |
    33→+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+
    34→|                    ORCHESTRATION WORKFLOWS (Callers)                   |
    35→+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+
    36→|                                                                       |
    37→|  pull-request.yml          Validates PRs (validate->plan->fast tests) |
    38→|  main-validation.yml       Proves main is deployable (validate->plan) |
    39→|  deploy.yml                Manual deployment (plan->approve->apply)   |
    40→|  nightly-quality.yml       Scheduled (full tests + drift detection)   |
    41→|                                                                       |
    42→+-----------------------------------------------------------------------+
    43→```
    44→
    45→### Key Principles
    46→
    47→1. **Manual Deployments** - All applies require explicit workflow_dispatch trigger
    48→2. **Fresh Plans** - Plans are generated immediately before apply (no stale artifacts)
    49→3. **Independent Environments** - Dev, QA, Prod deploy separately
    50→4. **Merge Validates, Doesn't Deploy** - Merging to main proves deployability without applying
    51→5. **Controlled Change Windows** - Production deployments restricted to approved times
    52→
    53→## Deploying to Environments
    54→
    55→### Prerequisites
    56→
    57→- Code merged to `main` branch
    58→- Main validation passed (automatic on merge)
    59→- Change window requirements met (for production)
    60→- Required approvals obtained
    61→
    62→### Deployment Process
    63→
    64→#### 1. Navigate to Deploy Workflow
    65→
    66→1. Go to **Actions** tab in GitHub
    67→2. Select **Deploy Infrastructure** workflow
    68→3. Click **Run workflow** button
    69→
    70→#### 2. Configure Deployment
    71→
    72→Fill in the workflow inputs:
    73→
    74→| Input | Description | Options |
    75→|-------|-------------|---------|
    76→| **environment** | Target environment | dev, qa, prod, shared |
    77→| **scope** | What to deploy | network, security, gke, platform, data, kong, gateway, litellm, argocd, all |
    78→| **dry_run** | Plan only (no apply) | true/false (default: false) |
    79→| **auto_approve** | Skip approval (dev only) | true/false (default: false) |
    80→
    81→**Example configurations:**
    82→
    83→```yaml
    84→# Deploy Kong to dev (auto-approved)
    85→environment: dev
    86→scope: kong
    87→dry_run: false
    88→auto_approve: true
    89→
    90→# Review prod GKE plan (no deployment)
    91→environment: prod
    92→scope: gke
    93→dry_run: true
    94→auto_approve: false
    95→
    96→# Deploy all services to QA (requires approval)
    97→environment: qa
    98→scope: all
    99→dry_run: false
   100→auto_approve: false
   101→```
   102→
   103→#### 3. Review Pre-flight Checks
   104→
   105→The workflow will:
   106→- Validate environment and scope inputs
   107→- Check change window (production only)
   108→- Map scope to Terraform directories
   109→- Verify auto-approve restrictions (dev only)
   110→
   111→#### 4. Review Plan Output
   112→
   113→The workflow generates a fresh Terraform plan:
   114→- View plan summary in workflow logs
   115→- Check resources to be added/changed/destroyed
   116→- Verify expected changes match intent
   117→
   118→#### 5. Approve Deployment (if required)
   119→
   120→For QA and Prod environments:
   121→1. GitHub will pause workflow for approval
   122→2. Review plan output
   123→3. Approve or reject deployment
   124→4. Timeout: 30 days (configurable)
   125→
   126→For Dev environment with auto_approve=true:
   127→- No approval gate (deployment proceeds automatically)
   128→
   129→#### 6. Monitor Deployment
   130→
   131→Watch the apply job progress:
   132→- Terraform apply runs with `-auto-approve`
   133→- Post-apply validation runs
   134→- Outputs captured for reference
   135→
   136→#### 7. Verify Deployment
   137→
   138→Post-deployment checks:
   139→- Smoke tests run automatically
   140→- Review outputs in workflow summary
   141→- Verify services are healthy
   142→
   143→### Scope Mapping
   144→
   145→| Scope | Directories (Dev/QA/Prod) | Order |
   146→|-------|---------------------------|-------|
   147→| `network` | `terraform/environments/{env}/gcp/network` | N/A |
   148→| `security` | `terraform/environments/{env}/gcp/security` | N/A |
   149→| `gke` | `terraform/environments/{env}/gcp/gke` | N/A |
   150→| `platform` | `terraform/environments/{env}/kubernetes/platform` | N/A |
   151→| `data` | `terraform/environments/{env}/gcp/data` | N/A |
   152→| `kong` | `terraform/environments/{env}/kubernetes/kong` | N/A |
   153→| `gateway` | `terraform/environments/{env}/kubernetes/gateway` | N/A |
   154→| `litellm` | `terraform/environments/{env}/kubernetes/litellm` | N/A |
   155→| `all` | All above in order | Sequential |
   156→
   157→**Shared environment special scopes:**
   158→
   159→| Scope | Directories (Shared) |
   160→|-------|---------------------|
   161→| `network` | `terraform/environments/shared/gcp/network` |
   162→| `security` | `terraform/environments/shared/gcp/security` |
   163→| `gke` | `terraform/environments/shared/gcp/gke` |
   164→| `platform` | `terraform/environments/shared/kubernetes/platform` |
   165→| `argocd` | `terraform/environments/shared/kubernetes/argocd`, `argocd-bootstrap` |
   166→
   167→## Change Windows
   168→
   169→### Development (dev)
   170→
   171→- **When:** Anytime
   172→- **Approval:** Auto-approve allowed
   173→- **Restrictions:** None
   174→- **Purpose:** Rapid iteration and testing
   175→
   176→### Quality Assurance (qa)
   177→
   178→- **When:** Business hours (08:00-18:00 UTC, Mon-Fri)
   179→- **Approval:** Manual approval required
   180→- **Restrictions:** Avoid during active testing cycles
   181→- **Purpose:** Controlled validation before production
   182→
   183→### Production (prod)
   184→
   185→- **When:** **Tuesday-Thursday, 20:00-23:00 UTC only**
   186→- **Approval:** Manual approval required + change ticket
   187→- **Restrictions:**
   188→  - Outside change window: Deployment blocked automatically
   189→  - Emergency exception: See [Emergency Procedures](#emergency-procedures)
   190→- **Purpose:** Minimize risk to production services
   191→
   192→### Shared Services (shared)
   193→
   194→- **When:** Business hours (08:00-18:00 UTC, Mon-Fri)
   195→- **Approval:** Manual approval required
   196→- **Restrictions:** ArgoCD changes require coordination with all environments
   197→- **Purpose:** Avoid impacting GitOps across all clusters
   198→
   199→## Emergency Procedures
   200→
   201→### Emergency Hotfix (Outside Change Window)
   202→
   203→For critical production issues requiring immediate fix:
   204→
   205→1. **Assess Impact**
   206→   - Is this a P0/P1 incident?
   207→   - Is immediate deployment necessary?
   208→   - Can it wait for next change window?
   209→
   210→2. **Get Approval**
   211→   - Notify on-call SRE/platform lead
   212→   - Document incident ticket (Jira)
   213→   - Get emergency change approval
   214→
   215→3. **Bypass Change Window**
   216→   - Change window check is automated
   217→   - Bypass requires modifying workflow (remove change window validation step)
   218→   - Alternative: Use `dry_run: true` to validate, then manually apply via `gcloud`
   219→
   220→4. **Emergency Deployment**
   221→   ```bash
   222→   # Option 1: Manual Terraform apply (fastest)
   223→   cd terraform/environments/prod/{scope}
   224→   terraform init -backend-config="bucket=kd-tfstate-prod" \
   225→     -backend-config="prefix=terraform/environments/prod/{scope}/terraform.tfstate"
   226→   terraform plan
   227→   terraform apply  # After review
   228→
   229→   # Option 2: Modify workflow (if time permits)
   230→   # Temporarily comment out change window check in deploy.yml
   231→   ```
   232→
   233→5. **Post-Incident**
   234→   - Document emergency deployment
   235→   - Restore workflow protections
   236→   - Post-mortem review
   237→
   238→### Rollback Procedure
   239→
   240→If deployment causes issues:
   241→
   242→1. **Assess Rollback Need**
   243→   - What broke?
   244→   - Can we roll forward (fix issue)?
   245→   - Is rollback safe (no state changes)?
   246→
   247→2. **Determine Rollback Strategy**
   248→
   249→   **Option A: Terraform Rollback (Safe)**
   250→   ```bash
   251→   # Revert Terraform code to previous version
   252→   git checkout <previous-commit>
   253→
   254→   # Run deploy workflow with reverted code
   255→   # This generates fresh plan with reverted config
   256→   ```
   257→
   258→   **Option B: Manual Resource Restore (Risky)**
   259→   ```bash
   260→   # Only for critical resources that can't wait
   261→   # Use Terraform state backup
   262→   gsutil cp gs://kd-tfstate-prod/terraform/environments/prod/{scope}/terraform.tfstate.backup ./
   263→   terraform state push terraform.tfstate.backup
   264→   ```
   265→
   266→3. **Validate Rollback**
   267→   - Run smoke tests
   268→   - Verify services are healthy
   269→   - Monitor metrics/logs
   270→
   271→4. **Post-Rollback**
   272→   - Root cause analysis
   273→   - Update tests to catch issue
   274→   - Plan proper fix
   275→
   276→### State Recovery
   277→
   278→If Terraform state is corrupted or lost:
   279→
   280→1. **Check State Backup**
   281→   ```bash
   282→   # List backups
   283→   gsutil ls gs://kd-tfstate-prod/terraform/environments/prod/{scope}/
   284→
   285→   # Download backup
   286→   gsutil cp gs://kd-tfstate-prod/terraform/environments/prod/{scope}/terraform.tfstate.backup ./
   287→
   288→   # Restore
   289→   terraform state push terraform.tfstate.backup
   290→   ```
   291→
   292→2. **Import Existing Resources**
   293→   ```bash
   294→   # If no backup, import resources manually
   295→   terraform import google_compute_network.vpc projects/{project}/global/networks/{network}
   296→   ```
   297→
   298→3. **Validate State**
   299→   ```bash
   300→   terraform plan  # Should show no changes if state is correct
   301→   ```
   302→
   303→## Troubleshooting
   304→
   305→### Common Issues
   306→
   307→#### Issue: PR Validation Takes Too Long
   308→
   309→**Symptoms:** PR validation workflow runs for >15 minutes
   310→
   311→**Causes:**
   312→- Fast test suite running full tests
   313→- Too many directories changed
   314→- GCP authentication issues
   315→
   316→**Solutions:**
   317→```bash
   318→# Check test suite configuration
   319→cat .github/workflows/reusable/terraform-test.yml | grep -A 5 "test_suite"
   320→
   321→# Verify fast tests are defined
   322→cd terraform/test
   323→grep -r "TestBasic"
   324→
   325→# Reduce parallel changes (split PR)
   326→git diff origin/main --name-only | grep "\.tf$" | wc -l
   327→```
   328→
   329→#### Issue: Plan Shows Unexpected Changes
   330→
   331→**Symptoms:** Terraform plan shows drift or unexpected resource changes
   332→
   333→**Causes:**
   334→- Manual changes in GCP console
   335→- Provider version change
   336→- Resource import/recreation
   337→
   338→**Solutions:**
   339→```bash
   340→# Check for drift
   341→terraform plan -detailed-exitcode
   342→
   343→# Review state
   344→terraform state list
   345→terraform state show <resource>
   346→
   347→# Compare with GCP
   348→gcloud compute networks describe <network> --project=<project>
   349→```
   350→
   351→#### Issue: Apply Fails with "Resource Already Exists"
   352→
   353→**Symptoms:** Apply fails because resource already exists
   354→
   355→**Causes:**
   356→- Resource created manually
   357→- State out of sync
   358→- Concurrent applies
   359→
   360→**Solutions:**
   361→```bash
   362→# Import existing resource
   363→terraform import <resource_type>.<name> <resource_id>
   364→
   365→# Example: Import GKE cluster
   366→terraform import google_container_cluster.cluster projects/<project>/locations/<region>/clusters/<name>
   367→
   368→# Verify import
   369→terraform plan  # Should show no changes
   370→```
   371→
   372→#### Issue: Workflow Stuck on Approval
   373→
   374→**Symptoms:** Deploy workflow waiting for approval indefinitely
   375→
   376→**Causes:**
   377→- No GitHub environment configured
   378→- Insufficient permissions
   379→- Approvers not notified
   380→
   381→**Solutions:**
   382→1. Check GitHub environment settings (Settings -> Environments)
   383→2. Verify approvers are configured
   384→3. Check if approval notification was sent
   385→4. Manually approve in Actions tab -> Review deployments
   386→
   387→#### Issue: Nightly Quality Checks Failing
   388→
   389→**Symptoms:** Nightly workflow reports failures
   390→
   391→**Causes:**
   392→- Terratest resources not cleaned up
   393→- Drift in production
   394→- Lab project quota exceeded
   395→
   396→**Solutions:**
   397→```bash
   398→# Check for orphaned resources
   399→gcloud compute instances list --project=kd-ix-eur-lab-platform | grep terratest
   400→
   401→# Clean up manually
   402→gcloud compute instances delete <instance> --project=kd-ix-eur-lab-platform
   403→
   404→# Check quotas
   405→gcloud compute project-info describe --project=kd-ix-eur-lab-platform | grep -A 20 quotas
   406→```
   407→
   408→### Debugging Workflows
   409→
   410→#### View Workflow Logs
   411→
   412→1. Go to **Actions** tab
   413→2. Click on workflow run
   414→3. Expand job steps
   415→4. Review logs for errors
   416→
   417→#### Download Artifacts
   418→
   419→```bash
   420→# Using GitHub CLI
   421→gh run download <run-id> --name terraform-apply-prod-<run-id>
   422→
   423→# View plan output
   424→cat apply_output.txt
   425→
   426→# View outputs
   427→cat outputs.json | jq
   428→```
   429→
   430→#### Re-run Failed Workflow
   431→
   432→1. Click on failed workflow run
   433→2. Click **Re-run jobs** -> **Re-run failed jobs**
   434→3. Or **Re-run all jobs** for clean slate
   435→
   436→## Workflow Reference
   437→
   438→### pull-request.yml
   439→
   440→**Trigger:** Pull request to main/develop
   441→
   442→**Purpose:** Fast validation (&lt;15 minutes)
   443→
   444→**Jobs:**
   445→1. Detect changed directories
   446→2. Validate Terraform (parallel)
   447→3. Generate plans (parallel)
   448→4. Run fast tests (if modules changed)
   449→
   450→**Outputs:**
   451→- PR comments with plan summaries
   452→- Validation status
   453→- Test results
   454→
   455→**Usage:** Automatic (triggered by PR)
   456→
   457→### main-validation.yml
   458→
   459→**Trigger:** Push to main branch
   460→
   461→**Purpose:** Prove main is deployable (doesn't deploy)
   462→
   463→**Jobs:**
   464→1. Detect changed directories
   465→2. Validate all changes
   466→3. Generate plans for all
   467→4. Tag release with semantic version
   468→
   469→**Outputs:**
   470→- GitHub release with version tag
   471→- Deployment readiness status
   472→- No actual deployment
   473→
   474→**Usage:** Automatic (triggered by merge)
   475→
   476→### deploy.yml
   477→
   478→**Trigger:** Manual (workflow_dispatch)
   479→
   480→**Purpose:** Deploy infrastructure to environment
   481→
   482→**Inputs:**
   483→- `environment`: dev, qa, prod, shared
   484→- `scope`: network, security, gke, platform, data, kong, gateway, litellm, argocd, all
   485→- `dry_run`: true/false
   486→- `auto_approve`: true/false (dev only)
   487→
   488→**Jobs:**
   489→1. Pre-flight checks (change window, permissions)
   490→2. Validate
   491→3. Plan (fresh)
   492→4. Approval gate (if required)
   493→5. Apply
   494→6. Post-deployment verification
   495→
   496→**Outputs:**
   497→- Deployment status
   498→- Terraform outputs
   499→- Verification results
   500→
   501→**Usage:**
   502→```bash
   503→# Via GitHub UI
   504→Actions -> Deploy Infrastructure -> Run workflow
   505→
   506→# Via GitHub CLI
   507→gh workflow run deploy.yml \
   508→  -f environment=prod \
   509→  -f scope=gke \
   510→  -f dry_run=false \
   511→  -f auto_approve=false
   512→```
   513→
   514→### nightly-quality.yml
   515→
   516→**Trigger:** Schedule (daily at 02:00 UTC) + manual
   517→
   518→**Purpose:** Comprehensive testing and drift detection
   519→
   520→**Inputs (manual trigger only):**
   521→- `skip_tests`: Skip full test suite
   522→- `skip_drift`: Skip drift detection
   523→
   524→**Jobs:**
   525→1. Full Terratest suite (90 minutes)
   526→2. Drift detection (all environments, all scopes)
   527→3. Daily quality report
   528→
   529→**Outputs:**
   530→- Test results summary
   531→- Drift detection report
   532→- GitHub issues for drift (if detected)
   533→
   534→**Usage:**
   535→```bash
   536→# Automatic: Runs daily at 02:00 UTC
   537→
   538→# Manual trigger (with options)
   539→gh workflow run nightly-quality.yml \
   540→  -f skip_tests=false \
   541→  -f skip_drift=false
   542→```
   543→
   544→## Reusable Workflows
   545→
   546→### terraform-validate.yml
   547→
   548→**Inputs:**
   549→- `working_directory`: Terraform directory
   550→- `terraform_version`: Terraform version (default: 1.5.0)
   551→- `enable_tfsec`: Enable security scan (default: true)
   552→- `tfsec_severity`: Minimum severity (default: MEDIUM)
   553→
   554→**Outputs:**
   555→- `validation_status`: success/failure
   556→- `tfsec_findings_count`: Number of findings
   557→
   558→**Usage:**
   559→```yaml
   560→uses: ./.github/workflows/reusable/terraform-validate.yml
   561→with:
   562→  working_directory: terraform/environments/dev/gcp/network
   563→```
   564→
   565→### terraform-plan.yml
   566→
   567→**Inputs:**
   568→- `working_directory`: Terraform directory
   569→- `environment`: dev, qa, prod, shared
   570→- `gcp_project_id`: GCP project
   571→- `backend_bucket`: State bucket
   572→- `backend_prefix`: State path
   573→- `comment_on_pr`: Post to PR (default: true)
   574→- `upload_artifact`: Upload plan (default: false)
   575→
   576→**Outputs:**
   577→- `plan_status`: success/failure/no_changes
   578→- `plan_summary`: First 50 lines
   579→- `changes_detected`: true/false
   580→- `artifact_name`: Artifact name (if uploaded)
   581→
   582→**Usage:**
   583→```yaml
   584→uses: ./.github/workflows/reusable/terraform-plan.yml
   585→secrets: inherit
   586→with:
   587→  working_directory: terraform/environments/dev/gcp/network
   588→  environment: dev
   589→  gcp_project_id: kd-ix-eur-dev-foundation
   590→  backend_bucket: kd-tfstate-dev
   591→  backend_prefix: terraform/environments/dev/gcp/network/terraform.tfstate
   592→```
   593→
   594→### terraform-apply.yml
   595→
   596→**Inputs:**
   597→- `working_directory`: Terraform directory
   598→- `environment`: dev, qa, prod
   599→- `gcp_project_id`: GCP project
   600→- `backend_bucket`: State bucket
   601→- `backend_prefix`: State path
   602→- `require_approval`: Use approval gate (default: true)
   603→
   604→**Outputs:**
   605→- `apply_status`: success/failure
   606→- `outputs`: Terraform outputs (JSON)
   607→- `deployment_time`: ISO 8601 timestamp
   608→
   609→**Usage:**
   610→```yaml
   611→uses: ./.github/workflows/reusable/terraform-apply.yml
   612→secrets: inherit
   613→with:
   614→  working_directory: terraform/environments/prod/gcp/network
   615→  environment: prod
   616→  gcp_project_id: kd-ix-eur-prod-foundation
   617→  backend_bucket: kd-tfstate-prod
   618→  backend_prefix: terraform/environments/prod/gcp/network/terraform.tfstate
   619→  require_approval: true
   620→```
   621→
   622→### terraform-test.yml
   623→
   624→**Inputs:**
   625→- `test_suite`: fast, full, specific
   626→- `test_pattern`: Go test regex (for specific)
   627→- `test_timeout`: Timeout (default: 30m)
   628→- `parallel_count`: Parallel tests (default: 4)
   629→- `gcp_project_id`: Lab project
   630→- `cleanup_on_failure`: Cleanup resources (default: true)
   631→
   632→**Outputs:**
   633→- `test_status`: passed/failed/skipped
   634→- `tests_run`: Count
   635→- `tests_passed`: Count
   636→- `duration`: Minutes
   637→
   638→**Usage:**
   639→```yaml
   640→uses: ./.github/workflows/reusable/terraform-test.yml
   641→secrets: inherit
   642→with:
   643→  test_suite: fast
   644→  gcp_project_id: kd-ix-eur-lab-platform
   645→```
   646→
   647→### terraform-drift.yml
   648→
   649→**Inputs:**
   650→- `working_directory`: Terraform directory
   651→- `environment`: dev, qa, prod, shared
   652→- `gcp_project_id`: GCP project
   653→- `backend_bucket`: State bucket
   654→- `backend_prefix`: State path
   655→- `notify_on_drift`: Create issue (default: true)
   656→- `notification_channel`: github, slack, teams (default: github)
   657→
   658→**Outputs:**
   659→- `drift_detected`: true/false
   660→- `drift_summary`: Summary text
   661→- `last_check_time`: ISO 8601 timestamp
   662→
   663→**Usage:**
   664→```yaml
   665→uses: ./.github/workflows/reusable/terraform-drift.yml
   666→secrets: inherit
   667→with:
   668→  working_directory: terraform/environments/prod/gcp/gke
   669→  environment: prod
   670→  gcp_project_id: kd-ix-eur-prod-platform
   671→  backend_bucket: kd-tfstate-prod
   672→  backend_prefix: terraform/environments/prod/gcp/gke/terraform.tfstate
   673→  notify_on_drift: true
   674→```
   675→
   676→## Best Practices
   677→
   678→### Before Deployment
   679→
   680→- Run `terraform plan` locally first
   681→- Review plan output carefully
   682→- Check for unexpected resource destruction
   683→- Verify GCP project and region
   684→- Ensure change window compliance (prod)
   685→- Notify team of deployment
   686→
   687→### During Deployment
   688→
   689→- Monitor workflow progress
   690→- Watch for errors in real-time
   691→- Keep communication channel open
   692→- Have rollback plan ready
   693→- Do not close browser until complete
   694→
   695→### After Deployment
   696→
   697→- Verify services are healthy
   698→- Check application functionality
   699→- Review Terraform outputs
   700→- Monitor metrics for anomalies
   701→- Update deployment log
   702→- Notify team of completion
   703→
   704→## Additional Resources
   705→
   706→- [Terraform Structure](/docs/infrastructure-ops/terraform-structure) - Repository layout and module organization
   707→- [Deployment Guide](/docs/infrastructure-ops/deployment-guide) - Step-by-step deployment procedures
   708→- [DNS Architecture](/docs/infrastructure-ops/dns-architecture) - DNS zone architecture on Google Cloud DNS
   709→- [Security Architecture](/docs/security/security-architecture) - Security controls and compliance
   710→- [GitHub Actions Documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/actions)
   711→- [Terraform Best Practices](https://www.terraform-best-practices.com/)
   712→

```json
  "content": "---\ntitle: Application Migration\nsidebar_label: Overview\nsidebar_position: 1\ndescription: Migration guide for moving Cloud Run services to the KIX-Infrastructure Kubernetes platform\n---\n\n# Application Migration\n\nThis section provides a comprehensive guide for migrating services from **GCP Cloud Run** to the **kix-infrastructure** Kubernetes platform. The migration adopts modern cloud-native patterns including Helm charts, GitOps-driven deployments, and a unified CI/CD pipeline.\n\n## Executive Summary\n\nThe platform is migrating approximately **24 microservices** from Cloud Run to a Kubernetes-native architecture. This migration delivers:\n\n- **Standardised packaging** with Helm charts for declarative Kubernetes deployment\n- **Secure container builds** with multi-stage Docker, vulnerability scanning, and image signing\n- **Unified CI/CD** via GitHub Actions for build/scan/push and ArgoCD for GitOps deployments\n- **Developer portal** via Backstage for service catalog, scaffolding, and documentation\n- **Dual ingress architecture** with Kong for APIs and GKE Gateway API + IAP for frontends\n\n**Key principles**: Simple, secure, auditable, repeatable.\n\n## Migration Scope\n\n### In Scope (24 Services)\n\n| Category | Count | Stack |\n|----------|-------|-------|\n| MCP Servers | 14 | Python (FastMCP) |\n| Business Apps | 4 | Python (FastAPI) + Node.js (Nuxt 3) |\n| Data Pipelines | 3 | Python |\n| Bots | 1 | Python |\n| Prototypes | 2 | Python |\n\n### Out of Scope\n\n- **CrewAI Flows & Tools** (12) — remain on current platform\n- **Infrastructure Repos** (4) — handled separately\n- **Config/Proxies** (2) — remain as-is\n\n### External Dependencies (Remain as Managed Services)\n\n- Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL 16+)\n- Firestore\n- Vertex AI (Gemini, Claude via Vertex, RAG Engine)\n- Cloud Storage (GCS)\n- Pub/Sub\n- Google APIs (Search, Maps, Drive, Chat)\n\n## Documentation Structure\n\n| Section | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| [Current State](current-state) | Cloud Run architecture, service inventory, and dependencies |\n| [Target Architecture](target-architecture) | GKE target architecture, repository model, and CI/CD flow |\n| [Migration Phases](migration-phases) | Phased migration strategy with priorities and timeline |\n| [Kubernetes & Helm](kubernetes-helm) | Helm chart design, namespaces, resource profiles, and autoscaling |\n| [Ingress & Networking](ingress-networking) | Dual ingress architecture, network policies, and load balancing |\n| [Docker Security](docker-security) | Secure multi-stage builds, base images, and hardening |\n| [CI/CD Pipeline](cicd-pipeline) | GitHub Actions pipeline stages and security gates |\n| [GitOps & Promotion](gitops-promotion) | ArgoCD GitOps flow, environment promotion, and rollback |\n| [Backstage & Onboarding](backstage-onboarding) | Developer portal integration and service onboarding |\n| [Security Architecture](security) | Runtime security, secrets management, and admission control |\n| [Observability](observability) | Monitoring, logging, tracing, and alerting |\n| [Operations & Risks](operations-risks) | Cost considerations, disaster recovery, and risk register |\n| [Appendix & Reference](appendix-reference) | Technology stack, namespace map, and repository mapping |\n\n## Service Migration Guides\n\nDetailed step-by-step guides for migrating specific services:\n\n| Service | Status | Guide |\n|---------|--------|-------|\n| IQ Portal | P0 (Pilot) | [IQ Portal Migration Guide](service-guides/iq-portal) |\n\n## Quick Links\n\n- **Platform Modules**: [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)\n- **Infrastructure Repo**: [kix-platform-infrastructure](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure)\n- **CI Workflows**: [kix-platform-ci-workflows](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows)\n\n---\n\n**Last Updated:** February 2026\n**Maintained By:** Platform Engineering Team\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "---\ntitle: Current State\nsidebar_label: Current State\nsidebar_position: 2\ndescription: Current Cloud Run architecture, service inventory, and GCP dependencies\n---\n\n# Current State\n\nThis document describes the existing architecture and service inventory before migration to kix-infrastructure.\n\n## Infrastructure Overview\n\n| Component | Technology | Notes |\n|-----------|------------|-------|\n| **Compute** | GCP Cloud Run (most services), GKE Autopilot (Kong, LiteLLM, ArgoCD), Cloud Functions (1 bot) | |\n| **Clusters** | GKE Autopilot | eu-west1/eu-west4, private with Cloud NAT |\n| **CI/CD** | Cloud Build (8 services), GitHub Actions (Terraform only) | |\n| **Registry** | GCP Artifact Registry | |\n| **IaC** | Terraform with modular structure | |\n\n## Service Inventory\n\n### Services In Scope (24 Services)\n\n| Category | Count | Stack | Description |\n|----------|-------|-------|-------------|\n| **MCP Servers** | 14 | Python (FastMCP) | Model Context Protocol servers for tool integration |\n| **Business Apps** | 4 | Python (FastAPI) + Node.js (Nuxt 3) | User-facing applications |\n| **Data Pipelines** | 3 | Python | RAG ingestion and data processing |\n| **Bots** | 1 | Python (Cloud Functions) | Automated agents |\n| **Prototypes** | 2 | Python | Proof-of-concept applications |\n\n### Service Catalogue\n\n#### MCP Servers (14)\n\n| Service | Repository | Stack | Purpose |\n|---------|------------|-------|---------|\n| kix-mcp-gateway | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Routes MCP requests to backends |\n| kix-mcp-ragengine | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | RAG retrieval and generation |\n| kix-mcp-channelhub | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Channel Hub integration |\n| kix-mcp-google-search | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Google Search API |\n| kix-mcp-googlemaps | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Google Maps API |\n| kix-mcp-docingestion | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Document ingestion |\n| kix-mcp-docx | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | DOCX processing |\n| kix-mcp-diagram | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Diagram generation |\n| kix-mcp-pptx | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | PowerPoint processing |\n| kix-mcp-markdown | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Markdown processing |\n| kix-mcp-webcrawler | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | Web crawling |\n| kix-mcp-crawl4ai | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | AI-powered crawling |\n| kix-mcp-gcs | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | GCS operations |\n| kix-mcp-client | konecta-ix-mcp-servers | Python FastMCP | MCP client library |\n\n#### Business Applications (4)\n\n| Service | Repository | Stack | Purpose |\n|---------|------------|-------|---------|\n| kix-usecase-iq-portal | konecta-ix-workloads | Nuxt 3 + Express | AI knowledge platform |\n| kix-app-hrfraud | konecta-ix-apps | Nuxt 3 + FastAPI | HR fraud detection |\n| kix-app-hrportal | konecta-ix-apps | Nuxt 3 | HR portal frontend |\n| kix-usecase-testiq | konecta-ix-workloads | Nuxt 3 | Test IQ application |\n\n#### Data Pipelines (3)\n\n| Service | Repository | Stack | Purpose |\n|---------|------------|-------|---------|\n| kix-usecase-rag-ingestion | konecta-ix-workloads | Python | RAG document ingestion |\n| kix-usecase-rag-googledrive | konecta-ix-workloads | Python | Google Drive RAG sync |\n| kix-demo-porfolioguru | konecta-ix-demos | Python | Portfolio analysis bot |\n\n#### Prototypes (2)\n\n| Service | Repository | Stack | Purpose |\n|---------|------------|-------|---------|\n| kix-demo-prototypes | konecta-ix-demos | Python | Various prototypes |\n\n### Out of Scope\n\n| Category | Count | Rationale |\n|----------|-------|-----------|\n| CrewAI Flows & Tools | 12 | Remain on current platform |\n| Infrastructure Repos | 4 | Handled separately |\n| Config/Proxies | 2 | Remain as-is |\n\n## GCP Service Dependencies\n\nServices retain these GCP managed services after migration:\n\n| Service | Project | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| **Cloud SQL** (PostgreSQL) | `kd-lab-464110` | Primary databases |\n| **Cloud Storage** | `kd-corp-dgt-svc-apps-dev` | Image/video/RAG file storage |\n| **Vertex AI** | `kd-lab-464110` | Gemini LLM + RAG retrieval |\n| **Cloud Tasks** | `kd-lab-464110` | Async RAG document ingestion |\n| **Secret Manager** | `kd-lab-464110` | Secrets storage |\n| **Firestore** | Various | Rate limiting, conversations |\n| **Pub/Sub** | Various | Async messaging |\n\n## Current CI/CD\n\n### Cloud Build (8 services)\n\nServices currently using Cloud Build:\n\n- Triggered from Bitbucket repositories\n- Build configs in `cloudbuild.yaml`\n- Push to Artifact Registry\n- Deploy to Cloud Run\n\n### GitHub Actions (Terraform)\n\nInfrastructure deployments only:\n\n- Terraform validate/plan/apply workflows\n- Environment-specific deployment gates\n- State stored in GCS\n\n## Current Limitations\n\n| Limitation | Impact | Resolution in kix |\n|------------|--------|-------------------|\n| No unified CI/CD | Fragmented build systems | Standard GitHub Actions pipeline |\n| No GitOps | Manual deployments | ArgoCD-driven GitOps |\n| Cloud Run cold starts | Latency on first request | Always-on K8s pods |\n| Limited observability | Fragmented metrics | Unified Prometheus/Grafana |\n| No service catalog | Discovery difficult | Backstage developer portal |\n| Per-service secrets | Management overhead | External Secrets Operator |\n\n---\n\n**Next**: [Target Architecture](target-architecture) — The destination state on kix-infrastructure.\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "---\ntitle: Target Architecture\nsidebar_label: Target Architecture\nsidebar_position: 3\ndescription: Target GKE architecture, repository model, and CI/CD flow\n---\n\n# Target Architecture\n\nThis document describes the target state architecture on kix-infrastructure.\n\n## High-Level Architecture\n\n```\n┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                           INTERNET                                        │\n└──────────┬───────────────────────────────────────┬────────────────────────┘\n           │ HTTPS (API traffic)                   │ HTTPS (Frontend traffic)\n           │                                       │\n┌──────────▼───────────────────┐   ┌───────────────▼───────────────────────┐\n│  GCP LB + Cloud Armor (WAF)  │   │  GCP LB + Gateway API + IAP +         │\n│   (API ingress via Kong)     │   │  Cloud Armor (Frontend ingress)       │\n└──────────┬───────────────────┘   └───────────────┬───────────────────────┘\n           │                                       │\n┌──────────▼───────────────────────────────────────▼────────────────────────┐\n│              KIX-INFRASTRUCTURE GKE CLUSTER (eu-west1)                    │\n│                        kd-ix-eur-{env}-gke                                │\n│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │  shared-kong (Kong Gateway)                                        │   │\n│  │    └── routes to {env}-api-services, {env}-mcp-servers             │   │\n│  │    └── routes to shared-litellm (LLM Proxy) → internal only        │   │\n│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │\n│                                                                           │\n│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │\n│  │  Application Namespaces (per environment × domain)                  │  │\n│  │                                                                     │  │\n│  │  {env}-api-services     │ FastAPI backends, business apps,          │  │\n│  │                           bots, data pipelines                      │  │\n│  │  {env}-mcp-servers      │ MCP protocol servers (SSE)                │  │\n│  │                                                                     │  │\n│  │  {env}-web-apps         │ Nuxt 3 frontends  → Frontend LB           │  │\n│  │                           (GKE Gateway API + IAM auth)              │  │\n│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │\n│  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │  shared-monitoring: Prometheus / Grafana / Jaeger / OTel           │   │\n│  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘   │\n│                                                                           │\n│  ┌───────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐  ┌──────────────┐ │\n│  │ shared-litellm│  │ shared-argocd│  │ shared-secrets│  │  shared-     │ │\n│  │ (behind Kong) │  │ (GitOps CD)  │  │ (ESO + CSI)   │  │  backstage   │ │\n│  └───────────────┘  └──────────────┘  └───────────────┘  └──────────────┘ │\n└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n         │                    │                    │                    │\n         ▼                    ▼                    ▼                    ▼\n┌─────────────┐  ┌───────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐ ┌─────────────────┐\n│  Cloud SQL  │  │  Vertex AI / GCS  │  │  GCP Secret Manager │ │  Cloud Storage  │\n│ (PostgreSQL)│  │  Pub/Sub / APIs   │  │                     │ │    (Buckets)    │\n└─────────────┘  └───────────────────┘  └─────────────────────┘ └─────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Dual Ingress Architecture\n\nTwo separate ingress paths with independent load balancers:\n\n| Path | Flow | Purpose |\n|------|------|---------|\n| **API** | Internet → GCP LB + Cloud Armor → Kong Ingress → `{env}-api-services` / `{env}-mcp-servers` | Machine-to-machine, API consumers |\n| **Frontend** | Internet → GCP LB + Cloud Armor + GKE Gateway API + IAP → `{env}-web-apps` | Browser users, human authentication |\n\n## Repository Model\n\nServices are organised across multiple GitHub Enterprise organisations under **Konecta Digital**. A central GitOps repo in the platform org holds all Helm value overrides and ArgoCD configuration.\n\n### Organisation Structure\n\n```\nGitHub Enterprise: Konecta Digital\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-platform (Platform Engineering)\n│   ├── kix-platform-infrastructure     # GCP infra + ArgoCD apps + GitOps values\n│   │   ├── argocd/                     # ApplicationSets\n│   │   └── environments/               # Per-env Helm value overrides for ALL services\n│   │       ├── dev/{service}.yaml\n│   │       ├── qa/{service}.yaml\n│   │       └── prod/{service}.yaml\n│   ├── kix-platform-modules            # Shared Terraform modules + Helm base chart\n│   │   ├── terraform/                  # Existing Terraform modules\n│   │   └── helm/base-service-chart/    # Shared Helm library chart (OCI)\n│   ├── kix-platform-ci-workflows       # Shared reusable GitHub Actions workflows\n│   │   └── .github/workflows/\n│   │       ├── standard-pipeline.yaml\n│   │       ├── _build-image.yaml\n│   │       ├── _scan-and-sign.yaml\n│   │       └── ...\n│   ├── kix-platform-service-template   # Template repo for bootstrapping\n│   ├── kix-platform-backstage          # Backstage config, plugins, catalog\n│   └── kix-platform-docs               # Documentation (TechDocs)\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-mcp-servers (MCP Integration Team)\n│   ├── kix-mcp-gateway\n│   ├── kix-mcp-ragengine\n│   └── kix-mcp-*                       # One per server (~14 repos)\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-apps (Apps Team)\n│   ├── kix-app-hrfraud\n│   └── kix-app-*\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-workloads (Use Case Teams)\n│   ├── kix-usecase-iq-portal\n│   └── kix-usecase-*\n│\n├── Org: konecta-ix-demos (Demo Team)\n│   └── kix-demo-*\n│\n└── Org: konecta-ix-services (Platform Services)\n    ├── kix-services-kong\n    ├── kix-services-litellm\n    ├── kix-services-monitoring\n    └── kix-services-backstage\n```\n\n### Service Repository Structure\n\nEach service repo follows a standard layout:\n\n```\nkix-mcp-gateway/\n├── src/                          # Application source code\n├── Dockerfile                    # Multi-stage Docker build\n├── catalog-info.yaml             # Backstage service catalog descriptor\n├── chart/                        # Service-specific Helm chart\n│   ├── Chart.yaml                # Depends on base-service-chart (OCI)\n│   ├── values.yaml               # Service defaults\n│   └── templates/                # Only if custom K8s resources needed\n├── docs/                         # TechDocs content (Backstage)\n│   └── index.md\n└── .github/workflows/\n    └── ci.yaml                   # Calls reusable workflows\n```\n\n## CI/CD Flow\n\n```\nDeveloper Push → Service Repo (any org) → Standard Pipeline\n  (e.g. konecta-ix-mcp-servers/kix-mcp-gateway)\n       │\n       ▼  Calls reusable workflows from:\n       konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows@v1\n       ├── Lint + Unit Tests\n       ├── Docker Build (BuildKit, multi-stage)\n       ├── Trivy Scan + SBOM (Syft)\n       ├── Push to Artifact Registry\n       └── Cosign Sign (OIDC)\n                │\n                ▼  Dispatch to:\n       konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure\n       Update environments/dev/{service}.yaml with new image tag\n                │\n                ▼\n       ArgoCD watches kix-platform-infrastructure\n         ├── Dev: Auto-sync\n         ├── QA: Manual sync (08:00-18:00 UTC)\n         └── Prod: Manual sync (Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)\n\n       Backstage (shared-backstage)\n         ├── Discovers catalog-info.yaml from all service repos\n         ├── Shows deployment status (ArgoCD + K8s plugins)\n         └── Aggregates docs, CI status, security findings\n```\n\n## Key Design Decisions\n\n| Decision | Rationale |\n|----------|-----------|\n| **Repo-per-service** | Team autonomy, clear ownership, independent release cycles |\n| **Centralised GitOps** | Single source of truth for what's deployed where |\n| **Shared base Helm chart** | Consistency, reduced maintenance, enforced standards |\n| **Cross-org workflow sharing** | DRY principle, single place to update pipeline logic |\n| **Dual ingress** | Different auth models for APIs vs. frontends |\n| **No service mesh (initially)** | Reduce complexity during migration; revisit post-migration |\n\n## Key Differences from Cloud Run\n\n| Aspect | Cloud Run | kix-infrastructure |\n|--------|-----------|-------------------|\n| **DB connection** | Unix socket via VPC connector | Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar (TCP) |\n| **Secrets** | Secret Manager direct access | External Secrets Operator → K8s Secrets |\n| **Scaling** | 0-10 instances (cold starts) | HPA 1-5+ replicas (always warm) |\n| **Ingress** | Direct HTTPS | Dual: Kong (API) + Gateway API + IAP (frontend) |\n| **CI/CD** | Cloud Build + Bitbucket | GitHub Actions + ArgoCD |\n| **Health checks** | Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK | K8s liveness/readiness probes |\n| **Observability** | Fragmented | Unified Prometheus/Grafana/Jaeger |\n\n---\n\n**Next**: [Migration Phases](migration-phases) — Phased approach to migration.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/migration-phases.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Migration Phases\nsidebar_label: Migration Phases\nsidebar_position: 4\ndescription: Phased migration strategy with priorities and timeline\n---\n\n# Migration Phases\n\nThis document describes the phased approach to migrating services from Cloud Run to kix-infrastructure.\n\n## Migration Context\n\n**No services are currently in production.** Services will migrate directly to new domains on kix-infrastructure — no dual-stack, no DNS cutover, no downtime concerns.\n\nAll application services are **stateless** — state lives in Cloud SQL, Firestore, and GCS. Container images contain no data; configuration is provided via ConfigMaps and Secrets.\n\n## Migration Priority\n\n| Priority | Services | Rationale |\n|----------|----------|-----------|\n| **P0 — Pilot** | kix-usecase-iq-portal (backend + frontend) | Validates entire platform: both ingress paths, Cloud SQL sidecar, standard pipeline, cross-org dispatch |\n| **P1 — Platform Core** | Kong, LiteLLM (behind Kong), MCP Gateway | Must be running before other services can function |\n| **P2 — MCP Servers** | Remaining 13 MCP servers | Foundation for AI/business workflows |\n| **P3 — Business Apps** | kix-app-hrfraud, kix-app-hrportal, kix-usecase-testiq | Remaining user-facing applications |\n| **P4 — Data & Demos** | RAG pipelines, bot, prototypes | Lower priority, migrate last |\n\n## Phase 1: Platform Foundation + Pilot\n\n**Focus**: Establish platform infrastructure and validate with the pilot service.\n\n### Platform Infrastructure Tasks\n\n| Task | Repository | Description |\n|------|------------|-------------|\n| Create CI workflows repo | `kix-platform-ci-workflows` | Standard pipeline with reusable workflows |\n| Add base Helm chart | `kix-platform-modules` | Publish to Artifact Registry as OCI chart |\n| Configure ArgoCD | `kix-platform-infrastructure` | ApplicationSets with git directory generator |\n| Create service template | `kix-platform-service-template` | Template repo for bootstrapping |\n| Install GitHub App | Cross-org | Enable cross-org dispatch for GitOps updates |\n| Configure OIDC | Per org | GitHub OIDC → GCP Workload Identity Pool |\n| Deploy Kong + LiteLLM | `shared-kong`, `shared-litellm` | API gateway and LLM proxy |\n| Deploy Backstage | `shared-backstage` | Developer portal with GitHub org discovery |\n\n### Pilot: IQ Portal\n\nDeploy `kix-usecase-iq-portal` as the first service through the full pipeline:\n\n| Validation Point | Description |\n|------------------|-------------|\n| **Backend deployment** | Kong API ingress, Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar, External Secrets |\n| **Frontend deployment** | GKE Gateway API + IAP ingress path, separate LB |\n| **Helm chart profiles** | Both API Service and Frontend profiles |\n| **Cross-org dispatch** | konecta-ix-workloads → konecta-ix-platform GitOps |\n| **Full CI pipeline** | Build, scan, sign, push, deploy |\n\n**Outcome**: Fix any issues in base chart, CI workflows, or ArgoCD config before scaling out.\n\n**Detailed guide**: [IQ Portal Migration Guide](service-guides/iq-portal)\n\n## Phase 2: MCP Servers\n\n**Focus**: Migrate all MCP servers using the validated platform.\n\n### Migration Order\n\n| Order | Service | Priority | Dependencies |\n|-------|---------|----------|--------------|\n| 1 | kix-mcp-gateway | P1 | None — gateway for other MCP servers |\n| 2 | kix-mcp-ragengine | P2 | Vertex AI RAG corpus |\n| 3 | kix-mcp-channelhub | P2 | Channel Hub external API |\n| 4 | kix-mcp-google-search | P2 | Google Search API |\n| 5-14 | Remaining MCP servers | P2-P3 | Various GCP APIs |\n\n### Per-Service Migration Steps\n\nFor each MCP server:\n\n1. Create repo from template in `konecta-ix-mcp-servers`\n2. Copy application code and update configuration\n3. Add `catalog-info.yaml` for Backstage\n4. Push to trigger CI pipeline\n5. Verify deployment in dev\n6. Promote to qa, then prod\n7. Decommission corresponding Cloud Run service\n\n### Expected Outcomes\n\n- All 14 MCP servers running on kix-infrastructure\n- Unified observability via Prometheus/Grafana\n- Service catalog populated in Backstage\n- Cloud Run MCP services decommissioned\n\n## Phase 3: Business Applications\n\n**Focus**: Migrate remaining user-facing applications.\n\n### Migration Order\n\n| Order | Service | Type | Notes |\n|-------|---------|------|-------|\n| 1 | kix-app-hrfraud | Multi-tier (Nuxt + FastAPI + Cloud SQL) | Similar pattern to IQ Portal |\n| 2 | kix-app-hrportal | Frontend only | Nuxt 3 SPA |\n| 3 | kix-usecase-testiq | Frontend only | Nuxt 3 SPA |\n\n### Multi-Tier App Pattern\n\nApps like `kix-app-hrfraud` deploy as **multi-container pods** (see [Kubernetes & Helm](kubernetes-helm#multi-tier-app-pattern)):\n\n- Nuxt frontend (port 3000) + FastAPI backend (port 8000) + cloud-sql-proxy sidecar (port 5432)\n- Frontend calls backend via `localhost:8000`\n- Both containers deploy atomically\n\n### Expected Outcomes\n\n- All business apps running on kix-infrastructure\n- Frontend apps behind GKE Gateway API + IAP\n- Cloud Run business app services decommissioned\n\n## Phase 4: Data Pipelines & Cleanup\n\n**Focus**: Migrate remaining services and decommission Cloud Run.\n\n### Migration Tasks\n\n| Service | Type | Notes |\n|---------|------|-------|\n| kix-usecase-rag-ingestion | Data pipeline | Cloud Tasks integration |\n| kix-usecase-rag-googledrive | Data pipeline | Google Drive API |\n| kix-demo-porfolioguru | Bot | Convert from Cloud Functions |\n| kix-demo-prototypes | Various | Low priority |\n\n### Cleanup Tasks\n\n| Task | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| Decommission Cloud Run | Delete all migrated Cloud Run services |\n| Remove Cloud Build configs | Delete `cloudbuild.yaml` from old repos |\n| Archive old repos | Mark Bitbucket repos as read-only |\n| Update DNS records | Point to kix-infrastructure LB IPs |\n\n## Post-Migration Activities\n\n### Validation\n\n- [ ] All services deployed and healthy\n- [ ] Prometheus metrics flowing\n- [ ] Backstage catalog complete\n- [ ] CI pipelines green for all services\n- [ ] ArgoCD sync status healthy\n\n### Documentation\n\n- [ ] Update runbooks with new procedures\n- [ ] Archive Cloud Run documentation\n- [ ] Train teams on new deployment process\n\n### Optimisation\n\n- [ ] Tune HPA thresholds based on traffic\n- [ ] Optimise resource requests/limits\n- [ ] Review network policies\n- [ ] Implement KEDA for scale-to-zero (if needed)\n\n## Migration Checklist Template\n\nUse this checklist for each service migration:\n\n```markdown\n## Service: {service-name}\n\n### Pre-Migration\n- [ ] Review current Cloud Run configuration\n- [ ] Document environment variables\n- [ ] Identify GCP service dependencies\n- [ ] Create service repo from template\n\n### Migration\n- [ ] Copy application code\n- [ ] Update Dockerfile (pin digests, remove HEALTHCHECK)\n- [ ] Configure Helm values\n- [ ] Add catalog-info.yaml\n- [ ] Create ExternalSecret for secrets\n- [ ] Push and trigger CI pipeline\n\n### Validation\n- [ ] Pod running (container + sidecars healthy)\n- [ ] Health probes passing\n- [ ] Ingress routing correctly\n- [ ] Logs visible in monitoring\n- [ ] Functional smoke tests pass\n\n### Cutover\n- [ ] Update DNS (if applicable)\n- [ ] Monitor for issues\n- [ ] Decommission Cloud Run service\n\n### Post-Migration\n- [ ] Service visible in Backstage\n- [ ] Metrics in Grafana dashboard\n- [ ] Alerts configured\n```\n\n---\n\n**Next**: [Kubernetes & Helm](kubernetes-helm) — Helm chart design and Kubernetes configuration.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/kubernetes-helm.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Kubernetes & Helm\nsidebar_label: Kubernetes & Helm\nsidebar_position: 5\ndescription: Helm chart strategy, namespace design, resource profiles, and autoscaling\n---\n\n# Kubernetes & Helm\n\nThis document covers Helm chart design, namespace strategy, resource profiles, health probes, and autoscaling configuration.\n\n## Helm Chart Strategy\n\n### Three-Layer Architecture\n\nThe platform uses a three-layer Helm architecture matching the multi-org, repo-per-service model:\n\n```\nLayer 1: Base Chart Library (platform org)\n         └── kix-platform-modules/helm/base-service-chart/\n             ├── Chart.yaml (type: library)\n             └── templates/_*.tpl\n\nLayer 2: Service Chart (each service repo)\n         └── kix-mcp-gateway/chart/\n             ├── Chart.yaml (depends on base chart)\n             └── values.yaml (service defaults)\n\nLayer 3: Environment Values (GitOps repo)\n         └── kix-platform-infrastructure/environments/\n             ├── dev/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml\n             ├── qa/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml\n             └── prod/kix-mcp-gateway.yaml\n```\n\n### Base Chart Library\n\nPublished to Artifact Registry as an OCI Helm chart. Contains templated resources:\n\n| Template | Purpose |\n|----------|---------|\n| `_deployment.tpl` | Pod spec, containers, sidecars |\n| `_service.tpl` | K8s Service definitions |\n| `_ingress.tpl` | Kong Ingress / HTTPRoute |\n| `_hpa.tpl` | HorizontalPodAutoscaler |\n| `_pdb.tpl` | PodDisruptionBudget |\n| `_networkpolicy.tpl` | Network isolation |\n| `_serviceaccount.tpl` | Workload Identity |\n| `_external-secret.tpl` | External Secrets CRD |\n\n### Service Chart\n\nEach service chart depends on the base chart and provides service-specific defaults:\n\n```yaml\n# chart/Chart.yaml\napiVersion: v2\nname: kix-mcp-gateway\nversion: 0.1.0\ntype: application\n\ndependencies:\n  - name: base-service-chart\n    version: \"~1.0.0\"\n    repository: \"oci://europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-platform/helm-charts\"\n```\n\n### Base Chart Versioning\n\n- Published to Artifact Registry as OCI Helm chart\n- Service charts pin to a specific version (`~1.0.0`)\n- Renovate automates version bumps across all orgs\n- Breaking changes require major version bump\n\n## Namespace Strategy\n\n### Environment × Domain Boundaries (16 Namespaces)\n\n| Namespace Pattern | Contents |\n|-------------------|----------|\n| `{env}-api-services` | FastAPI backends, business apps, bots, data pipelines |\n| `{env}-mcp-servers` | MCP protocol servers |\n| `{env}-web-apps` | Nuxt 3 frontends |\n| `shared-kong` | Kong API Gateway |\n| `shared-litellm` | LiteLLM proxy (Kong-only access) |\n| `shared-argocd` | ArgoCD (existing) |\n| `shared-backstage` | Backstage developer portal |\n| `shared-monitoring` | Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger |\n| `shared-devsecops` | SonarQube, Dependency-Track |\n| `shared-cert-manager` | TLS certificate management |\n\n### Namespace Configuration\n\nEach namespace receives:\n\n- **ResourceQuotas**: CPU/memory limits per namespace\n- **LimitRanges**: Default container limits\n- **NetworkPolicies**: Default deny-all ingress\n- **RBAC bindings**: Team-scoped access\n\n## Resource Profiles\n\n### Standard Profiles\n\n| Profile | CPU Request | CPU Limit | Mem Request | Mem Limit | Use Case |\n|---------|-------------|-----------|-------------|-----------|----------|\n| **API Service** | 100m | 500m | 256Mi | 512Mi | FastAPI APIs, MCP servers, bots, data pipelines |\n| **API Heavy** | 250m | 1000m | 512Mi | 1Gi | RAG ingestion, document processing, ML dependencies |\n| **Frontend** | 50m | 200m | 128Mi | 256Mi | Nuxt 3 SSR |\n\n### Cloud SQL Proxy Sidecar\n\nWhen using Cloud SQL Auth Proxy:\n\n```yaml\nresources:\n  requests:\n    cpu: 50m\n    memory: 64Mi\n  limits:\n    cpu: 200m\n    memory: 128Mi\n```\n\n## Health Probes\n\n### Probe Configuration by Service Type\n\n| Service Type | Liveness | Readiness | Startup |\n|--------------|----------|-----------|---------|\n| **FastAPI** | `GET /healthz` :8000 | `GET /readyz` :8000 (checks DB) | N/A |\n| **MCP Server (SSE)** | `GET /healthz` :8000, period=30s | `GET /readyz` :8000, initial=30s | N/A |\n| **Nuxt 3** | `GET /` :3000 | `GET /_health` :3000 | N/A |\n| **Heavy services** | `GET /healthz` :8000 | `GET /readyz` :8000 | failureThreshold=18 (3min) |\n\n### Example Probe Configuration\n\n```yaml\nprobes:\n  liveness:\n    httpGet:\n      path: /healthz\n      port: 8080\n    initialDelaySeconds: 30\n    periodSeconds: 15\n    failureThreshold: 3\n  readiness:\n    httpGet:\n      path: /readyz\n      port: 8080\n    initialDelaySeconds: 40\n    periodSeconds: 10\n    failureThreshold: 3\n  startup:\n    httpGet:\n      path: /healthz\n      port: 8080\n    initialDelaySeconds: 10\n    periodSeconds: 5\n    failureThreshold: 12  # 60s max startup\n```\n\n## Autoscaling (HPA)\n\n### HPA Configuration by Tier\n\n| Tier | Min | Max | Target CPU | Scale-Down Window |\n|------|-----|-----|------------|-------------------|\n| **User-facing** (frontends, public APIs) | 2 | 20 | 60% | 5 min |\n| **Internal APIs, MCP servers** | 2 | 10 | 75% | 10 min |\n| **Data pipelines, bots** | 1 | 5 | 80% | 15 min |\n\n### Custom Metrics\n\nVia Prometheus Adapter:\n\n- Request rate\n- Pub/Sub queue depth\n- Active SSE connections\n\n### Example HPA Values\n\n```yaml\nautoscaling:\n  enabled: true\n  minReplicas: 2\n  maxReplicas: 10\n  targetCPUUtilization: 70\n  targetMemoryUtilization: 80\n```\n\n## Cloud SQL Auth Proxy Sidecar\n\nServices connecting to Cloud SQL use the **Cloud SQL Auth Proxy** as a sidecar container.\n\n### Why Sidecar (Not Shared Proxy)\n\n| Benefit | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **Per-pod authentication** | Workload Identity per service account |\n| **Least-privilege IAM** | Each service has minimum required permissions |\n| **No bottleneck** | Scales with application pods |\n| **Google-recommended** | Official pattern for GKE |\n\n### How It Works\n\n```\n┌────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  Pod                                       │\n│  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌────────────────┐  │\n│  │  App Container   │  │  cloud-sql-    │  │\n│  │  (FastAPI)       │──│  proxy sidecar │────────▶ Cloud SQL\n│  │  :8000           │  │  :5432 (TCP)   │  │\n│  └──────────────────┘  └────────────────┘  │\n│  localhost:5432                            │\n└────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n### Helm Values Configuration\n\n```yaml\ncloudSqlProxy:\n  enabled: true\n  instanceConnectionName: \"project:region:instance\"\n  port: 5432\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: 50m\n      memory: 64Mi\n    limits:\n      cpu: 200m\n      memory: 128Mi\n```\n\n### Connection String Pattern\n\n```\nDATABASE_URL=postgresql+asyncpg://user@localhost:5432/dbname\n```\n\nNo password needed when using `--auto-iam-authn` with Workload Identity.\n\n## Multi-Tier App Pattern\n\nTwo-tiered apps (Nuxt frontend + FastAPI backend) deploy as **multi-container pods**.\n\n### Pod Layout\n\n```\n┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  Pod: kix-app-hrfraud                                            │\n│                                                                  │\n│  ┌──────────────────┐   localhost:8000   ┌────────────────────┐  │\n│  │  frontend        │ ─────────────────▶ │  backend           │  │\n│  │  (Nuxt SSR)      │                    │  (FastAPI)         │  │\n│  │  :3000           │                    │  :8000             │  │\n│  └──────────────────┘                    └─────────┬──────────┘  │\n│                                                    │ localhost   │\n│                                          ┌─────────▼──────────┐  │\n│                                          │  cloud-sql-proxy   │  │\n│                                          │  :5432             │───▶ Cloud SQL\n│                                          └────────────────────┘  │\n└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n         ▲                    ▲\n         │                    │\n    Frontend LB          API LB (Kong)\n  (Gateway API + IAP)   (internal routes)\n```\n\n### Benefits\n\n| Benefit | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **localhost communication** | Zero network hop between frontend and backend |\n| **Atomic deploys** | Both containers deploy together, no version skew |\n| **Shared lifecycle** | Same release cycle, same Helm chart |\n| **Simpler networking** | No inter-service DNS, K8s Service only for external access |\n| **Fewer resources** | One Deployment, one HPA, one PDB |\n\n### When NOT to Use\n\n- Services needing independent scaling\n- Very different resource profiles\n- Loosely coupled services with different release cadences\n- Static SPA frontends (serve from CDN instead)\n\n### Helm Values Example\n\n```yaml\ncontainers:\n  backend:\n    image:\n      repository: eu-docker.pkg.dev/project/kix-services/kix-app-hrfraud-backend\n      tag: sha-abc1234\n    port: 8000\n    env:\n      DATABASE_URL: \"postgresql+asyncpg://user@localhost:5432/dbname\"\n      LITELLM_ENDPOINT: \"http://kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/llm\"\n\n  frontend:\n    image:\n      repository: eu-docker.pkg.dev/project/kix-services/kix-app-hrfraud-frontend\n      tag: sha-def5678\n    port: 3000\n    env:\n      API_BASE: \"http://localhost:8000\"\n\ncloudSqlProxy:\n  enabled: true\n  instanceConnectionName: \"project:eu-west1:hrfraud-db\"\n\nservices:\n  api:\n    port: 8000\n    ingress:\n      class: kong\n      path: /api/hrfraud\n  frontend:\n    port: 3000\n    ingress:\n      class: gateway-api\n      host: hrfraud.app.konecta.io\n```\n\n## ArgoCD ApplicationSet Strategy\n\n### Git Directory Generator\n\nArgoCD watches `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure`. Each file in `environments/{env}/` defines an application.\n\n| Environment | Sync Policy | Change Window |\n|-------------|-------------|---------------|\n| **Dev** | `automated` (auto-sync) | 24/7 |\n| **QA** | `automated: false` (manual) | 08:00-18:00 UTC |\n| **Prod** | `automated: false` (manual) | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |\n\n### ArgoCD Projects\n\n- `dev-project` — RBAC for dev namespace deployments\n- `qa-project` — RBAC for qa namespace deployments\n- `prod-project` — RBAC for prod namespace deployments\n\n### Sync Waves\n\n- **Wave 0**: Infrastructure resources (namespaces, secrets, configmaps)\n- **Wave 10**: Application deployments\n\n---\n\n**Next**: [Ingress & Networking](ingress-networking) — Dual ingress architecture and network policies.\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/ingress-networking.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Ingress & Networking\nsidebar_label: Ingress & Networking\nsidebar_position: 6\ndescription: Dual ingress architecture, network policies, and load balancing\n---\n\n# Ingress & Networking\n\nThis document covers the dual ingress architecture, network policies, and load balancing configuration.\n\n## Dual Load Balancer Architecture\n\nTwo separate ingress paths with independent load balancers provide isolation between API and frontend traffic.\n\n### Path 1: API / Backend Ingress (Kong)\n\n```\nInternet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF)\n         → Kong Ingress Controller (shared-kong)\n         → {env}-api-services, {env}-mcp-servers\n```\n\n| Component | Purpose |\n|-----------|---------|\n| **GCP LB** | External load balancer with static IP |\n| **Cloud Armor** | WAF with OWASP top-10 rules, DDoS protection |\n| **Kong** | API routing, rate limiting, authentication plugins, CORS |\n\n#### Kong Configuration\n\n- Routes configured via `konghq.com/plugins` annotations per Ingress resource\n- Rate limiting per consumer/route\n- JWT validation for API auth\n- CORS headers for cross-origin requests\n\n### Path 2: Frontend Ingress (GKE Gateway API + IAP)\n\n```\nInternet → GCP External LB + Cloud Armor (WAF) + GKE Gateway API\n         → IAP (Identity-Aware Proxy) / IAM authentication\n         → {env}-web-apps (Nuxt 3 frontends)\n```\n\n| Component | Purpose |\n|-----------|---------|\n| **GKE Gateway API** | Native GKE ingress using `Gateway` and `HTTPRoute` resources |\n| **IAP** | Google-managed identity layer — authenticates users via Google/OIDC |\n| **Cloud Armor** | Same WAF policies as API path |\n\n#### IAP Configuration\n\n- Configured via `BackendConfig` with IAP enabled\n- Access controlled by IAM policies on the IAP resource\n- Only authorised users/groups can reach the frontend\n- No Kong involvement for frontend traffic\n\n### Internal: LiteLLM via Kong\n\nAll LLM traffic routes through Kong to LiteLLM:\n\n```\nAny service pod → Kong (internal route)\n               → shared-litellm (LiteLLM proxy)\n               → Vertex AI / LLM providers\n```\n\n| Benefit | Description |\n|---------|-------------|\n| **Centralised access control** | Rate limiting, auth on LLM traffic |\n| **Usage metering** | Consistent billing attribution |\n| **Audit trail** | All LLM requests logged |\n| **No direct access** | NetworkPolicy blocks direct LiteLLM access |\n\n## Network Policies\n\n### Default Deny\n\nAll application namespaces have **default deny ingress**:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: NetworkPolicy\nmetadata:\n  name: default-deny-ingress\n  namespace: dev-api-services\nspec:\n  podSelector: {}\n  policyTypes:\n    - Ingress\n```\n\n### GCP Health Check IPs\n\n**MANDATORY** for any service behind a load balancer:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: NetworkPolicy\nmetadata:\n  name: allow-gcp-health-checks\nspec:\n  podSelector: {}\n  ingress:\n    - from:\n        - ipBlock:\n            cidr: 35.191.0.0/16\n        - ipBlock:\n            cidr: 130.211.0.0/22\n      ports:\n        - protocol: TCP\n          port: 8080\n```\n\n:::warning\n70% of network policy issues stem from missing GCP health check IPs. Without these rules, backends are marked unhealthy and traffic doesn't flow.\n:::\n\n### Allow from Kong\n\nFor API services:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: NetworkPolicy\nmetadata:\n  name: allow-from-kong\nspec:\n  podSelector:\n    matchLabels:\n      app: kix-mcp-gateway\n  ingress:\n    - from:\n        - namespaceSelector:\n            matchLabels:\n              name: shared-kong\n      ports:\n        - protocol: TCP\n          port: 8080\n```\n\n### Allow from Gateway API\n\nFor frontend services:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: NetworkPolicy\nmetadata:\n  name: allow-from-gateway\nspec:\n  podSelector:\n    matchLabels:\n      app: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n  ingress:\n    - from:\n        - namespaceSelector:\n            matchLabels:\n              name: shared-gateway\n      ports:\n        - protocol: TCP\n          port: 3000\n```\n\n### LiteLLM Isolation\n\nLiteLLM only accepts traffic from Kong:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: NetworkPolicy\nmetadata:\n  name: litellm-kong-only\n  namespace: shared-litellm\nspec:\n  podSelector:\n    matchLabels:\n      app: litellm\n  ingress:\n    - from:\n        - namespaceSelector:\n            matchLabels:\n              name: shared-kong\n      ports:\n        - protocol: TCP\n          port: 8000\n```\n\n## TLS Configuration\n\n### Certificate Management\n\n- **cert-manager** in `shared-cert-manager` namespace\n- **Let's Encrypt** issuer for automatic certificates\n- Separate certificates per domain\n\n### DNS by Environment\n\n| Environment | API Domain | Frontend Domain |\n|-------------|------------|-----------------|\n| Dev | `dev.api.konecta.io` | `dev.app.konecta.io` |\n| QA | `qa.api.konecta.io` | `qa.app.konecta.io` |\n| Prod | `api.konecta.io` | `app.konecta.io` |\n\n### Service-Specific Domains\n\nFrontend services get dedicated subdomains:\n\n| Service | Dev | Prod |\n|---------|-----|------|\n| IQ Portal | `iq-portal.dev.konecta.io` | `iq-portal.app.konecta.io` |\n| HR Fraud | `hrfraud.dev.konecta.io` | `hrfraud.app.konecta.io` |\n\n## Ingress Resource Examples\n\n### Kong Ingress (API)\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: Ingress\nmetadata:\n  name: kix-mcp-gateway\n  namespace: dev-mcp-servers\n  annotations:\n    kubernetes.io/ingress.class: kong\n    konghq.com/strip-path: \"true\"\n    konghq.com/protocols: \"https\"\n    konghq.com/plugins: rate-limiting-plugin\nspec:\n  rules:\n    - host: api.konecta.io\n      http:\n        paths:\n          - path: /mcp-gateway\n            pathType: Prefix\n            backend:\n              service:\n                name: kix-mcp-gateway\n                port:\n                  number: 8080\n  tls:\n    - hosts:\n        - api.konecta.io\n      secretName: api-tls-secret\n```\n\n### GKE Gateway API (Frontend)\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: HTTPRoute\nmetadata:\n  name: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n  namespace: dev-api-services\nspec:\n  parentRefs:\n    - name: frontend-gateway\n      namespace: shared-gateway\n  hostnames:\n    - iq-portal.dev.konecta.io\n  rules:\n    - matches:\n        - path:\n            type: PathPrefix\n            value: /\n      backendRefs:\n        - name: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n          port: 3000\n```\n\n### BackendConfig for IAP\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: cloud.google.com/v1\nkind: BackendConfig\nmetadata:\n  name: kix-usecase-iq-portal-iap\n  namespace: dev-api-services\nspec:\n  iap:\n    enabled: true\n    oauthclientCredentials:\n      secretName: iap-oauth-credentials\n```\n\n## Internal Service Communication\n\n### ClusterIP Services\n\nInternal services use ClusterIP (no external access):\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: v1\nkind: Service\nmetadata:\n  name: kix-mcp-gateway\n  namespace: dev-mcp-servers\nspec:\n  type: ClusterIP\n  selector:\n    app: kix-mcp-gateway\n  ports:\n    - port: 8080\n      targetPort: 8080\n```\n\n### Service Discovery\n\nServices access each other via:\n\n```\nservice.namespace.svc.cluster.local\n```\n\nExamples:\n- Kong: `kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local`\n- LiteLLM (via Kong): `kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/llm`\n- MCP Gateway: `kix-mcp-gateway.dev-mcp-servers.svc.cluster.local`\n\n## Cloud Armor WAF Rules\n\nBoth load balancers use Cloud Armor with OWASP top-10 protection:\n\n| Rule | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| SQL injection | Block SQLi attempts |\n| XSS | Block cross-site scripting |\n| Remote file inclusion | Block RFI attacks |\n| Local file inclusion | Block LFI attempts |\n| Protocol attack | Block HTTP protocol violations |\n| Scanner detection | Block known scanner signatures |\n| DDoS protection | Rate limiting at edge |\n\n## Service Mesh Decision\n\n**Decision: No service mesh initially.**\n\n| Consideration | Rationale |\n|---------------|-----------|\n| **Kong handles ingress** | Traffic management, auth, rate limiting already covered |\n| **Migration complexity** | Adding Istio/Linkerd during migration adds risk |\n| **Resource overhead** | Sidecar proxies add CPU/memory overhead |\n| **Revisit post-migration** | Consider if mTLS or advanced traffic management needed |\n\n---\n\n**Next**: [Docker Security](docker-security) — Secure multi-stage builds and hardening.\n"
}
```
