```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/app-migration/service-guides/iq-portal.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: IQ Portal Migration Guide\nsidebar_label: IQ Portal\nsidebar_position: 2\ndescription: Complete migration guide for IQ Portal from Cloud Run to kix-infrastructure\n---\n\n# IQ Portal Migration Guide\n\n**Service**: kon-iq-portal → `kix-usecase-iq-portal`\n**Target Org**: `konecta-ix-workloads`\n**Phase**: P0 (Pilot)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Current State Assessment\n\n### Architecture\n\nIQ Portal is a **single-container monolith**: an Express.js (TypeScript) backend serves both the API (`/api/*`) and a pre-generated Nuxt 4 SPA (static files from `/dist`). Everything runs on port **8080** in production.\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  Cloud Run (kon-iq-portal)              │\n│                                         │\n│  Express.js :8080                       │\n│  ├── /api/*  → API routes               │\n│  ├── /docs   → Swagger UI               │\n│  └── /*      → Static Nuxt SPA          │\n│                                         │\n│  Cloud SQL (Unix socket)                │\n│  /cloudsql/project:region:instance      │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n### Current Tech Stack\n\n| Component | Detail |\n|-----------|--------|\n| Runtime | Node.js 22 (Alpine) |\n| Frontend | Nuxt 4 (SPA, `ssr: false`, pre-generated static) |\n| Backend | Express 5 (TypeScript, via `tsx`) |\n| Database | PostgreSQL on Cloud SQL (`kd-lab-464110:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform`) |\n| DB Connection | Unix socket via VPC connector (`DB_HOST=/cloudsql/...`) |\n| Migrations | Custom SQL/TS runner (33 migrations, runs on startup) |\n| Auth | Google OAuth → JWT in HttpOnly cookies |\n| Storage | GCS (3 buckets: images, videos, RAG docs) |\n| AI | Vertex AI (Gemini) + RAG corpus |\n| Async Jobs | Cloud Tasks → kon-rag-ingestion |\n| Email | ChannelHub (external Cognito-auth API) |\n| CI/CD | Cloud Build from Bitbucket |\n| Port | 8080 (production), 3002 (dev) |\n\n### GCP Service Dependencies\n\n| Service | Project | Used For |\n|---------|---------|----------|\n| Cloud SQL (PostgreSQL) | `kd-lab-464110` | Primary database |\n| Cloud Storage | `kd-corp-dgt-svc-apps-dev` | Image/video/RAG file storage |\n| Vertex AI | `kd-lab-464110` | Gemini LLM + RAG retrieval |\n| Cloud Tasks | `kd-lab-464110` | Async RAG document ingestion |\n| Secret Manager | `kd-lab-464110` | JWT secret, DB password, RAG token |\n\n---\n\n## 2. Target State\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  kix-infrastructure GKE Cluster                                     │\n│                                                                     │\n│  Namespace: dev-api-services (then qa-api-services, prod-...)       │\n│                                                                     │\n│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │\n│  │  Pod: kix-usecase-iq-portal                                   │  │\n│  │                                                               │  │\n│  │  ┌──────────────────────┐   ┌──────────────────────────────┐  │  │\n│  │  │  app (Express)       │   │  cloud-sql-proxy sidecar     │  │  │\n│  │  │  :8080               │──▶│  :5432 (TCP)                 │──────▶ Cloud SQL\n│  │  │  /api/* + SPA        │   │  --private-ip                │  │  │\n│  │  └──────────────────────┘   └──────────────────────────────┘  │  │\n│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │\n│      │                                                              │\n│      ▼                                                              │\n│  Service: kix-usecase-iq-portal :8080                               │\n│      │                         │                                    │\n│      ▼                         ▼                                    │\n│  Kong Ingress              GKE Gateway API                          │\n│  /api/iq-portal/*          iq-portal.app.konecta.io                 │\n│  (API consumers)           (browser users via IAP)                  │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n### Key Differences from Cloud Run\n\n| Aspect | Cloud Run | kix-infrastructure |\n|--------|-----------|-------------------|\n| DB connection | Unix socket via VPC connector | Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar (TCP `localhost:5432`) |\n| Secrets | Secret Manager direct access | External Secrets Operator → K8s Secrets |\n| Scaling | 0-10 instances | HPA 1-5 replicas |\n| Ingress | Direct HTTPS | Dual: Kong (API) + Gateway API + IAP (frontend) |\n| CI/CD | Cloud Build + Bitbucket | GitHub Actions + ArgoCD |\n| Health checks | Dockerfile HEALTHCHECK | K8s liveness/readiness probes |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Pre-requisites\n\nBefore starting, ensure:\n\n- [ ] Access to `konecta-ix-workloads` GitHub org (write)\n- [ ] Access to `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-infrastructure` GitOps repo (write)\n- [ ] Access to `konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows` (read)\n- [ ] `kix-platform-modules` base Helm chart published to Artifact Registry\n- [ ] ArgoCD ApplicationSet configured with git directory generator\n- [ ] Shared Kong gateway operational in `shared-kong` namespace\n- [ ] GKE Gateway API + IAP configured on frontend LB\n- [ ] External Secrets Operator running in `shared-secrets` namespace\n- [ ] GCP Workload Identity pool configured for GitHub Actions OIDC\n- [ ] Backstage operational in `shared-backstage` namespace\n- [ ] Cloud SQL instance accessible from GKE cluster (private IP, same VPC)\n- [ ] GCP service account for IQ Portal workload with IAM roles:\n  - `roles/cloudsql.client`\n  - `roles/storage.objectAdmin`\n  - `roles/aiplatform.user`\n  - `roles/cloudtasks.enqueuer`\n  - `roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor`\n\n---\n\n## Step 1: Create the New Repository\n\n### 1.1 Create repo from template\n\n```bash\ngh repo create konecta-ix-workloads/kix-usecase-iq-portal \\\n  --template konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-service-template \\\n  --private\n```\n\n### 1.2 Clone and set up\n\n```bash\ngit clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-workloads/kix-usecase-iq-portal.git\ncd kix-usecase-iq-portal\n```\n\n### 1.3 Copy application code\n\nCopy the entire application source from the current `kon-iq-portal` repo. Final structure:\n\n```\nkix-usecase-iq-portal/\n├── backend/              # Express.js API\n├── src/                  # Nuxt 4 frontend\n├── public/               # Static assets\n├── scripts/              # Deployment scripts\n├── nuxt.config.ts\n├── package.json\n├── package-lock.json\n├── tsconfig*.json\n├── version.txt\n├── Dockerfile            # Modified (see Step 3)\n├── docker-entrypoint.sh\n├── .dockerignore\n├── catalog-info.yaml     # New (Backstage)\n├── chart/                # New (Helm chart)\n│   ├── Chart.yaml\n│   └── values.yaml\n├── docs/                 # New (TechDocs)\n│   └── index.md\n├── .github/workflows/    # New (CI)\n│   └── ci.yaml\n├── CODEOWNERS\n└── .env.example\n```\n\n### 1.4 Remove Cloud Build config\n\n```bash\nrm cloudbuild.yaml\n```\n\n### 1.5 Add CODEOWNERS\n\n```\n# Default owners\n* @konecta-ix-workloads/iq-portal-team\n```\n\n### 1.6 Add .dockerignore\n\n```\nnode_modules\n.nuxt\n.output\ndist\n.git\n.github\nchart\ndocs\n*.md\n.env*\n!.env.example\n```\n\n### 1.7 Add catalog-info.yaml\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: backstage.io/v1alpha1\nkind: Component\nmetadata:\n  name: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n  description: Konecta IQ Portal — AI-powered knowledge platform\n  annotations:\n    github.com/project-slug: konecta-ix-workloads/kix-usecase-iq-portal\n    argocd/app-name: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n    backstage.io/kubernetes-id: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n    backstage.io/kubernetes-namespace: dev-api-services,qa-api-services,prod-api-services\n    backstage.io/techdocs-ref: dir:.\n    sonarqube.org/project-key: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n  tags:\n    - nodejs\n    - express\n    - nuxt\n    - typescript\n    - cloud-sql\nspec:\n  type: service\n  lifecycle: production\n  owner: group:iq-portal-team\n  system: kix-platform\n  dependsOn:\n    - resource:cloud-sql-iqplatform\n    - component:kix-services-kong\n    - component:kix-mcp-gateway\n```\n\n### 1.8 Add TechDocs\n\nCreate `docs/index.md`:\n\n```markdown\n# IQ Portal\n\nAI-powered knowledge platform for Konecta.\n\n## API\n\nSwagger documentation available at `/docs` on the running service.\n\n## Runbook\n\n- Health check: `GET /health` (liveness), `GET /api/health` (readiness)\n- Logs: `kubectl logs -n {env}-api-services -l app=kix-usecase-iq-portal -c app`\n- Database: Cloud SQL `kd-lab-464110:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform`\n```\n\nCreate `mkdocs.yml`:\n\n```yaml\nsite_name: IQ Portal\nnav:\n  - Home: index.md\nplugins:\n  - techdocs-core\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Application Code Changes\n\n### 2.1 Database connection\n\n**No code changes required.** The existing `database.ts` supports both Unix socket (Cloud Run) and TCP (K8s):\n\n```typescript\n// backend/db/database.ts — already handles both\nconst pool = process.env.DATABASE_URL\n    ? new Pool({ connectionString: process.env.DATABASE_URL })\n    : new Pool({\n        host: process.env.DB_HOST || 'localhost',\n        port: parseInt(process.env.DB_PORT || '5432'),\n        // ...\n    });\n```\n\nOn kix, environment variables will be:\n```\nDB_HOST=localhost        # Cloud SQL Auth Proxy sidecar\nDB_PORT=5432\n```\n\n### 2.2 Add root-level health endpoint\n\nAdd to `backend/routes/index.ts`:\n\n```typescript\n// K8S HEALTH PROBES\n// Lightweight endpoint for Kubernetes liveness probes\napp.get('/health', (req, res) => {\n    res.status(200).json({ status: 'ok' });\n});\n```\n\nKeep `/api/health` for detailed checks (DB + GCP connectivity).\n\n### 2.3 Environment variables\n\nAll changes are via Helm values, no code changes:\n\n| Variable | kix Value |\n|----------|-----------|\n| `DB_HOST` | `localhost` (sidecar) |\n| `DB_PORT` | `5432` |\n| `MCP_GATEWAY_URL` | `http://kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/mcp-gateway` |\n| `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | `https://iq-portal.app.konecta.io` |\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Dockerfile Changes\n\n### 3.1 Pin base image digests\n\nReplace all `FROM node:22-alpine` with pinned digests:\n\n```dockerfile\nFROM node:22-alpine@sha256:<current-digest> AS dependencies\n```\n\n### 3.2 Remove HEALTHCHECK instruction\n\nDelete the `HEALTHCHECK` block (lines 142-157) — K8s probes replace it.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Helm Chart\n\n### 4.1 Chart.yaml\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: v2\nname: kix-usecase-iq-portal\nversion: 0.1.0\ntype: application\n\ndependencies:\n  - name: base-service-chart\n    version: \"~1.0.0\"\n    repository: \"oci://europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-platform/helm-charts\"\n```\n\n### 4.2 values.yaml\n\n```yaml\nbase-service-chart:\n  nameOverride: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n\n  image:\n    repository: europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-platform/docker/kix-usecase-iq-portal\n    tag: \"latest\"  # Overridden by CI\n\n  containers:\n    app:\n      port: 8080\n      env:\n        NODE_ENV: \"production\"\n        PORT: \"8080\"\n        DB_HOST: \"localhost\"\n        DB_PORT: \"5432\"\n        DB_NAME: \"iqplatform\"\n        GCP_PROJECT_ID: \"kd-corp-dgt-svc-apps-dev\"\n        GCP_LOCATION: \"europe-west1\"\n        VERTEX_AI_PROJECT_ID: \"kd-lab-464110\"\n        VERTEX_AI_LOCATION: \"us-central1\"\n        VERTEX_AI_MODEL: \"gemini-2.5-flash\"\n        RAG_PROJECT_ID: \"kd-lab-464110\"\n        RAG_LOCATION: \"europe-west4\"\n        RAG_CORPUS_ID: \"4532873024948404224\"\n        MCP_GATEWAY_URL: \"http://kong.shared-kong.svc.cluster.local/mcp-gateway\"\n        CLOUD_TASKS_PROJECT_ID: \"kd-lab-464110\"\n        CLOUD_TASKS_QUEUE: \"rag-ingestion-poc\"\n        CLOUD_TASKS_LOCATION: \"europe-west1\"\n        CHANNELHUB_URL: \"https://api.konecta.cloud/one-channel-hub/prd\"\n        CHANNELHUB_LOGIN_URL: \"https://ch-prd.auth.eu-west-1.amazoncognito.com\"\n        LOG_LEVEL: \"info\"\n      envFrom:\n        - secretRef:\n            name: kix-usecase-iq-portal-secrets\n      resources:\n        requests:\n          cpu: 250m\n          memory: 256Mi\n        limits:\n          cpu: \"1\"\n          memory: 512Mi\n\n  cloudSqlProxy:\n    enabled: true\n    instanceConnectionName: \"kd-lab-464110:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform\"\n    port: 5432\n\n  serviceAccount:\n    create: true\n    annotations:\n      iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: kix-iq-portal@kd-ix-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com\n\n  probes:\n    liveness:\n      httpGet:\n        path: /health\n        port: 8080\n      initialDelaySeconds: 30\n      periodSeconds: 15\n    readiness:\n      httpGet:\n        path: /api/health\n        port: 8080\n      initialDelaySeconds: 40\n      periodSeconds: 10\n    startup:\n      httpGet:\n        path: /health\n        port: 8080\n      initialDelaySeconds: 10\n      periodSeconds: 5\n      failureThreshold: 12\n\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 5\n    targetCPUUtilization: 70\n\n  services:\n    api:\n      port: 8080\n      ingress:\n        class: kong\n        path: /api/iq-portal\n        stripPath: true\n    frontend:\n      port: 8080\n      ingress:\n        class: gateway-api\n        host: iq-portal.app.konecta.io\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: GitHub Actions CI Workflow\n\nCreate `.github/workflows/ci.yaml`:\n\n```yaml\nname: CI Pipeline\n\non:\n  push:\n    branches: [main, develop, staging]\n  pull_request:\n    branches: [main]\n\npermissions:\n  contents: read\n  id-token: write\n  security-events: write\n\njobs:\n  pipeline:\n    uses: konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-ci-workflows/.github/workflows/standard-pipeline.yaml@v1\n    with:\n      service-name: kix-usecase-iq-portal\n      dockerfile-path: ./Dockerfile\n      chart-path: ./chart\n      node-version: \"22\"\n      build-args: |\n        VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID=${{ vars.VITE_GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID }}\n        NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT=${{ vars.ENVIRONMENT }}\n        COMMIT_SHA=${{ github.sha }}\n      skip-tests: true\n    secrets: inherit\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Secrets Migration\n\n### 6.1 Create secrets in GCP Secret Manager\n\n```bash\nPROJECT=kd-ix-platform\n\n# For each secret:\necho -n \"value\" | gcloud secrets create kix-iq-portal-db-password \\\n  --project=$PROJECT --replication-policy=automatic --data-file=-\n```\n\n### 6.2 Create ExternalSecret\n\nAdd to `chart/templates/external-secret.yaml`:\n\n```yaml\napiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1\nkind: ExternalSecret\nmetadata:\n  name: kix-usecase-iq-portal-secrets\nspec:\n  refreshInterval: 1h\n  secretStoreRef:\n    name: gcp-secret-store\n    kind: SecretStore\n  target:\n    name: kix-usecase-iq-portal-secrets\n    creationPolicy: Owner\n  data:\n    - secretKey: DB_PASSWORD\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-db-password\n    - secretKey: DB_USER\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-db-user\n    - secretKey: JWT_SECRET\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-jwt-secret\n    - secretKey: GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-google-client-id\n    - secretKey: RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-rag-pipeline-token\n    - secretKey: CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_ID\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-channelhub-client-id\n    - secretKey: CHANNELHUB_CLIENT_SECRET\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-channelhub-client-secret\n    - secretKey: CHANNELHUB_CONNECTION_ID\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-channelhub-connection-id\n    - secretKey: GCS_IMAGE_BUCKET\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-gcs-image-bucket\n    - secretKey: GCS_VIDEO_BUCKET\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-gcs-video-bucket\n    - secretKey: GCS_RAG_BUCKET\n      remoteRef:\n        key: kix-iq-portal-gcs-rag-bucket\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: GitOps Configuration\n\n### 7.1 Add environment values\n\nIn `kix-platform-infrastructure`, create:\n\n```\nenvironments/\n├── dev/api-services/kix-usecase-iq-portal.yaml\n├── qa/api-services/kix-usecase-iq-portal.yaml\n└── prod/api-services/kix-usecase-iq-portal.yaml\n```\n\n### 7.2 Dev environment values\n\n```yaml\n# environments/dev/api-services/kix-usecase-iq-portal.yaml\nbase-service-chart:\n  image:\n    tag: \"sha-<initial-commit>\"\n\n  containers:\n    app:\n      env:\n        NODE_ENV: \"development\"\n        NUXT_PUBLIC_ENVIRONMENT: \"development\"\n        ALLOWED_ORIGINS: \"https://iq-portal.dev.konecta.io\"\n        LOG_LEVEL: \"debug\"\n\n  autoscaling:\n    minReplicas: 1\n    maxReplicas: 2\n\n  services:\n    frontend:\n      ingress:\n        host: iq-portal.dev.konecta.io\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Database Migration\n\n### Strategy\n\nThe app runs migrations automatically on startup. This is safe because:\n\n- The Cloud SQL instance already exists with the schema\n- Migrations are idempotent (tracked in `schema_migrations` table)\n- No schema changes are part of this migration\n\n### Connectivity test\n\n```bash\n# Deploy test pod with cloud-sql-proxy\nkubectl run sql-test --rm -it \\\n  --image=gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.14.3 \\\n  --namespace=dev-api-services \\\n  --serviceaccount=kix-usecase-iq-portal \\\n  -- --private-ip kd-lab-464110:europe-west1:konecta-iqplatform\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 9: Verification & Smoke Tests\n\n### 9.1 Deployment checklist\n\n```bash\nNAMESPACE=dev-api-services\n\n# 1. Pod running (2/2 = app + cloud-sql-proxy)\nkubectl get pods -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-usecase-iq-portal\n\n# 2. Both containers healthy\nkubectl describe pod -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-usecase-iq-portal | grep -A5 \"Conditions\"\n\n# 3. Logs check\nkubectl logs -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-usecase-iq-portal -c app | grep \"running on\"\n\n# 4. Cloud SQL proxy connected\nkubectl logs -n $NAMESPACE -l app=kix-usecase-iq-portal -c cloud-sql-proxy | grep \"ready\"\n```\n\n### 9.2 Functional smoke tests\n\n```bash\nBASE_URL=\"https://iq-portal.dev.konecta.io\"\n\n# Health check\ncurl -s $BASE_URL/health | jq .\n# Expected: {\"status\":\"ok\"}\n\n# Full health check\ncurl -s $BASE_URL/api/health | jq .\n# Expected: {\"status\":\"ok\",\"checks\":{\"database\":\"ok\",\"gcp\":\"ok\"}}\n\n# Version\ncurl -s $BASE_URL/api/version | jq .\n\n# Frontend loads\ncurl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" $BASE_URL/\n# Expected: 200\n\n# API auth check\ncurl -s -o /dev/null -w \"%{http_code}\" $BASE_URL/api/agents\n# Expected: 401\n```\n\n---\n\n## Step 10: DNS & Domain Cutover\n\n### New domains\n\n| Path | Domain |\n|------|--------|\n| Frontend (browser) | `iq-portal.app.konecta.io` |\n| Frontend (dev) | `iq-portal.dev.konecta.io` |\n| API (machine) | `api.konecta.io/api/iq-portal` |\n\n### Decommission Cloud Run\n\nOnce verified:\n\n```bash\n# Stop Cloud Run\ngcloud run services update kon-iq-portal \\\n  --region=europe-west1 \\\n  --max-instances=0\n\n# After confidence period (1-2 weeks), delete\ngcloud run services delete kon-iq-portal \\\n  --region=europe-west1\n```\n\n---\n\n## Appendix A: Full Environment Variable Mapping\n\n| Variable | Source on Cloud Run | Source on kix |\n|----------|-------------------|--------------|\n| `NODE_ENV` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `PORT` | Default (8080) | Helm `env` |\n| `DB_HOST` | Terraform (`/cloudsql/...`) | Helm `env` (`localhost`) |\n| `DB_PORT` | Default (5432) | Helm `env` |\n| `DB_NAME` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `DB_USER` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |\n| `DB_PASSWORD` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |\n| `JWT_SECRET` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |\n| `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |\n| `GCP_PROJECT_ID` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `GCP_LOCATION` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `VERTEX_AI_*` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `RAG_*` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `RAG_PIPELINE_TOKEN` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |\n| `GCS_*_BUCKET` | Secret Manager | ExternalSecret |\n| `CLOUD_TASKS_*` | Terraform | Helm `env` |\n| `MCP_GATEWAY_URL` | Default | Helm `env` |\n| `CHANNELHUB_*` | Terraform/Secret Manager | Helm `env` / ExternalSecret |\n| `LOG_LEVEL` | Default | Helm `env` |\n| `ALLOWED_ORIGINS` | Terraform | Helm `env` (per-env) |\n\n---\n\n## Appendix B: Rollback Procedure\n\n### Via Git\n\n```bash\ncd kix-platform-infrastructure\ngit revert HEAD\ngit push\n# ArgoCD syncs automatically\n```\n\n### Via kubectl\n\n```bash\nkubectl rollout undo deployment/kix-usecase-iq-portal -n dev-api-services\n```\n\n### Fall back to Cloud Run\n\n```bash\ngcloud run services update kon-iq-portal \\\n  --region=europe-west1 \\\n  --max-instances=10\n```\n"
}
```

> AGENT

I've successfully integrated the migration documentation into the Docusaurus site. Here's a summary:

## Created Structure

**New sidebar category**: "Application Migration" with 16 documentation pages:

```
docs/app-migration/
├── index.md                 # Overview and navigation
├── current-state.md         # Cloud Run architecture, service inventory
├── target-architecture.md   # GKE target, repo model, CI/CD flow
├── migration-phases.md      # 4-phase strategy, priorities
├── kubernetes-helm.md       # Helm charts, namespaces, resources, HPA
├── ingress-networking.md    # Dual ingress, network policies
├── docker-security.md       # Multi-stage builds, hardening
├── cicd-pipeline.md         # GitHub Actions stages, security gates
├── gitops-promotion.md      # ArgoCD flow, promotion, rollback
├── backstage-onboarding.md  # Developer portal, service onboarding
├── security.md              # Runtime security, secrets, admission
├── observability.md         # Prometheus, Grafana, Jaeger, alerting
├── operations-risks.md      # Cost, DR, risk register
├── appendix-reference.md    # Tech stack, namespace/repo mapping
└── service-guides/
    ├── index.md             # Service guides overview
    └── iq-portal.md         # Complete IQ Portal migration guide
```

## Documentation Gaps Identified

### High Priority (Should Be Created)

1. **Additional Service Migration Guides** - Only IQ Portal is documented. Need guides for:
   - MCP Gateway (P1 priority)
   - HR Fraud (similar multi-tier pattern)
   - A generic MCP server template guide

2. **Base Helm Chart Implementation** - The docs reference `base-service-chart` but the actual chart templates aren't provided. This would live in `kix-platform-modules/helm/`.

3. **Shared CI Workflows** - The docs reference `kix-platform-ci-workflows` but the actual workflow YAMLs aren't provided.

### Medium Priority (Would Improve Completeness)

4. **Platform Setup Guides** - Pre-requisites are listed but no setup guides for:

   - ArgoCD ApplicationSet configuration (complete YAML)
   - GitHub App for cross-org dispatch

5. **IAP Configuration** - Frontend ingress mentions IAP but lacks step-by-step setup.

6. **Backstage Software Templates** - Mentioned but actual template definitions not provided.

7. **Grafana Dashboard JSON** - Mentioned but no exportable templates.

8. **Complete AlertManager Config** - Partial examples given but not complete configuration.

### Lower Priority (Nice to Have)

9. **Automated Smoke Test Suite** - Manual commands provided but no automation.

10. **Migration Runbook Automation** - Checklists are manual; could be scripted.

11. **Terraform Modules Documentation** - Referenced but live in `kix-platform-modules`.

12. **Cloud SQL Initial Setup** - Assumes existing database; no setup guide for new instances.

     1→---
     2→title: Troubleshooting Guide
     3→description: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Troubleshooting Guide
     7→
     8→This guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation.
     9→
    10→<Tip>
    11→Use the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands.
    12→</Tip>
    13→
    14→## Installation & Deployment Issues
    15→
    16→### Helm Installation Failures
    17→
    18→<AccordionGroup>
    19→  <Accordion title="Issue: Helm chart installation fails with 'values validation error'">
    20→    **Cause**: Missing or incorrectly formatted required values in `my-values.yaml`
    21→
    22→    **Solution**:
    23→    - Verify all required variables are present in your values file
    24→    - Check YAML syntax for formatting errors (indentation, quotes)
    25→    - Use `helm lint` to validate the chart before installation
    26→    - Review [Prepare Configuration File guide](/crewai/install#step-4-prepare-configuration-file-my-valuesyaml)
    27→  </Accordion>
    28→
    29→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' errors after deployment">
    30→    **Cause**: Container registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist
    31→
    32→    **Solution**:
    33→    - Verify `image.registries[].password` is correctly base64-encoded
    34→    - Confirm the service account has access to the container registry
    35→    - Check that the image tag exists in the specified registry
    36→    - Review Kubernetes events: `kubectl describe pod <pod-name>`
    37→  </Accordion>
    38→</AccordionGroup>
    39→
    40→### Database Connection Issues
    41→
    42→<AccordionGroup>
    43→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors">
    44→    **Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable
    45→
    46→    **Solution**:
    47→    - Verify `DB_PASSWORD` is correct and matches the database user
    48→    - Confirm database host is reachable from the Kubernetes cluster
    49→    - Check network policies allow traffic to the database
    50→    - Test connection manually:
    51→      ```bash
    52→      kubectl exec -it <pod> -- psql -h <host> -U postgres
    53→      ```
    54→  </Accordion>
    55→
    56→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Database migration fails' on first deployment">
    57→    **Cause**: Database schema initialization incomplete
    58→
    59→    **Solution**:
    60→    - Ensure database is fully initialized before deploying application
    61→    - Check database logs for migration errors
    62→    - Verify user has proper privileges for schema creation
    63→    - Run migrations manually if needed:
    64→      ```bash
    65→      kubectl exec -it <pod> -- rake db:migrate
    66→      ```
    67→  </Accordion>
    68→</AccordionGroup>
    69→
    70→## Authentication & Authorization Issues
    71→
    72→### WorkOS Authentication
    73→
    74→<AccordionGroup>
    75→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page">
    76→    **Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured
    77→
    78→    **Solution**:
    79→    - Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value
    80→    - Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
    81→    - Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets
    82→    - Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled
    83→    - Check browser console for authentication errors
    84→    - Review application logs:
    85→      ```bash
    86→      kubectl logs -f <pod-name>
    87→      ```
    88→  </Accordion>
    89→
    90→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error">
    91→    **Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch
    92→
    93→    <Warning>
    94→    **IMPORTANT**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes
    95→    </Warning>
    96→
    97→    **Solution**:
    98→    - Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)
    99→    - ❌ **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)
   100→    - Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long
   101→    - Ensure the same password is used across all replicas
   102→    - Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager
   103→    - Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:
   104→      ```bash
   105→      kubectl rollout restart deployment/<name>
   106→      ```
   107→  </Accordion>
   108→
   109→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option">
   110→    **Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method
   111→
   112→    **Solution**:
   113→    - Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings
   114→    - Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode
   115→    - Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured
   116→  </Accordion>
   117→
   118→  <Accordion title="Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails">
   119→    **Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project
   120→
   121→    **Solution**:
   122→    - Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured
   123→    - Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain
   124→    - Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs
   125→    - Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials
   126→  </Accordion>
   127→</AccordionGroup>
   128→
   129→### CLI Authentication Issues
   130→
   131→<AccordionGroup>
   132→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'crew ai login' command fails">
   133→    **Cause**: WorkOS Connect not properly configured or OIDC middleware disabled
   134→
   135→    **Solution**:
   136→    - Verify `oauth.enabled: true` in Helm values
   137→    - Confirm WorkOS Connect is enabled in WorkOS dashboard
   138→    - Check that CLI can reach the authentication endpoint
   139→    - Review application logs for OIDC errors
   140→    - Try manual token generation as workaround
   141→  </Accordion>
   142→
   143→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Invalid OIDC token' errors in CLI">
   144→    **Cause**: Token expired or WorkOS Connect endpoint misconfigured
   145→
   146→    **Solution**:
   147→    - Re-authenticate: `crew ai login` again
   148→    - Verify token expiration time is reasonable
   149→    - Check clock skew between CLI environment and server
   150→    - Confirm WorkOS Connect is properly integrated
   151→  </Accordion>
   152→</AccordionGroup>
   153→
   154→### OAuth Configuration Issues
   155→
   156→<AccordionGroup>
   157→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'couldn't find key OAUTH_DB_HOST in Secret' error when enabling built-in integrations">
   158→    **Cause**: OAuth enabled after initial deployment without providing `OAUTH_DB_HOST` secret
   159→
   160→    <Warning>
   161→    When enabling `oauth: true` in Helm values, you must also provide `OAUTH_DB_HOST`
   162→    </Warning>
   163→
   164→    **Solution**:
   165→
   166→    Add to your Kubernetes secret:
   167→    ```bash
   168→    kubectl patch secret crewai-secrets -p \
   169→      '{"data": {"OAUTH_DB_HOST": "'$(echo -n '10.0.0.2' | base64)'"}}'
   170→    ```
   171→
   172→    Replace `10.0.0.2` with your actual OAuth database host
   173→
   174→    For initial deployments, configure OAuth from the start to auto-create all required secrets
   175→
   176→    Reference: [PostgreSQL OAuth Database Configuration](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/reference/chart-values/postgres#param-postgres-oauth-database)
   177→
   178→    Restart deployment after updating secret:
   179→    ```bash
   180→    kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-web
   181→    ```
   182→  </Accordion>
   183→</AccordionGroup>
   184→
   185→## Network & Connectivity Issues
   186→
   187→### Ingress & Load Balancer
   188→
   189→<AccordionGroup>
   190→  <Accordion title="Issue: External IP remains 'pending'">
   191→    **Cause**: Static IP resource not provisioned or ingress misconfigured
   192→
   193→    **Solution**:
   194→    - Verify static IP exists in GCP:
   195→      ```bash
   196→      gcloud compute addresses list
   197→      ```
   198→    - Check ingress annotations reference correct IP name
   199→    - Confirm load balancer backend services are healthy
   200→    - Review GCP Console > Network Services > Load Balancing
   201→  </Accordion>
   202→
   203→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Connection refused' when accessing application">
   204→    **Cause**: Service not accessible or firewall blocking traffic
   205→
   206→    **Solution**:
   207→    - Verify pod is running: `kubectl get pods`
   208→    - Check service is created: `kubectl get svc`
   209→    - Test internal connectivity:
   210→      ```bash
   211→      kubectl port-forward svc/<service> 8080:80
   212→      ```
   213→    - Review firewall rules in GCP Console > VPC Network > Firewall rules
   214→  </Accordion>
   215→</AccordionGroup>
   216→
   217→### DNS Resolution
   218→
   219→<AccordionGroup>
   220→  <Accordion title="Issue: Domain name not resolving">
   221→    **Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay
   222→
   223→    **Solution**:
   224→    - Verify DNS A record points to load balancer IP
   225→    - Wait for DNS propagation (up to 48 hours)
   226→    - Test DNS:
   227→      ```bash
   228→      nslookup your-domain.com
   229→      # or
   230→      dig your-domain.com
   231→      ```
   232→    - Check GCP Cloud DNS records if using Cloud DNS
   233→  </Accordion>
   234→
   235→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Invalid certificate' or SSL errors">
   236→    **Cause**: SSL certificate not provisioned or misconfigured
   237→
   238→    **Solution**:
   239→    - Verify managed certificate is created:
   240→      ```bash
   241→      kubectl get managedcertificate
   242→      ```
   243→    - Check certificate is in "Active" state
   244→    - Confirm domain in FrontendConfig matches certificate domains
   245→    - Review GCP Console > Network Services > Cloud Armor & Security Policies
   246→  </Accordion>
   247→</AccordionGroup>
   248→
   249→## Storage & Persistence Issues
   250→
   251→### Cloud Storage / S3 Access
   252→
   253→<AccordionGroup>
   254→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket">
   255→    **Cause**: AWS credentials invalid or lack permissions
   256→
   257→    **Solution**:
   258→    - Verify `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` are correct HMAC keys
   259→    - Confirm service account has `storage.objects.create` permission
   260→    - Check bucket exists and is accessible
   261→    - Test access:
   262→      ```bash
   263→      kubectl exec -it <pod> -- aws s3 ls s3://your-bucket/
   264→      ```
   265→  </Accordion>
   266→
   267→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'No such bucket' errors">
   268→    **Cause**: Bucket name wrong or not accessible from cluster
   269→
   270→    **Solution**:
   271→    - Verify `AWS_BUCKET` and `LOGS_BUCKET` match actual bucket names
   272→    - Confirm buckets exist in GCP Cloud Storage
   273→    - Check bucket region matches `AWS_REGION`
   274→    - Verify endpoint is correct: `https://storage.googleapis.com`
   275→  </Accordion>
   276→</AccordionGroup>
   277→
   278→### Volume Mounting
   279→
   280→<AccordionGroup>
   281→  <Accordion title="Issue: Persistent volume not mounting">
   282→    **Cause**: Volume not created or mount configuration incorrect
   283→
   284→    **Solution**:
   285→    - Check volume exists: `kubectl get pv`
   286→    - Verify PVC is bound: `kubectl get pvc`
   287→    - Check pod logs for mount errors
   288→    - Describe pod to see mount status:
   289→      ```bash
   290→      kubectl describe pod <pod-name>
   291→      ```
   292→  </Accordion>
   293→</AccordionGroup>
   294→
   295→## Container Registry Issues
   296→
   297→### GCP Service Account Authentication
   298→
   299→<AccordionGroup>
   300→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' or 'unauthorized: authentication required' when pulling images">
   301→    **Cause**: Container registry credentials missing, incorrect, or not properly base64-encoded
   302→
   303→    **Solution**:
   304→
   305→    **Step 1: Create GCP Service Account Key** (if not already created):
   306→    ```bash
   307→    gcloud iam service-accounts keys create sa-key.json \
   308→      --iam-account=<service-account-email>
   309→    ```
   310→
   311→    **Step 2: Base64 Encode the Key** (critical for Docker registry auth):
   312→    ```bash
   313→    cat sa-key.json | base64 -w 0 > sa-key.base64
   314→    # Or without line wrapping:
   315→    base64 sa-key.json > sa-key.base64
   316→    ```
   317→
   318→    **Step 3: Create Kubernetes Secret** with encoded credentials:
   319→    ```bash
   320→    kubectl create secret docker-registry gcp-registry-secret \
   321→      --docker-server="europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev" \
   322→      --docker-username="_json_key_base64" \
   323→      --docker-password="$(cat sa-key.base64)"
   324→    ```
   325→
   326→    **Step 4: Reference Secret in Pod Spec** (Helm values):
   327→    ```yaml
   328→    image:
   329→      registries:
   330→        - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
   331→          username: "_json_key_base64"
   332→          password: "[REDACTED]"  # Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON
   333→    ```
   334→
   335→    <Warning>
   336→    **CRITICAL SECURITY CONSIDERATIONS**:
   337→    - Never commit `sa-key.json` to version control - it provides full GCP access
   338→    - Never store raw JSON in Helm values files - use Cloud Secret Manager or Kubernetes secrets
   339→    - Rotate keys regularly - GCP recommends quarterly rotation minimum
   340→    - Delete old keys after rotation to prevent compromise
   341→    - Use service account impersonation when possible instead of key-based authentication
   342→    - Restrict key permissions - grant only necessary roles (e.g., `roles/storage.objectViewer` for artifact registry)
   343→    </Warning>
   344→
   345→    <Info>
   346→    **Secure Key Management Workflow**:
   347→
   348→    ```bash
   349→    # 1. Create key (temporary file only)
   350→    gcloud iam service-accounts keys create /tmp/sa-key.json \
   351→      --iam-account=<service-account-email>
   352→
   353→    # 2. Base64 encode and store in Cloud Secret Manager
   354→    gcloud secrets create gcp-registry-key \
   355→      --data-file=/tmp/sa-key.json
   356→
   357→    # 3. Immediately delete local copy
   358→    shred -vfz -n 10 /tmp/sa-key.json
   359→
   360→    # 4. Reference secret in Kubernetes (do NOT use raw JSON)
   361→    # Use external-secrets-operator or sealed-secrets to sync from Cloud Secret Manager
   362→    ```
   363→    </Info>
   364→  </Accordion>
   365→
   366→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'access denied' when pulling from private registry">
   367→    **Cause**: Service account lacks necessary permissions
   368→
   369→    **Solution**:
   370→    - Verify service account has `roles/artifactregistry.reader` role
   371→    - Check:
   372→      ```bash
   373→      gcloud projects get-iam-policy <project-id>
   374→      ```
   375→    - Grant if missing:
   376→      ```bash
   377→      gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding <project-id> \
   378→        --member=serviceAccount:<sa-email> \
   379→        --role=roles/artifactregistry.reader
   380→      ```
   381→  </Accordion>
   382→</AccordionGroup>
   383→
   384→## Performance & Resource Issues
   385→
   386→### CPU & Memory
   387→
   388→<AccordionGroup>
   389→  <Accordion title="Issue: Pods getting OOMKilled (out of memory)">
   390→    **Cause**: Application memory usage exceeds resource limits
   391→
   392→    **Solution**:
   393→    - Increase memory limits in Helm values
   394→    - Profile application memory usage
   395→    - Check for memory leaks in logs
   396→    - Increase replica count to distribute load
   397→  </Accordion>
   398→
   399→  <Accordion title="Issue: High CPU usage or slow response times">
   400→    **Cause**: Insufficient CPU allocation or application bottleneck
   401→
   402→    **Solution**:
   403→    - Increase CPU limits in Helm values
   404→    - Enable horizontal pod autoscaling
   405→    - Profile application CPU usage
   406→    - Check for database query performance issues
   407→  </Accordion>
   408→</AccordionGroup>
   409→
   410→### Scaling Issues
   411→
   412→<AccordionGroup>
   413→  <Accordion title="Issue: Auto-scaling not working">
   414→    **Cause**: HPA (Horizontal Pod Autoscaler) not configured or metrics unavailable
   415→
   416→    **Solution**:
   417→    - Verify HPA is created: `kubectl get hpa`
   418→    - Check metrics are available:
   419→      ```bash
   420→      kubectl top nodes
   421→      kubectl top pods
   422→      ```
   423→    - Ensure metrics-server is installed:
   424→      ```bash
   425→      kubectl get deployment metrics-server -n kube-system
   426→      ```
   427→    - Review HPA status:
   428→      ```bash
   429→      kubectl describe hpa <hpa-name>
   430→      ```
   431→  </Accordion>
   432→</AccordionGroup>
   433→
   434→## Logging & Monitoring
   435→
   436→### Log Collection Issues
   437→
   438→<AccordionGroup>
   439→  <Accordion title="Issue: Application logs not appearing in Cloud Logging">
   440→    **Cause**: Log sink not configured or permissions missing
   441→
   442→    **Solution**:
   443→    - Verify Stackdriver/Cloud Logging is enabled
   444→    - Check log sink filters are correct
   445→    - Confirm service account has `logging.logEntries.create` permission
   446→    - Test logging locally:
   447→      ```bash
   448→      kubectl logs -f <pod-name>
   449→      ```
   450→  </Accordion>
   451→</AccordionGroup>
   452→
   453→### Metrics & Monitoring
   454→
   455→<AccordionGroup>
   456→  <Accordion title="Issue: No metrics visible in monitoring dashboard">
   457→    **Cause**: Metrics not being collected or exported
   458→
   459→    **Solution**:
   460→    - Verify monitoring agent is running
   461→    - Check metrics endpoints are accessible
   462→    - Confirm Prometheus or equivalent scraper is configured
   463→    - Review monitoring configuration in values file
   464→  </Accordion>
   465→</AccordionGroup>
   466→
   467→## Kubernetes Issues
   468→
   469→### Pod Issues
   470→
   471→<AccordionGroup>
   472→  <Accordion title="Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state">
   473→    **Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues
   474→
   475→    **Solution**:
   476→    - Check node availability: `kubectl get nodes`
   477→    - Describe pod to see pending reason:
   478→      ```bash
   479→      kubectl describe pod <pod-name>
   480→      ```
   481→    - Check resource requests vs available resources
   482→    - Scale up node pool if needed
   483→  </Accordion>
   484→
   485→  <Accordion title="Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'">
   486→    **Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error
   487→
   488→    **Solution**:
   489→    - Check pod logs:
   490→      ```bash
   491→      kubectl logs <pod-name>
   492→      ```
   493→    - Check previous logs if container restarted:
   494→      ```bash
   495→      kubectl logs <pod-name> --previous
   496→      ```
   497→    - Verify all environment variables are set correctly
   498→    - Test application locally with same configuration
   499→  </Accordion>
   500→</AccordionGroup>
   501→
   502→### Secret Management
   503→
   504→<AccordionGroup>
   505→  <Accordion title="Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets">
   506→    **Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly
   507→
   508→    **Solution**:
   509→    - Verify secret exists: `kubectl get secrets`
   510→    - Check secret data:
   511→      ```bash
   512→      kubectl describe secret <secret-name>
   513→      ```
   514→    - Confirm secret is referenced in pod spec
   515→    - Verify pod has restarted after secret creation
   516→    - Check YAML syntax for secret mounting
   517→  </Accordion>
   518→
   519→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Secret key not found' errors">
   520→    **Cause**: Secret key name doesn't match environment variable reference
   521→
   522→    **Solution**:
   523→    - List secret keys:
   524→      ```bash
   525→      kubectl describe secret <secret-name>
   526→      ```
   527→    - Verify key names match pod environment variable references
   528→    - Check for typos in key names
   529→    - Recreate secret if keys are incorrect
   530→  </Accordion>
   531→</AccordionGroup>
   532→
   533→## Image Building Issues
   534→
   535→### BuildKit/Image Build Failures
   536→
   537→<AccordionGroup>
   538→  <Accordion title="Issue: Buildkit pod crashes or enters CrashLoopBackOff">
   539→    **Cause**: Container image building failures, resource constraints, or configuration issues
   540→
   541→    **Solution**:
   542→
   543→    Check buildkit pod logs:
   544→    ```bash
   545→    kubectl logs deploy/crewai-buildkit
   546→    kubectl logs deploy/crewai-buildkit --previous
   547→    ```
   548→
   549→    Check pod resource requests vs available resources:
   550→    ```bash
   551→    kubectl describe pod <buildkit-pod-name>
   552→    ```
   553→
   554→    Other checks:
   555→    - Verify registry credentials are accessible to buildkit pod
   556→    - Check for disk space issues:
   557→      ```bash
   558→      kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-buildkit -- df -h
   559→      ```
   560→  </Accordion>
   561→
   562→  <Accordion title="Issue: Image fails to build or push to registry">
   563→    **Cause**: Build failure, registry authentication, or network connectivity
   564→
   565→    **Solution**:
   566→
   567→    Execute into buildkit pod for debugging:
   568→    ```bash
   569→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-buildkit -- /bin/bash
   570→    ```
   571→
   572→    Verify registry credentials from within pod
   573→
   574→    Check network connectivity to registry:
   575→    ```bash
   576→    kubectl exec deploy/crewai-buildkit -- curl -I https://europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev
   577→    ```
   578→
   579→    Other steps:
   580→    - Review buildkit logs for specific error messages
   581→    - Restart buildkit deployment if hung:
   582→      ```bash
   583→      kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-buildkit
   584→      ```
   585→  </Accordion>
   586→
   587→  <Accordion title="Issue: Feature flags not recognized or disabled">
   588→    **Cause**: Feature flag configuration not loaded or overridden
   589→
   590→    **Solution**:
   591→
   592→    Check feature flag status from Rails runner:
   593→    ```bash
   594→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   595→      bundle exec rails runner 'Flipper.enabled?(:flag_name)'
   596→    ```
   597→
   598→    Verify flag is enabled in Flipper configuration
   599→
   600→    Check Rails cache is cleared after flag changes:
   601→    ```bash
   602→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   603→      bundle exec rails runner 'Rails.cache.clear'
   604→    ```
   605→
   606→    Restart web deployment to pick up flag changes:
   607→    ```bash
   608→    kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-web
   609→    ```
   610→  </Accordion>
   611→</AccordionGroup>
   612→
   613→## Application Features Issues
   614→
   615→### Crew Studio LLM Compatibility
   616→
   617→<AccordionGroup>
   618→  <Accordion title="Issue: Cannot select Gemini or other specific LLM models in Crew Studio">
   619→    **Cause**: LLM model not enabled for Crew Studio or incompatible model version
   620→
   621→    <Info>
   622→    Use models equivalent to **GPT-4 or higher** for best performance with Crew Studio.
   623→    </Info>
   624→
   625→    **Solution**:
   626→    - Verify the LLM is enabled in your LLM Connection configuration
   627→    - Compatible LLMs must be:
   628→      - Enabled in your LLM Connection
   629→      - Compatible with Crew Studio (check CrewAI documentation for supported models)
   630→      - Configured with sufficient context windows and capabilities
   631→
   632→    **Supported models typically include**:
   633→    - OpenAI: GPT-4, GPT-4 Turbo, GPT-4o
   634→    - Other enterprise-grade LLMs equivalent to GPT-4
   635→
   636→    <Warning>
   637→    Some models like Gemini may require additional configuration or may not be fully supported in the current version. Check CrewAI release notes or support documentation for LLM compatibility list.
   638→    </Warning>
   639→  </Accordion>
   640→</AccordionGroup>
   641→
   642→## User and Data Management
   643→
   644→### Removing Users for WorkOS Integration Testing
   645→
   646→When transitioning to WorkOS authentication, you may need to remove existing test users to ensure proper WorkOS sign-in flow and user provisioning.
   647→
   648→<AccordionGroup>
   649→  <Accordion title="Issue: Need to remove user account to test WorkOS sign-in">
   650→    **Cause**: Testing WorkOS authentication or cleaning up development/test users
   651→
   652→    **Solution**:
   653→
   654→    **Step 1: List all users**:
   655→    ```bash
   656→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   657→      bundle exec rails runner \
   658→      'User.select(:id, :email).each { |u| puts "ID: #{u.id} | Email: #{u.email}" }'
   659→    ```
   660→
   661→    **Step 2: Delete single user by email**:
   662→    ```bash
   663→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   664→      bundle exec rails runner \
   665→      "User.find_by_email('user@example.com')&.destroy!"
   666→    ```
   667→
   668→    **Step 3: If deletion fails with foreign key constraint error**:
   669→
   670→    <Warning>
   671→    Error: `PG::ForeignKeyViolation: ERROR: update or delete on table "users" violates foreign key constraint`
   672→
   673→    Cause: User has associated chat_sessions that must be deleted first
   674→    </Warning>
   675→
   676→    Use the deep clean method to remove user and all related data:
   677→    ```bash
   678→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   679→      bundle exec rails runner '
   680→      user = User.find_by(email: "user@example.com")
   681→      if user
   682→        session_ids = ChatSession.where(user_id: user.id).pluck(:id)
   683→        ChatMessage.where(chat_session_id: session_ids).delete_all
   684→        ChatSession.where(id: session_ids).delete_all
   685→        user.destroy!
   686→        puts "Deep clean complete: User, Sessions, and Messages deleted."
   687→      else
   688→        puts "User not found"
   689→      end
   690→      '
   691→    ```
   692→  </Accordion>
   693→
   694→  <Accordion title="Issue: Add user as owner after WorkOS setup">
   695→    **Cause**: Provisioning new admin accounts or assigning ownership
   696→
   697→    **Solution**:
   698→
   699→    **Create owner in primary organization** (org_id=1):
   700→    ```bash
   701→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   702→      bundle exec rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults \
   703→      'factory:add_owner[1,admin@example.com]'
   704→    ```
   705→
   706→    **Create owner in secondary organization** (org_id=2):
   707→    ```bash
   708→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   709→      bundle exec rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults \
   710→      'factory:add_owner[2,admin@example.com]'
   711→    ```
   712→  </Accordion>
   713→
   714→  <Accordion title="Issue: Clean up test users in bulk">
   715→    **Cause**: Resetting development environment after testing
   716→
   717→    **Solution**:
   718→    ```bash
   719→    # Delete all users except admins (example pattern)
   720→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   721→      bundle exec rails runner '
   722→      User.where.not(email: ["admin@example.com", "protected@example.com"]).destroy_all
   723→      '
   724→    ```
   725→
   726→    <Warning>
   727→    These operations are destructive and cannot be easily undone. Ensure backups are current before bulk deletions.
   728→    </Warning>
   729→  </Accordion>
   730→</AccordionGroup>
   731→
   732→## Getting Help
   733→
   734→### Debug Commands Reference
   735→
   736→<Tip>
   737→Quick reference for common debugging commands
   738→</Tip>
   739→
   740→```bash
   741→# View pod logs
   742→kubectl logs <pod-name>
   743→kubectl logs -f <pod-name>  # Follow logs
   744→kubectl logs <pod-name> --previous  # View previous container logs
   745→
   746→# Describe resources for detailed status
   747→kubectl describe pod <pod-name>
   748→kubectl describe deployment <deployment-name>
   749→kubectl describe svc <service-name>
   750→
   751→# Execute commands in pod
   752→kubectl exec -it <pod-name> -- /bin/bash
   753→kubectl exec -it <pod-name> -- env  # Check environment variables
   754→
   755→# Check resource status
   756→kubectl get pods
   757→kubectl get svc
   758→kubectl get pvc
   759→kubectl get secrets
   760→
   761→# View Helm release status
   762→helm status <release-name>
   763→helm get values <release-name>  # View current values
   764→
   765→# Port forwarding for testing
   766→kubectl port-forward svc/<service-name> 8080:80
   767→```

   792→### Reporting Issues
   793→
   794→When reporting issues, include:
   795→- Complete error messages and stack traces
   796→- Output of relevant kubectl commands
   797→- Kubernetes event logs
   798→- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)
   799→- Configuration details (without secrets)
   800→- CrewAI version and deployment environment
