     1→---
     2→title: GCP CrewAI Installation Guide
     3→description: Install CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud using Cloud Shell
     4→---
     5→
     6→# ☁️ CrewAI Platform Installation on Google Cloud
     7→
     8→A step-by-step guide to install the CrewAI Platform using Google Cloud Shell, including project setup, configuration, and platform deployment.
     9→For the official installation documentation, see [CrewAI Enterprise Installation Guide](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/installation/installation).
    10→
    11→
    12→<Info>
    13→Google Cloud Shell is a free, browser-based terminal that comes pre-configured with the Google Cloud SDK, kubectl, and other tools you need for installation.
    14→</Info>
    15→
    16→<Warning>
    17→**Prerequisites: GCP Infrastructure Setup Required**
    18→
    19→Before installing CrewAI Platform, you must first set up your GCP infrastructure including:
    20→- GCP Organization and Project structure
    21→- GKE Kubernetes cluster
    22→- Cloud SQL database
    23→- Cloud Storage buckets
    24→- Networking and security configuration
    25→
    26→Follow the [**GCP Infrastructure Setup Guide**](/crewai/gcp-setup) to provision these resources before proceeding with this installation.
    27→</Warning>
    28→
    29→## 📋 Prerequisites
    30→
    31→Before you begin, ensure you have:
    32→
    33→<CardGroup cols={2}>
    34→  <Card title="GCP Project" icon="project">
    35→    An active GCP project with billing enabled
    36→  </Card>
    37→  <Card title="GKE Cluster" icon="container">
    38→    A running Google Kubernetes Engine cluster with sufficient resources
    39→  </Card>
    40→  <Card title="Cloud Shell Access" icon="terminal">
    41→    Access to Google Cloud Console
    42→  </Card>
    43→  <Card title="Registry Credentials" icon="key">
    44→    CrewAI enterprise registry credentials from the customer portal
    45→  </Card>
    46→</CardGroup>
    47→
    48→<Warning>
    49→**Get Your Credentials**
    50→
    51→Access your registry credentials via: [https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai/install](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai/install)
    52→
    53→You'll need:
    54→- Customer email address
    55→- Secret authentication token
    56→
    57→**Need help accessing the portal?** Contact:
    58→- nathan.booth@konecta.com
    59→- mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com
    60→</Warning>
    61→
    62→## 🚀 Installation Steps
    63→
    64→### Step 1: Set Your Project ID
    65→
    66→Open Google Cloud Console and launch Cloud Shell, then set your project:
    67→
    68→<CodeGroup>
    69→```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
    70→# Set your GCP project ID
    71→export PROJECT_ID="your-gcp-project-id"
    72→
    73→# Verify the project is set
    74→gcloud config set project $PROJECT_ID
    75→gcloud config get-value project
    76→```
    77→
    78→```powershell Local Windows
    79→# Set your GCP project ID
    80→$PROJECT_ID = "your-gcp-project-id"
    81→
    82→# Verify the project is set
    83→gcloud config set project $PROJECT_ID
    84→gcloud config get-value project
    85→```
    86→</CodeGroup>
    87→
    88→<Check>
    89→Your Cloud Shell will now use this project for all subsequent commands.
    90→</Check>
    91→
    92→### Step 2: Create Project Metadata Folder
    93→
    94→Create a folder to organize and store your CrewAI deployment metadata:
    95→
    96→<Steps>
    97→  <Step title="Create Metadata Directory">
    98→    ```bash
    99→    # Create a folder to store CrewAI configuration and metadata
   100→    mkdir -p ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata
   101→    
   102→    # Navigate to the directory
   103→    cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID
   104→    
   105→    # Verify the structure
   106→    ls -la
   107→    ```
   108→  </Step>
   109→  
   110→  <Step title="Create Supporting Directories">
   111→    ```bash
   112→    # Create subdirectories for organization
   113→    mkdir -p metadata/secrets
   114→    mkdir -p metadata/backups
   115→    mkdir -p metadata/logs
   116→    
   117→    # Create a README for documentation
   118→    cat > metadata/README.md << 'EOF'
   119→    # CrewAI Deployment Metadata
   120→    
   121→    Project ID: $PROJECT_ID
   122→    Created: $(date)
   123→    
   124→    ## Directory Structure
   125→    - secrets/: Sensitive configuration files (DO NOT COMMIT)
   126→    - backups/: Backup of configurations and values
   127→    - logs/: Installation and upgrade logs
   128→    
   129→    ## Important Files
   130→    - my-values.yaml: Helm chart values (store in secrets/)
   131→    
   132→    EOF
   133→    
   134→    cat metadata/README.md
   135→    ```
   136→  </Step>
   137→</Steps>
   138→
   139→### Step 3: Configure Installation via Enterprise Portal
   140→
   141→Access the CrewAI Enterprise Portal to configure and retrieve your installation details:
   142→
   143→<Steps>
   144→  <Step title="Access the Install Tab">
   145→    Log in to [https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai) and navigate to the **Install** tab.
   146→  </Step>
   147→  
   148→  <Step title="Configure Instance Details">
   149→    Provide the following information:
   150→    
   151→    **Instance Configuration:**
   152→    - **Instance Name:** FQDN format (e.g., `dev.crewai.konecta.com`)
   153→    - **Kubernetes Distribution:** Google GKE
   154→    - **Cluster Network Availability:** Outbound requests allowed
   155→    
   156→    **Registry Access:**
   157→    - Select: "My workstation can access the internet, the registry AND the cluster"
   158→  </Step>
   159→  
   160→  <Step title="Retrieve Installation Instructions">
   161→    The portal will generate and display your complete Helm Online Install workflow, including:
   162→    - App version selection
   163→    - Authentication credentials and export commands
   164→    - Custom values file template
   165→    - Preflight validation instructions
   166→    - Helm chart installation command with your specific version and values
   167→  </Step>
   168→</Steps>
   169→
   170→The portal provides a complete installation sequence with the following steps:
   171→
   172→<Steps>
   173→  <Step title="Select a Version">
   174→    Choose your desired app version from the portal's version selector.
   175→  </Step>
   176→
   177→  <Step title="Export Credentials and Log In">
   178→    Copy and run the authentication commands provided by the portal:
   179→
   180→    ```bash
   181→    export AUTH_TOKEN=<token>
   182→    helm registry login registry.crewai.com --username mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com --password $AUTH_TOKEN
   183→    ```
   184→  </Step>
   185→
   186→  <Step title="Create Values Override File">
   187→    Create a `my-values.yaml` file to customize your installation (detailed in Step 4 below).
   188→  </Step>
   189→
   190→  <Step title="Install Preflight Plugin">
   191→    Install the preflight validation plugin on GCP Cloud Shell:
   192→
   193→    ```bash
   194→    curl https://krew.sh/preflight | bash
   195→    ```
   196→  </Step>
   197→
   198→  <Step title="Install the Chart">
   199→    Run the Helm installation command with your specific version:
   200→
   201→    ```bash
   202→    helm install crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   203→      --version 0.3.1 \
   204→      --values my-values.yaml
   205→    ```
   206→  </Step>
   207→</Steps>
   208→
   209→<Info>
   210→**Important:** Copy the exact commands provided by the portal, as they contain your unique authentication token and version-specific installation details.
   211→</Info>
   212→
   213→### Step 4: Prepare Configuration File (my-values.yaml)
   214→
   215→Use the values template provided by the enterprise portal as a starting point. Here's a reference configuration:
   216→
   217→<Steps>
   218→  <Step title="Get Portal Template">
   219→    The portal provides a pre-configured `my-values.yaml` template customized for your instance. Download or copy this template as your base configuration.
   220→  </Step>
   221→  
   222→  <Step title="Customize Your Configuration">
   223→    Edit the template with your specific settings:
   224→
   225→    <CodeGroup>
   226→    ```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
   227→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   228→    cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata
   229→
   230→    # Open in editor (nano or vim)
   231→    nano my-values.yaml
   232→    ```
   233→
   234→    ```powershell Windows
   235→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   236→    cd ~\crewai-deployment\$PROJECT_ID\metadata
   237→
   238→    # Open in editor
   239→    notepad my-values.yaml
   240→    ```
   241→    </CodeGroup>
   242→
   243→    <Warning>
   244→    **Security: Secrets Management**
   245→
   246→    **NEVER commit `my-values.yaml` to version control if it contains secrets!**
   247→
   248→    Instead:
   249→    1. Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager
   250→    2. Use CI/CD pipeline to inject secrets
   251→    3. Or create a `.gitignore` entry and manage separately
   252→    </Warning>
   253→  </Step>
   254→
   255→  <Step title="Key Configuration Parameters">
   256→    Configure these essential parameters in your `my-values.yaml`:
   257→
   258→    ### Application Configuration
   259→
   260→    <ParamField path="envVars.APPLICATION_HOST" type="string" required>
   261→      Your domain name (FQDN) for the CrewAI instance
   262→
   263→      **Example:** `dev.crewai.konecta.com`
   264→    </ParamField>
   265→
   266→    <ParamField path="envVars.AUTH_PROVIDER" type="string" required>
   267→      Authentication provider to use
   268→
   269→      **Options:** `workos`, `entra_id`, `google`, `local`
   270→    </ParamField>
   271→
   272→    ### Database Configuration
   273→
   274→    <ParamField path="envVars.DB_HOST" type="string" required>
   275→      Private IP address of your Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance
   276→
   277→      **Example:** `10.29.0.2`
   278→    </ParamField>
   279→
   280→    <ParamField path="envVars.DB_PORT" type="string" default="5432">
   281→      PostgreSQL database port
   282→    </ParamField>
   283→
   284→    <ParamField path="envVars.DB_USER" type="string" default="postgres">
   285→      Database username
   286→    </ParamField>
   287→
   288→    <ParamField path="secrets.DB_PASSWORD" type="string" required>
   289→      Database password (store in Secret Manager)
   290→    </ParamField>
   291→
   292→    ### Storage Configuration (S3-compatible GCS)
   293→
   294→    <ParamField path="envVars.STORAGE_SERVICE" type="string" default="amazon">
   295→      Storage service type (use "amazon" for S3-compatible GCS)
   296→    </ParamField>
   297→
   298→    <ParamField path="envVars.AWS_REGION" type="string" required>
   299→      GCP region for Cloud Storage buckets
   300→
   301→      **Example:** `europe-west1`
   302→    </ParamField>
   303→
   304→    <ParamField path="envVars.AWS_BUCKET" type="string" required>
   305→      Main data storage bucket name
   306→
   307→      **Example:** `your-project-crewai-data`
   308→    </ParamField>
   309→
   310→    <ParamField path="envVars.LOGS_BUCKET" type="string" required>
   311→      Logs storage bucket name
   312→
   313→      **Example:** `your-project-crewai-logs`
   314→    </ParamField>
   315→
   316→    <ParamField path="envVars.AWS_ENDPOINT" type="string" default="https://storage.googleapis.com">
   317→      S3-compatible endpoint for GCS
   318→    </ParamField>
   319→
   320→    <ParamField path="secrets.AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID" type="string" required>
   321→      GCS HMAC access key ID (from service account)
   322→    </ParamField>
   323→
   324→    <ParamField path="secrets.AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY" type="string" required>
   325→      GCS HMAC secret access key
   326→    </ParamField>
   327→
   328→    ### WorkOS Authentication
   329→
   330→    <ParamField path="envVars.WORKOS_CLIENT_ID" type="string" required>
   331→      WorkOS client ID from your WorkOS dashboard
   332→
   333→      **Format:** `client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX`
   334→    </ParamField>
   335→
   336→    <ParamField path="envVars.WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN" type="string" required>
   337→      WorkOS AuthKit domain
   338→
   339→      **Example:** `your-domain.authkit.app`
   340→    </ParamField>
   341→
   342→    <ParamField path="secrets.WORKOS_API_KEY" type="string" required>
   343→      WorkOS API key (store in Secret Manager)
   344→    </ParamField>
   345→
   346→    <ParamField path="secrets.WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD" type="string" required>
   347→      32-character random string for session encryption
   348→
   349→      **Generate with:** `openssl rand -hex 16`
   350→    </ParamField>
   351→
   352→    ### Container Registry
   353→
   354→    <ParamField path="envVars.CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE" type="string">
   355→      Custom container registry for CrewAI images
   356→
   357→      **Example:** `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/your-project/crewai-enterprise`
   358→    </ParamField>
   359→
   360→    <ParamField path="image.registries[].host" type="string" required>
   361→      Container registry hostname
   362→
   363→      **Example:** `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev`
   364→    </ParamField>
   365→
   366→    <ParamField path="image.registries[].username" type="string" default="_json_key_base64">
   367→      Registry authentication username (use `_json_key_base64` for GCP)
   368→    </ParamField>
   369→
   370→    <ParamField path="image.registries[].password" type="string" required>
   371→      Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON key
   372→    </ParamField>
   373→
   374→    ### Web Service
   375→
   376→    <ParamField path="web.replicaCount" type="number" default="1">
   377→      Number of web server replicas
   378→
   379→      **Development:** `1` | **Production:** `3`
   380→    </ParamField>
   381→
   382→    <ParamField path="web.ingress.enabled" type="boolean" default="true">
   383→      Enable ingress controller
   384→    </ParamField>
   385→
   386→    <ParamField path="web.ingress.host" type="string" required>
   387→      Ingress hostname (should match APPLICATION_HOST)
   388→    </ParamField>
   389→
   390→    ### Worker Service
   391→
   392→    <ParamField path="worker.replicaCount" type="number" default="1">
   393→      Number of background worker replicas
   394→
   395→      **Development:** `1` | **Production:** `3`
   396→    </ParamField>
   397→
   398→    ### OAuth Configuration
   399→
   400→    <ParamField path="oauth.enabled" type="boolean" default="false">
   401→      Enable OAuth for third-party integrations
   402→    </ParamField>
   403→
   404→    <ParamField path="secrets.OAUTH_DB_HOST" type="string">
   405→      OAuth database host (required if oauth.enabled = true)
   406→    </ParamField>
   407→  </Step>
   408→
   409→  <Step title="Reference: Sample Configuration Structure">
   410→    ```yaml
   411→    # CrewAI Platform Helm Values Configuration
   412→    # This example shows both Development and Production settings
   413→    
   414→    postgres:
   415→      enabled: false  # Use external Cloud SQL database
   416→    
   417→    minio:
   418→      enabled: false  # Use external GCP Cloud Storage (S3-compatible)
   419→    
   420→    envVars:
   421→      # Application Configuration
   422→      APPLICATION_HOST: "dev.crewai.konecta.com"  # Your domain name
   423→      
   424→      # Database Configuration
   425→      DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"  # Private IP of Cloud SQL instance
   426→      DB_PORT: "5432"
   427→      DB_USER: "postgres"
   428→      
   429→      # Container Registry Configuration
   430→      CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/your-project/crewai-enterprise"
   431→      
   432→      # Authentication Provider
   433→      AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
   434→      WORKOS_CLIENT_ID: "client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX"  # from WorkOS dashboard
   435→      WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: "your-domain.authkit.app"
   436→      
   437→      # Storage Configuration (S3-compatible GCP Cloud Storage)
   438→      STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"  # S3-compatible interface
   439→      AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   440→      AWS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-data"
   441→      LOGS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-logs"
   442→      LOGS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   443→      AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"  # GCS S3-compatible endpoint
   444→      LOGS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   445→    
   446→    secrets:
   447→      # Database Credentials
   448→      DB_PASSWORD: "[REDACTED]"  # Store in GCP Secret Manager
   449→      
   450→      # GCS HMAC Keys (tied to service account)
   451→      AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: "[REDACTED]"  # From Cloud Storage HMAC keys
   452→      AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: "[REDACTED]"
   453→      
   454→      # Authentication Secrets
   455→      WORKOS_API_KEY: "[REDACTED]"  # from WorkOS API keys
   456→      OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET: "[REDACTED]"  # Random secure string
   457→      
   458→      # OAuth Configuration
   459→      OAUTH_DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"
   460→      WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD: "[REDACTED]"
   461→    
   462→    # Container Registry Authentication
   463→    image:
   464→      registries:
   465→        - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
   466→          username: "_json_key_base64"
   467→          password: "[REDACTED]"  # Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON
   468→    
   469→    # Web Service Configuration
   470→    web:
   471→      # DEVELOPMENT: Single replica for cost savings
   472→      replicaCount: 1
   473→      
   474→      # PRODUCTION: Multiple replicas for zero-downtime deployments
   475→      # replicaCount: 3
   476→      # terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60  # Graceful shutdown time
   477→      
   478→      port: 80
   479→      useHttps: false
   480→      enableSslFromPuma: false
   481→      
   482→      # Ingress Configuration (GCP Load Balancer)
   483→      ingress:
   484→        enabled: true
   485→        className: ""  # Empty for GCP Ingress
   486→        host: "dev.crewai.konecta.com"
   487→        annotations:
   488→          # GCP-specific annotations
   489→          networking.gke.io/managed-certificates: gcp-domains-ssl-cert
   490→          kubernetes.io/ingress.global-static-ip-name: "dev-crewai-konecta-com-external-ip"
   491→          kubernetes.io/ingress.class: "gce"
   492→          networking.gke.io/v1beta1.FrontendConfig: "gcp-sslpolicy"
   493→    
   494→    # Background Job Processing
   495→    worker:
   496→      # DEVELOPMENT: Single worker for cost savings
   497→      replicaCount: 1
   498→      
   499→      # PRODUCTION: Multiple workers for parallel job processing
   500→      # replicaCount: 3
   501→    
   502→    # GCP Configuration
   503→    gcp:
   504→      ssldomains:
   505→        - "dev.crewai.konecta.com"
   506→      sslPolicy: "dev-crewai-ssl-policy"
   507→    
   508→    # OAuth Configuration
   509→    oauth:
   510→      enabled: true
   511→    ```
   512→
   513→    <Note>
   514→    **Infrastructure Note**: The static IP address resource must be provisioned in GCP before installation. See the [GCP Infrastructure Setup Guide](/crewai/gcp-setup) for details on creating Compute Address resources, SSL policies, and managed certificates.
   515→    </Note>
   516→  </Step>
   517→</Steps>
   518→
   519→### Key Configuration Differences: Development vs Production
   520→
   521→<Tabs>
   522→  <Tab title="Development">
   523→    ```yaml
   524→    # Development Configuration (Cost-optimized)
   525→    
   526→    web:
   527→      replicaCount: 1  # Single instance to save costs
   528→    
   529→    worker:
   530→      replicaCount: 1  # Single worker for background jobs
   531→    
   532→    # DNS: dev.crewai.konecta.com
   533→    # Resources: Minimal (adequate for testing/validation)
   534→    # Scaling: Manual - adjust replicaCount as needed
   535→    ```
   536→
   537→    <Tip>
   538→    Development environments typically use:
   539→    - Single replicas for all services
   540→    - Smaller machine types (e2-small, e2-medium)
   541→    - Reduced resource limits
   542→    - Shared infrastructure when possible
   543→    </Tip>
   544→  </Tab>
   545→
   546→  <Tab title="Production">
   547→    ```yaml
   548→    # Production Configuration (High Availability)
   549→    
   550→    web:
   551→      replicaCount: 3  # Three replicas for zero-downtime deployments
   552→      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60  # Allow graceful shutdown
   553→    
   554→    worker:
   555→      replicaCount: 3  # Distribute background job processing across replicas
   556→    
   557→    # Auto-scaling (if enabled):
   558→    # minReplicas: 3
   559→    # maxReplicas: 10
   560→    # targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   561→    
   562→    # DNS: crewai.konecta.com (or prod domain)
   563→    # Resources: Production-grade (CPU/Memory allocations)
   564→    # Scaling: Auto-scaling enabled
   565→    ```
   566→
   567→    <Tip>
   568→    Production environments should use:
   569→    - Multiple replicas (minimum 3 for high availability)
   570→    - Larger machine types (e2-standard or higher)
   571→    - Resource requests and limits properly configured
   572→    - Horizontal Pod Autoscaling (HPA) enabled
   573→    - Separate infrastructure from development
   574→    </Tip>
   575→  </Tab>
   576→</Tabs>
   577→
   578→### Sensitive Fields Security
   579→
   580→<Warning>
   581→**Never Store Secrets in my-values.yaml**
   582→
   583→The following fields must **NEVER** be committed to version control:
   584→
   585→- `DB_PASSWORD`: Store in Google Cloud Secret Manager
   586→- `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`: GCS HMAC credentials
   587→- `WORKOS_API_KEY`: WorkOS authentication token
   588→- `OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET`: Cryptographic key for session management
   589→- `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD`: Password for WorkOS integration
   590→- `image.registries[].password`: Container registry credentials
   591→
   592→**Recommended approach:**
   593→1. Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager
   594→2. Use Kubernetes External Secrets Operator (ESO) to sync them
   595→3. Reference Secret Manager values in your deployment pipeline
   596→4. Or use GCP Workload Identity for automatic credential injection
   597→</Warning>
   598→
   599→### Step 5: Prepare Your Environment
   600→
   601→Ensure your cluster is ready for deployment:
   602→
   603→<Steps>
   604→  <Step title="Verify Cloud SQL Connection">
   605→    If using Google Cloud SQL, retrieve your connection details:
   606→    
   607→    ```bash
   608→    # List your Cloud SQL instances
   609→    gcloud sql instances list
   610→
   611→    # Get specific instance details
   612→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db \
   613→      --format="get(connectionName,ipAddresses[0].ipAddress)"
   614→
   615→    # For Private IP (recommended for Cloud SQL)
   616→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db \
   617→      --format="get(ipAddresses[0].ipAddress)"
   618→    ```
   619→    
   620→    <Info>
   621→    **Connection String Format**
   622→
   623→    For Cloud SQL Private IP:
   624→    ```
   625→    DB_HOST: your-instance.c.your-project.internal
   626→    ```
   627→    </Info>
   628→  </Step>
   629→
   630→  <Step title="Verify Default Storage Class">
   631→    Ensure your cluster has a default storage class:
   632→    
   633→    ```bash
   634→    # Check current storage classes
   635→    kubectl get storageclass
   636→
   637→    # Set standard as default (if not already)
   638→    kubectl patch storageclass standard \
   639→      -p '{"metadata": {"annotations":{"storageclass.kubernetes.io/is-default-class":"true"}}}'
   640→    ```
   641→    
   642→    <Check>
   643→    **Storage Class Ready**
   644→
   645→    Your cluster now has a default storage class for persistent volumes.
   646→    </Check>
   647→  </Step>
   648→
   649→  <Step title="Install Preflight Validation Plugin">
   650→    Install the preflight plugin from the portal command:
   651→    
   652→    ```bash
   653→    # From portal step 4: Install preflight plugin
   654→    curl https://krew.sh/preflight | bash
   655→    
   656→    # Add Krew to PATH
   657→    export PATH="${KREW_ROOT:-$HOME/.krew}/bin:$PATH"
   658→    
   659→    # Verify installation
   660→    kubectl preflight --version
   661→    ```
   662→  </Step>
   663→</Steps>
   664→
   665→### Step 6: Run Preflight Validation
   666→
   667→Validate your cluster meets all CrewAI requirements before installation:
   668→
   669→```bash
   670→# Run preflight checks with your values file
   671→helm template oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   672→  --values my-values.yaml \
   673→  | kubectl preflight -
   674→
   675→# View detailed results
   676→echo "Preflight check complete. Review any warnings or errors above."
   677→```
   678→
   679→<Warning>
   680→**Address Preflight Issues**
   681→
   682→The preflight check validates:
   683→- ✓ Cluster resources (CPU, memory)
   684→- ✓ Storage classes
   685→- ✓ RBAC permissions
   686→- ✓ Kubernetes version compatibility
   687→
   688→Address any failures before proceeding to installation.
   689→</Warning>
   690→
   691→### Step 7: Install the CrewAI Platform
   692→
   693→Deploy the CrewAI Platform using the Helm command from the portal:
   694→
   695→<Steps>
   696→  <Step title="Execute the Helm Install Command">
   697→    Use the exact command provided by the enterprise portal (from Step 3):
   698→    
   699→    ```bash
   700→    # From portal step 5: Run the exact command provided
   701→    helm install crewai-platform \
   702→      oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   703→      --version <VERSION_FROM_PORTAL> \
   704→      --values my-values.yaml \
   705→      --namespace default \
   706→      --create-namespace
   707→    ```
   708→  </Step>
   709→  
   710→  <Step title="Monitor Installation Progress">
   711→    <CodeGroup>
   712→    ```bash Watch Pods
   713→    # Watch pod creation in real-time
   714→    kubectl get pods -w
   715→    ```
   716→
   717→    ```bash Check Status
   718→    # Or in another Cloud Shell tab:
   719→    kubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform
   720→    ```
   721→    </CodeGroup>
   722→
   723→    <Info>
   724→    **Installation Time**
   725→
   726→    Installation typically takes 5-15 minutes depending on image sizes and cluster resources.
   727→    </Info>
   728→  </Step>
   729→  
   730→  <Step title="Verify Installation Success">
   731→    ```bash
   732→    # Check all pods are running
   733→    kubectl get pods
   734→
   735→    # Check services
   736→    kubectl get svc
   737→
   738→    # View installation logs
   739→    kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=50
   740→
   741→    # Check for any startup errors
   742→    kubectl describe pod $(kubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')
   743→    ```
   744→  </Step>
   745→</Steps>
   746→
   747→### Step 8: Upgrade the Platform
   748→
   749→To upgrade to a newer version of CrewAI Platform:
   750→
   751→<Steps>
   752→  <Step title="Prepare for Upgrade">
   753→    <CodeGroup>
   754→    ```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
   755→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   756→    cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata
   757→
   758→    # Backup your current values file
   759→    cp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)
   760→
   761→    # Check available chart versions
   762→    helm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions
   763→    ```
   764→
   765→    ```powershell Windows
   766→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   767→    cd ~\crewai-deployment\$PROJECT_ID\metadata
   768→
   769→    # Backup your current values file
   770→    Copy-Item my-values.yaml "my-values.yaml.backup.$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss')"
   771→
   772→    # Check available chart versions
   773→    helm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions
   774→    ```
   775→    </CodeGroup>
   776→  </Step>
   777→
   778→  <Step title="Review Changes">
   779→    ```bash
   780→    # Preview what will change
   781→    helm upgrade crewai-platform \
   782→      oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   783→      --values my-values.yaml \
   784→      --dry-run --debug
   785→    ```
   786→
   787→    <Tip>
   788→    Review the output carefully to understand what resources will be modified during the upgrade.
   789→    </Tip>
   790→  </Step>
   791→  
   792→  <Step title="Execute Upgrade">
   793→    ```bash
   794→    # Perform the upgrade
   795→    helm upgrade crewai-platform \
   796→      oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   797→      --values my-values.yaml
   798→    
   799→    # Monitor the rollout
   800→    kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web
   801→    
   802→    # Verify upgrade success
   803→    kubectl get pods
   804→    ```
   805→  </Step>
   806→</Steps>
   807→
   808→<Check>
   809→**Upgrade Complete**
   810→
   811→Your platform is now running the latest version!
   812→</Check>
   813→
   814→## 📊 Verification Steps
   815→
   816→Confirm your installation is successful:
   817→
   818→<CardGroup cols={2}>
   819→  <Card title="Check Pod Status" icon="activity">
   820→    ```bash
   821→    kubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform
   822→    ```
   823→    All pods should show `Running` status.
   824→  </Card>
   825→  
   826→  <Card title="Check Services" icon="network-wired">
   827→    ```bash
   828→    kubectl get svc -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform
   829→    ```
   830→    Services should have ClusterIP or LoadBalancer assigned.
   831→  </Card>
   832→
   833→  <Card title="View Logs" icon="file-lines">
   834→    ```bash
   835→    kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=100
   836→    ```
   837→    Look for successful startup messages.
   838→  </Card>
   839→
   840→  <Card title="Check Resource Usage" icon="chart-simple">
   841→    ```bash
   842→    kubectl top pods
   843→    kubectl top nodes
   844→    ```
   845→    Verify cluster has sufficient capacity.
   846→  </Card>
   847→</CardGroup>
   848→
   849→## 🐛 Troubleshooting
   850→
   851→### Common Issues and Solutions
   852→
   853→<AccordionGroup>
   854→  <Accordion title="Pods Not Starting" icon="alert-circle">
   855→    ```bash
   856→    # Check pod status and events
   857→    kubectl describe pod <POD_NAME>
   858→    
   859→    # View pod logs for errors
   860→    kubectl logs <POD_NAME>
   861→    
   862→    # Check for image pull errors
   863→    kubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   864→    ```
   865→    
   866→    **Common Causes:**
   867→    - Registry authentication failed → Re-check credentials
   868→    - Insufficient cluster resources → Scale cluster
   869→    - Missing default storage class → Run Step 7 again
   870→  </Accordion>
   871→  
   872→  <Accordion title="Registry Authentication Failed" icon="lock">
   873→    ```bash
   874→    # Re-authenticate with the registry
   875→    helm registry login registry.crewai.com \
   876→      --username $CREWAI_EMAIL \
   877→      --password $CREWAI_TOKEN \
   878→      --insecure
   879→    
   880→    # Clear cache and retry
   881→    rm -rf ~/.config/helm/registry/config.json
   882→    helm registry login registry.crewai.com \
   883→      --username $CREWAI_EMAIL \
   884→      --password $CREWAI_TOKEN
   885→    ```
   886→  </Accordion>
   887→  
   888→  <Accordion title="Database Connection Errors">
   889→    ```bash
   890→    # Test Cloud SQL connectivity from pod
   891→    kubectl exec -it <POD_NAME> -- psql \
   892→      -h $DB_HOST \
   893→      -U $DB_USER \
   894→      -d $POSTGRES_DB
   895→
   896→    # Check firewall rules
   897→    gcloud compute firewall-rules list --filter="name~'gke'"
   898→
   899→    # For Cloud SQL, verify Private IP connectivity
   900→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db --format='get(ipAddresses)'
   901→    ```
   902→
   903→    **Common Issues:**
   904→    - Private IP not enabled on Cloud SQL instance
   905→    - VPC peering not configured correctly
   906→    - Firewall rules blocking GKE to Cloud SQL traffic
   907→  </Accordion>
   908→  
   909→  <Accordion title="Storage Issues">
   910→    ```bash
   911→    # Check persistent volumes
   912→    kubectl get pv
   913→    kubectl get pvc
   914→
   915→    # Describe problematic PVC
   916→    kubectl describe pvc <PVC_NAME>
   917→
   918→    # Check storage class
   919→    kubectl get storageclass standard
   920→    ```
   921→
   922→    <Tip>
   923→    Ensure your GKE cluster has the appropriate IAM permissions to create persistent disks in your project.
   924→    </Tip>
   925→  </Accordion>
   926→  
   927→  <Accordion title="Generate Support Bundle">
   928→    If you need help from CrewAI support, generate a diagnostic bundle:
   929→
   930→    ```bash
   931→    # Install support-bundle plugin (if not already done)
   932→    kubectl krew install support-bundle
   933→
   934→    # Generate the bundle
   935→    kubectl support-bundle --load-cluster-specs
   936→
   937→    # Upload the resulting .tar.gz file to CrewAI support
   938→    ```
   939→
   940→    <Warning>
   941→    **Sanitize Sensitive Data**: Before sharing the support bundle, review it for any sensitive information like passwords or API keys.
   942→    </Warning>
   943→  </Accordion>
   944→</AccordionGroup>
   945→
   946→## 📝 Installation Log and Backup
   947→
   948→Keep records of your installation:
   949→
   950→<CodeGroup>
   951→```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
   952→# Save installation timestamp
   953→echo "Installation Date: $(date)" >> metadata/installation.log
   954→
   955→# Backup your configuration
   956→cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID
   957→tar -czf metadata/backups/crewai-config-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).tar.gz metadata/
   958→
   959→# Export cluster information
   960→kubectl cluster-info dump --output-directory=metadata/logs/cluster-dump-$(date +%Y%m%d)
   961→
   962→# Save Helm release information
   963→helm list --all-namespaces > metadata/logs/helm-releases.txt
   964→kubectl describe nodes > metadata/logs/nodes-info.txt
   965→```
   966→
   967→```powershell Windows
   968→# Save installation timestamp
   969→"Installation Date: $(Get-Date)" | Out-File -Append metadata\installation.log
   970→
   971→# Backup your configuration
   972→cd ~\crewai-deployment\$PROJECT_ID
   973→Compress-Archive -Path metadata\* -DestinationPath "metadata\backups\crewai-config-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss').zip"
   974→
   975→# Save Helm release information
   976→helm list --all-namespaces | Out-File metadata\logs\helm-releases.txt
   977→kubectl describe nodes | Out-File metadata\logs\nodes-info.txt
   978→```
   979→</CodeGroup>
   980→
   981→<Note>
   982→Regularly backup your configuration files and keep a history of changes for rollback purposes.
   983→</Note>
   984→
   985→## 🎯 Next Steps
   986→
   987→After successful installation:
   988→
   989→<Steps>
   990→  <Step title="Complete Post-Installation Setup">
   991→    Follow the [Post-Installation Guide](/crewai/post-install) to:
   992→    - Initialize the internal organization
   993→    - Set up default permissions
   994→    - Configure admin access
   995→  </Step>
   996→  
   997→  <Step title="Configure Access">
   998→    Set up access to your CrewAI instance:
   999→    - Port forwarding for development
  1000→    - Ingress for production access
  1001→    - LoadBalancer service configuration
  1002→  </Step>
  1003→  
  1004→  <Step title="Enable Studio V2 (Optional)">
  1005→    Enable the next-generation interface and agent capabilities as described in the [Post-Installation Guide](/crewai/post-install).
  1006→  </Step>
  1007→  
  1008→  <Step title="Review Configuration Guide">
  1009→    Explore advanced configuration options in the [Configuration Guide](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/configuration/configuration).
  1010→  </Step>
  1011→</Steps>
  1012→

  1014→**Installation Complete! 🎉**

  1016→Your CrewAI Platform is now installed on Google Cloud. Proceed to the [Post-Installation Guide](/crewai/post-install) to complete setup and access the application.

     1→---
     2→title: Crew AI Post Install
     3→description: Post Installation Steps
     4→---
     5→
     6→# 🎉 Post-Installation Setup for CrewAI Platform
     7→
     8→## 🌟 Overview
     9→
    10→After successfully deploying the CrewAI Platform infrastructure, these initial setup steps are necessary to configure the core system, set up permissions, and prepare the environment for use.
    11→
    12→<Warning>
    13→**Important**
    14→
    15→Complete these setup steps immediately after deployment. Skipping any steps may result in missing functionality or access issues.
    16→</Warning>
    17→
    18→## ⚙️ Initial Setup Tasks
    19→
    20→These tasks must be run immediately after installation to configure the internal organization and administration access.
    21→
    22→<Steps>
    23→<Step title="Initialize Internal Organization">
    24→Run the following command to initialize the required internal organization, typically named "Default."
    25→
    26→```bash theme={null}
    27→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- rake studio:install_internal_organization
    28→```
    29→
    30→</Step>
    31→<Step title="Set Up Default Permissions">
    32→Configure the default permission set and assign the initial owner role. **Replace `admin@company.com` with your desired administrator's email address.**
    33→
    34→```bash theme={null}
    35→# Replace with your admin email
    36→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
    37→  rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults 'factory:add_owner[2,admin@company.com]'
    38→```
    39→
    40→</Step>
    41→<Step title="Fix Missing 'Operate' Tab (Troubleshoot)">
    42→If the **"Operate"** tab is missing in the Web UI, you may need to manually assign the `Owner` role to your user via the Rails console.
    43→
    44→**1. Shell into the `crewai-web` pod:**
    45→
    46→```bash theme={null}
    47→kubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -- /bin/bash
    48→```
    49→
    50→**2. Start the Rails console:**
    51→
    52→```bash theme={null}
    53→bin/rails c
    54→```
    55→
    56→**3. Define the role assignment function (paste this code block):**
    57→
    58→```ruby
    59→def assign_owner_role(user_email)
    60→  user = User.find_by(email: user_email)
    61→  organization = Organization.find_by(name: "Default")
    62→  owner_role = organization.roles.find_by(name: "Owner")
    63→  membership = organization.memberships.find_by(user_id: user.id)
    64→  membership.update!(role_id: owner_role.id)
    65→end
    66→```
    67→
    68→**4. Run the assignment function (replace with your user email):**
    69→
    70→```ruby
    71→assign_owner_role("nathan.booth@konecta.com")
    72→```
    73→
    74→**5. Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and refresh your browser.**
    75→</Step>
    76→</Steps>
    77→
    78→## 🚀 Enable Studio V2 (Optional)
    79→
    80→<Info>
    81→**What is Studio V2?**
    82→
    83→Studio V2 is the next-generation interface and agent capability for CrewAI, providing enhanced features and improved user experience.
    84→</Info>
    85→
    86→<Steps>
    87→<Step title="Check LLM Connection and Delete Old Crews">
    88→Ensure you have an LLM connection named **`studio-v2`** configured. Then, shell into the web-service pod and delete any old Studio crew deployments.
    89→
    90→**1. Shell into the web-service pod and run:**
    91→
    92→```bash theme={null}
    93→bin/rails c
    94→```
    95→
    96→**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**
    97→
    98→```ruby
    99→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)
   100→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)
   101→```
   102→
   103→</Step>
   104→<Step title="Install the Studio Agent">
   105→Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent. This process can take up to **\~10 minutes**.
   106→
   107→```bash theme={null}
   108→INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install
   109→```
   110→
   111→</Step>
   112→<Step title="Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools">
   113→Continuously check the agent status until it reports **"Crew is Online"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.
   114→
   115→```bash theme={null}
   116→# Check status - wait until it says "Crew is Online"
   117→rake studio:agent:status
   118→
   119→# Sync and re-index tools
   120→rake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools
   121→rake studio:tools:reindex_tools
   122→```
   123→
   124→</Step>
   125→<Step title="Install the Studio Runner">
   126→Install the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for 10 minutes for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.
   127→
   128→```bash theme={null}
   129→INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install
   130→```
   131→
   132→</Step>
   133→</Steps>
   134→
   135→## 🌐 Accessing the Application
   136→
   137→Use the appropriate method to access the CrewAI Web UI based on your deployment environment.
   138→
   139→### Development Access (Port Forwarding)
   140→
   141→Ideal for quick testing or development environments.
   142→
   143→```bash theme={null}
   144→# Forward web service port (2603 is the external port, 80 is the internal service port)
   145→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web 2603:80
   146→
   147→# Access in browser
   148→open http://localhost:2603
   149→```
   150→
   151→### Production Access (Ingress / LoadBalancer)
   152→
   153→| Method | Command to Find Endpoint | Access URL Example |
   154→| :--- | :--- | :--- |
   155→| **Ingress** | `kubectl get ing crewai-ingress` | `https://crewai.company.com` (If hostname configured) |
   156→| **LoadBalancer** | `kubectl get svc crewai-web` | `http://<EXTERNAL-IP>:80` (Use the EXTERNAL-IP from the output) |
   157→
   158→-----
   159→
   160→## ✅ Verification Steps
   161→
   162→After completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:
   163→
   164→1.  **Access the Web UI** using one of the methods above.
   165→2.  **Log in** with your configured authentication provider.
   166→3.  **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of administrative links/tabs.
   167→4.  **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project.
   168→5.  **Check background workers** for successful job processing:
   169→```bash theme={null}
   170→kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker --tail=50
   171→```
   172→
   173→<Check>
   174→**Congratulations! 🎉**
   175→
   176→Your CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!
   177→

     2→title: GCP Infrastructure Setup
     3→description: Production-grade Terraform IaC for CrewAI platform deployment on Google Cloud Platform with enterprise security and compliance

     5→
     6→# 🚀 GCP Infrastructure Setup Blueprint
     7→
     8→A **production-grade Terraform Infrastructure-as-Code (IaC)** blueprint that automates the complete deployment of the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on **Google Cloud Platform (GCP)**. This solution handles everything from organizational hierarchy setup to a fully configured, secure Kubernetes cluster with all required backend services, monitoring, logging, and compliance controls.
     9→
    10→## 📋 Table of Contents
    11→
    12→- [Project Overview](#project-overview)
    13→- [What Gets Created](#what-gets-created)
    14→- [Technology Stack](#technology-stack)
    15→- [Prerequisites](#prerequisites)
    16→- [Quick Start Guide](#quick-start-guide)
    17→- [Project Structure](#project-structure)
    18→- [Configuration Guide](#configuration-guide)
    19→- [Deployment Instructions](#deployment-instructions)
    20→- [Key Features](#key-features)
    21→- [Terraform Workflow](#terraform-workflow)
    22→- [Troubleshooting](#troubleshooting)
    23→- [Contributing](#contributing)
    24→- [License](#license)
    25→
    26→## 📖 Project Overview
    27→
    28→### Purpose
    29→
    30→This blueprint provides a **complete, enterprise-grade infrastructure** for deploying the CrewAI platform on GCP. Rather than manually configuring dozens of GCP services, you provide a few configuration variables, and Terraform automatically provisions:
    31→
    32→- Organizational folder hierarchy
    33→- GCP projects with proper tagging and billing
    34→- GCP APIs and services
    35→- Networking infrastructure (VPC, NAT, firewalls)
    36→- Kubernetes cluster (GKE Standard)
    37→- Database servers (Cloud SQL PostgreSQL with HA and read replicas)
    38→- Storage buckets (with encryption and versioning)
    39→- Secrets management
    40→- IAM roles and service accounts
    41→- Monitoring and logging infrastructure
    42→
    43→### Architecture Overview
    44→
    45→```
    46→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    47→│                    GCP Organization                              │
    48→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    49→│                                                                   │
    50→│  ┌──────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │
    51→│  │  Folder Hierarchy│        │  GCP Project Infrastructure  │   │
    52→│  │  (Org Structure) │        │  ├─ APIs (50+)              │   │
    53→│  │  ├─ Root         │        │  ├─ Service Accounts        │   │
    54→│  │  ├─ Env          │        │  ├─ IAM Roles               │   │
    55→│  │  ├─ Region       │        │  ├─ Billing & Budget        │   │
    56→│  │  └─ Country      │        │  └─ Cloud Functions         │   │
    57→│  └──────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────────┘   │
    58→│           │                            │                         │
    59→│           └─────────────┬──────────────┘                         │
    60→│                         │                                        │
    61→│        ┌────────────────┴───────────────┐                       │
    62→│        │  CrewAI Platform Infrastructure │                       │
    63→│        │                                │                       │
    64→│  ┌─────┴──────────┐  ┌────────────────┐│ ┌──────────────────┐  │
    65→│  │   Networking   │  │   Storage      ││ │   Kubernetes     │  │
    66→│  │   ├─ VPC       │  │   ├─ Data      ││ │   ├─ Cluster     │  │
    67→│  │   ├─ Subnets   │  │   ├─ Logs      ││ │   ├─ Namespaces  │  │
    68→│  │   ├─ NAT       │  │   └─ Backups   ││ │   └─ Add-ons     │  │
    69→│  │   └─ Firewall  │  └────────────────┘│ └──────────────────┘  │
    70→│  └────────────────┘                    │                       │
    71→│                                ┌───────┴──────────┐             │
    72→│                                │  Database        │             │
    73→│                                │  ├─ Primary DB   │             │
    74→│                                │  ├─ Replicas     │             │
    75→│                                │  └─ Backups      │             │
    76→│                                └──────────────────┘             │
    77→│                                                                  │
    78→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    79→```
    80→
    81→## 🎯 What Gets Created
    82→
    83→### 📦 Project Foundation
    84→
    85→<CardGroup cols={2}>
    86→  <Card title="GCP Project" icon="cube">
    87→    Automatically created with organizational labels and metadata
    88→  </Card>
    89→  <Card title="Folder Hierarchy" icon="folder-open">

    92→  <Card title="APIs" icon="zap">

   105→
   106→### 🚀 CrewAI Platform Infrastructure
   107→
   108→#### Compute & Orchestration
   109→
   110→**GKE Cluster** - Google Kubernetes Engine Standard cluster
   111→
   112→<CardGroup cols={2}>
   113→  <Card title="Private Nodes" icon="lock">
   114→    Enhanced security with no public IPs
   115→  </Card>
   116→  <Card title="Public Control Plane" icon="sliders-horizontal">
   117→    Accessible for administration and monitoring
   118→  </Card>
   119→  <Card title="Auto Upgrades" icon="refresh-cw">
   120→    Automated node version management
   121→  </Card>
   122→  <Card title="Observability" icon="activity">
   123→    Comprehensive logging and monitoring integration
   124→  </Card>
   125→  <Card title="Fine-grained Access" icon="key">
   126→    Custom service accounts with least-privilege
   127→  </Card>
   128→</CardGroup>
   129→
   130→#### Data Layer
   131→- **Cloud SQL PostgreSQL**:
   132→  - Regional HA (High Availability) across zones
   133→  - PostgreSQL 16 with automatic backups
   134→  - Read replicas for scaling read operations
   135→  - Point-in-time recovery (7 days retention)
   136→  - Private networking (no public IP)
   137→  - SSL/TLS encryption enforced
   138→  
   139→#### Storage
   140→- **GCS Buckets**:
   141→  - Data bucket (versioning, encryption enabled)
   142→  - Logs bucket (lifecycle policies)
   143→  - Automatic backup and retention policies
   144→
   145→#### Networking
   146→- **VPC Network**: Custom VPC with multi-zone subnets
   147→- **Cloud NAT**: Secure outbound connectivity for private nodes
   148→- **Firewall Rules**: 
   149→  - Internal communication allowed
   150→  - GKE cluster communication configured
   151→  - SSH access restricted
   152→- **Secondary IP Ranges**: Dedicated ranges for pods and services
   153→
   154→#### Security & Secrets
   155→- **Secret Manager**: Secure storage for database credentials
   156→- **IAM Roles**: Fine-grained access control
   157→- **Artifact Registry**: Private Docker image repository
   158→- **SSL/TLS**: Encrypted communication throughout
   159→
   160→### 📊 Monitoring & Observability
   161→
   162→- **Cloud Logging**: Centralized application and system logs
   163→- **Cloud Monitoring**: Metrics and alerting
   164→- **Pub/Sub**: Event-driven notifications
   165→- **Cloud Scheduler**: Job automation
   166→
   167→## 🛠️ Technology Stack
   168→
   169→### Infrastructure as Code
   170→
   171→| Component | Technology | Version |
   172→|-----------|-----------|---------|
   173→| **IaC Framework** | Terraform | ≥ 1.0.0 |
   174→| **Primary Cloud** | Google Cloud Platform (GCP) | Latest |
   175→| **State Management** | AWS S3 (Konecta Standard) | - |
   176→| **Linting** | TFLint | Latest |
   177→| **Formatting** | Terraform fmt | Built-in |
   178→
   179→### GCP Services
   180→
   181→| Service | Purpose | Configuration |
   182→|---------|---------|----------------|
   183→| **Compute Engine** | VM infrastructure | Auto-managed by GKE |
   184→| **Kubernetes Engine (GKE)** | Container orchestration | Standard cluster, private nodes |
   185→| **Cloud SQL** | Managed PostgreSQL | Regional HA with replicas |
   186→| **Cloud Storage (GCS)** | Object storage | Encrypted, versioned buckets |
   187→| **VPC Network** | Virtual networking | Multi-zone, custom subnets |
   188→| **Cloud NAT** | Secure egress | For private node outbound traffic |
   189→| **Cloud Load Balancing** | Traffic distribution | Auto-configured with GKE |
   190→| **Secret Manager** | Secrets storage | Database credentials |
   191→| **Artifact Registry** | Docker images | Private repository |
   192→| **Cloud Pub/Sub** | Messaging | Budget alerts, events |
   193→| **Cloud Scheduler** | Job execution | Automated tasks |
   194→| **Cloud Functions** | Serverless compute | Budget monitoring |
   195→
   196→### Terraform Providers
   197→
   198→```hcl
   199→google       ~> 7.0   # GCP resource management
   200→kubernetes   ~> 2.23  # Kubernetes resource management
   201→random       ~> 3.0   # Random password generation
   202→http         ~> 3.4   # IP detection for security groups
   203→```
   204→
   205→## 📋 Prerequisites
   206→
   207→<Warning>
   208→Missing prerequisites can cause deployment failures. Ensure all tools are installed and properly configured.
   209→</Warning>
   210→
   211→### Required Tools
   212→
   213→<Tabs>
   214→  <Tab title="macOS (Homebrew)">
   215→  ```bash
   216→  brew install terraform gcloud-cli kubectl awscli
   217→  ```
   218→  </Tab>
   219→  <Tab title="Ubuntu/Debian">
   220→  ```bash
   221→  sudo apt-get update && \
   222→  sudo apt-get install -y terraform google-cloud-sdk kubectl awscli
   223→  ```
   224→  </Tab>
   225→  <Tab title="Windows (Chocolatey)">
   226→  ```powershell
   227→  # Install Terraform
   228→  choco install terraform
   229→  
   230→  # Install Google Cloud SDK
   231→  choco install google-cloud-sdk
   232→  
   233→  # Install kubectl
   234→  choco install kubernetes-cli
   235→  
   236→  # Install AWS CLI
   237→  choco install awscli
   238→  ```
   239→  
   240→  <Info>
   241→  **Don't have Chocolatey?**
   242→  
   243→  Install Chocolatey first: https://chocolatey.org/install
   244→  
   245→  Or use **winget** (Windows Package Manager):
   246→  ```powershell
   247→  winget install -e --id Hashicorp.Terraform
   248→  winget install -e --id Google.CloudSDK
   249→  winget install -e --id Kubernetes.kubectl
   250→  winget install -e --id Amazon.AWSCLI
   251→  ```
   252→  </Info>
   253→  </Tab>
   254→  <Tab title="Manual Installation">
   255→  - [Terraform](https://www.terraform.io/downloads.html) - Version 1.0.0 or higher
   256→  - [Google Cloud SDK](https://cloud.google.com/sdk/docs/install) - Latest version
   257→  - [kubectl](https://kubernetes.io/docs/tasks/tools/) - Latest version
   258→  - [AWS CLI v2](https://aws.amazon.com/cli/) - For state management
   259→  </Tab>

   308→## 🚀 Quick Start Guide
   309→
   310→<Steps>
   311→  <Step title="Clone Repository" icon="git">
   312→    ```bash
   313→    git clone git@bitbucket.org:grupokonecta/crewai-gcp-infra-prod.git
   314→    cd crewai-gcp-infra-prod
   315→    ```
   316→  </Step>
   317→  
   318→  <Step title="Authenticate">
   319→    ```bash
   320→    # Authenticate with GCP
   321→    gcloud auth application-default login
   322→    
   323→    # Configure AWS SSO for Terraform state
   324→    aws sso login --profile default_core_irlanda
   325→    ```
   326→  </Step>
   327→  
   328→  <Step title="Configure Variables">
   329→    ```bash
   330→    cp terraform.auto.tfvars.example terraform.auto.tfvars
   331→    # Edit terraform.auto.tfvars with your values
   332→    ```
   333→  </Step>
   334→  
   335→  <Step title="Validate">
   336→    ```bash
   337→    make validate
   338→    ```
   339→    This runs:
   340→    - `terraform fmt` - Code formatting
   341→    - `terraform validate` - Syntax checking  
   342→    - `tflint` - Best practices linting
   343→  </Step>
   344→  
   345→  <Step title="Initialize & Plan">
   346→    ```bash
   347→    make init
   348→    make plan
   349→    ```
   350→    Preview the 70+ resources that will be created.
   351→  </Step>
   352→  
   353→  <Step title="Deploy Infrastructure" icon="check">
   354→    ```bash
   355→    make apply
   356→    ```
   357→    <Info>
   358→    **Deployment Time:** This takes approximately 15-20 minutes. Grab a coffee!
   359→    </Info>
   360→  </Step>
   361→  
   362→  <Step title="Verify Deployment">
   363→    ```bash
   364→    gcloud container clusters get-credentials crewai-cluster \
   365→      --region <your-region> \
   366→      --project <your-project-id>
   367→    
   368→    kubectl get nodes
   369→    ```
   370→  </Step>
   371→</Steps>

   373→## 📁 Project Structure
   374→
   375→### Root Directory Files
   376→
   377→```
   378→crewai-gcp-infra-prod/
   379→├── main.tf                    # Main module orchestration (70+ resources)
   380→├── variables.tf               # Input variable definitions
   381→├── outputs.tf                 # Output definitions
   382→├── backend.tf                 # Terraform state backend (AWS S3)
   383→├── terraform.auto.tfvars      # Variable values (GIT IGNORED - production secrets)

   390→└── modules/                   # Terraform modules directory

   537→## ⚙️ Configuration Guide
   538→
   539→### Production Security Checklist
   540→
   541→Before deploying to production, ensure:
   542→
   543→<CardGroup cols={2}>
   544→  <Card title="Access Control" icon="lock">
   545→    - Service account with least-privilege IAM roles
   546→    - VPC endpoint isolation enabled
   547→    - Workload Identity configured
   548→  </Card>
   549→  <Card title="Data Protection" icon="shield">
   550→    - Cloud SQL SSL/TLS enforced
   551→    - Secret Manager for all credentials
   552→    - Encryption at rest and in transit
   553→  </Card>
   554→  <Card title="Monitoring" icon="activity">
   555→    - Cloud Monitoring alerts configured
   556→    - Audit logging enabled
   557→    - VPC Flow Logs enabled
   558→  </Card>
   559→  <Card title="Compliance" icon="check">
   560→    - Resource naming follows Konecta standards
   561→    - Proper tagging for cost allocation
   562→    - Budget alerts configured
   563→  </Card>
   564→</CardGroup>
   565→
   566→### Essential Variables
   567→
   568→All variables are defined in [variables.tf](variables.tf). Key variables you must configure:
   569→
   570→#### GCP Organization Structure
   571→
   572→```hcl
   573→# Organizational hierarchy configuration (5-level structure)
   574→organization_id        = "123456789"      # GCP Organization ID
   575→root_folder_name       = "Global IT"      # Root folder name
   576→solution_folder_name   = "CrewAI"         # Level 0: Solution/Product folder
   577→env_folder_name        = "PROD"           # Level 1: Environment (DEV, PROD, STG, LGC)
   578→region_folder_name     = "CORP"           # Level 2: Region/Hub (CORP, LATAM, IBERIA)
   579→country_folder_name    = "CO"             # Level 3: Country (CO, BR, AR, etc.)
   580→```
   581→
   582→#### Project Configuration
   583→
   584→```hcl
   585→# Project metadata
   586→project_id   = "kn-co-globalit-svc-crewai-dev"  # Unique project ID
   587→project_name = "CREWAI-DEV-CO"                  # Display name
   588→description  = "CrewAI Platform Development"
   589→gcp_region   = "us-central1"                     # GCP region
   590→billing_account = "0156A6-7B8C9D-E2F3A4"       # Billing account ID
   591→```
   592→
   593→#### Project Tags
   594→
   595→```hcl
   596→# Organizational tagging
   597→department   = "Global IT"
   598→owner        = "your-name@company.com"
   599→country      = "CO"                    # ISO 3166 alpha-2 code
   600→client       = "Konecta"
   601→campaign     = "CrewAI Initiative"
   602→ceco         = "1234"                  # Cost center code
   603→creation_date = "2025-12-29"
   604→```
   605→
   606→#### CrewAI Platform Configuration
   607→
   608→```hcl
   609→# Network Configuration
   610→vpc_cidr   = "10.0.0.0/16"     # CIDR block for VPC
   611→zone_count = 3                  # Number of zones (2-3 for HA)
   612→
   613→# Storage Configuration
   614→gcs_crewai_data_suffix = "crewai-data"   # Bucket suffix: {project_id}-{suffix}
   615→gcs_crewai_logs_suffix = "crewai-logs"   # Bucket suffix: {project_id}-{suffix}
   616→
   617→# Cluster Configuration
   618→cluster_name                = "crewai-cluster"
   619→cluster_initial_node_count  = 1             # Starting number of nodes
   620→cluster_node_machine_type   = "e2-standard-4"  # Machine type for nodes
   621→cluster_authorized_networks = ["203.0.113.25/32"]  # External IPs for kubectl access (auto-detection adds Terraform runner IP)
   622→
   623→# Database Configuration
   624→db_instance_name      = "crewai-db"
   625→db_instance_tier      = "db-custom-4-16384"  # 4 vCPU, 16GB RAM
   626→db_edition            = "ENTERPRISE"         # Cloud SQL edition
   627→db_database_name      = "crewai"
   628→db_master_username    = "crewai_admin"
   629→reader_instance_count = 2                    # Read replicas
   630→```
   631→
   632→#### External IP & Network Access Configuration
   633→
   634→<CardGroup cols={2}>
   635→  <Card title="Auto-Detection" icon="zap">
   636→    Terraform runner IP automatically detected and authorized
   637→  </Card>
   638→  <Card title="Additional IPs" icon="lock">
   639→    Specify external IPs for team members needing kubectl access
   640→  </Card>
   641→  <Card title="CIDR Format" icon="shield">
   642→    Use CIDR notation: single IP = `/32`, subnet = appropriate mask
   643→  </Card>
   644→  <Card title="Private Cluster" icon="key">
   645→    Leave empty for private-only access (recommended for production)
   646→  </Card>
   647→</CardGroup>
   648→
   649→```hcl
   650→# Configure external IP access to GKE cluster API endpoint
   651→cluster_authorized_networks = [
   652→  "203.0.113.25/32",      # Jane's office IP
   653→  "198.51.100.0/24",      # Company VPN subnet
   654→  "192.0.2.50/32"         # CI/CD pipeline IP
   655→]
   656→
   657→# Note: The Terraform runner's IP is automatically added during deployment
   658→# Use "gcloud compute networks peerings list" to see VPC peerings
   659→# Use "gcloud container clusters describe" to view authorized networks
   660→```
   661→
   662→**How to find your external IP:**
   663→
   664→```bash
   665→# Get your current external IP
   666→curl -s https://checkip.amazonaws.com
   667→# Output: 203.0.113.25
   668→
   669→# Add to configuration
   670→cluster_authorized_networks = ["203.0.113.25/32"]
   671→
   672→# For entire subnet/VPN
   673→cluster_authorized_networks = ["198.51.100.0/24"]
   674→```
   675→
   676→**Verify authorized networks after deployment:**
   677→
   678→```bash
   679→gcloud container clusters describe crewai-cluster \
   680→  --region <your-region> \
   681→  --project <your-project-id> \
   682→  --format="value(masterAuthorizedNetworks.cidrBlocks[].cidrBlock)"
   683→```
   684→
   685→#### Budget Configuration
   686→
   687→```hcl
   688→# Budget monitoring
   689→budget            = "800"                # Monthly budget in EUR
   690→sa_control_spent  = "budget-controller"  # Service account for budget checks
   691→```
   692→
   693→### Variable Validation
   694→
   695→The configuration includes built-in validations:
   696→
   697→```hcl
   698→# VPC CIDR must be valid
   699→vpc_cidr = "10.0.0.0/16"  # ✅ Valid CIDR block
   700→
   701→# Zone count must be 2 or 3 for HA
   702→zone_count = 3  # ✅ Between 2-3
   703→
   704→# Machine type influences costs
   705→cluster_node_machine_type = "e2-standard-4"  # ✅ Valid GCP machine type
   706→```
   707→
   708→## 🚀 Deployment Instructions
   709→
   710→### Step 1: Prepare Your Environment
   711→
   712→<Warning>
   713→Ensure you have completed all prerequisites. Incorrect configuration can result in failed deployments or unexpected charges.
   714→</Warning>
   715→
   716→<Tabs>
   717→  <Tab title="Clone & Authenticate">
   718→    ```bash
   719→    # Clone repository
   720→    git clone git@bitbucket.org:grupokonecta/crewai-gcp-infra-prod.git
   721→    cd crewai-gcp-infra-prod
   722→    
   723→    # Authenticate with GCP (production service account recommended)
   724→    gcloud auth application-default login
   725→    # Or for service account: gcloud auth activate-service-account --key-file=key.json
   726→    ```
   727→  </Tab>
   728→  <Tab title="Configure State Management">
   729→    ```bash
   730→    # Setup AWS credentials for Terraform state
   731→    aws sso login --profile default_core_irlanda
   732→    
   733→    # Verify access
   734→    aws sts get-caller-identity --profile default_core_irlanda
   735→    ```
   736→  </Tab>
   737→  <Tab title="Verify Installation">
   738→    ```bash
   739→    terraform version      # Should show 1.0.0 or higher
   740→    gcloud --version       # Should show latest
   741→    kubectl version --client
   742→    tflint --version
   743→    ```
   744→  </Tab>
   745→</Tabs>
   746→
   747→### Step 2: Configure Variables
   748→
   749→```bash
   750→cp terraform.auto.tfvars.example terraform.auto.tfvars
   751→nano terraform.auto.tfvars  # or vim, code, etc.
   752→```
   753→
   754→<Info>
   755→**Required Configuration Sections**
   756→
   757→<AccordionGroup>
   758→  <Accordion title="Organizational Settings">
   759→    - `organization_id`: Your GCP Organization ID
   760→    - `root_folder_name`: Root folder name (e.g., "Global IT")
   761→    - `solution_folder_name`: Solution/Product folder (e.g., "CrewAI")
   762→    - `env_folder_name`: Environment (DEV, PROD, STG)
   763→    - `region_folder_name`: Region/Hub (CORP, LATAM, IBERIA)
   764→    - `country_folder_name`: Country code (CO, BR, AR, etc.)
   765→  </Accordion>
   766→  <Accordion title="Project Details">
   767→    - `project_id`: Unique project ID
   768→    - `project_name`: Display name
   769→    - `gcp_region`: GCP region (e.g., us-central1)
   770→    - `billing_account`: Billing account ID
   771→  </Accordion>
   772→  <Accordion title="Billing & Tags">
   773→    - `budget`: Monthly budget (EUR)
   774→    - `department`: Department name
   775→    - `country`: ISO country code
   776→    - `ceco`: Cost center code
   777→  </Accordion>
   778→  <Accordion title="CrewAI Platform">
   779→    - `vpc_cidr`: VPC CIDR block
   780→    - `cluster_name`: GKE cluster name
   781→    - `db_instance_tier`: Database tier
   782→    - `cluster_node_machine_type`: Node machine type
   783→  </Accordion>
   784→</AccordionGroup>
   785→</Info>
   786→
   787→### Step 3: Validate Configuration
   788→
   789→```bash
   790→make validate
   791→```
   792→
   793→<Info>
   794→**What Gets Validated**
   795→
   796→- <Badge>✓</Badge> **terraform fmt** - Formats your code
   797→- <Badge>✓</Badge> **terraform validate** - Checks HCL syntax
   798→- <Badge>✓</Badge> **tflint** - Checks best practices
   799→
   800→Expected output: `Success: no issues found`
   801→</Info>
   802→
   803→### Step 4: Initialize Terraform
   804→
   805→```bash
   806→make init
   807→```
   808→
   809→<Check>
   810→Terraform downloads required providers and initializes the state backend. This is a one-time operation.
   811→</Check>
   812→
   813→### Step 5: Preview Infrastructure Changes
   814→
   815→```bash
   816→make plan
   817→```
   818→
   819→<Info>
   820→**Plan Output**
   821→
   822→Expected: `Plan: 70 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy`
   823→
   824→This shows the 70+ resources that will be created in GCP.
   825→</Info>
   826→
   827→```bash
   828→# Optional: Save plan to file for review
   829→terraform plan -out=tfplan
   830→```
   831→
   832→### Step 6: Deploy Infrastructure
   833→
   834→```bash
   835→make apply
   836→```
   837→
   838→<Warning>
   839→**Confirm Deployment**
   840→
   841→When prompted `Do you want to perform these actions?`, type **yes** to confirm.
   842→
   843→This will create 70+ actual resources in GCP. Deployment takes **15-20 minutes**.
   844→</Warning>
   845→
   846→<Check>
   847→Terraform will output important information you'll need later:
   848→- <Badge>Cluster endpoint</Badge>
   849→- <Badge>Database connection details</Badge>
   850→- <Badge>Bucket names</Badge>
   851→- <Badge>Service account details</Badge>
   852→
   853→**Save this output for later reference!**
   854→</Check>
   855→
   856→### Step 7: Verify Deployment
   857→
   858→<Tabs>
   859→  <Tab title="Cluster Access">
   860→    ```bash
   861→    gcloud container clusters get-credentials crewai-cluster \
   862→      --region <your-gcp-region> \
   863→      --project <your-project-id>
   864→    
   865→    # Verify connectivity
   866→    kubectl get nodes
   867→    ```
   868→  </Tab>
   869→  <Tab title="Cluster Resources">
   870→    ```bash
   871→    kubectl get pods --all-namespaces
   872→    kubectl get services --all-namespaces
   873→    kubectl get deployments --all-namespaces
   874→    ```
   875→  </Tab>
   876→  <Tab title="Database">
   877→    ```bash
   878→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db \
   879→      --project <your-project-id>
   880→    ```
   881→  </Tab>
   882→  <Tab title="Storage">
   883→    ```bash
   884→    gsutil ls
   885→    gsutil ls -r gs://<bucket-name>/
   886→    ```
   887→  </Tab>
   888→</Tabs>
   889→
   890→## 🎯 Key Features
   891→
   892→<CardGroup cols={2}>
   893→  <Card title="Complete Automation" icon="zap">
   894→    Single `make apply` command creates 70+ GCP resources. No manual configuration needed.
   895→  </Card>
   896→  <Card title="Enterprise Security" icon="shield">
   897→    Private nodes, encrypted DBs, Secret Manager, fine-grained IAM, VPC isolation.
   898→  </Card>
   899→  <Card title="High Availability" icon="activity">
   900→    Multi-zone database with failover, read replicas, HA cluster, point-in-time recovery.
   901→  </Card>
   902→  <Card title="Production Ready" icon="check">
   903→    Comprehensive logging, budget alerts, auto-upgrades, health checks, versioned storage.
   904→  </Card>
   905→  <Card title="Cost Optimized" icon="dollar-sign">
   906→    Auto-scaling, committed discounts, right-sizing, budget monitoring, efficient scheduling.
   907→  </Card>
   908→  <Card title="Smart Network Access" icon="network">
   909→    Auto-detects Terraform runner IP, configurable external IPs for team kubectl access.
   910→  </Card>
   911→  <Card title="Developer Friendly" icon="code">
   912→    Clear Makefile, full docs, modular design, CI/CD ready, pre-commit hooks.
   913→  </Card>
   914→</CardGroup>
   915→
   916→## 🔄 Terraform Workflow
   917→
   918→### Understanding Terraform State
   919→
   920→```
   921→┌──────────────┐       ┌─────────────────┐       ┌──────────────────┐
   922→│   Your Code  │ ---→  │ Terraform State │ ---→  │  GCP Resources   │
   923→│  (.tf files) │       │   (AWS S3)      │       │  (70+ services)  │
   924→└──────────────┘       └─────────────────┘       └──────────────────┘
   925→       ↑                                                   │
   926→       │                                                   │
   927→       └───────────────── terraform plan/apply ──────────┘
   928→```
   929→
   930→State is stored in **AWS S3** (Konecta standard):
   931→- **Bucket**: `konecta-terraform-cuentas`
   932→- **Key**: `gcp/global-it/dev/dev-corp/dev-corp-co/kn-co-globalit-svc-crewai-dev`
   933→- **Encryption**: Enabled by default
   934→
   935→### Common Terraform Commands
   936→
   937→```bash
   938→# ✅ Format code (idiomatic style)
   939→make format
   940→terraform fmt -recursive
   941→
   942→# ✅ Validate syntax
   943→make validate
   944→terraform validate
   945→
   946→# ✅ Lint best practices
   947→make lint
   948→tflint --recursive
   949→
   950→# ✅ Preview changes
   951→make plan
   952→terraform plan -out=tfplan
   953→
   954→# ✅ Apply changes
   955→make apply
   956→terraform apply tfplan
   957→
   958→# ✅ Destroy infrastructure
   959→make destroy
   960→terraform destroy
   961→
   962→# ✅ View state
   963→terraform state list
   964→terraform state show <resource>
   965→
   966→# ✅ Refresh state
   967→terraform refresh
   968→
   969→# ✅ Get outputs
   970→terraform output
   971→```
   972→
   973→### Advanced Operations
   974→
   975→#### Targeting Specific Resources
   976→
   977→```bash
   978→# Apply changes to specific module
   979→terraform apply -target=module.crewai_platform.module.cluster
   980→
   981→# Useful for testing individual components
   982→```
   983→
   984→#### Importing Existing Resources
   985→
   986→```bash
   987→# If resources exist outside Terraform
   988→terraform import google_kubernetes_cluster.main \
   989→  projects/PROJECT_ID/zones/ZONE/clusters/CLUSTER_NAME
   990→```
   991→
   992→#### Debugging
   993→
   994→```bash
   995→# Enable debug logging
   996→TF_LOG=DEBUG terraform plan
   997→
   998→# Save detailed logs
   999→TF_LOG=DEBUG terraform apply -out=tfplan > debug.log 2>&1
  1000→```
  1001→
  1002→## 📊 Resource Overview
  1003→
  1004→### Approximate Resource Count by Module
  1005→
  1006→| Module | Resource Count | Deployment Time | Production Notes |
  1007→|--------|---|---|---|
  1008→| Folders | 3 | 30 seconds | Organizational hierarchy |
  1009→| Projects | 1 | 1 minute | Tagged and labeled |
  1010→| APIs | 50+ | 2 minutes | All enterprise APIs enabled |
  1011→| Service Accounts | 3-5 | 1 minute | Workload Identity enabled |
  1012→| IAM Roles | 10+ | 2 minutes | Least-privilege configuration |
  1013→| Networking | 8-10 | 2 minutes | Multi-zone, VPC Flow Logs |
  1014→| Storage | 2 | 30 seconds | Versioning and encryption |
  1015→| Database | 3-5 | 10-15 minutes | HA with read replicas |
  1016→| Cluster | 1 | 12-18 minutes | GKE Standard, private nodes |
  1017→| Monitoring | 5+ | 1 minute | Cloud Monitoring and Logging |
  1018→| **Total** | **80-90** | **20-30 minutes** | **Complete production setup** |
  1019→
  1020→### Cost Estimation - Production Configuration
  1021→
  1022→Based on production-recommended configuration:
  1023→
  1024→| Component | Estimated Cost | Configuration |
  1025→|-----------|---|---|
  1026→| GKE Cluster | €200-350/month | 3 nodes, HA, auto-scaling |
  1027→| Cloud SQL (HA + replicas) | €400-550/month | db-custom-4-16384 + 2 read replicas |
  1028→| VPC & Load Balancing | €50-100/month | Multi-zone, Cloud NAT |
  1029→| Storage (GCS) | €15-25/month | Data + logs with versioning |
  1030→| Monitoring & Logging | €20-40/month | Cloud Monitoring, Logging, Alerting |
  1031→| Secrets & Services | €15-30/month | Secret Manager, Artifact Registry |
  1032→| Cloud Functions | €10-20/month | Budget monitoring |
  1033→| **Total Production** | **€710-1,115/month** | **Fully HA enterprise config** |
  1034→
  1035→<Tip>
  1036→**Cost Optimization Options**
  1037→
  1038→<AccordionGroup>
  1039→  <Accordion title="For Development Environments">
  1040→    - Use `e2-standard-2` instead of `e2-standard-4`
  1041→    - Set `cluster_initial_node_count = 1`
  1042→    - Set `reader_instance_count = 0` (no replicas)
  1043→    - Set `db_instance_tier = "db-custom-2-7680"` (2 vCPU, 7.5GB RAM)
  1044→    - Disable advanced monitoring and logging to reduce costs
  1045→      - **Estimated Savings**: €400-500/month
  1046→    </Accordion>
  1047→    <Accordion title="For Production Environments">
  1048→      - Use `e2-standard-4` or higher for nodes
  1049→      - Set `cluster_initial_node_count = 3` (HA configuration)
  1050→      - Set `reader_instance_count = 2` (read replicas for scaling)
  1051→      - Use `db-custom-4-16384` or higher (4+ vCPU, 16GB+ RAM)
  1052→      - Enable Cloud Armor for DDoS protection
  1053→      - Enable binary logging for point-in-time recovery
  1054→      - Implement VPC Service Controls for additional security
  1055→      - **Recommended**: €800-1,000/month budget
  1056→    </Accordion>
  1057→  </AccordionGroup>
  1058→</Tip>
  1059→
  1060→## 🐛 Troubleshooting
  1061→
  1062→### Common Issues and Solutions
  1063→
  1064→<AccordionGroup>
  1065→  <Accordion title="Terraform Init Fails" icon="alert-circle">
  1066→    **Error**: `Error: Failed to download module`
  1067→    
  1068→    ```bash
  1069→    # Clear cache and retry
  1070→    rm -rf .terraform
  1071→    terraform init
  1072→    
  1073→    # Check provider configuration
  1074→    cat backend.tf
  1075→    ```
  1076→  </Accordion>
  1077→  
  1078→  <Accordion title="Authentication Errors" icon="lock">
  1079→    **Error**: `Error: Google Cloud credentials not found`
  1080→    
  1081→    ```bash
  1082→    # Re-authenticate with GCP
  1083→    gcloud auth application-default login
  1084→    gcloud auth list
  1085→    
  1086→    # For AWS state backend
  1087→    aws sso login --profile default_core_irlanda
  1088→    ```
  1089→  </Accordion>
  1090→  
  1091→  <Accordion title="API Not Enabled" icon="zap">
  1092→    **Error**: `Error: Service [...] has not been used in project`
  1093→    
  1094→    ```bash
  1095→    # APIs auto-enable in modules/apis
  1096→    make plan
  1097→    make apply
  1098→    
  1099→    # Or enable manually
  1100→    gcloud services enable compute.googleapis.com --project=PROJECT_ID
  1101→    ```
  1102→  </Accordion>
  1103→  
  1104→  <Accordion title="Insufficient Quota" icon="alert">
  1105→    **Error**: `Error: Quota exceeded for quota metric`
  1106→    
  1107→    ```bash
  1108→    gcloud compute project-info describe --project=PROJECT_ID
  1109→    ```
  1110→    
  1111→    Then request quota increase in GCP Console:
  1112→    **IAM & Admin** → **Quotas** → **Select service** → **Request quota increase**
  1113→  </Accordion>
  1114→  
  1115→  <Accordion title="GKE Cluster Timeout" icon="clock">
  1116→    **Error**: `Error: timeout while waiting for cluster creation`
  1117→    
  1118→    ```bash
  1119→    gcloud container clusters describe crewai-cluster \
  1120→      --region=REGION \
  1121→      --project=PROJECT_ID
  1122→    
  1123→    gcloud container operations list --project=PROJECT_ID
  1124→    
  1125→    # If stuck, retry
  1126→    make destroy
  1127→    make apply
  1128→    ```
  1129→  </Accordion>
  1130→  
  1131→  <Accordion title="Database Connection Fails" icon="database">
  1132→    **Error**: `Error: failed to connect to database`
  1133→    
  1134→    ```bash
  1135→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db --project=PROJECT_ID
  1136→    gcloud compute firewall-rules list --project=PROJECT_ID
  1137→    gcloud services vpc-peerings list \
  1138→      --service=servicenetworking.googleapis.com \
  1139→      --project=PROJECT_ID
  1140→    ```
  1141→  </Accordion>
  1142→</AccordionGroup>
  1143→
  1144→### Getting Help
  1145→
  1146→<CardGroup cols={2}>
  1147→  <Card title="Debug Terraform" icon="wrench">
  1148→    ```bash
  1149→    TF_LOG=DEBUG terraform apply
  1150→    terraform output
  1151→    ```
  1152→  </Card>

  1157→    ```

  1163→    ```
  1164→  </Card>
  1165→</CardGroup>
