  1175→- [Networking Module](modules/crewai/networking/README.md) - VPC configuration
  1176→- [Cluster Add-ons Module](modules/crewai/cluster-addons/README.md) - K8s resources
  1177→
  1178→### External References
  1179→
  1180→- [Terraform Documentation](https://www.terraform.io/docs)
  1181→- [Google Cloud Provider Documentation](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)
  1182→- [GKE Best Practices](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/best-practices)
  1183→- [Cloud SQL Best Practices](https://cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/best-practices)
  1184→
  1185→## 🤝 Contributing
  1186→
  1187→### Development Setup
  1188→
  1189→```bash
  1190→# Clone repository
  1191→git clone git@bitbucket.org:grupokonecta/crewai-gcp-infra-prod.git
  1192→cd crewai-gcp-infra-prod
  1193→
  1194→# Create feature branch
  1195→git checkout -b feature/your-feature-name
  1196→
  1197→# Validate changes
  1198→make validate
  1199→
  1200→# Commit with clear messages
  1201→git commit -m "feat: add new feature description"
  1202→
  1203→# Push and create pull request
  1204→git push origin feature/your-feature-name
  1205→```
  1206→
  1207→### Code Style
  1208→
  1209→All code must pass:
  1210→
  1211→```bash
  1212→# Format code consistently
  1213→terraform fmt -recursive
  1214→
  1215→# Validate syntax
  1216→terraform validate
  1217→
  1218→# Check best practices
  1219→tflint --recursive
  1220→```
  1221→
  1222→### Pre-commit Hooks
  1223→
  1224→Enable pre-commit hooks to validate automatically:
  1225→
  1226→```bash
  1227→# Install pre-commit
  1228→pip install pre-commit
  1229→
  1230→# Install hooks
  1231→pre-commit install
  1232→
  1233→# Run manually
  1234→pre-commit run --all-files
  1235→```
  1236→
  1237→### Contribution Guidelines
  1238→
  1239→1. **Create Feature Branch**: `git checkout -b feature/description`
  1240→2. **Write Code**: Follow Terraform style guide
  1241→3. **Run Validation**: `make validate` (must pass)
  1242→4. **Test Deployment**: `terraform plan` in test environment
  1243→5. **Update Documentation**: Keep README and module docs current
  1244→6. **Submit PR**: Include clear description of changes
  1245→7. **Code Review**: Address feedback from maintainers
  1246→8. **Merge**: Squash commits into main
  1247→
  1248→### Reporting Issues
  1249→
  1250→When reporting bugs, include:
  1251→
  1252→- Terraform version: `terraform version`
  1253→- GCP SDK version: `gcloud --version`
  1254→- Exact error message and stack trace
  1255→- Steps to reproduce the issue
  1256→- Your environment details (region, configuration)
  1257→
  1258→**Example Issue Template**:
  1259→
  1260→```markdown
  1261→## Bug Report
  1262→
  1263→### Description
  1264→Brief description of the issue
  1265→
  1266→### Steps to Reproduce
  1267→1. Configure variables as follows...
  1268→2. Run `terraform plan`
  1269→3. Observe error...
  1270→
  1271→### Expected Behavior
  1272→Should create resources without errors
  1273→
  1274→### Actual Behavior
  1275→Error message:
  1276→\`\`\`
  1277→[complete error text]
  1278→\`\`\`
  1279→
  1280→### Environment
  1281→- Terraform version: 1.5.0
  1282→- GCP SDK version: 455.0.0
  1283→- Region: us-central1
  1284→- Configuration: [relevant variables]
  1285→```
  1286→
  1287→## � Production Deployment Checklist
  1288→
  1289→Before deploying to production, complete this checklist:
  1290→
  1291→<AccordionGroup>
  1292→  <Accordion title="Infrastructure Readiness">
  1293→    - [ ] GCP Organization and billing account configured
  1294→    - [ ] Service account created with appropriate IAM roles
  1295→    - [ ] AWS S3 state backend access verified
  1296→    - [ ] All variables configured in terraform.auto.tfvars
  1297→    - [ ] Budget alerts configured (€1,000/month recommended)
  1298→    - [ ] Network isolation and firewall rules reviewed
  1299→  </Accordion>
  1300→  
  1301→  <Accordion title="Security & Compliance">
  1302→    - [ ] SSL/TLS certificates configured
  1303→    - [ ] Database credentials stored in Secret Manager
  1304→    - [ ] Workload Identity enabled for service accounts
  1305→    - [ ] VPC Service Controls implemented (if required)
  1306→    - [ ] Cloud Armor DDoS protection enabled
  1307→    - [ ] Audit logging enabled for all resources
  1308→    - [ ] VPC Flow Logs enabled for network monitoring
  1309→  </Accordion>
  1310→  
  1311→  <Accordion title="Monitoring & Logging">
  1312→    - [ ] Cloud Monitoring dashboards created
  1313→    - [ ] Alert policies configured for critical metrics
  1314→    - [ ] Cloud Logging retention set appropriately
  1315→    - [ ] Log-based metrics configured
  1316→    - [ ] Error reporting configured
  1317→    - [ ] Cloud Trace enabled for performance monitoring
  1318→  </Accordion>
  1319→  
  1320→  <Accordion title="Data & Backups">
  1321→    - [ ] Cloud SQL backups configured (automated)
  1322→    - [ ] Point-in-time recovery verified (7-day retention)
  1323→    - [ ] GCS bucket versioning enabled
  1324→    - [ ] Backup retention policies configured
  1325→    - [ ] Disaster recovery plan documented
  1326→  </Accordion>
  1327→  
  1328→  <Accordion title="Testing & Validation">
  1329→    - [ ] terraform validate passed
  1330→    - [ ] tflint checks passed with no errors
  1331→    - [ ] Plan reviewed for correctness (terraform plan)
  1332→    - [ ] Dry-run tested in non-production environment
  1333→    - [ ] Database connectivity verified
  1334→    - [ ] Kubernetes cluster connectivity verified
  1335→    - [ ] Application deployment tested
  1336→  </Accordion>
  1337→</AccordionGroup>

     1→---
     2→title: GCP CrewAI Installation Guide
     3→description: Install CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud using Cloud Shell
     4→---
     5→
     6→# ☁️ CrewAI Platform Installation on Google Cloud
     7→
     8→A step-by-step guide to install the CrewAI Platform using Google Cloud Shell, including project setup, configuration, and platform deployment.
     9→For the official installation documentation, see [CrewAI Enterprise Installation Guide](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/installation/installation).
    10→
    11→
    12→<Info>
    13→Google Cloud Shell is a free, browser-based terminal that comes pre-configured with the Google Cloud SDK, kubectl, and other tools you need for installation.
    14→</Info>
    15→
    16→<Warning>
    17→**Prerequisites: GCP Infrastructure Setup Required**
    18→
    19→Before installing CrewAI Platform, you must first set up your GCP infrastructure including:
    20→- GCP Organization and Project structure
    21→- GKE Kubernetes cluster
    22→- Cloud SQL database
    23→- Cloud Storage buckets
    24→- Networking and security configuration
    25→
    26→Follow the [**GCP Infrastructure Setup Guide**](/crewai/gcp-setup) to provision these resources before proceeding with this installation.
    27→</Warning>
    28→
    29→## 📋 Prerequisites
    30→
    31→Before you begin, ensure you have:
    32→
    33→<CardGroup cols={2}>
    34→  <Card title="GCP Project" icon="project">
    35→    An active GCP project with billing enabled
    36→  </Card>
    37→  <Card title="GKE Cluster" icon="container">
    38→    A running Google Kubernetes Engine cluster with sufficient resources
    39→  </Card>
    40→  <Card title="Cloud Shell Access" icon="terminal">
    41→    Access to Google Cloud Console
    42→  </Card>
    43→  <Card title="Registry Credentials" icon="key">
    44→    CrewAI enterprise registry credentials from the customer portal
    45→  </Card>
    46→</CardGroup>
    47→
    48→<Warning>
    49→**Get Your Credentials**
    50→
    51→Access your registry credentials via: [https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai/install](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai/install)
    52→
    53→You'll need:
    54→- Customer email address
    55→- Secret authentication token
    56→
    57→**Need help accessing the portal?** Contact:
    58→- nathan.booth@konecta.com
    59→- mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com
    60→</Warning>
    61→
    62→## 🚀 Installation Steps
    63→
    64→### Step 1: Set Your Project ID
    65→
    66→Open Google Cloud Console and launch Cloud Shell, then set your project:
    67→
    68→<CodeGroup>
    69→```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
    70→# Set your GCP project ID
    71→export PROJECT_ID="your-gcp-project-id"
    72→
    73→# Verify the project is set
    74→gcloud config set project $PROJECT_ID
    75→gcloud config get-value project
    76→```
    77→
    78→```powershell Local Windows
    79→# Set your GCP project ID
    80→$PROJECT_ID = "your-gcp-project-id"
    81→
    82→# Verify the project is set
    83→gcloud config set project $PROJECT_ID
    84→gcloud config get-value project
    85→```
    86→</CodeGroup>
    87→
    88→<Check>
    89→Your Cloud Shell will now use this project for all subsequent commands.
    90→</Check>
    91→
    92→### Step 2: Create Project Metadata Folder
    93→
    94→Create a folder to organize and store your CrewAI deployment metadata:
    95→
    96→<Steps>
    97→  <Step title="Create Metadata Directory">
    98→    ```bash
    99→    # Create a folder to store CrewAI configuration and metadata
   100→    mkdir -p ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata
   101→    
   102→    # Navigate to the directory
   103→    cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID
   104→    
   105→    # Verify the structure
   106→    ls -la
   107→    ```
   108→  </Step>
   109→  
   110→  <Step title="Create Supporting Directories">
   111→    ```bash
   112→    # Create subdirectories for organization
   113→    mkdir -p metadata/secrets
   114→    mkdir -p metadata/backups
   115→    mkdir -p metadata/logs
   116→    
   117→    # Create a README for documentation
   118→    cat > metadata/README.md << 'EOF'
   119→    # CrewAI Deployment Metadata
   120→    
   121→    Project ID: $PROJECT_ID
   122→    Created: $(date)
   123→    
   124→    ## Directory Structure
   125→    - secrets/: Sensitive configuration files (DO NOT COMMIT)
   126→    - backups/: Backup of configurations and values
   127→    - logs/: Installation and upgrade logs
   128→    
   129→    ## Important Files
   130→    - my-values.yaml: Helm chart values (store in secrets/)
   131→    
   132→    EOF
   133→    
   134→    cat metadata/README.md
   135→    ```
   136→  </Step>
   137→</Steps>
   138→
   139→### Step 3: Configure Installation via Enterprise Portal
   140→
   141→Access the CrewAI Enterprise Portal to configure and retrieve your installation details:
   142→
   143→<Steps>
   144→  <Step title="Access the Install Tab">
   145→    Log in to [https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai) and navigate to the **Install** tab.
   146→  </Step>
   147→  
   148→  <Step title="Configure Instance Details">
   149→    Provide the following information:
   150→    
   151→    **Instance Configuration:**
   152→    - **Instance Name:** FQDN format (e.g., `dev.crewai.konecta.com`)
   153→    - **Kubernetes Distribution:** Google GKE
   154→    - **Cluster Network Availability:** Outbound requests allowed
   155→    
   156→    **Registry Access:**
   157→    - Select: "My workstation can access the internet, the registry AND the cluster"
   158→  </Step>
   159→  
   160→  <Step title="Retrieve Installation Instructions">
   161→    The portal will generate and display your complete Helm Online Install workflow, including:
   162→    - App version selection
   163→    - Authentication credentials and export commands
   164→    - Custom values file template
   165→    - Preflight validation instructions
   166→    - Helm chart installation command with your specific version and values
   167→  </Step>
   168→</Steps>
   169→
   170→The portal provides a complete installation sequence with the following steps:
   171→
   172→<Steps>
   173→  <Step title="Select a Version">
   174→    Choose your desired app version from the portal's version selector.
   175→  </Step>
   176→
   177→  <Step title="Export Credentials and Log In">
   178→    Copy and run the authentication commands provided by the portal:
   179→
   180→    ```bash
   181→    export AUTH_TOKEN=<token>
   182→    helm registry login registry.crewai.com --username mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com --password $AUTH_TOKEN
   183→    ```
   184→  </Step>
   185→
   186→  <Step title="Create Values Override File">
   187→    Create a `my-values.yaml` file to customize your installation (detailed in Step 4 below).
   188→  </Step>
   189→
   190→  <Step title="Install Preflight Plugin">
   191→    Install the preflight validation plugin on GCP Cloud Shell:
   192→
   193→    ```bash
   194→    curl https://krew.sh/preflight | bash
   195→    ```
   196→  </Step>
   197→
   198→  <Step title="Install the Chart">
   199→    Run the Helm installation command with your specific version:
   200→
   201→    ```bash
   202→    helm install crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   203→      --version 0.3.1 \
   204→      --values my-values.yaml
   205→    ```
   206→  </Step>
   207→</Steps>
   208→
   209→<Info>
   210→**Important:** Copy the exact commands provided by the portal, as they contain your unique authentication token and version-specific installation details.
   211→</Info>
   212→
   213→### Step 4: Prepare Configuration File (my-values.yaml)
   214→
   215→Use the values template provided by the enterprise portal as a starting point. Here's a reference configuration:
   216→
   217→<Steps>
   218→  <Step title="Get Portal Template">
   219→    The portal provides a pre-configured `my-values.yaml` template customized for your instance. Download or copy this template as your base configuration.
   220→  </Step>
   221→  
   222→  <Step title="Customize Your Configuration">
   223→    Edit the template with your specific settings:
   224→
   225→    <CodeGroup>
   226→    ```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
   227→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   228→    cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata
   229→
   230→    # Open in editor (nano or vim)
   231→    nano my-values.yaml
   232→    ```
   233→
   234→    ```powershell Windows
   235→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   236→    cd ~\crewai-deployment\$PROJECT_ID\metadata
   237→
   238→    # Open in editor
   239→    notepad my-values.yaml
   240→    ```
   241→    </CodeGroup>
   242→
   243→    <Warning>
   244→    **Security: Secrets Management**
   245→
   246→    **NEVER commit `my-values.yaml` to version control if it contains secrets!**
   247→
   248→    Instead:
   249→    1. Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager
   250→    2. Use CI/CD pipeline to inject secrets
   251→    3. Or create a `.gitignore` entry and manage separately
   252→    </Warning>
   253→  </Step>
   254→
   255→  <Step title="Key Configuration Parameters">
   256→    Configure these essential parameters in your `my-values.yaml`:
   257→
   258→    ### Application Configuration
   259→
   260→    <ParamField path="envVars.APPLICATION_HOST" type="string" required>
   261→      Your domain name (FQDN) for the CrewAI instance
   262→
   263→      **Example:** `dev.crewai.konecta.com`
   264→    </ParamField>
   265→
   266→    <ParamField path="envVars.AUTH_PROVIDER" type="string" required>
   267→      Authentication provider to use
   268→
   269→      **Options:** `workos`, `entra_id`, `google`, `local`
   270→    </ParamField>
   271→
   272→    ### Database Configuration
   273→
   274→    <ParamField path="envVars.DB_HOST" type="string" required>
   275→      Private IP address of your Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance
   276→
   277→      **Example:** `10.29.0.2`
   278→    </ParamField>
   279→
   280→    <ParamField path="envVars.DB_PORT" type="string" default="5432">
   281→      PostgreSQL database port
   282→    </ParamField>
   283→
   284→    <ParamField path="envVars.DB_USER" type="string" default="postgres">
   285→      Database username
   286→    </ParamField>
   287→
   288→    <ParamField path="secrets.DB_PASSWORD" type="string" required>
   289→      Database password (store in Secret Manager)
   290→    </ParamField>
   291→
   292→    ### Storage Configuration (S3-compatible GCS)
   293→
   294→    <ParamField path="envVars.STORAGE_SERVICE" type="string" default="amazon">
   295→      Storage service type (use "amazon" for S3-compatible GCS)
   296→    </ParamField>
   297→
   298→    <ParamField path="envVars.AWS_REGION" type="string" required>
   299→      GCP region for Cloud Storage buckets
   300→
   301→      **Example:** `europe-west1`
   302→    </ParamField>
   303→
   304→    <ParamField path="envVars.AWS_BUCKET" type="string" required>
   305→      Main data storage bucket name
   306→
   307→      **Example:** `your-project-crewai-data`
   308→    </ParamField>
   309→
   310→    <ParamField path="envVars.LOGS_BUCKET" type="string" required>
   311→      Logs storage bucket name
   312→
   313→      **Example:** `your-project-crewai-logs`
   314→    </ParamField>
   315→
   316→    <ParamField path="envVars.AWS_ENDPOINT" type="string" default="https://storage.googleapis.com">
   317→      S3-compatible endpoint for GCS
   318→    </ParamField>
   319→
   320→    <ParamField path="secrets.AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID" type="string" required>
   321→      GCS HMAC access key ID (from service account)
   322→    </ParamField>
   323→
   324→    <ParamField path="secrets.AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY" type="string" required>
   325→      GCS HMAC secret access key
   326→    </ParamField>
   327→
   328→    ### WorkOS Authentication
   329→
   330→    <ParamField path="envVars.WORKOS_CLIENT_ID" type="string" required>
   331→      WorkOS client ID from your WorkOS dashboard
   332→
   333→      **Format:** `client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX`
   334→    </ParamField>
   335→
   336→    <ParamField path="envVars.WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN" type="string" required>
   337→      WorkOS AuthKit domain
   338→
   339→      **Example:** `your-domain.authkit.app`
   340→    </ParamField>
   341→
   342→    <ParamField path="secrets.WORKOS_API_KEY" type="string" required>
   343→      WorkOS API key (store in Secret Manager)
   344→    </ParamField>
   345→
   346→    <ParamField path="secrets.WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD" type="string" required>
   347→      32-character random string for session encryption
   348→
   349→      **Generate with:** `openssl rand -hex 16`
   350→    </ParamField>
   351→
   352→    ### Container Registry
   353→
   354→    <ParamField path="envVars.CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE" type="string">
   355→      Custom container registry for CrewAI images
   356→
   357→      **Example:** `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/your-project/crewai-enterprise`
   358→    </ParamField>
   359→
   360→    <ParamField path="image.registries[].host" type="string" required>
   361→      Container registry hostname
   362→
   363→      **Example:** `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev`
   364→    </ParamField>
   365→
   366→    <ParamField path="image.registries[].username" type="string" default="_json_key_base64">
   367→      Registry authentication username (use `_json_key_base64` for GCP)
   368→    </ParamField>
   369→
   370→    <ParamField path="image.registries[].password" type="string" required>
   371→      Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON key
   372→    </ParamField>
   373→
   374→    ### Web Service
   375→
   376→    <ParamField path="web.replicaCount" type="number" default="1">
   377→      Number of web server replicas
   378→
   379→      **Development:** `1` | **Production:** `3`
   380→    </ParamField>
   381→
   382→    <ParamField path="web.ingress.enabled" type="boolean" default="true">
   383→      Enable ingress controller
   384→    </ParamField>
   385→
   386→    <ParamField path="web.ingress.host" type="string" required>
   387→      Ingress hostname (should match APPLICATION_HOST)
   388→    </ParamField>
   389→
   390→    ### Worker Service
   391→
   392→    <ParamField path="worker.replicaCount" type="number" default="1">
   393→      Number of background worker replicas
   394→
   395→      **Development:** `1` | **Production:** `3`
   396→    </ParamField>
   397→
   398→    ### OAuth Configuration
   399→
   400→    <ParamField path="oauth.enabled" type="boolean" default="false">
   401→      Enable OAuth for third-party integrations
   402→    </ParamField>
   403→
   404→    <ParamField path="secrets.OAUTH_DB_HOST" type="string">
   405→      OAuth database host (required if oauth.enabled = true)
   406→    </ParamField>
   407→  </Step>
   408→
   409→  <Step title="Reference: Sample Configuration Structure">
   410→    ```yaml
   411→    # CrewAI Platform Helm Values Configuration
   412→    # This example shows both Development and Production settings
   413→    
   414→    postgres:
   415→      enabled: false  # Use external Cloud SQL database
   416→    
   417→    minio:
   418→      enabled: false  # Use external GCP Cloud Storage (S3-compatible)
   419→    
   420→    envVars:
   421→      # Application Configuration
   422→      APPLICATION_HOST: "dev.crewai.konecta.com"  # Your domain name
   423→      
   424→      # Database Configuration
   425→      DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"  # Private IP of Cloud SQL instance
   426→      DB_PORT: "5432"
   427→      DB_USER: "postgres"
   428→      
   429→      # Container Registry Configuration
   430→      CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/your-project/crewai-enterprise"
   431→      
   432→      # Authentication Provider
   433→      AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
   434→      WORKOS_CLIENT_ID: "client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX"  # from WorkOS dashboard
   435→      WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: "your-domain.authkit.app"
   436→      
   437→      # Storage Configuration (S3-compatible GCP Cloud Storage)
   438→      STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"  # S3-compatible interface
   439→      AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   440→      AWS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-data"
   441→      LOGS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-logs"
   442→      LOGS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   443→      AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"  # GCS S3-compatible endpoint
   444→      LOGS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   445→    
   446→    secrets:
   447→      # Database Credentials
   448→      DB_PASSWORD: "[REDACTED]"  # Store in GCP Secret Manager
   449→      
   450→      # GCS HMAC Keys (tied to service account)
   451→      AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: "[REDACTED]"  # From Cloud Storage HMAC keys
   452→      AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: "[REDACTED]"
   453→      
   454→      # Authentication Secrets
   455→      WORKOS_API_KEY: "[REDACTED]"  # from WorkOS API keys
   456→      OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET: "[REDACTED]"  # Random secure string
   457→      
   458→      # OAuth Configuration
   459→      OAUTH_DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"
   460→      WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD: "[REDACTED]"
   461→    
   462→    # Container Registry Authentication
   463→    image:
   464→      registries:
   465→        - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
   466→          username: "_json_key_base64"
   467→          password: "[REDACTED]"  # Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON
   468→    
   469→    # Web Service Configuration
   470→    web:
   471→      # DEVELOPMENT: Single replica for cost savings
   472→      replicaCount: 1
   473→      
   474→      # PRODUCTION: Multiple replicas for zero-downtime deployments
   475→      # replicaCount: 3
   476→      # terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60  # Graceful shutdown time
   477→      
   478→      port: 80
   479→      useHttps: false
   480→      enableSslFromPuma: false
   481→      
   482→      # Ingress Configuration (GCP Load Balancer)
   483→      ingress:
   484→        enabled: true
   485→        className: ""  # Empty for GCP Ingress
   486→        host: "dev.crewai.konecta.com"
   487→        annotations:
   488→          # GCP-specific annotations
   489→          networking.gke.io/managed-certificates: gcp-domains-ssl-cert
   490→          kubernetes.io/ingress.global-static-ip-name: "dev-crewai-konecta-com-external-ip"
   491→          kubernetes.io/ingress.class: "gce"
   492→          networking.gke.io/v1beta1.FrontendConfig: "gcp-sslpolicy"
   493→    
   494→    # Background Job Processing
   495→    worker:
   496→      # DEVELOPMENT: Single worker for cost savings
   497→      replicaCount: 1
   498→      
   499→      # PRODUCTION: Multiple workers for parallel job processing
   500→      # replicaCount: 3
   501→    
   502→    # GCP Configuration
   503→    gcp:
   504→      ssldomains:
   505→        - "dev.crewai.konecta.com"
   506→      sslPolicy: "dev-crewai-ssl-policy"
   507→    
   508→    # OAuth Configuration
   509→    oauth:
   510→      enabled: true
   511→    ```
   512→
   513→    <Note>
   514→    **Infrastructure Note**: The static IP address resource must be provisioned in GCP before installation. See the [GCP Infrastructure Setup Guide](/crewai/gcp-setup) for details on creating Compute Address resources, SSL policies, and managed certificates.
   515→    </Note>
   516→  </Step>
   517→</Steps>
   518→
   519→### Key Configuration Differences: Development vs Production
   520→
   521→<Tabs>
   522→  <Tab title="Development">
   523→    ```yaml
   524→    # Development Configuration (Cost-optimized)
   525→    
   526→    web:
   527→      replicaCount: 1  # Single instance to save costs
   528→    
   529→    worker:
   530→      replicaCount: 1  # Single worker for background jobs
   531→    
   532→    # DNS: dev.crewai.konecta.com
   533→    # Resources: Minimal (adequate for testing/validation)
   534→    # Scaling: Manual - adjust replicaCount as needed
   535→    ```
   536→
   537→    <Tip>
   538→    Development environments typically use:
   539→    - Single replicas for all services
   540→    - Smaller machine types (e2-small, e2-medium)
   541→    - Reduced resource limits
   542→    - Shared infrastructure when possible
   543→    </Tip>
   544→  </Tab>
   545→
   546→  <Tab title="Production">
   547→    ```yaml
   548→    # Production Configuration (High Availability)
   549→    
   550→    web:
   551→      replicaCount: 3  # Three replicas for zero-downtime deployments
   552→      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60  # Allow graceful shutdown
   553→    
   554→    worker:
   555→      replicaCount: 3  # Distribute background job processing across replicas
   556→    
   557→    # Auto-scaling (if enabled):
   558→    # minReplicas: 3
   559→    # maxReplicas: 10
   560→    # targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   561→    
   562→    # DNS: crewai.konecta.com (or prod domain)
   563→    # Resources: Production-grade (CPU/Memory allocations)
   564→    # Scaling: Auto-scaling enabled
   565→    ```
   566→
   567→    <Tip>
   568→    Production environments should use:
   569→    - Multiple replicas (minimum 3 for high availability)
   570→    - Larger machine types (e2-standard or higher)
   571→    - Resource requests and limits properly configured
   572→    - Horizontal Pod Autoscaling (HPA) enabled
   573→    - Separate infrastructure from development
   574→    </Tip>
   575→  </Tab>
   576→</Tabs>
   577→
   578→### Sensitive Fields Security
   579→
   580→<Warning>
   581→**Never Store Secrets in my-values.yaml**
   582→
   583→The following fields must **NEVER** be committed to version control:
   584→
   585→- `DB_PASSWORD`: Store in Google Cloud Secret Manager
   586→- `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`: GCS HMAC credentials
   587→- `WORKOS_API_KEY`: WorkOS authentication token
   588→- `OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET`: Cryptographic key for session management
   589→- `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD`: Password for WorkOS integration
   590→- `image.registries[].password`: Container registry credentials
   591→
   592→**Recommended approach:**
   593→1. Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager
   594→2. Use Kubernetes External Secrets Operator (ESO) to sync them
   595→3. Reference Secret Manager values in your deployment pipeline
   596→4. Or use GCP Workload Identity for automatic credential injection
   597→</Warning>
   598→
   599→### Step 5: Prepare Your Environment
   600→
   601→Ensure your cluster is ready for deployment:
   602→
   603→<Steps>
   604→  <Step title="Verify Cloud SQL Connection">
   605→    If using Google Cloud SQL, retrieve your connection details:
   606→    
   607→    ```bash
   608→    # List your Cloud SQL instances
   609→    gcloud sql instances list
   610→
   611→    # Get specific instance details
   612→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db \
   613→      --format="get(connectionName,ipAddresses[0].ipAddress)"
   614→
   615→    # For Private IP (recommended for Cloud SQL)
   616→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db \
   617→      --format="get(ipAddresses[0].ipAddress)"
   618→    ```
   619→    
   620→    <Info>
   621→    **Connection String Format**
   622→
   623→    For Cloud SQL Private IP:
   624→    ```
   625→    DB_HOST: your-instance.c.your-project.internal
   626→    ```
   627→    </Info>
   628→  </Step>
   629→
   630→  <Step title="Verify Default Storage Class">
   631→    Ensure your cluster has a default storage class:
   632→    
   633→    ```bash
   634→    # Check current storage classes
   635→    kubectl get storageclass
   636→
   637→    # Set standard as default (if not already)
   638→    kubectl patch storageclass standard \
   639→      -p '{"metadata": {"annotations":{"storageclass.kubernetes.io/is-default-class":"true"}}}'
   640→    ```
   641→    
   642→    <Check>
   643→    **Storage Class Ready**
   644→
   645→    Your cluster now has a default storage class for persistent volumes.
   646→    </Check>
   647→  </Step>
   648→
   649→  <Step title="Install Preflight Validation Plugin">
   650→    Install the preflight plugin from the portal command:
   651→    
   652→    ```bash
   653→    # From portal step 4: Install preflight plugin
   654→    curl https://krew.sh/preflight | bash
   655→    
   656→    # Add Krew to PATH
   657→    export PATH="${KREW_ROOT:-$HOME/.krew}/bin:$PATH"
   658→    
   659→    # Verify installation
   660→    kubectl preflight --version
   661→    ```
   662→  </Step>
   663→</Steps>
   664→
   665→### Step 6: Run Preflight Validation
   666→
   667→Validate your cluster meets all CrewAI requirements before installation:
   668→
   669→```bash
   670→# Run preflight checks with your values file
   671→helm template oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   672→  --values my-values.yaml \
   673→  | kubectl preflight -
   674→
   675→# View detailed results
   676→echo "Preflight check complete. Review any warnings or errors above."
   677→```
   678→
   679→<Warning>
   680→**Address Preflight Issues**
   681→
   682→The preflight check validates:
   683→- ✓ Cluster resources (CPU, memory)
   684→- ✓ Storage classes
   685→- ✓ RBAC permissions
   686→- ✓ Kubernetes version compatibility
   687→
   688→Address any failures before proceeding to installation.
   689→</Warning>
   690→
   691→### Step 7: Install the CrewAI Platform
   692→
   693→Deploy the CrewAI Platform using the Helm command from the portal:
   694→
   695→<Steps>
   696→  <Step title="Execute the Helm Install Command">
   697→    Use the exact command provided by the enterprise portal (from Step 3):
   698→    
   699→    ```bash
   700→    # From portal step 5: Run the exact command provided
   701→    helm install crewai-platform \
   702→      oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   703→      --version <VERSION_FROM_PORTAL> \
   704→      --values my-values.yaml \
   705→      --namespace default \
   706→      --create-namespace
   707→    ```
   708→  </Step>
   709→  
   710→  <Step title="Monitor Installation Progress">
   711→    <CodeGroup>
   712→    ```bash Watch Pods
   713→    # Watch pod creation in real-time
   714→    kubectl get pods -w
   715→    ```
   716→
   717→    ```bash Check Status
   718→    # Or in another Cloud Shell tab:
   719→    kubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform
   720→    ```
   721→    </CodeGroup>
   722→
   723→    <Info>
   724→    **Installation Time**
   725→
   726→    Installation typically takes 5-15 minutes depending on image sizes and cluster resources.
   727→    </Info>
   728→  </Step>
   729→  
   730→  <Step title="Verify Installation Success">
   731→    ```bash
   732→    # Check all pods are running
   733→    kubectl get pods
   734→
   735→    # Check services
   736→    kubectl get svc
   737→
   738→    # View installation logs
   739→    kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=50
   740→
   741→    # Check for any startup errors
   742→    kubectl describe pod $(kubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -o jsonpath='{.items[0].metadata.name}')
   743→    ```
   744→  </Step>
   745→</Steps>
   746→
   747→### Step 8: Upgrade the Platform
   748→
   749→To upgrade to a newer version of CrewAI Platform:
   750→
   751→<Steps>
   752→  <Step title="Prepare for Upgrade">
   753→    <CodeGroup>
   754→    ```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
   755→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   756→    cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata
   757→
   758→    # Backup your current values file
   759→    cp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)
   760→
   761→    # Check available chart versions
   762→    helm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions
   763→    ```
   764→
   765→    ```powershell Windows
   766→    # Navigate to your metadata directory
   767→    cd ~\crewai-deployment\$PROJECT_ID\metadata
   768→
   769→    # Backup your current values file
   770→    Copy-Item my-values.yaml "my-values.yaml.backup.$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss')"
   771→
   772→    # Check available chart versions
   773→    helm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions
   774→    ```
   775→    </CodeGroup>
   776→  </Step>
   777→
   778→  <Step title="Review Changes">
   779→    ```bash
   780→    # Preview what will change
   781→    helm upgrade crewai-platform \
   782→      oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   783→      --values my-values.yaml \
   784→      --dry-run --debug
   785→    ```
   786→
   787→    <Tip>
   788→    Review the output carefully to understand what resources will be modified during the upgrade.
   789→    </Tip>
   790→  </Step>
   791→  
   792→  <Step title="Execute Upgrade">
   793→    ```bash
   794→    # Perform the upgrade
   795→    helm upgrade crewai-platform \
   796→      oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   797→      --values my-values.yaml
   798→    
   799→    # Monitor the rollout
   800→    kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web
   801→    
   802→    # Verify upgrade success
   803→    kubectl get pods
   804→    ```
   805→  </Step>
   806→</Steps>
   807→
   808→<Check>
   809→**Upgrade Complete**
   810→
   811→Your platform is now running the latest version!
   812→</Check>

   817→
   818→<CardGroup cols={2}>

   824→  </Card>

   830→    Services should have ClusterIP or LoadBalancer assigned.
   831→  </Card>

   833→  <Card title="View Logs" icon="file-lines">

   836→    ```
   837→    Look for successful startup messages.
   838→  </Card>
   839→

   848→
   849→## 🐛 Troubleshooting
   850→
   851→### Common Issues and Solutions
   852→
   853→<AccordionGroup>
   854→  <Accordion title="Pods Not Starting" icon="alert-circle">
   855→    ```bash
   856→    # Check pod status and events
   857→    kubectl describe pod <POD_NAME>
   858→    
   859→    # View pod logs for errors
   860→    kubectl logs <POD_NAME>
   861→    
   862→    # Check for image pull errors
   863→    kubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   864→    ```
   865→    
   866→    **Common Causes:**
   867→    - Registry authentication failed → Re-check credentials
   868→    - Insufficient cluster resources → Scale cluster
   869→    - Missing default storage class → Run Step 7 again
   870→  </Accordion>
   871→  
   872→  <Accordion title="Registry Authentication Failed" icon="lock">
   873→    ```bash
   874→    # Re-authenticate with the registry
   875→    helm registry login registry.crewai.com \
   876→      --username $CREWAI_EMAIL \
   877→      --password $CREWAI_TOKEN \
   878→      --insecure
   879→    
   880→    # Clear cache and retry
   881→    rm -rf ~/.config/helm/registry/config.json
   882→    helm registry login registry.crewai.com \
   883→      --username $CREWAI_EMAIL \
   884→      --password $CREWAI_TOKEN
   885→    ```
   886→  </Accordion>
   887→  
   888→  <Accordion title="Database Connection Errors">
   889→    ```bash
   890→    # Test Cloud SQL connectivity from pod
   891→    kubectl exec -it <POD_NAME> -- psql \
   892→      -h $DB_HOST \
   893→      -U $DB_USER \
   894→      -d $POSTGRES_DB
   895→
   896→    # Check firewall rules
   897→    gcloud compute firewall-rules list --filter="name~'gke'"
   898→
   899→    # For Cloud SQL, verify Private IP connectivity
   900→    gcloud sql instances describe crewai-db --format='get(ipAddresses)'
   901→    ```
   902→
   903→    **Common Issues:**
   904→    - Private IP not enabled on Cloud SQL instance
   905→    - VPC peering not configured correctly
   906→    - Firewall rules blocking GKE to Cloud SQL traffic
   907→  </Accordion>
   908→  
   909→  <Accordion title="Storage Issues">
   910→    ```bash
   911→    # Check persistent volumes
   912→    kubectl get pv
   913→    kubectl get pvc
   914→
   915→    # Describe problematic PVC
   916→    kubectl describe pvc <PVC_NAME>
   917→
   918→    # Check storage class
   919→    kubectl get storageclass standard
   920→    ```
   921→
   922→    <Tip>
   923→    Ensure your GKE cluster has the appropriate IAM permissions to create persistent disks in your project.
   924→    </Tip>
   925→  </Accordion>
   926→  
   927→  <Accordion title="Generate Support Bundle">
   928→    If you need help from CrewAI support, generate a diagnostic bundle:
   929→
   930→    ```bash
   931→    # Install support-bundle plugin (if not already done)
   932→    kubectl krew install support-bundle
   933→
   934→    # Generate the bundle
   935→    kubectl support-bundle --load-cluster-specs
   936→
   937→    # Upload the resulting .tar.gz file to CrewAI support
   938→    ```
   939→
   940→    <Warning>
   941→    **Sanitize Sensitive Data**: Before sharing the support bundle, review it for any sensitive information like passwords or API keys.
   942→    </Warning>
   943→  </Accordion>
   944→</AccordionGroup>
   945→
   946→## 📝 Installation Log and Backup
   947→
   948→Keep records of your installation:
   949→
   950→<CodeGroup>
   951→```bash Cloud Shell / Unix
   952→# Save installation timestamp
   953→echo "Installation Date: $(date)" >> metadata/installation.log
   954→
   955→# Backup your configuration
   956→cd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID
   957→tar -czf metadata/backups/crewai-config-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).tar.gz metadata/
   958→
   959→# Export cluster information
   960→kubectl cluster-info dump --output-directory=metadata/logs/cluster-dump-$(date +%Y%m%d)
   961→
   962→# Save Helm release information
   963→helm list --all-namespaces > metadata/logs/helm-releases.txt
   964→kubectl describe nodes > metadata/logs/nodes-info.txt
   965→```
   966→
   967→```powershell Windows
   968→# Save installation timestamp
   969→"Installation Date: $(Get-Date)" | Out-File -Append metadata\installation.log
   970→
   971→# Backup your configuration
   972→cd ~\crewai-deployment\$PROJECT_ID
   973→Compress-Archive -Path metadata\* -DestinationPath "metadata\backups\crewai-config-$(Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmmss').zip"
   974→
   975→# Save Helm release information
   976→helm list --all-namespaces | Out-File metadata\logs\helm-releases.txt
   977→kubectl describe nodes | Out-File metadata\logs\nodes-info.txt
   978→```
   979→</CodeGroup>
   980→
   981→<Note>
   982→Regularly backup your configuration files and keep a history of changes for rollback purposes.
   983→</Note>

     1→# Third-Party Integrations
     2→
     3→This section covers integration guidance for popular third-party services and platforms with CrewAI installations.
     4→
     5→## WorkOS
     6→
     7→WorkOS is the authentication provider for CrewAI installations, offering secure enterprise-grade SSO and user management capabilities integrated with Google Workspace authentication.
     8→
     9→### Overview
    10→- Enterprise authentication provider for CrewAI using **AuthKit**
    11→- AuthKit configured to use **Google Auth platform** on the GCP Project hosting CrewAI
    12→- Sign-in methods:
    13→  - Google Workspace OIDC
    14→  - Magic Auth (passwordless email authentication)
    15→- **WorkOS Connect** provides OIDC middleware for CLI login to CrewAI via WorkOS
    16→- User and organization management
    17→- Seamless integration with Kubernetes deployments
    18→- Supports both Web UI and CLI authentication flows
    19→
    20→### Prerequisites
    21→- Active WorkOS account and organization
    22→- API credentials from WorkOS dashboard
    23→- AuthKit domain setup (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
    24→
    25→### Configuration
    26→
    27→#### CrewAI Configuration Variables
    28→
    29→These environment variables configure how CrewAI connects to WorkOS: 
    30→
    31→```yaml
    32→# Authentication Provider Selection
    33→AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
    34→
    35→# OAuth Configuration
    36→oauth:
    37→  enabled: true  # Enables OAuth flow for user authentication
    38→OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET: "[REDACTED]"  # Secure cookie encryption key for session management
    39→```
    40→
    41→#### WorkOS Configuration Variables
    42→
    43→These environment variables configure WorkOS AuthKit integration:
    44→ See [Install Guide: Step 4 - Prepare Configuration File](/crewai/install#step-4-prepare-configuration-file-my-valuesyaml) for detailed setup instructions
    45→
    46→```yaml
    47→# Public Credentials (safe to commit)
    48→WORKOS_CLIENT_ID: "client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX"  # from WorkOS dashboard
    49→WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: "your-domain.authkit.app"    # AuthKit domain for Google Auth integration
    50→
    51→# Secrets (MUST be stored in Cloud Secret Manager / Kubernetes secrets - NEVER commit)
    52→WORKOS_API_KEY: "[REDACTED]"  # API key from WorkOS dashboard
    53→WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD: "[REDACTED]"  # 32-bit randomly generated string for session encryption
    54→```
    55→
    56→#### WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD Generation
    57→
    58→The `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` must be a secure, randomly generated 32-character string:
    59→
    60→```bash
    61→# Generate using OpenSSL
    62→openssl rand -hex 16
    63→
    64→# Or using Python
    65→python -c "import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(16))"
    66→
    67→# Or using Node.js
    68→node -e "console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(16).toString('hex'))"
    69→```
    70→
    71→Example output: `a3f7d8c2e4b9f1a6c8d3e7b2f9a4c1d5`
    72→
    73→### Setup Steps
    74→
    75→1. **Create WorkOS Account and Organization** - Register in WorkOS dashboard
    76→
    77→2. **Configure AuthKit with Google Auth**:
    78→   - Set up Google OAuth provider on your GCP Project
    79→   - Configure AuthKit to authenticate against Google Workspace
    80→   - Configure sign-in methods: Google Workspace OIDC and Magic Auth
    81→
    82→3. **Generate Credentials in WorkOS Dashboard**:
    83→   - Copy Client ID (public - safe to commit)
    84→   - Generate API Key (secret - store in Cloud Secret Manager)
    85→   - Note AuthKit Domain (`your-domain.authkit.app`)
    86→
    87→4. **Generate Session Encryption Key**:
    88→   - Generate a 32-character random string for `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD`
    89→   - Use command: `openssl rand -hex 16`
    90→
    91→5. **Configure WorkOS Connect for CLI Usage**:
    92→   - Enable WorkOS Connect for OIDC middleware
    93→   - Configure CLI authentication endpoint
    94→   - Enables passwordless CLI login via `crew ai login` command
    95→
    96→6. **Add Credentials to Kubernetes Values File**:
    97→   - Public values: `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID`, `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN`, `AUTH_PROVIDER`
    98→   - Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager (not in values file)
    99→
   100→
   101→### Current Setup
   102→
   103→Based on workspace configuration:
   104→- **Provider**: WorkOS with AuthKit
   105→- **Identity Provider**: Google Auth on GCP Project
   106→- **Sign-In Methods**: Google Workspace OIDC + Magic Auth
   107→- **CLI Access**: WorkOS Connect OIDC middleware enabled
   108→- **Authentication Domain**: `your-domain.authkit.app`
   109→- **Deployment**: Kubernetes with Helm (GCP)
   110→- **Secrets Management**: Google Cloud Secret Manager
   111→- **Cookie Security**: Password-protected session cookies (32-bit random key)
   112→- **Web UI**: OAuth-enabled authentication
   113→- **CLI**: OIDC-based login via `crew ai login`
   114→
   115→### Common Integrations
   116→- **Web UI**: Single Sign-On (SSO) with Google Workspace OIDC
   117→- **Web UI**: Passwordless authentication via Magic Auth
   118→- **CLI**: OIDC-based login for `crew ai` command-line interface
   119→- **CLI**: Secure token management via WorkOS Connect
   120→- User and organization provisioning
   121→- Directory synchronization with Google Workspace
   122→- Multi-tenant user management
   123→
   124→### Security Best Practices
   125→- Never commit `WORKOS_API_KEY` or `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` to version control
   126→- Store all secrets in Cloud Secret Manager (GCP) or equivalent
   127→- Rotate API keys regularly
   128→- Use environment-specific configurations (dev/prod)
   129→- Enable HTTPS for production deployments
   130→
   131→### Testing
   132→- Local testing with AuthKit domain
   133→- Test SSO flows in development environment
   134→- Validate user provisioning and organization sync
   135→- Integration testing with Kubernetes deployment
   136→
   137→### Troubleshooting
   138→- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches dashboard configuration
   139→- Check `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted
   140→- Ensure `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored and accessible
   141→- Review Kubernetes secret mounts and volume mappings
   142→- Check application logs for authentication errors
   143→
   144→### Related Documentation
   145→- See [GCP Setup Guide](/crewai/gcp-setup) for Cloud Secret Manager configuration
   146→- Refer to [Install Guide](/crewai/install) for complete Helm values setup
   147→- WorkOS official documentation: https://workos.com/docs
   148→

     1→---
     2→title: Frequently Asked Questions
     3→description: Common questions and answers about CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Frequently Asked Questions
     7→
     8→<Info>
     9→This FAQ covers common questions about installing, configuring, and operating CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud Platform.
    10→</Info>
    11→
    12→## General Questions
    13→
    14→<AccordionGroup>
    15→  <Accordion title="What is CrewAI Platform?" icon="question">
    16→    CrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.
    17→
    18→    **Key Features:**
    19→    - Multi-agent orchestration and coordination
    20→    - Role-based agent specialization
    21→    - Task delegation and workflow management
    22→    - Enterprise security and compliance
    23→    - GCP-native integration
    24→  </Accordion>
    25→
    26→  <Accordion title="What are the system requirements?" icon="server">
    27→    **Minimum Requirements for Development:**
    28→    - GKE cluster with 3 nodes (e2-standard-2 or higher)
    29→    - 8 vCPUs and 16 GB RAM total
    30→    - 100 GB persistent storage
    31→    - Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-f1-micro minimum)
    32→    - 2 Cloud Storage buckets
    33→
    34→    **Recommended Requirements for Production:**
    35→    - GKE cluster with 6+ nodes (e2-standard-4 or higher)
    36→    - 24+ vCPUs and 48+ GB RAM total
    37→    - 500 GB persistent storage
    38→    - Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-n1-standard-2 or higher)
    39→    - Multi-regional Cloud Storage buckets
    40→    - High availability configuration across 2+ regions
    41→  </Accordion>
    42→
    43→  <Accordion title="How much does it cost to run CrewAI on GCP?" icon="dollar-sign">
    44→    Costs vary based on environment and usage patterns:
    45→
    46→    **Development Environment (estimated):**
    47→    - GKE cluster: $150-200/month
    48→    - Cloud SQL: $25-50/month
    49→    - Cloud Storage: $5-15/month
    50→    - Networking: $10-20/month
    51→    - **Total: ~$190-285/month**
    52→
    53→    **Production Environment (estimated):**
    54→    - GKE cluster: $500-800/month
    55→    - Cloud SQL with HA: $200-400/month
    56→    - Cloud Storage with multi-region: $30-60/month
    57→    - Load balancing and networking: $50-100/month
    58→    - Cloud Armor and security: $20-40/month
    59→    - **Total: ~$800-1,400/month**
    60→
    61→    <Tip>
    62→    Use committed use discounts (CUDs) and sustained use discounts to reduce costs by 30-50%. See [Cost Optimization](/architecture/overview#cost-optimization) for strategies.
    63→    </Tip>
    64→  </Accordion>
    65→</AccordionGroup>
    66→
    67→## Installation & Setup
    68→
    69→<AccordionGroup>
    70→  <Accordion title="How long does installation take?" icon="clock">
    71→    **Typical Installation Timeline:**
    72→    - Infrastructure provisioning (Terraform): 15-30 minutes
    73→    - CrewAI Platform Helm deployment: 10-15 minutes
    74→    - Post-installation configuration: 5-10 minutes
    75→    - **Total: 30-55 minutes**
    76→
    77→    First-time installations may take longer due to:
    78→    - API enablement (5-10 minutes)
    79→    - Initial image pulls from registry
    80→    - DNS propagation for custom domains (up to 48 hours, typically 5-10 minutes)
    81→  </Accordion>
    82→
    83→  <Accordion title="Can I install CrewAI without using Terraform?" icon="code">
    84→    While Terraform is the recommended approach for reproducible infrastructure, you can manually provision resources through the GCP Console:
    85→
    86→    **Manual Setup Steps:**
    87→    1. Create VPC network and subnets
    88→    2. Provision GKE cluster
    89→    3. Create Cloud SQL instance
    90→    4. Create Cloud Storage buckets
    91→    5. Configure IAM roles and service accounts
    92→    6. Deploy CrewAI via Helm
    93→
    94→    <Warning>
    95→    Manual setup is **not recommended** for production environments due to:
    96→    - Lack of version control and auditability
    97→    - Higher risk of configuration drift
    98→    - Difficulty reproducing environments
    99→    - Complex rollback procedures
   100→    </Warning>
   101→  </Accordion>
   102→
   103→  <Accordion title="What authentication methods are supported?" icon="key">
   104→    CrewAI Platform supports multiple authentication providers:
   105→
   106→    **Supported Providers:**
   107→    - **WorkOS** (recommended for enterprise SSO)
   108→    - **Microsoft Entra ID** (formerly Azure AD)
   109→    - **Google Workspace**
   110→    - **Local authentication** (development only)
   111→
   112→    Configure the provider in your `my-values.yaml`:
   113→    ```yaml
   114→    envVars:
   115→      AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
   116→    ```
   117→
   118→    <Note>
   119→    Local authentication should only be used for development and testing. Production environments must use SSO-based authentication (WorkOS, Entra ID, or Google).
   120→    </Note>
   121→  </Accordion>
   122→
   123→  <Accordion title="Do I need a custom domain?" icon="globe">
   124→    **Development:** Not required - you can use port-forwarding or a temporary LoadBalancer IP
   125→
   126→    **Production:** **Strongly recommended** for:
   127→    - Professional appearance
   128→    - SSL/TLS certificate management
   129→    - OAuth callback URL configuration
   130→    - Stable endpoint for integrations
   131→
   132→    **Setup Requirements:**
   133→    1. Domain registered and managed (e.g., via Cloud DNS)
   134→    2. DNS A record pointing to Load Balancer IP
   135→    3. Managed SSL certificate provisioned
   136→    4. Ingress configured with domain name
   137→  </Accordion>
   138→</AccordionGroup>
   139→
   140→## Configuration & Customization
   141→
   142→<AccordionGroup>
   143→  <Accordion title="How do I scale my deployment?" icon="chart-line">
   144→    **Horizontal Scaling (Recommended):**
   145→    ```yaml
   146→    # In my-values.yaml
   147→    web:
   148→      replicaCount: 3  # Increase for more capacity
   149→
   150→    worker:
   151→      replicaCount: 5  # Scale workers independently
   152→    ```
   153→
   154→    **Auto-scaling with HPA:**
   155→    ```yaml
   156→    web:
   157→      autoscaling:
   158→        enabled: true
   159→        minReplicas: 3
   160→        maxReplicas: 10
   161→        targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   162→    ```
   163→
   164→    After updating values:
   165→    ```bash
   166→    helm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   167→      --values my-values.yaml
   168→    ```
   169→  </Accordion>
   170→
   171→  <Accordion title="Can I use an existing database?" icon="database">
   172→    **Yes!** CrewAI supports using an external PostgreSQL database:
   173→
   174→    ```yaml
   175→    # Disable bundled PostgreSQL
   176→    postgres:
   177→      enabled: false
   178→
   179→    # Configure external database
   180→    envVars:
   181→      DB_HOST: "your-db-instance.example.com"
   182→      DB_PORT: "5432"
   183→      DB_USER: "crewai_user"
   184→      DB_NAME: "crewai_production"
   185→
   186→    secrets:
   187→      DB_PASSWORD=[REDACTED]"
   188→    ```
   189→
   190→    **Requirements:**
   191→    - PostgreSQL 12 or higher
   192→    - Network connectivity from GKE to database
   193→    - Database created with UTF8 encoding
   194→  </Accordion>
   195→
   196→  <Accordion title="How do I configure custom storage?" icon="folder">
   197→    CrewAI uses S3-compatible storage. For GCP, configure Cloud Storage with HMAC keys:
   198→
   199→    **Step 1: Create HMAC Keys**
   200→    ```bash
   201→    gcloud storage hmac create SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL
   202→    ```
   203→
   204→    **Step 2: Configure Storage**
   205→    ```yaml
   206→    envVars:
   207→      STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"
   208→      AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   209→      AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   210→      AWS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-data"
   211→      LOGS_BUCKET: "your-project-crewai-logs"
   212→
   213→    secrets:
   214→      AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: "GOOG1E..."
   215→      AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: "your-secret-key"
   216→    ```
   217→  </Accordion>
   218→
   219→  <Accordion title="Can I customize resource limits?" icon="sliders">
   220→    **Yes!** Resource requests and limits can be customized per service:
   221→
   222→    ```yaml
   223→    web:
   224→      resources:
   225→        requests:
   226→          cpu: "500m"
   227→          memory: "1Gi"
   228→        limits:
   229→          cpu: "2000m"
   230→          memory: "4Gi"
   231→
   232→    worker:
   233→      resources:
   234→        requests:
   235→          cpu: "1000m"
   236→          memory: "2Gi"
   237→        limits:
   238→          cpu: "4000m"
   239→          memory: "8Gi"
   240→    ```
   241→
   242→    <Tip>
   243→    Set requests to typical usage and limits to peak capacity. Monitor actual usage with `kubectl top pods` and adjust accordingly.
   244→    </Tip>
   245→  </Accordion>
   246→</AccordionGroup>
   247→
   248→## Operations & Troubleshooting
   249→
   250→<AccordionGroup>
   251→  <Accordion title="How do I upgrade to a new version?" icon="arrow-up">
   252→    **Step 1: Backup Configuration**
   253→    ```bash
   254→    cp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d)
   255→    ```
   256→
   257→    **Step 2: Check Available Versions**
   258→    ```bash
   259→    helm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions
   260→    ```
   261→
   262→    **Step 3: Perform Upgrade**
   263→    ```bash
   264→    helm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \
   265→      --version NEW_VERSION \
   266→      --values my-values.yaml
   267→    ```
   268→
   269→    **Step 4: Monitor Rollout**
   270→    ```bash
   271→    kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web
   272→    kubectl get pods -w
   273→    ```
   274→
   275→    See [Installation Guide - Step 8](/crewai/install#step-8-upgrade-the-platform) for detailed upgrade instructions.
   276→  </Accordion>
   277→
   278→  <Accordion title="How do I back up my data?" icon="floppy-disk">
   279→    **Database Backups:**
   280→    - Cloud SQL automated daily backups (enabled by default)
   281→    - On-demand backup: `gcloud sql backups create --instance=INSTANCE_NAME`
   282→    - Point-in-time recovery (PITR) available within 7 days
   283→
   284→    **Configuration Backups:**
   285→    ```bash
   286→    # Backup Helm values
   287→    kubectl get secret -n default -o yaml > secrets-backup.yaml
   288→
   289→    # Backup entire namespace
   290→    kubectl get all -n default -o yaml > namespace-backup.yaml
   291→    ```
   292→
   293→    **Storage Backups:**
   294→    - Cloud Storage versioning enabled
   295→    - Cross-region replication configured
   296→    - Lifecycle policies for retention
   297→  </Accordion>
   298→
   299→  <Accordion title="What monitoring is available?" icon="chart-mixed">
   300→    **Built-in Monitoring:**
   301→    - Cloud Monitoring dashboards (GKE and Cloud SQL)
   302→    - Cloud Logging for centralized logs
   303→    - Cloud Trace for distributed tracing
   304→    - Uptime checks for availability
   305→
   306→    **Access Metrics:**
   307→    ```bash
   308→    # View pod metrics
   309→    kubectl top pods
   310→
   311→    # View logs
   312→    kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=100
   313→
   314→    # Check cluster status
   315→    kubectl get nodes
   316→    kubectl get pods
   317→    ```
   318→
   319→    **Custom Dashboards:**
   320→    Navigate to Cloud Console → Monitoring → Dashboards to create custom views.
   321→  </Accordion>
   322→
   323→  <Accordion title="How do I troubleshoot pod failures?" icon="triangle-exclamation">
   324→    **Step 1: Check Pod Status**
   325→    ```bash
   326→    kubectl get pods
   327→    kubectl describe pod POD_NAME
   328→    ```
   329→
   330→    **Step 2: View Logs**
   331→    ```bash
   332→    kubectl logs POD_NAME
   333→    kubectl logs POD_NAME --previous  # For crashed pods
   334→    ```
   335→
   336→    **Step 3: Check Events**
   337→    ```bash
   338→    kubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   339→    ```
   340→
   341→    **Common Issues:**
   342→    - **ImagePullBackOff**: Registry authentication failed
   343→    - **CrashLoopBackOff**: Application error - check logs
   344→    - **Pending**: Insufficient cluster resources
   345→    - **OOMKilled**: Out of memory - increase limits
   346→
   347→    See [Troubleshooting Guide](/crewai/troubleshooting) for detailed solutions.
   348→  </Accordion>
   349→</AccordionGroup>
   350→
   351→## Security & Compliance
   352→
   353→<AccordionGroup>
   354→  <Accordion title="Is CrewAI Platform secure?" icon="shield">
   355→    **Security Features:**
   356→    - ✅ End-to-end encryption (TLS 1.2+)
   357→    - ✅ Encryption at rest (Cloud SQL, Cloud Storage)
   358→    - ✅ IAM-based access control
   359→    - ✅ Network isolation via VPC
   360→    - ✅ Cloud Armor DDoS protection
   361→    - ✅ Identity-Aware Proxy (IAP) support
   362→    - ✅ Regular security updates
   363→    - ✅ Audit logging enabled
   364→
   365→    **Best Practices:**
   366→    - Use Workload Identity for service account authentication
   367→    - Store secrets in Cloud Secret Manager
   368→    - Enable binary authorization for container images
   369→    - Implement least-privilege IAM roles
   370→    - Regular vulnerability scanning
   371→  </Accordion>
   372→
   373→  <Accordion title="How do I manage secrets securely?" icon="lock">
   374→    **Recommended Approach: Cloud Secret Manager**
   375→
   376→    **Step 1: Store Secret**
   377→    ```bash
   378→    echo -n "your-secret-value" | gcloud secrets create SECRET_NAME --data-file=-
   379→    ```
   380→
   381→    **Step 2: Grant Access**
   382→    ```bash
   383→    gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding SECRET_NAME \
   384→      --member="serviceAccount:SA_EMAIL" \
   385→      --role="roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor"
   386→    ```
   387→
   388→    **Step 3: Reference in Helm Values**
   389→    ```yaml
   390→    # Use External Secrets Operator
   391→    secrets:
   392→      DB_PASSWORD:
   393→        secretRef:
   394→          name: db-password
   395→          key: password
   396→    ```
   397→
   398→    <Warning>
   399→    **Never commit secrets to version control!** Always use Secret Manager or external secret management solutions.
   400→    </Warning>
   401→  </Accordion>
   402→
   403→  <Accordion title="Is CrewAI compliant with regulations?" icon="scale-balanced">
   404→    CrewAI Platform can be configured to meet various compliance requirements:
   405→
   406→    **Supported Compliance Frameworks:**
   407→    - **SOC 2 Type II**: With proper GCP organization policies
   408→    - **GDPR**: Data residency and privacy controls available
   409→    - **HIPAA**: Available with Business Associate Agreement (BAA)
   410→    - **ISO 27001**: GCP infrastructure is certified
   411→
   412→    **Compliance Configuration:**
   413→    - Data residency: Deploy in specific GCP regions
   414→    - Audit logging: Enabled by default
   415→    - Data encryption: At rest and in transit
   416→    - Access controls: IAM and RBAC
   417→
   418→    <Note>
   419→    Compliance is a shared responsibility. While GCP and CrewAI provide compliant infrastructure, your implementation and operational practices must also meet regulatory requirements.
   420→    </Note>
   421→  </Accordion>
   422→</AccordionGroup>
   423→
   424→## Integration & Development
   425→
   426→<AccordionGroup>
   427→  <Accordion title="How do I integrate with external services?" icon="plug">
   428→    CrewAI supports integrations through:
   429→
   430→    **1. OAuth Configuration**
   431→    ```yaml
   432→    oauth:
   433→      enabled: true
   434→
   435→    secrets:
   436→      OAUTH_DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"
   437→    ```
   438→
   439→    **2. API Access**
   440→    - RESTful API for programmatic access
   441→    - Webhook support for event-driven workflows
   442→    - SDK support (Python, Node.js)
   443→
   444→    **3. Third-Party Integrations**
   445→    - Vertex AI for ML models
   446→    - 11Labs for voice synthesis
   447→    - Custom integrations via API
   448→
   449→    See [Third-Party Integrations](/crewai/third-party) for detailed guides.
   450→  </Accordion>
   451→
   452→  <Accordion title="Can I use custom AI models?" icon="brain">
   453→    **Yes!** CrewAI supports custom model integration:
   454→
   455→    **Vertex AI Models:**
   456→    - Deploy custom models to Vertex AI
   457→    - Configure endpoint URLs in CrewAI
   458→    - Use Workload Identity for authentication
   459→
   460→    **External Models:**
   461→    - OpenAI API
   462→    - Anthropic Claude API
   463→    - Custom inference endpoints
   464→
   465→    **Configuration:**
   466→    ```yaml
   467→    envVars:
   468→      MODEL_PROVIDER: "vertex"
   469→      MODEL_ENDPOINT: "https://REGION-aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1/projects/PROJECT/locations/REGION/endpoints/ENDPOINT"
   470→    ```
   471→  </Accordion>
   472→
   473→  <Accordion title="How do I access the API?" icon="code">
   474→    **API Access Methods:**
   475→
   476→    **1. Port Forwarding (Development)**
   477→    ```bash
   478→    kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-platform-web 8080:80
   479→    # Access at http://localhost:8080
   480→    ```
   481→
   482→    **2. LoadBalancer (Production)**
   483→    ```bash
   484→    kubectl get svc crewai-platform-web
   485→    # Access at LoadBalancer IP or configured domain
   486→    ```
   487→
   488→    **3. API Authentication**
   489→    - Obtain API key from CrewAI admin console
   490→    - Include in request headers: `Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY`
   491→
   492→    **Example API Call:**
   493→    ```bash
   494→    curl -X GET "https://crewai.example.com/api/v1/agents" \
   495→      -H "Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY"
   496→    ```
   497→  </Accordion>
   498→</AccordionGroup>
