     1→---
     2→title: Troubleshooting Guide
     3→description: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation
     4→---
     5→
     6→# Troubleshooting Guide
     7→
     8→This guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation.
     9→
    10→<Tip>
    11→Use the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands.
    12→</Tip>
    13→
    14→## Installation & Deployment Issues
    15→
    16→### Helm Installation Failures
    17→
    18→<AccordionGroup>
    19→  <Accordion title="Issue: Helm chart installation fails with 'values validation error'">
    20→    **Cause**: Missing or incorrectly formatted required values in `my-values.yaml`
    21→
    22→    **Solution**:
    23→    - Verify all required variables are present in your values file
    24→    - Check YAML syntax for formatting errors (indentation, quotes)
    25→    - Use `helm lint` to validate the chart before installation
    26→    - Review [Prepare Configuration File guide](/crewai/install#step-4-prepare-configuration-file-my-valuesyaml)
    27→  </Accordion>
    28→
    29→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' errors after deployment">
    30→    **Cause**: Container registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist
    31→
    32→    **Solution**:
    33→    - Verify `image.registries[].password` is correctly base64-encoded
    34→    - Confirm the service account has access to the container registry
    35→    - Check that the image tag exists in the specified registry
    36→    - Review Kubernetes events: `kubectl describe pod <pod-name>`
    37→  </Accordion>
    38→</AccordionGroup>
    39→
    40→### Database Connection Issues
    41→
    42→<AccordionGroup>
    43→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors">
    44→    **Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable
    45→
    46→    **Solution**:
    47→    - Verify `DB_PASSWORD` is correct and matches the database user
    48→    - Confirm database host is reachable from the Kubernetes cluster
    49→    - Check network policies allow traffic to the database
    50→    - Test connection manually:
    51→      ```bash
    52→      kubectl exec -it <pod> -- psql -h <host> -U postgres
    53→      ```
    54→  </Accordion>
    55→
    56→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Database migration fails' on first deployment">
    57→    **Cause**: Database schema initialization incomplete
    58→
    59→    **Solution**:
    60→    - Ensure database is fully initialized before deploying application
    61→    - Check database logs for migration errors
    62→    - Verify user has proper privileges for schema creation
    63→    - Run migrations manually if needed:
    64→      ```bash
    65→      kubectl exec -it <pod> -- rake db:migrate
    66→      ```
    67→  </Accordion>
    68→</AccordionGroup>
    69→
    70→## Authentication & Authorization Issues
    71→
    72→### WorkOS Authentication
    73→
    74→<AccordionGroup>
    75→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page">
    76→    **Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured
    77→
    78→    **Solution**:
    79→    - Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value
    80→    - Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
    81→    - Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets
    82→    - Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled
    83→    - Check browser console for authentication errors
    84→    - Review application logs:
    85→      ```bash
    86→      kubectl logs -f <pod-name>
    87→      ```
    88→  </Accordion>
    89→
    90→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error">
    91→    **Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch
    92→
    93→    <Warning>
    94→    **IMPORTANT**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes
    95→    </Warning>
    96→
    97→    **Solution**:
    98→    - Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)
    99→    - ❌ **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)
   100→    - Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long
   101→    - Ensure the same password is used across all replicas
   102→    - Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager
   103→    - Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:
   104→      ```bash
   105→      kubectl rollout restart deployment/<name>
   106→      ```
   107→  </Accordion>
   108→
   109→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option">
   110→    **Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method
   111→
   112→    **Solution**:
   113→    - Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings
   114→    - Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode
   115→    - Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured
   116→  </Accordion>
   117→
   118→  <Accordion title="Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails">
   119→    **Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project
   120→
   121→    **Solution**:
   122→    - Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured
   123→    - Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain
   124→    - Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs
   125→    - Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials
   126→  </Accordion>
   127→</AccordionGroup>
   128→
   129→### CLI Authentication Issues
   130→
   131→<AccordionGroup>
   132→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'crew ai login' command fails">
   133→    **Cause**: WorkOS Connect not properly configured or OIDC middleware disabled
   134→
   135→    **Solution**:
   136→    - Verify `oauth.enabled: true` in Helm values
   137→    - Confirm WorkOS Connect is enabled in WorkOS dashboard
   138→    - Check that CLI can reach the authentication endpoint
   139→    - Review application logs for OIDC errors
   140→    - Try manual token generation as workaround
   141→  </Accordion>
   142→
   143→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Invalid OIDC token' errors in CLI">
   144→    **Cause**: Token expired or WorkOS Connect endpoint misconfigured
   145→
   146→    **Solution**:
   147→    - Re-authenticate: `crew ai login` again
   148→    - Verify token expiration time is reasonable
   149→    - Check clock skew between CLI environment and server
   150→    - Confirm WorkOS Connect is properly integrated
   151→  </Accordion>
   152→</AccordionGroup>
   153→
   154→### OAuth Configuration Issues
   155→
   156→<AccordionGroup>
   157→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'couldn't find key OAUTH_DB_HOST in Secret' error when enabling built-in integrations">
   158→    **Cause**: OAuth enabled after initial deployment without providing `OAUTH_DB_HOST` secret
   159→
   160→    <Warning>
   161→    When enabling `oauth: true` in Helm values, you must also provide `OAUTH_DB_HOST`
   162→    </Warning>
   163→
   164→    **Solution**:
   165→
   166→    Add to your Kubernetes secret:
   167→    ```bash
   168→    kubectl patch secret crewai-secrets -p \
   169→      '{"data": {"OAUTH_DB_HOST": "'$(echo -n '10.0.0.2' | base64)'"}}'
   170→    ```
   171→
   172→    Replace `10.0.0.2` with your actual OAuth database host
   173→
   174→    For initial deployments, configure OAuth from the start to auto-create all required secrets
   175→
   176→    Reference: [PostgreSQL OAuth Database Configuration](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/reference/chart-values/postgres#param-postgres-oauth-database)
   177→
   178→    Restart deployment after updating secret:
   179→    ```bash
   180→    kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-web
   181→    ```
   182→  </Accordion>
   183→</AccordionGroup>
   184→
   185→## Network & Connectivity Issues
   186→
   187→### Ingress & Load Balancer
   188→
   189→<AccordionGroup>
   190→  <Accordion title="Issue: External IP remains 'pending'">
   191→    **Cause**: Static IP resource not provisioned or ingress misconfigured
   192→
   193→    **Solution**:
   194→    - Verify static IP exists in GCP:
   195→      ```bash
   196→      gcloud compute addresses list
   197→      ```
   198→    - Check ingress annotations reference correct IP name
   199→    - Confirm load balancer backend services are healthy
   200→    - Review GCP Console > Network Services > Load Balancing
   201→  </Accordion>
   202→
   203→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Connection refused' when accessing application">
   204→    **Cause**: Service not accessible or firewall blocking traffic
   205→
   206→    **Solution**:
   207→    - Verify pod is running: `kubectl get pods`
   208→    - Check service is created: `kubectl get svc`
   209→    - Test internal connectivity:
   210→      ```bash
   211→      kubectl port-forward svc/<service> 8080:80
   212→      ```
   213→    - Review firewall rules in GCP Console > VPC Network > Firewall rules
   214→  </Accordion>
   215→</AccordionGroup>
   216→
   217→### DNS Resolution
   218→
   219→<AccordionGroup>
   220→  <Accordion title="Issue: Domain name not resolving">
   221→    **Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay
   222→
   223→    **Solution**:
   224→    - Verify DNS A record points to load balancer IP
   225→    - Wait for DNS propagation (up to 48 hours)
   226→    - Test DNS:
   227→      ```bash
   228→      nslookup your-domain.com
   229→      # or
   230→      dig your-domain.com
   231→      ```
   232→    - Check GCP Cloud DNS records if using Cloud DNS
   233→  </Accordion>
   234→
   235→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Invalid certificate' or SSL errors">
   236→    **Cause**: SSL certificate not provisioned or misconfigured
   237→
   238→    **Solution**:
   239→    - Verify managed certificate is created:
   240→      ```bash
   241→      kubectl get managedcertificate
   242→      ```
   243→    - Check certificate is in "Active" state
   244→    - Confirm domain in FrontendConfig matches certificate domains
   245→    - Review GCP Console > Network Services > Cloud Armor & Security Policies
   246→  </Accordion>
   247→</AccordionGroup>
   248→
   249→## Storage & Persistence Issues
   250→
   251→### Cloud Storage / S3 Access
   252→
   253→<AccordionGroup>
   254→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket">
   255→    **Cause**: AWS credentials invalid or lack permissions
   256→
   257→    **Solution**:
   258→    - Verify `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` are correct HMAC keys
   259→    - Confirm service account has `storage.objects.create` permission
   260→    - Check bucket exists and is accessible
   261→    - Test access:
   262→      ```bash
   263→      kubectl exec -it <pod> -- aws s3 ls s3://your-bucket/
   264→      ```
   265→  </Accordion>
   266→
   267→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'No such bucket' errors">
   268→    **Cause**: Bucket name wrong or not accessible from cluster
   269→
   270→    **Solution**:
   271→    - Verify `AWS_BUCKET` and `LOGS_BUCKET` match actual bucket names
   272→    - Confirm buckets exist in GCP Cloud Storage
   273→    - Check bucket region matches `AWS_REGION`
   274→    - Verify endpoint is correct: `https://storage.googleapis.com`
   275→  </Accordion>
   276→</AccordionGroup>
   277→
   278→### Volume Mounting
   279→
   280→<AccordionGroup>
   281→  <Accordion title="Issue: Persistent volume not mounting">
   282→    **Cause**: Volume not created or mount configuration incorrect
   283→
   284→    **Solution**:
   285→    - Check volume exists: `kubectl get pv`
   286→    - Verify PVC is bound: `kubectl get pvc`
   287→    - Check pod logs for mount errors
   288→    - Describe pod to see mount status:
   289→      ```bash
   290→      kubectl describe pod <pod-name>
   291→      ```
   292→  </Accordion>
   293→</AccordionGroup>
   294→
   295→## Container Registry Issues
   296→
   297→### GCP Service Account Authentication
   298→
   299→<AccordionGroup>
   300→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' or 'unauthorized: authentication required' when pulling images">
   301→    **Cause**: Container registry credentials missing, incorrect, or not properly base64-encoded
   302→
   303→    **Solution**:
   304→
   305→    **Step 1: Create GCP Service Account Key** (if not already created):
   306→    ```bash
   307→    gcloud iam service-accounts keys create sa-key.json \
   308→      --iam-account=<service-account-email>
   309→    ```
   310→
   311→    **Step 2: Base64 Encode the Key** (critical for Docker registry auth):
   312→    ```bash
   313→    cat sa-key.json | base64 -w 0 > sa-key.base64
   314→    # Or without line wrapping:
   315→    base64 sa-key.json > sa-key.base64
   316→    ```
   317→
   318→    **Step 3: Create Kubernetes Secret** with encoded credentials:
   319→    ```bash
   320→    kubectl create secret docker-registry gcp-registry-secret \
   321→      --docker-server="europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev" \
   322→      --docker-username="_json_key_base64" \
   323→      --docker-password="$(cat sa-key.base64)"
   324→    ```
   325→
   326→    **Step 4: Reference Secret in Pod Spec** (Helm values):
   327→    ```yaml
   328→    image:
   329→      registries:
   330→        - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
   331→          username: "_json_key_base64"
   332→          password: "[REDACTED]"  # Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON
   333→    ```
   334→
   335→    <Warning>
   336→    **CRITICAL SECURITY CONSIDERATIONS**:
   337→    - Never commit `sa-key.json` to version control - it provides full GCP access
   338→    - Never store raw JSON in Helm values files - use Cloud Secret Manager or Kubernetes secrets
   339→    - Rotate keys regularly - GCP recommends quarterly rotation minimum
   340→    - Delete old keys after rotation to prevent compromise
   341→    - Use service account impersonation when possible instead of key-based authentication
   342→    - Restrict key permissions - grant only necessary roles (e.g., `roles/storage.objectViewer` for artifact registry)
   343→    </Warning>
   344→
   345→    <Info>
   346→    **Secure Key Management Workflow**:
   347→
   348→    ```bash
   349→    # 1. Create key (temporary file only)
   350→    gcloud iam service-accounts keys create /tmp/sa-key.json \
   351→      --iam-account=<service-account-email>
   352→
   353→    # 2. Base64 encode and store in Cloud Secret Manager
   354→    gcloud secrets create gcp-registry-key \
   355→      --data-file=/tmp/sa-key.json
   356→
   357→    # 3. Immediately delete local copy
   358→    shred -vfz -n 10 /tmp/sa-key.json
   359→
   360→    # 4. Reference secret in Kubernetes (do NOT use raw JSON)
   361→    # Use external-secrets-operator or sealed-secrets to sync from Cloud Secret Manager
   362→    ```
   363→    </Info>
   364→  </Accordion>
   365→
   366→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'access denied' when pulling from private registry">
   367→    **Cause**: Service account lacks necessary permissions
   368→
   369→    **Solution**:
   370→    - Verify service account has `roles/artifactregistry.reader` role
   371→    - Check:
   372→      ```bash
   373→      gcloud projects get-iam-policy <project-id>
   374→      ```
   375→    - Grant if missing:
   376→      ```bash
   377→      gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding <project-id> \
   378→        --member=serviceAccount:<sa-email> \
   379→        --role=roles/artifactregistry.reader
   380→      ```
   381→  </Accordion>
   382→</AccordionGroup>
   383→
   384→## Performance & Resource Issues
   385→
   386→### CPU & Memory
   387→
   388→<AccordionGroup>
   389→  <Accordion title="Issue: Pods getting OOMKilled (out of memory)">
   390→    **Cause**: Application memory usage exceeds resource limits
   391→
   392→    **Solution**:
   393→    - Increase memory limits in Helm values
   394→    - Profile application memory usage
   395→    - Check for memory leaks in logs
   396→    - Increase replica count to distribute load
   397→  </Accordion>
   398→
   399→  <Accordion title="Issue: High CPU usage or slow response times">
   400→    **Cause**: Insufficient CPU allocation or application bottleneck
   401→
   402→    **Solution**:
   403→    - Increase CPU limits in Helm values
   404→    - Enable horizontal pod autoscaling
   405→    - Profile application CPU usage
   406→    - Check for database query performance issues
   407→  </Accordion>
   408→</AccordionGroup>
   409→
   410→### Scaling Issues
   411→
   412→<AccordionGroup>
   413→  <Accordion title="Issue: Auto-scaling not working">
   414→    **Cause**: HPA (Horizontal Pod Autoscaler) not configured or metrics unavailable
   415→
   416→    **Solution**:
   417→    - Verify HPA is created: `kubectl get hpa`
   418→    - Check metrics are available:
   419→      ```bash
   420→      kubectl top nodes
   421→      kubectl top pods
   422→      ```
   423→    - Ensure metrics-server is installed:
   424→      ```bash
   425→      kubectl get deployment metrics-server -n kube-system
   426→      ```
   427→    - Review HPA status:
   428→      ```bash
   429→      kubectl describe hpa <hpa-name>
   430→      ```
   431→  </Accordion>
   432→</AccordionGroup>
   433→
   434→## Logging & Monitoring
   435→
   436→### Log Collection Issues
   437→
   438→<AccordionGroup>
   439→  <Accordion title="Issue: Application logs not appearing in Cloud Logging">
   440→    **Cause**: Log sink not configured or permissions missing
   441→
   442→    **Solution**:
   443→    - Verify Stackdriver/Cloud Logging is enabled
   444→    - Check log sink filters are correct
   445→    - Confirm service account has `logging.logEntries.create` permission
   446→    - Test logging locally:
   447→      ```bash
   448→      kubectl logs -f <pod-name>
   449→      ```
   450→  </Accordion>
   451→</AccordionGroup>
   452→
   453→### Metrics & Monitoring
   454→
   455→<AccordionGroup>
   456→  <Accordion title="Issue: No metrics visible in monitoring dashboard">
   457→    **Cause**: Metrics not being collected or exported
   458→
   459→    **Solution**:
   460→    - Verify monitoring agent is running
   461→    - Check metrics endpoints are accessible
   462→    - Confirm Prometheus or equivalent scraper is configured
   463→    - Review monitoring configuration in values file
   464→  </Accordion>
   465→</AccordionGroup>
   466→
   467→## Kubernetes Issues
   468→
   469→### Pod Issues
   470→
   471→<AccordionGroup>
   472→  <Accordion title="Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state">
   473→    **Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues
   474→
   475→    **Solution**:
   476→    - Check node availability: `kubectl get nodes`
   477→    - Describe pod to see pending reason:
   478→      ```bash
   479→      kubectl describe pod <pod-name>
   480→      ```
   481→    - Check resource requests vs available resources
   482→    - Scale up node pool if needed
   483→  </Accordion>
   484→
   485→  <Accordion title="Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'">
   486→    **Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error
   487→
   488→    **Solution**:
   489→    - Check pod logs:
   490→      ```bash
   491→      kubectl logs <pod-name>
   492→      ```
   493→    - Check previous logs if container restarted:
   494→      ```bash
   495→      kubectl logs <pod-name> --previous
   496→      ```
   497→    - Verify all environment variables are set correctly
   498→    - Test application locally with same configuration
   499→  </Accordion>
   500→</AccordionGroup>
   501→
   502→### Secret Management
   503→
   504→<AccordionGroup>
   505→  <Accordion title="Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets">
   506→    **Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly
   507→
   508→    **Solution**:
   509→    - Verify secret exists: `kubectl get secrets`
   510→    - Check secret data:
   511→      ```bash
   512→      kubectl describe secret <secret-name>
   513→      ```
   514→    - Confirm secret is referenced in pod spec
   515→    - Verify pod has restarted after secret creation
   516→    - Check YAML syntax for secret mounting
   517→  </Accordion>
   518→
   519→  <Accordion title="Issue: 'Secret key not found' errors">
   520→    **Cause**: Secret key name doesn't match environment variable reference
   521→
   522→    **Solution**:
   523→    - List secret keys:
   524→      ```bash
   525→      kubectl describe secret <secret-name>
   526→      ```
   527→    - Verify key names match pod environment variable references
   528→    - Check for typos in key names
   529→    - Recreate secret if keys are incorrect
   530→  </Accordion>
   531→</AccordionGroup>
   532→
   533→## Image Building Issues
   534→
   535→### BuildKit/Image Build Failures
   536→
   537→<AccordionGroup>
   538→  <Accordion title="Issue: Buildkit pod crashes or enters CrashLoopBackOff">
   539→    **Cause**: Container image building failures, resource constraints, or configuration issues
   540→
   541→    **Solution**:
   542→
   543→    Check buildkit pod logs:
   544→    ```bash
   545→    kubectl logs deploy/crewai-buildkit
   546→    kubectl logs deploy/crewai-buildkit --previous
   547→    ```
   548→
   549→    Check pod resource requests vs available resources:
   550→    ```bash
   551→    kubectl describe pod <buildkit-pod-name>
   552→    ```
   553→
   554→    Other checks:
   555→    - Verify registry credentials are accessible to buildkit pod
   556→    - Check for disk space issues:
   557→      ```bash
   558→      kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-buildkit -- df -h
   559→      ```
   560→  </Accordion>
   561→
   562→  <Accordion title="Issue: Image fails to build or push to registry">
   563→    **Cause**: Build failure, registry authentication, or network connectivity
   564→
   565→    **Solution**:
   566→
   567→    Execute into buildkit pod for debugging:
   568→    ```bash
   569→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-buildkit -- /bin/bash
   570→    ```
   571→
   572→    Verify registry credentials from within pod
   573→
   574→    Check network connectivity to registry:
   575→    ```bash
   576→    kubectl exec deploy/crewai-buildkit -- curl -I https://europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev
   577→    ```
   578→
   579→    Other steps:
   580→    - Review buildkit logs for specific error messages
   581→    - Restart buildkit deployment if hung:
   582→      ```bash
   583→      kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-buildkit
   584→      ```
   585→  </Accordion>
   586→
   587→  <Accordion title="Issue: Feature flags not recognized or disabled">
   588→    **Cause**: Feature flag configuration not loaded or overridden
   589→
   590→    **Solution**:
   591→
   592→    Check feature flag status from Rails runner:
   593→    ```bash
   594→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   595→      bundle exec rails runner 'Flipper.enabled?(:flag_name)'
   596→    ```
   597→
   598→    Verify flag is enabled in Flipper configuration
   599→
   600→    Check Rails cache is cleared after flag changes:
   601→    ```bash
   602→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   603→      bundle exec rails runner 'Rails.cache.clear'
   604→    ```
   605→
   606→    Restart web deployment to pick up flag changes:
   607→    ```bash
   608→    kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-web
   609→    ```
   610→  </Accordion>
   611→</AccordionGroup>
   612→
   613→## Application Features Issues
   614→
   615→### Crew Studio LLM Compatibility
   616→
   617→<AccordionGroup>
   618→  <Accordion title="Issue: Cannot select Gemini or other specific LLM models in Crew Studio">
   619→    **Cause**: LLM model not enabled for Crew Studio or incompatible model version
   620→
   621→    <Info>
   622→    Use models equivalent to **GPT-4 or higher** for best performance with Crew Studio.
   623→    </Info>
   624→
   625→    **Solution**:
   626→    - Verify the LLM is enabled in your LLM Connection configuration
   627→    - Compatible LLMs must be:
   628→      - Enabled in your LLM Connection
   629→      - Compatible with Crew Studio (check CrewAI documentation for supported models)
   630→      - Configured with sufficient context windows and capabilities
   631→
   632→    **Supported models typically include**:
   633→    - OpenAI: GPT-4, GPT-4 Turbo, GPT-4o
   634→    - Other enterprise-grade LLMs equivalent to GPT-4
   635→
   636→    <Warning>
   637→    Some models like Gemini may require additional configuration or may not be fully supported in the current version. Check CrewAI release notes or support documentation for LLM compatibility list.
   638→    </Warning>
   639→  </Accordion>
   640→</AccordionGroup>
   641→
   642→## User and Data Management
   643→
   644→### Removing Users for WorkOS Integration Testing
   645→
   646→When transitioning to WorkOS authentication, you may need to remove existing test users to ensure proper WorkOS sign-in flow and user provisioning.
   647→
   648→<AccordionGroup>
   649→  <Accordion title="Issue: Need to remove user account to test WorkOS sign-in">
   650→    **Cause**: Testing WorkOS authentication or cleaning up development/test users
   651→
   652→    **Solution**:
   653→
   654→    **Step 1: List all users**:
   655→    ```bash
   656→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   657→      bundle exec rails runner \
   658→      'User.select(:id, :email).each { |u| puts "ID: #{u.id} | Email: #{u.email}" }'
   659→    ```
   660→
   661→    **Step 2: Delete single user by email**:
   662→    ```bash
   663→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   664→      bundle exec rails runner \
   665→      "User.find_by_email('user@example.com')&.destroy!"
   666→    ```
   667→
   668→    **Step 3: If deletion fails with foreign key constraint error**:
   669→
   670→    <Warning>
   671→    Error: `PG::ForeignKeyViolation: ERROR: update or delete on table "users" violates foreign key constraint`
   672→
   673→    Cause: User has associated chat_sessions that must be deleted first
   674→    </Warning>
   675→
   676→    Use the deep clean method to remove user and all related data:
   677→    ```bash
   678→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   679→      bundle exec rails runner '
   680→      user = User.find_by(email: "user@example.com")
   681→      if user
   682→        session_ids = ChatSession.where(user_id: user.id).pluck(:id)
   683→        ChatMessage.where(chat_session_id: session_ids).delete_all
   684→        ChatSession.where(id: session_ids).delete_all
   685→        user.destroy!
   686→        puts "Deep clean complete: User, Sessions, and Messages deleted."
   687→      else
   688→        puts "User not found"
   689→      end
   690→      '
   691→    ```
   692→  </Accordion>
   693→
   694→  <Accordion title="Issue: Add user as owner after WorkOS setup">
   695→    **Cause**: Provisioning new admin accounts or assigning ownership
   696→
   697→    **Solution**:
   698→
   699→    **Create owner in primary organization** (org_id=1):
   700→    ```bash
   701→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   702→      bundle exec rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults \
   703→      'factory:add_owner[1,admin@example.com]'
   704→    ```
   705→
   706→    **Create owner in secondary organization** (org_id=2):
   707→    ```bash
   708→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   709→      bundle exec rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults \
   710→      'factory:add_owner[2,admin@example.com]'
   711→    ```
   712→  </Accordion>
   713→
   714→  <Accordion title="Issue: Clean up test users in bulk">
   715→    **Cause**: Resetting development environment after testing
   716→
   717→    **Solution**:
   718→    ```bash
   719→    # Delete all users except admins (example pattern)
   720→    kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
   721→      bundle exec rails runner '
   722→      User.where.not(email: ["admin@example.com", "protected@example.com"]).destroy_all
   723→      '
   724→    ```
   725→
   726→    <Warning>
   727→    These operations are destructive and cannot be easily undone. Ensure backups are current before bulk deletions.
   728→    </Warning>
   729→  </Accordion>
   730→</AccordionGroup>
   731→
   732→## Getting Help
   733→
   734→### Debug Commands Reference
   735→
   736→<Tip>
   737→Quick reference for common debugging commands
   738→</Tip>
   739→
   740→```bash
   741→# View pod logs
   742→kubectl logs <pod-name>
   743→kubectl logs -f <pod-name>  # Follow logs
   744→kubectl logs <pod-name> --previous  # View previous container logs
   745→
   746→# Describe resources for detailed status
   747→kubectl describe pod <pod-name>
   748→kubectl describe deployment <deployment-name>
   749→kubectl describe svc <service-name>
   750→
   751→# Execute commands in pod
   752→kubectl exec -it <pod-name> -- /bin/bash
   753→kubectl exec -it <pod-name> -- env  # Check environment variables
   754→
   755→# Check resource status
   756→kubectl get pods
   757→kubectl get svc
   758→kubectl get pvc
   759→kubectl get secrets
   760→
   761→# View Helm release status
   762→helm status <release-name>
   763→helm get values <release-name>  # View current values
   764→
   765→# Port forwarding for testing
   766→kubectl port-forward svc/<service-name> 8080:80
   767→```

   782→    kubectl Cheat Sheet from Kubernetes docs
   783→  </Card>
   784→</CardGroup>
   785→

   787→**Additional Resources**:
   788→- [Helm Documentation](https://helm.sh/docs/)
   789→- [Google Kubernetes Engine Documentation](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs)
   790→</Info>
   791→
   792→### Reporting Issues
   793→
   794→When reporting issues, include:
   795→- Complete error messages and stack traces
   796→- Output of relevant kubectl commands
   797→- Kubernetes event logs
   798→- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)
   799→- Configuration details (without secrets)
   800→- CrewAI version and deployment environment
   801→

  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/index.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: CrewAI Enterprise\nsidebar_label: CrewAI Enterprise\nsidebar_position: 1\ndescription: Documentation for CrewAI Enterprise Platform deployment on Google Cloud Platform\n---\n\n# CrewAI Enterprise Platform\n\nThis section provides comprehensive documentation for deploying and operating the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on Google Cloud Platform (GCP).\n\n## Overview\n\nCrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.\n\n## Documentation Structure\n\n| Document | Description |\n|----------|-------------|\n| [GCP Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) | Production-grade Terraform IaC blueprint for GCP infrastructure |\n| [Installation Guide](install) | Step-by-step CrewAI Platform installation using Helm |\n| [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) | Initial configuration and access setup after deployment |\n| [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) | Solutions for common issues during installation and operation |\n| [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) | Integration guides for WorkOS and other services |\n| [FAQ](faq) | Frequently asked questions about CrewAI Platform |\n\n## Quick Start\n\n### Prerequisites\n\nBefore deploying CrewAI, ensure you have:\n\n- **GCP Organization** with billing enabled\n- **GKE Cluster** with sufficient resources\n- **Cloud SQL** PostgreSQL instance\n- **Cloud Storage** buckets for data and logs\n- **CrewAI Registry** credentials from the enterprise portal\n\n### Deployment Workflow\n\n1. **Provision Infrastructure** — Follow the [GCP Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) guide to deploy the required GCP resources using Terraform\n2. **Install CrewAI** — Use the [Installation Guide](install) to deploy CrewAI Platform via Helm\n3. **Configure Access** — Complete the [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) to initialize the platform and configure authentication\n4. **Verify Deployment** — Run verification steps and troubleshoot any issues using the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting)\n\n## Key Features\n\n- **Multi-agent Orchestration** — Coordinate multiple AI agents working together\n- **Role-based Specialization** — Define agent roles and responsibilities\n- **Task Delegation** — Automatic workflow management and task assignment\n- **Enterprise Security** — SSO, encryption, IAM, and compliance controls\n- **GCP-native Integration** — Optimized for Google Cloud Platform\n\n## Architecture Overview\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    GCP Organization                              │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                                   │\n│  ┌──────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │\n│  │  Folder Hierarchy│        │  GCP Project Infrastructure  │   │\n│  │  (Org Structure) │        │  ├─ APIs (50+)              │   │\n│  │  ├─ Root         │        │  ├─ Service Accounts        │   │\n│  │  ├─ Env          │        │  ├─ IAM Roles               │   │\n│  │  └─ Region       │        │  └─ Billing & Budget        │   │\n│  └──────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────────┘   │\n│           │                            │                         │\n│           └─────────────┬──────────────┘                         │\n│                         │                                        │\n│        ┌────────────────┴───────────────┐                       │\n│        │  CrewAI Platform Infrastructure │                       │\n│        │                                │                       │\n│  ┌─────┴──────────┐  ┌────────────────┐│ ┌──────────────────┐  │\n│  │   Networking   │  │   Storage      ││ │   Kubernetes     │  │\n│  │   ├─ VPC       │  │   ├─ Data      ││ │   ├─ Cluster     │  │\n│  │   ├─ Subnets   │  │   ├─ Logs      ││ │   ├─ Namespaces  │  │\n│  │   └─ NAT       │  │   └─ Backups   ││ │   └─ Add-ons     │  │\n│  └────────────────┘  └────────────────┘│ └──────────────────┘  │\n│                                ┌───────┴──────────┐             │\n│                                │  Database        │             │\n│                                │  ├─ Primary DB   │             │\n│                                │  ├─ Replicas     │             │\n│                                │  └─ Backups      │             │\n│                                └──────────────────┘             │\n│                                                                  │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Support\n\n- **Internal Support**: Platform Engineering team via Slack #platform-engineering\n- **CrewAI Enterprise Portal**: [https://enterprise.crewai.com](https://enterprise.crewai.com)\n- **Official Documentation**: [https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)\n\n---\n\n**Contact**: nathan.booth@konecta.com, mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/install.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: GCP CrewAI Installation Guide\nsidebar_label: Installation Guide\nsidebar_position: 3\ndescription: Install CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud using Cloud Shell\n---\n\n# CrewAI Platform Installation on Google Cloud\n\nA step-by-step guide to install the CrewAI Platform using Google Cloud Shell, including project setup, configuration, and platform deployment. For the official installation documentation, see [CrewAI Enterprise Installation Guide](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/installation/installation).\n\n:::info\nGoogle Cloud Shell is a free, browser-based terminal that comes pre-configured with the Google Cloud SDK, kubectl, and other tools you need for installation.\n:::\n\n:::warning Prerequisites: GCP Infrastructure Setup Required\nBefore installing CrewAI Platform, you must first set up your GCP infrastructure including:\n- GCP Organization and Project structure\n- GKE Kubernetes cluster\n- Cloud SQL database\n- Cloud Storage buckets\n- Networking and security configuration\n\nFollow the [GCP Infrastructure Setup Guide](gcp-setup) to provision these resources before proceeding with this installation.\n:::\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nBefore you begin, ensure you have:\n\n| Requirement | Description |\n|-------------|-------------|\n| **GCP Project** | An active GCP project with billing enabled |\n| **GKE Cluster** | A running Google Kubernetes Engine cluster with sufficient resources |\n| **Cloud Shell Access** | Access to Google Cloud Console |\n| **Registry Credentials** | CrewAI enterprise registry credentials from the customer portal |\n\n:::warning Get Your Credentials\nAccess your registry credentials via: [https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai/install](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai/install)\n\nYou'll need:\n- Customer email address\n- Secret authentication token\n\n**Need help accessing the portal?** Contact:\n- nathan.booth@konecta.com\n- mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com\n:::\n\n## Installation Steps\n\n### Step 1: Set Your Project ID\n\nOpen Google Cloud Console and launch Cloud Shell, then set your project:\n\n**Cloud Shell / Unix:**\n```bash\n# Set your GCP project ID\nexport PROJECT_ID=\"your-gcp-project-id\"\n\n# Verify the project is set\ngcloud config set project $PROJECT_ID\ngcloud config get-value project\n```\n\n**Local Windows (PowerShell):**\n```powershell\n# Set your GCP project ID\n$PROJECT_ID = \"your-gcp-project-id\"\n\n# Verify the project is set\ngcloud config set project $PROJECT_ID\ngcloud config get-value project\n```\n\nYour Cloud Shell will now use this project for all subsequent commands.\n\n### Step 2: Create Project Metadata Folder\n\nCreate a folder to organize and store your CrewAI deployment metadata:\n\n**1. Create Metadata Directory:**\n```bash\n# Create a folder to store CrewAI configuration and metadata\nmkdir -p ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata\n\n# Navigate to the directory\ncd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID\n\n# Verify the structure\nls -la\n```\n\n**2. Create Supporting Directories:**\n```bash\n# Create subdirectories for organization\nmkdir -p metadata/secrets\nmkdir -p metadata/backups\nmkdir -p metadata/logs\n\n# Create a README for documentation\ncat > metadata/README.md << 'EOF'\n# CrewAI Deployment Metadata\n\nProject ID: $PROJECT_ID\nCreated: $(date)\n\n## Directory Structure\n- secrets/: Sensitive configuration files (DO NOT COMMIT)\n- backups/: Backup of configurations and values\n- logs/: Installation and upgrade logs\n\n## Important Files\n- my-values.yaml: Helm chart values (store in secrets/)\n\nEOF\n\ncat metadata/README.md\n```\n\n### Step 3: Configure Installation via Enterprise Portal\n\nAccess the CrewAI Enterprise Portal to configure and retrieve your installation details:\n\n1. **Access the Install Tab** — Log in to [https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai) and navigate to the **Install** tab.\n\n2. **Configure Instance Details** — Provide the following information:\n   - **Instance Name:** FQDN format (e.g., `dev.crewai.konecta.com`)\n   - **Kubernetes Distribution:** Google GKE\n   - **Cluster Network Availability:** Outbound requests allowed\n   - **Registry Access:** Select: \"My workstation can access the internet, the registry AND the cluster\"\n\n3. **Retrieve Installation Instructions** — The portal will generate your complete Helm installation workflow.\n\n**Portal-generated installation sequence:**\n\n1. **Select a Version** — Choose your desired app version from the portal's version selector.\n\n2. **Export Credentials and Log In:**\n   ```bash\n   export AUTH_TOKEN=<token>\n   helm registry login registry.crewai.com --username mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com --password $AUTH_TOKEN\n   ```\n\n3. **Create Values Override File** — Create a `my-values.yaml` file to customize your installation (detailed in Step 4 below).\n\n4. **Install Preflight Plugin:**\n   ```bash\n   curl https://krew.sh/preflight | bash\n   ```\n\n5. **Install the Chart:**\n   ```bash\n   helm install crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n     --version 0.3.1 \\\n     --values my-values.yaml\n   ```\n\n:::info\nCopy the exact commands provided by the portal, as they contain your unique authentication token and version-specific installation details.\n:::\n\n### Step 4: Prepare Configuration File (my-values.yaml)\n\nUse the values template provided by the enterprise portal as a starting point.\n\n**1. Get Portal Template** — The portal provides a pre-configured `my-values.yaml` template customized for your instance.\n\n**2. Customize Your Configuration:**\n\n**Cloud Shell / Unix:**\n```bash\n# Navigate to your metadata directory\ncd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata\n\n# Open in editor (nano or vim)\nnano my-values.yaml\n```\n\n**Windows:**\n```powershell\n# Navigate to your metadata directory\ncd ~\\crewai-deployment\\$PROJECT_ID\\metadata\n\n# Open in editor\nnotepad my-values.yaml\n```\n\n:::warning Security: Secrets Management\n**NEVER commit `my-values.yaml` to version control if it contains secrets!**\n\nInstead:\n1. Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager\n2. Use CI/CD pipeline to inject secrets\n3. Or create a `.gitignore` entry and manage separately\n:::\n\n#### Key Configuration Parameters\n\n**Application Configuration:**\n\n| Parameter | Type | Description |\n|-----------|------|-------------|\n| `envVars.APPLICATION_HOST` | string (required) | Your domain name (FQDN) for the CrewAI instance. Example: `dev.crewai.konecta.com` |\n| `envVars.AUTH_PROVIDER` | string (required) | Authentication provider. Options: `workos`, `entra_id`, `google`, `local` |\n\n**Database Configuration:**\n\n| Parameter | Type | Description |\n|-----------|------|-------------|\n| `envVars.DB_HOST` | string (required) | Private IP address of your Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance. Example: `10.29.0.2` |\n| `envVars.DB_PORT` | string | PostgreSQL database port. Default: `5432` |\n| `envVars.DB_USER` | string | Database username. Default: `postgres` |\n| `secrets.DB_PASSWORD` | string (required) | Database password (store in Secret Manager) |\n\n**Storage Configuration (S3-compatible GCS):**\n\n| Parameter | Type | Description |\n|-----------|------|-------------|\n| `envVars.STORAGE_SERVICE` | string | Storage service type. Default: `amazon` (for S3-compatible GCS) |\n| `envVars.AWS_REGION` | string (required) | GCP region for Cloud Storage buckets. Example: `europe-west1` |\n| `envVars.AWS_BUCKET` | string (required) | Main data storage bucket name |\n| `envVars.LOGS_BUCKET` | string (required) | Logs storage bucket name |\n| `envVars.AWS_ENDPOINT` | string | S3-compatible endpoint. Default: `https://storage.googleapis.com` |\n| `secrets.AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` | string (required) | GCS HMAC access key ID |\n| `secrets.AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` | string (required) | GCS HMAC secret access key |\n\n**WorkOS Authentication:**\n\n| Parameter | Type | Description |\n|-----------|------|-------------|\n| `envVars.WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` | string (required) | WorkOS client ID. Format: `client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX` |\n| `envVars.WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` | string (required) | WorkOS AuthKit domain. Example: `your-domain.authkit.app` |\n| `secrets.WORKOS_API_KEY` | string (required) | WorkOS API key (store in Secret Manager) |\n| `secrets.WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` | string (required) | 32-character random string. Generate with: `openssl rand -hex 16` |\n\n#### Sample Configuration Structure\n\n```yaml\n# CrewAI Platform Helm Values Configuration\n\npostgres:\n  enabled: false  # Use external Cloud SQL database\n\nminio:\n  enabled: false  # Use external GCP Cloud Storage (S3-compatible)\n\nenvVars:\n  # Application Configuration\n  APPLICATION_HOST: \"dev.crewai.konecta.com\"\n\n  # Database Configuration\n  DB_HOST: \"10.29.0.2\"  # Private IP of Cloud SQL instance\n  DB_PORT: \"5432\"\n  DB_USER: \"postgres\"\n\n  # Container Registry Configuration\n  CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE: \"europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/your-project/crewai-enterprise\"\n\n  # Authentication Provider\n  AUTH_PROVIDER: \"workos\"\n  WORKOS_CLIENT_ID: \"client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX\"\n  WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: \"your-domain.authkit.app\"\n\n  # Storage Configuration (S3-compatible GCP Cloud Storage)\n  STORAGE_SERVICE: \"amazon\"\n  AWS_REGION: \"europe-west1\"\n  AWS_BUCKET: \"your-project-crewai-data\"\n  LOGS_BUCKET: \"your-project-crewai-logs\"\n  AWS_ENDPOINT: \"https://storage.googleapis.com\"\n\nsecrets:\n  # Database Credentials\n  DB_PASSWORD: \"[REDACTED]\"  # Store in GCP Secret Manager\n\n  # GCS HMAC Keys\n  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: \"[REDACTED]\"\n  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: \"[REDACTED]\"\n\n  # Authentication Secrets\n  WORKOS_API_KEY: \"[REDACTED]\"\n  WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD: \"[REDACTED]\"\n\n# Container Registry Authentication\nimage:\n  registries:\n    - host: \"europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev\"\n      username: \"_json_key_base64\"\n      password: \"[REDACTED]\"  # Base64-encoded GCP service account JSON\n\n# Web Service Configuration\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 1  # PRODUCTION: Use 3 for HA\n  port: 80\n  useHttps: false\n  enableSslFromPuma: false\n\n  ingress:\n    enabled: true\n    className: \"\"\n    host: \"dev.crewai.konecta.com\"\n    annotations:\n      networking.gke.io/managed-certificates: gcp-domains-ssl-cert\n      kubernetes.io/ingress.global-static-ip-name: \"dev-crewai-konecta-com-external-ip\"\n      kubernetes.io/ingress.class: \"gce\"\n\n# Background Job Processing\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 1  # PRODUCTION: Use 3 for parallel processing\n\n# GCP Configuration\ngcp:\n  ssldomains:\n    - \"dev.crewai.konecta.com\"\n  sslPolicy: \"dev-crewai-ssl-policy\"\n\n# OAuth Configuration\noauth:\n  enabled: true\n```\n\n:::note Infrastructure Note\nThe static IP address resource must be provisioned in GCP before installation. See the [GCP Infrastructure Setup Guide](gcp-setup) for details.\n:::\n\n### Key Configuration Differences: Development vs Production\n\n**Development Configuration (Cost-optimized):**\n```yaml\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 1  # Single instance to save costs\n\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 1  # Single worker for background jobs\n\n# DNS: dev.crewai.konecta.com\n# Resources: Minimal (adequate for testing/validation)\n```\n\n**Production Configuration (High Availability):**\n```yaml\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 3  # Three replicas for zero-downtime deployments\n  terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60\n\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 3  # Distribute background job processing\n```\n\n:::warning Sensitive Fields Security\nThe following fields must **NEVER** be committed to version control:\n\n- `DB_PASSWORD`\n- `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` / `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`\n- `WORKOS_API_KEY`\n- `OAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET`\n- `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD`\n- `image.registries[].password`\n\n**Recommended**: Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager and use External Secrets Operator.\n:::\n\n### Step 5: Prepare Your Environment\n\n**1. Verify Cloud SQL Connection:**\n```bash\n# List your Cloud SQL instances\ngcloud sql instances list\n\n# Get specific instance details\ngcloud sql instances describe crewai-db \\\n  --format=\"get(connectionName,ipAddresses[0].ipAddress)\"\n```\n\n**2. Verify Default Storage Class:**\n```bash\n# Check current storage classes\nkubectl get storageclass\n\n# Set standard as default (if not already)\nkubectl patch storageclass standard \\\n  -p '{\"metadata\": {\"annotations\":{\"storageclass.kubernetes.io/is-default-class\":\"true\"}}}'\n```\n\n**3. Install Preflight Validation Plugin:**\n```bash\ncurl https://krew.sh/preflight | bash\n\n# Add Krew to PATH\nexport PATH=\"${KREW_ROOT:-$HOME/.krew}/bin:$PATH\"\n\n# Verify installation\nkubectl preflight --version\n```\n\n### Step 6: Run Preflight Validation\n\n```bash\n# Run preflight checks with your values file\nhelm template oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --values my-values.yaml \\\n  | kubectl preflight -\n```\n\n:::warning Address Preflight Issues\nThe preflight check validates:\n- Cluster resources (CPU, memory)\n- Storage classes\n- RBAC permissions\n- Kubernetes version compatibility\n\nAddress any failures before proceeding to installation.\n:::\n\n### Step 7: Install the CrewAI Platform\n\n**1. Execute the Helm Install Command:**\n```bash\nhelm install crewai-platform \\\n  oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --version <VERSION_FROM_PORTAL> \\\n  --values my-values.yaml \\\n  --namespace default \\\n  --create-namespace\n```\n\n**2. Monitor Installation Progress:**\n```bash\n# Watch pod creation in real-time\nkubectl get pods -w\n\n# Or check status\nkubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform\n```\n\n:::info Installation Time\nInstallation typically takes 5-15 minutes depending on image sizes and cluster resources.\n:::\n\n**3. Verify Installation Success:**\n```bash\n# Check all pods are running\nkubectl get pods\n\n# Check services\nkubectl get svc\n\n# View installation logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=50\n```\n\n### Step 8: Upgrade the Platform\n\n**1. Prepare for Upgrade:**\n```bash\ncd ~/crewai-deployment/$PROJECT_ID/metadata\n\n# Backup your current values file\ncp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S)\n\n# Check available chart versions\nhelm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions\n```\n\n**2. Review Changes:**\n```bash\nhelm upgrade crewai-platform \\\n  oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --values my-values.yaml \\\n  --dry-run --debug\n```\n\n**3. Execute Upgrade:**\n```bash\nhelm upgrade crewai-platform \\\n  oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --values my-values.yaml\n\n# Monitor the rollout\nkubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web\n\n# Verify upgrade success\nkubectl get pods\n```\n\n## Verification Steps\n\n| Check | Command | Expected Result |\n|-------|---------|-----------------|\n| **Pod Status** | `kubectl get pods -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform` | All pods `Running` |\n| **Services** | `kubectl get svc -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform` | ClusterIP or LoadBalancer assigned |\n| **Logs** | `kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=100` | Successful startup messages |\n| **Resources** | `kubectl top pods` | Cluster has sufficient capacity |\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Pods Not Starting\n\n```bash\nkubectl describe pod <POD_NAME>\nkubectl logs <POD_NAME>\nkubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n```\n\n**Common Causes:**\n- Registry authentication failed — Re-check credentials\n- Insufficient cluster resources — Scale cluster\n- Missing default storage class — Run Step 5 again\n\n### Registry Authentication Failed\n\n```bash\n# Re-authenticate with the registry\nhelm registry login registry.crewai.com \\\n  --username $CREWAI_EMAIL \\\n  --password $CREWAI_TOKEN\n\n# Clear cache and retry\nrm -rf ~/.config/helm/registry/config.json\n```\n\n### Database Connection Errors\n\n```bash\n# Test Cloud SQL connectivity from pod\nkubectl exec -it <POD_NAME> -- psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $POSTGRES_DB\n\n# Check firewall rules\ngcloud compute firewall-rules list --filter=\"name~'gke'\"\n```\n\nFor detailed troubleshooting, see the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting).\n\n## Next Steps\n\n1. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize the internal organization and configure admin access\n2. **Configure Access** — Set up port forwarding (dev) or ingress (production)\n3. **Enable Studio V2** — Optional next-generation interface setup\n4. **Review [Configuration Guide](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/configuration/configuration)** — Explore advanced options\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n- [Official Installation Docs](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/installation/installation)\n- [Configuration Guide](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/configuration/configuration)\n- [Customer Portal](https://enterprise.crewai.com/crewai)\n"
}
```

  "content": "---\ntitle: GCP Infrastructure Setup\nsidebar_label: GCP Infrastructure Setup\nsidebar_position: 2\ndescription: Production-grade Terraform IaC for CrewAI platform deployment on Google Cloud Platform\n---\n\n# GCP Infrastructure Setup Blueprint\n\nA **production-grade Terraform Infrastructure-as-Code (IaC)** blueprint that automates the complete deployment of the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on **Google Cloud Platform (GCP)**. This solution handles everything from organizational hierarchy setup to a fully configured, secure Kubernetes cluster with all required backend services.\n\n## What Gets Created\n\n### Project Foundation\n\n| Component | Description |\n|-----------|-------------|\n| **GCP Project** | Automatically created with organizational labels and metadata |\n| **Folder Hierarchy** | Multi-level structure: Environment → Region → Country |\n| **APIs** | Enables all required Google Cloud APIs (50+) |\n| **Service Accounts** | Dedicated accounts with least-privilege IAM roles |\n| **Budget Management** | Automated budget alerts with Cloud Functions |\n| **Audit Logging** | Centralized logging for organization audits |\n\n### CrewAI Platform Infrastructure\n\n#### Compute & Orchestration\n\n**GKE Cluster** — Google Kubernetes Engine Standard cluster with:\n- Private nodes with no public IPs\n- Public control plane for administration\n- Automated node version management\n- Comprehensive logging and monitoring integration\n- Custom service accounts with least-privilege\n\n#### Data Layer\n\n**Cloud SQL PostgreSQL:**\n- Regional HA (High Availability) across zones\n- PostgreSQL 16 with automatic backups\n- Read replicas for scaling read operations\n- Point-in-time recovery (7 days retention)\n- Private networking (no public IP)\n- SSL/TLS encryption enforced\n\n#### Storage\n\n**GCS Buckets:**\n- Data bucket (versioning, encryption enabled)\n- Logs bucket (lifecycle policies)\n- Automatic backup and retention policies\n\n#### Networking\n\n- **VPC Network** — Custom VPC with multi-zone subnets\n- **Cloud NAT** — Secure outbound connectivity for private nodes\n- **Firewall Rules** — Internal communication, GKE cluster, SSH restrictions\n- **Secondary IP Ranges** — Dedicated ranges for pods and services\n\n#### Security & Secrets\n\n- **Secret Manager** — Secure storage for database credentials\n- **IAM Roles** — Fine-grained access control\n- **Artifact Registry** — Private Docker image repository\n- **SSL/TLS** — Encrypted communication throughout\n\n## Technology Stack\n\n### Infrastructure as Code\n\n| Component | Technology | Version |\n|-----------|-----------|---------|\n| **IaC Framework** | Terraform | >= 1.0.0 |\n| **Primary Cloud** | Google Cloud Platform | Latest |\n| **State Management** | AWS S3 (Konecta Standard) | - |\n| **Linting** | TFLint | Latest |\n\n### GCP Services\n\n| Service | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|\n| **GKE** | Container orchestration (Standard cluster, private nodes) |\n| **Cloud SQL** | Managed PostgreSQL (Regional HA with replicas) |\n| **Cloud Storage** | Object storage (Encrypted, versioned buckets) |\n| **VPC Network** | Virtual networking (Multi-zone, custom subnets) |\n| **Cloud NAT** | Secure egress for private nodes |\n| **Secret Manager** | Database credentials |\n| **Artifact Registry** | Private Docker repository |\n| **Cloud Pub/Sub** | Budget alerts, events |\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n:::warning\nMissing prerequisites can cause deployment failures. Ensure all tools are installed and properly configured.\n:::\n\n### Required Tools\n\n**macOS (Homebrew):**\n```bash\nbrew install terraform gcloud-cli kubectl awscli\n```\n\n**Ubuntu/Debian:**\n```bash\nsudo apt-get update && \\\nsudo apt-get install -y terraform google-cloud-sdk kubectl awscli\n```\n\n**Windows (Chocolatey):**\n```powershell\nchoco install terraform google-cloud-sdk kubernetes-cli awscli\n```\n\n### GCP Requirements\n\n| Requirement | Description |\n|-------------|-------------|\n| **GCP Organization** | Active organization account with proper access |\n| **Billing Account** | Linked to your organization |\n| **Organization Admin Role** | To create folders and projects |\n| **Credentials** | Service account with appropriate permissions |\n\n### AWS Requirements (for Terraform State)\n\n| Requirement | Description |\n|-------------|-------------|\n| **AWS Account** | Access to S3 bucket `konecta-terraform-cuentas` |\n| **SSO Profile** | `default_core_irlanda` configured |\n| **S3 Permissions** | Read/write access to state bucket |\n\n### Verify Installation\n\n```bash\nterraform version\ngcloud --version\nkubectl version --client\naws --version\n```\n\n## Quick Start Guide\n\n**1. Clone Repository:**\n```bash\ngit clone git@bitbucket.org:grupokonecta/crewai-gcp-infra-prod.git\ncd crewai-gcp-infra-prod\n```\n\n**2. Authenticate:**\n```bash\n# Authenticate with GCP\ngcloud auth application-default login\n\n# Configure AWS SSO for Terraform state\naws sso login --profile default_core_irlanda\n```\n\n**3. Configure Variables:**\n```bash\ncp terraform.auto.tfvars.example terraform.auto.tfvars\n# Edit terraform.auto.tfvars with your values\n```\n\n**4. Validate:**\n```bash\nmake validate\n```\n\n**5. Initialize & Plan:**\n```bash\nmake init\nmake plan\n```\n\n**6. Deploy Infrastructure:**\n```bash\nmake apply\n```\n\n:::info Deployment Time\nThis takes approximately 15-20 minutes.\n:::\n\n**7. Verify Deployment:**\n```bash\ngcloud container clusters get-credentials crewai-cluster \\\n  --region <your-region> \\\n  --project <your-project-id>\n\nkubectl get nodes\n```\n\n## Configuration Guide\n\n### Essential Variables\n\n#### GCP Organization Structure\n\n```hcl\norganization_id        = \"123456789\"\nroot_folder_name       = \"Global IT\"\nsolution_folder_name   = \"CrewAI\"\nenv_folder_name        = \"PROD\"\nregion_folder_name     = \"CORP\"\ncountry_folder_name    = \"CO\"\n```\n\n#### Project Configuration\n\n```hcl\nproject_id      = \"kn-co-globalit-svc-crewai-dev\"\nproject_name    = \"CREWAI-DEV-CO\"\ndescription     = \"CrewAI Platform Development\"\ngcp_region      = \"us-central1\"\nbilling_account = \"0156A6-7B8C9D-E2F3A4\"\n```\n\n#### CrewAI Platform Configuration\n\n```hcl\n# Network Configuration\nvpc_cidr   = \"10.0.0.0/16\"\nzone_count = 3\n\n# Storage Configuration\ngcs_crewai_data_suffix = \"crewai-data\"\ngcs_crewai_logs_suffix = \"crewai-logs\"\n\n# Cluster Configuration\ncluster_name                = \"crewai-cluster\"\ncluster_initial_node_count  = 1\ncluster_node_machine_type   = \"e2-standard-4\"\ncluster_authorized_networks = [\"203.0.113.25/32\"]\n\n# Database Configuration\ndb_instance_name      = \"crewai-db\"\ndb_instance_tier      = \"db-custom-4-16384\"\ndb_edition            = \"ENTERPRISE\"\ndb_database_name      = \"crewai\"\ndb_master_username    = \"crewai_admin\"\nreader_instance_count = 2\n```\n\n#### External IP & Network Access Configuration\n\n```hcl\n# Configure external IP access to GKE cluster API endpoint\ncluster_authorized_networks = [\n  \"203.0.113.25/32\",      # Jane's office IP\n  \"198.51.100.0/24\",      # Company VPN subnet\n  \"192.0.2.50/32\"         # CI/CD pipeline IP\n]\n```\n\n**Find your external IP:**\n```bash\ncurl -s https://checkip.amazonaws.com\n```\n\n## Module Dependency Graph\n\n```\nfolders\n  ↓\nprojects → iam_pre ← billing\n  ↓         ↓ ↓ ↓\n apis ← service_accounts\n  ↓         ↓\ntopics  cfunctions\n  ↓         ↓\nbuckets ←──┘\n  ↓\n iam\n  ↓\ncrewai-platform/\n  ├── networking\n  ├── repository\n  ├── storage\n  ├── database ← networking\n  ├── secrets ← database\n  ├── cluster ← networking, repository\n  └── cluster-addons ← cluster, secrets\n```\n\n## Deployment Instructions\n\n### Step 1: Prepare Your Environment\n\n**Clone & Authenticate:**\n```bash\ngit clone git@bitbucket.org:grupokonecta/crewai-gcp-infra-prod.git\ncd crewai-gcp-infra-prod\n\ngcloud auth application-default login\n```\n\n**Configure State Management:**\n```bash\naws sso login --profile default_core_irlanda\naws sts get-caller-identity --profile default_core_irlanda\n```\n\n### Step 2: Configure Variables\n\n```bash\ncp terraform.auto.tfvars.example terraform.auto.tfvars\nnano terraform.auto.tfvars\n```\n\n### Step 3: Validate Configuration\n\n```bash\nmake validate\n```\n\n### Step 4: Initialize Terraform\n\n```bash\nmake init\n```\n\n### Step 5: Preview Infrastructure Changes\n\n```bash\nmake plan\n```\n\nExpected: `Plan: 70 to add, 0 to change, 0 to destroy`\n\n### Step 6: Deploy Infrastructure\n\n```bash\nmake apply\n```\n\n:::warning Confirm Deployment\nWhen prompted, type **yes** to confirm. Deployment takes **15-20 minutes**.\n:::\n\n### Step 7: Verify Deployment\n\n**Cluster Access:**\n```bash\ngcloud container clusters get-credentials crewai-cluster \\\n  --region <your-gcp-region> \\\n  --project <your-project-id>\n\nkubectl get nodes\n```\n\n**Cluster Resources:**\n```bash\nkubectl get pods --all-namespaces\nkubectl get services --all-namespaces\n```\n\n**Database:**\n```bash\ngcloud sql instances describe crewai-db --project <your-project-id>\n```\n\n## Resource Overview\n\n| Module | Resource Count | Deployment Time |\n|--------|---------------|-----------------|\n| Folders | 3 | 30 seconds |\n| Projects | 1 | 1 minute |\n| APIs | 50+ | 2 minutes |\n| Service Accounts | 3-5 | 1 minute |\n| IAM Roles | 10+ | 2 minutes |\n| Networking | 8-10 | 2 minutes |\n| Storage | 2 | 30 seconds |\n| Database | 3-5 | 10-15 minutes |\n| Cluster | 1 | 12-18 minutes |\n| **Total** | **80-90** | **20-30 minutes** |\n\n### Cost Estimation\n\n**Development Environment (~$190-285/month):**\n| Component | Cost |\n|-----------|------|\n| GKE cluster | $150-200/month |\n| Cloud SQL | $25-50/month |\n| Cloud Storage | $5-15/month |\n| Networking | $10-20/month |\n\n**Production Environment (~$800-1,400/month):**\n| Component | Cost |\n|-----------|------|\n| GKE cluster (HA) | $500-800/month |\n| Cloud SQL with HA | $200-400/month |\n| Cloud Storage | $30-60/month |\n| Load balancing | $50-100/month |\n| Cloud Armor | $20-40/month |\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Terraform Init Fails\n\n```bash\nrm -rf .terraform\nterraform init\n```\n\n### Authentication Errors\n\n```bash\ngcloud auth application-default login\ngcloud auth list\naws sso login --profile default_core_irlanda\n```\n\n### API Not Enabled\n\n```bash\ngcloud services enable compute.googleapis.com --project=PROJECT_ID\n```\n\n### Insufficient Quota\n\n```bash\ngcloud compute project-info describe --project=PROJECT_ID\n```\nRequest quota increase in GCP Console: **IAM & Admin** → **Quotas**\n\n### GKE Cluster Timeout\n\n```bash\ngcloud container clusters describe crewai-cluster \\\n  --region=REGION \\\n  --project=PROJECT_ID\n\ngcloud container operations list --project=PROJECT_ID\n```\n\n## Production Deployment Checklist\n\n### Infrastructure Readiness\n- [ ] GCP Organization and billing account configured\n- [ ] Service account created with appropriate IAM roles\n- [ ] AWS S3 state backend access verified\n- [ ] All variables configured in terraform.auto.tfvars\n- [ ] Budget alerts configured\n- [ ] Network isolation and firewall rules reviewed\n\n### Security & Compliance\n- [ ] SSL/TLS certificates configured\n- [ ] Database credentials stored in Secret Manager\n- [ ] Workload Identity enabled for service accounts\n- [ ] Audit logging enabled for all resources\n- [ ] VPC Flow Logs enabled\n\n### Testing & Validation\n- [ ] terraform validate passed\n- [ ] tflint checks passed\n- [ ] Plan reviewed for correctness\n- [ ] Database connectivity verified\n- [ ] Kubernetes cluster connectivity verified\n\n## Next Steps\n\nAfter infrastructure provisioning:\n\n1. **[Install CrewAI Platform](install)** — Deploy the application using Helm\n2. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize organization and configure access\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n- [Terraform Documentation](https://www.terraform.io/docs)\n- [Google Cloud Provider](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)\n- [GKE Best Practices](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/best-practices)\n- [Cloud SQL Best Practices](https://cloud.google.com/sql/docs/postgres/best-practices)\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/post-install.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Post-Installation Setup\nsidebar_label: Post-Installation Setup\nsidebar_position: 4\ndescription: Post installation steps for CrewAI Platform\n---\n\n# Post-Installation Setup for CrewAI Platform\n\n## Overview\n\nAfter successfully deploying the CrewAI Platform infrastructure, these initial setup steps are necessary to configure the core system, set up permissions, and prepare the environment for use.\n\n:::warning Important\nComplete these setup steps immediately after deployment. Skipping any steps may result in missing functionality or access issues.\n:::\n\n## Initial Setup Tasks\n\nThese tasks must be run immediately after installation to configure the internal organization and administration access.\n\n### Step 1: Initialize Internal Organization\n\nRun the following command to initialize the required internal organization, typically named \"Default.\"\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- rake studio:install_internal_organization\n```\n\n### Step 2: Set Up Default Permissions\n\nConfigure the default permission set and assign the initial owner role. **Replace `admin@company.com` with your desired administrator's email address.**\n\n```bash\n# Replace with your admin email\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \\\n  rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults 'factory:add_owner[2,admin@company.com]'\n```\n\n### Step 3: Fix Missing 'Operate' Tab (Troubleshoot)\n\nIf the **\"Operate\"** tab is missing in the Web UI, you may need to manually assign the `Owner` role to your user via the Rails console.\n\n**1. Shell into the `crewai-web` pod:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -- /bin/bash\n```\n\n**2. Start the Rails console:**\n```bash\nbin/rails c\n```\n\n**3. Define the role assignment function (paste this code block):**\n```ruby\ndef assign_owner_role(user_email)\n  user = User.find_by(email: user_email)\n  organization = Organization.find_by(name: \"Default\")\n  owner_role = organization.roles.find_by(name: \"Owner\")\n  membership = organization.memberships.find_by(user_id: user.id)\n  membership.update!(role_id: owner_role.id)\nend\n```\n\n**4. Run the assignment function (replace with your user email):**\n```ruby\nassign_owner_role(\"nathan.booth@konecta.com\")\n```\n\n**5. Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and refresh your browser.**\n\n## Enable Studio V2 (Optional)\n\n:::info What is Studio V2?\nStudio V2 is the next-generation interface and agent capability for CrewAI, providing enhanced features and improved user experience.\n:::\n\n### Step 1: Check LLM Connection and Delete Old Crews\n\nEnsure you have an LLM connection named **`studio-v2`** configured. Then, shell into the web-service pod and delete any old Studio crew deployments.\n\n**1. Shell into the web-service pod and run:**\n```bash\nbin/rails c\n```\n\n**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**\n```ruby\nProvisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)\nProvisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)\n```\n\n### Step 2: Install the Studio Agent\n\nExit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent. This process can take up to **~10 minutes**.\n\n```bash\nINTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install\n```\n\n### Step 3: Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools\n\nContinuously check the agent status until it reports **\"Crew is Online\"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.\n\n```bash\n# Check status - wait until it says \"Crew is Online\"\nrake studio:agent:status\n\n# Sync and re-index tools\nrake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools\nrake studio:tools:reindex_tools\n```\n\n### Step 4: Install the Studio Runner\n\nInstall the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for 10 minutes for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.\n\n```bash\nINTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install\n```\n\n## Accessing the Application\n\nUse the appropriate method to access the CrewAI Web UI based on your deployment environment.\n\n### Development Access (Port Forwarding)\n\nIdeal for quick testing or development environments.\n\n```bash\n# Forward web service port (2603 is the external port, 80 is the internal service port)\nkubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web 2603:80\n\n# Access in browser\nopen http://localhost:2603\n```\n\n### Production Access (Ingress / LoadBalancer)\n\n| Method | Command to Find Endpoint | Access URL Example |\n|--------|--------------------------|-------------------|\n| **Ingress** | `kubectl get ing crewai-ingress` | `https://crewai.company.com` |\n| **LoadBalancer** | `kubectl get svc crewai-web` | `http://<EXTERNAL-IP>:80` |\n\n## Verification Steps\n\nAfter completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:\n\n1. **Access the Web UI** using one of the methods above\n2. **Log in** with your configured authentication provider\n3. **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of administrative links/tabs\n4. **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project\n5. **Check background workers** for successful job processing:\n   ```bash\n   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker --tail=50\n   ```\n\n:::success Congratulations!\nYour CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!\n\nFor more information, visit the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) or the [FAQ](faq).\n:::\n"
}
```
