{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/troubleshooting.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Troubleshooting Guide\nsidebar_label: Troubleshooting\nsidebar_position: 5\ndescription: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation\n---\n\n# Troubleshooting Guide\n\nThis guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation.\n\n:::tip\nUse the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands.\n:::\n\n## Installation & Deployment Issues\n\n### Helm Installation Failures\n\n#### Issue: Helm chart installation fails with 'values validation error'\n\n**Cause**: Missing or incorrectly formatted required values in `my-values.yaml`\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify all required variables are present in your values file\n- Check YAML syntax for formatting errors (indentation, quotes)\n- Use `helm lint` to validate the chart before installation\n- Review [Prepare Configuration File guide](install#step-4-prepare-configuration-file-my-valuesyaml)\n\n#### Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' errors after deployment\n\n**Cause**: Container registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `image.registries[].password` is correctly base64-encoded\n- Confirm the service account has access to the container registry\n- Check that the image tag exists in the specified registry\n- Review Kubernetes events: `kubectl describe pod <pod-name>`\n\n### Database Connection Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors\n\n**Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `DB_PASSWORD` is correct and matches the database user\n- Confirm database host is reachable from the Kubernetes cluster\n- Check network policies allow traffic to the database\n- Test connection manually:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl exec -it <pod> -- psql -h <host> -U postgres\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Database migration fails' on first deployment\n\n**Cause**: Database schema initialization incomplete\n\n**Solution**:\n- Ensure database is fully initialized before deploying application\n- Check database logs for migration errors\n- Verify user has proper privileges for schema creation\n- Run migrations manually if needed:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl exec -it <pod> -- rake db:migrate\n  ```\n\n## Authentication & Authorization Issues\n\n### WorkOS Authentication\n\n#### Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page\n\n**Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value\n- Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)\n- Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets\n- Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled\n- Check browser console for authentication errors\n- Review application logs:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl logs -f <pod-name>\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error\n\n**Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch\n\n:::warning IMPORTANT\n`WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes\n:::\n\n**Solution**:\n- Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)\n- **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)\n- Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long\n- Ensure the same password is used across all replicas\n- Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager\n- Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl rollout restart deployment/<name>\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option\n\n**Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method\n\n**Solution**:\n- Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings\n- Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode\n- Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured\n\n#### Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails\n\n**Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured\n- Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain\n- Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs\n- Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials\n\n### CLI Authentication Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'crew ai login' command fails\n\n**Cause**: WorkOS Connect not properly configured or OIDC middleware disabled\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `oauth.enabled: true` in Helm values\n- Confirm WorkOS Connect is enabled in WorkOS dashboard\n- Check that CLI can reach the authentication endpoint\n- Review application logs for OIDC errors\n- Try manual token generation as workaround\n\n### OAuth Configuration Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'couldn't find key OAUTH_DB_HOST in Secret' error\n\n**Cause**: OAuth enabled after initial deployment without providing `OAUTH_DB_HOST` secret\n\n:::warning\nWhen enabling `oauth: true` in Helm values, you must also provide `OAUTH_DB_HOST`\n:::\n\n**Solution**:\n\nAdd to your Kubernetes secret:\n```bash\nkubectl patch secret crewai-secrets -p \\\n  '{\"data\": {\"OAUTH_DB_HOST\": \"'$(echo -n '10.0.0.2' | base64)'\"}}'\n```\n\nReplace `10.0.0.2` with your actual OAuth database host.\n\nRestart deployment after updating secret:\n```bash\nkubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-web\n```\n\n## Network & Connectivity Issues\n\n### Ingress & Load Balancer\n\n#### Issue: External IP remains 'pending'\n\n**Cause**: Static IP resource not provisioned or ingress misconfigured\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify static IP exists in GCP:\n  ```bash\n  gcloud compute addresses list\n  ```\n- Check ingress annotations reference correct IP name\n- Confirm load balancer backend services are healthy\n- Review GCP Console > Network Services > Load Balancing\n\n#### Issue: 'Connection refused' when accessing application\n\n**Cause**: Service not accessible or firewall blocking traffic\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify pod is running: `kubectl get pods`\n- Check service is created: `kubectl get svc`\n- Test internal connectivity:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl port-forward svc/<service> 8080:80\n  ```\n- Review firewall rules in GCP Console > VPC Network > Firewall rules\n\n### DNS Resolution\n\n#### Issue: Domain name not resolving\n\n**Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify DNS A record points to load balancer IP\n- Wait for DNS propagation (up to 48 hours)\n- Test DNS:\n  ```bash\n  nslookup your-domain.com\n  dig your-domain.com\n  ```\n- Check GCP Cloud DNS records if using Cloud DNS\n\n#### Issue: 'Invalid certificate' or SSL errors\n\n**Cause**: SSL certificate not provisioned or misconfigured\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify managed certificate is created:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl get managedcertificate\n  ```\n- Check certificate is in \"Active\" state\n- Confirm domain in FrontendConfig matches certificate domains\n\n## Storage & Persistence Issues\n\n### Cloud Storage / S3 Access\n\n#### Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket\n\n**Cause**: AWS credentials invalid or lack permissions\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID` and `AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` are correct HMAC keys\n- Confirm service account has `storage.objects.create` permission\n- Check bucket exists and is accessible\n- Test access:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl exec -it <pod> -- aws s3 ls s3://your-bucket/\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'No such bucket' errors\n\n**Cause**: Bucket name wrong or not accessible from cluster\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `AWS_BUCKET` and `LOGS_BUCKET` match actual bucket names\n- Confirm buckets exist in GCP Cloud Storage\n- Check bucket region matches `AWS_REGION`\n- Verify endpoint is correct: `https://storage.googleapis.com`\n\n### Volume Mounting\n\n#### Issue: Persistent volume not mounting\n\n**Cause**: Volume not created or mount configuration incorrect\n\n**Solution**:\n- Check volume exists: `kubectl get pv`\n- Verify PVC is bound: `kubectl get pvc`\n- Check pod logs for mount errors\n- Describe pod to see mount status:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl describe pod <pod-name>\n  ```\n\n## Container Registry Issues\n\n### GCP Service Account Authentication\n\n#### Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' or 'unauthorized: authentication required'\n\n**Cause**: Container registry credentials missing, incorrect, or not properly base64-encoded\n\n**Solution**:\n\n**Step 1: Create GCP Service Account Key:**\n```bash\ngcloud iam service-accounts keys create sa-key.json \\\n  --iam-account=<service-account-email>\n```\n\n**Step 2: Base64 Encode the Key:**\n```bash\ncat sa-key.json | base64 -w 0 > sa-key.base64\n```\n\n**Step 3: Create Kubernetes Secret:**\n```bash\nkubectl create secret docker-registry gcp-registry-secret \\\n  --docker-server=\"europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev\" \\\n  --docker-username=\"_json_key_base64\" \\\n  --docker-password=\"$(cat sa-key.base64)\"\n```\n\n:::warning Critical Security Considerations\n- Never commit `sa-key.json` to version control\n- Never store raw JSON in Helm values files\n- Rotate keys regularly (quarterly minimum)\n- Delete old keys after rotation\n- Restrict key permissions to only necessary roles\n:::\n\n#### Issue: 'access denied' when pulling from private registry\n\n**Cause**: Service account lacks necessary permissions\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify service account has `roles/artifactregistry.reader` role\n- Grant if missing:\n  ```bash\n  gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding <project-id> \\\n    --member=serviceAccount:<sa-email> \\\n    --role=roles/artifactregistry.reader\n  ```\n\n## Performance & Resource Issues\n\n### CPU & Memory\n\n#### Issue: Pods getting OOMKilled (out of memory)\n\n**Cause**: Application memory usage exceeds resource limits\n\n**Solution**:\n- Increase memory limits in Helm values\n- Profile application memory usage\n- Check for memory leaks in logs\n- Increase replica count to distribute load\n\n#### Issue: High CPU usage or slow response times\n\n**Cause**: Insufficient CPU allocation or application bottleneck\n\n**Solution**:\n- Increase CPU limits in Helm values\n- Enable horizontal pod autoscaling\n- Profile application CPU usage\n- Check for database query performance issues\n\n### Scaling Issues\n\n#### Issue: Auto-scaling not working\n\n**Cause**: HPA not configured or metrics unavailable\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify HPA is created: `kubectl get hpa`\n- Check metrics are available:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl top nodes\n  kubectl top pods\n  ```\n- Ensure metrics-server is installed:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl get deployment metrics-server -n kube-system\n  ```\n- Review HPA status:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl describe hpa <hpa-name>\n  ```\n\n## Kubernetes Issues\n\n### Pod Issues\n\n#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state\n\n**Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues\n\n**Solution**:\n- Check node availability: `kubectl get nodes`\n- Describe pod to see pending reason:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl describe pod <pod-name>\n  ```\n- Check resource requests vs available resources\n- Scale up node pool if needed\n\n#### Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'\n\n**Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error\n\n**Solution**:\n- Check pod logs:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl logs <pod-name>\n  kubectl logs <pod-name> --previous\n  ```\n- Verify all environment variables are set correctly\n- Test application locally with same configuration\n\n### Secret Management\n\n#### Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets\n\n**Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify secret exists: `kubectl get secrets`\n- Check secret data: `kubectl describe secret <secret-name>`\n- Confirm secret is referenced in pod spec\n- Verify pod has restarted after secret creation\n\n## User and Data Management\n\n### Removing Users for WorkOS Integration Testing\n\n#### Issue: Need to remove user account to test WorkOS sign-in\n\n**Solution**:\n\n**List all users:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \\\n  bundle exec rails runner \\\n  'User.select(:id, :email).each { |u| puts \"ID: #{u.id} | Email: #{u.email}\" }'\n```\n\n**Delete single user by email:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \\\n  bundle exec rails runner \\\n  \"User.find_by_email('user@example.com')&.destroy!\"\n```\n\n**If deletion fails with foreign key constraint error:**\n\nUse the deep clean method to remove user and all related data:\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \\\n  bundle exec rails runner '\n  user = User.find_by(email: \"user@example.com\")\n  if user\n    session_ids = ChatSession.where(user_id: user.id).pluck(:id)\n    ChatMessage.where(chat_session_id: session_ids).delete_all\n    ChatSession.where(id: session_ids).delete_all\n    user.destroy!\n    puts \"Deep clean complete: User, Sessions, and Messages deleted.\"\n  else\n    puts \"User not found\"\n  end\n  '\n```\n\n#### Issue: Add user as owner after WorkOS setup\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Create owner in primary organization (org_id=1)\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \\\n  bundle exec rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults \\\n  'factory:add_owner[1,admin@example.com]'\n\n# Create owner in secondary organization (org_id=2)\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \\\n  bundle exec rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults \\\n  'factory:add_owner[2,admin@example.com]'\n```\n\n## Debug Commands Reference\n\n:::tip Quick Reference\nQuick reference for common debugging commands\n:::\n\n```bash\n# View pod logs\nkubectl logs <pod-name>\nkubectl logs -f <pod-name>  # Follow logs\nkubectl logs <pod-name> --previous  # View previous container logs\n\n# Describe resources for detailed status\nkubectl describe pod <pod-name>\nkubectl describe deployment <deployment-name>\nkubectl describe svc <service-name>\n\n# Execute commands in pod\nkubectl exec -it <pod-name> -- /bin/bash\nkubectl exec -it <pod-name> -- env  # Check environment variables\n\n# Check resource status\nkubectl get pods\nkubectl get svc\nkubectl get pvc\nkubectl get secrets\n\n# View Helm release status\nhelm status <release-name>\nhelm get values <release-name>  # View current values\n\n# Port forwarding for testing\nkubectl port-forward svc/<service-name> 8080:80\n```\n\n## Support Resources\n\n| Resource | Link |\n|----------|------|\n| GCP Setup Issues | [GCP Setup Guide](gcp-setup) |\n| Installation Issues | [Installation Guide](install) |\n| Third-Party Integrations | [Third-Party Guide](third-party) |\n| kubectl Cheat Sheet | [Kubernetes Docs](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/cheatsheet/) |\n\n### Reporting Issues\n\nWhen reporting issues, include:\n- Complete error messages and stack traces\n- Output of relevant kubectl commands\n- Kubernetes event logs\n- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)\n- Configuration details (without secrets)\n- CrewAI version and deployment environment\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/third-party.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Third-Party Integrations\nsidebar_label: Third-Party Integrations\nsidebar_position: 6\ndescription: Integration guidance for third-party services and platforms with CrewAI\n---\n\n# Third-Party Integrations\n\nThis section covers integration guidance for popular third-party services and platforms with CrewAI installations.\n\n## WorkOS\n\nWorkOS is the authentication provider for CrewAI installations, offering secure enterprise-grade SSO and user management capabilities integrated with Google Workspace authentication.\n\n### Overview\n\n- Enterprise authentication provider for CrewAI using **AuthKit**\n- AuthKit configured to use **Google Auth platform** on the GCP Project hosting CrewAI\n- Sign-in methods:\n  - Google Workspace OIDC\n  - Magic Auth (passwordless email authentication)\n- **WorkOS Connect** provides OIDC middleware for CLI login to CrewAI via WorkOS\n- User and organization management\n- Seamless integration with Kubernetes deployments\n- Supports both Web UI and CLI authentication flows\n\n### Prerequisites\n\n- Active WorkOS account and organization\n- API credentials from WorkOS dashboard\n- AuthKit domain setup (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)\n\n### Configuration\n\n#### CrewAI Configuration Variables\n\nThese environment variables configure how CrewAI connects to WorkOS:\n\n```yaml\n# Authentication Provider Selection\nAUTH_PROVIDER: \"workos\"  # or \"entra_id\", \"google\", \"local\"\n\n# OAuth Configuration\noauth:\n  enabled: true  # Enables OAuth flow for user authentication\nOAUTH_COOKIE_SECRET: \"[REDACTED]\"  # Secure cookie encryption key\n```\n\n#### WorkOS Configuration Variables\n\nThese environment variables configure WorkOS AuthKit integration. See [Install Guide: Step 4](install#step-4-prepare-configuration-file-my-valuesyaml) for detailed setup instructions.\n\n```yaml\n# Public Credentials (safe to commit)\nWORKOS_CLIENT_ID: \"client_XXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX\"  # from WorkOS dashboard\nWORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: \"your-domain.authkit.app\"    # AuthKit domain\n\n# Secrets (MUST be stored in Cloud Secret Manager - NEVER commit)\nWORKOS_API_KEY: \"[REDACTED]\"  # API key from WorkOS dashboard\nWORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD: \"[REDACTED]\"  # 32-character random string\n```\n\n#### WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD Generation\n\nThe `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` must be a secure, randomly generated 32-character string:\n\n```bash\n# Generate using OpenSSL\nopenssl rand -hex 16\n\n# Or using Python\npython -c \"import secrets; print(secrets.token_hex(16))\"\n\n# Or using Node.js\nnode -e \"console.log(require('crypto').randomBytes(16).toString('hex'))\"\n```\n\nExample output: `a3f7d8c2e4b9f1a6c8d3e7b2f9a4c1d5`\n\n### Setup Steps\n\n1. **Create WorkOS Account and Organization** — Register in WorkOS dashboard\n\n2. **Configure AuthKit with Google Auth:**\n   - Set up Google OAuth provider on your GCP Project\n   - Configure AuthKit to authenticate against Google Workspace\n   - Configure sign-in methods: Google Workspace OIDC and Magic Auth\n\n3. **Generate Credentials in WorkOS Dashboard:**\n   - Copy Client ID (public - safe to commit)\n   - Generate API Key (secret - store in Cloud Secret Manager)\n   - Note AuthKit Domain (`your-domain.authkit.app`)\n\n4. **Generate Session Encryption Key:**\n   - Generate a 32-character random string for `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD`\n   - Use command: `openssl rand -hex 16`\n\n5. **Configure WorkOS Connect for CLI Usage:**\n   - Enable WorkOS Connect for OIDC middleware\n   - Configure CLI authentication endpoint\n   - Enables passwordless CLI login via `crew ai login` command\n\n6. **Add Credentials to Kubernetes Values File:**\n   - Public values: `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID`, `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN`, `AUTH_PROVIDER`\n   - Store secrets in Google Cloud Secret Manager (not in values file)\n\n### Current Setup\n\nBased on workspace configuration:\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **Provider** | WorkOS with AuthKit |\n| **Identity Provider** | Google Auth on GCP Project |\n| **Sign-In Methods** | Google Workspace OIDC + Magic Auth |\n| **CLI Access** | WorkOS Connect OIDC middleware enabled |\n| **Authentication Domain** | `your-domain.authkit.app` |\n| **Deployment** | Kubernetes with Helm (GCP) |\n| **Secrets Management** | Google Cloud Secret Manager |\n| **Cookie Security** | Password-protected session cookies (32-character key) |\n| **Web UI** | OAuth-enabled authentication |\n| **CLI** | OIDC-based login via `crew ai login` |\n\n### Common Integrations\n\n- **Web UI**: Single Sign-On (SSO) with Google Workspace OIDC\n- **Web UI**: Passwordless authentication via Magic Auth\n- **CLI**: OIDC-based login for `crew ai` command-line interface\n- **CLI**: Secure token management via WorkOS Connect\n- User and organization provisioning\n- Directory synchronization with Google Workspace\n- Multi-tenant user management\n\n### Security Best Practices\n\n- Never commit `WORKOS_API_KEY` or `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` to version control\n- Store all secrets in Cloud Secret Manager (GCP) or equivalent\n- Rotate API keys regularly\n- Use environment-specific configurations (dev/prod)\n- Enable HTTPS for production deployments\n\n### Testing\n\n- Local testing with AuthKit domain\n- Test SSO flows in development environment\n- Validate user provisioning and organization sync\n- Integration testing with Kubernetes deployment\n\n### Troubleshooting\n\n- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches dashboard configuration\n- Check `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted\n- Ensure `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored and accessible\n- Review Kubernetes secret mounts and volume mappings\n- Check application logs for authentication errors\n\n### Related Documentation\n\n- See [GCP Setup Guide](gcp-setup) for Cloud Secret Manager configuration\n- Refer to [Install Guide](install) for complete Helm values setup\n- WorkOS official documentation: https://workos.com/docs\n\n## Integration Best Practices\n\n### Security\n\n- API key management\n- Credential rotation\n- Scope and permission minimization\n- Audit logging\n\n### Performance\n\n- Rate limiting strategies\n- Caching considerations\n- Async operations\n- Monitoring and alerting\n\n### Reliability\n\n- Error handling patterns\n- Retry logic\n- Fallback mechanisms\n- Health checks\n\n## Monitoring and Maintenance\n\n- Health checks and status monitoring\n- Logging integration events\n- Metrics and analytics\n- Update and version management\n\n## Support and Resources\n\n- [WorkOS Documentation](https://workos.com/docs)\n- [CrewAI Enterprise Documentation](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)\n- Community forums and discussions\n- Support channels: support@konecta.com\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/faq.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Frequently Asked Questions\nsidebar_label: FAQ\nsidebar_position: 7\ndescription: Common questions and answers about CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud\n---\n\n# Frequently Asked Questions\n\n:::info\nThis FAQ covers common questions about installing, configuring, and operating CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud Platform.\n:::\n\n## General Questions\n\n### What is CrewAI Platform?\n\nCrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.\n\n**Key Features:**\n- Multi-agent orchestration and coordination\n- Role-based agent specialization\n- Task delegation and workflow management\n- Enterprise security and compliance\n- GCP-native integration\n\n### What are the system requirements?\n\n**Minimum Requirements for Development:**\n- GKE cluster with 3 nodes (e2-standard-2 or higher)\n- 8 vCPUs and 16 GB RAM total\n- 100 GB persistent storage\n- Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-f1-micro minimum)\n- 2 Cloud Storage buckets\n\n**Recommended Requirements for Production:**\n- GKE cluster with 6+ nodes (e2-standard-4 or higher)\n- 24+ vCPUs and 48+ GB RAM total\n- 500 GB persistent storage\n- Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-n1-standard-2 or higher)\n- Multi-regional Cloud Storage buckets\n- High availability configuration across 2+ regions\n\n### How much does it cost to run CrewAI on GCP?\n\nCosts vary based on environment and usage patterns:\n\n**Development Environment (estimated):**\n| Component | Cost |\n|-----------|------|\n| GKE cluster | $150-200/month |\n| Cloud SQL | $25-50/month |\n| Cloud Storage | $5-15/month |\n| Networking | $10-20/month |\n| **Total** | **~$190-285/month** |\n\n**Production Environment (estimated):**\n| Component | Cost |\n|-----------|------|\n| GKE cluster | $500-800/month |\n| Cloud SQL with HA | $200-400/month |\n| Cloud Storage multi-region | $30-60/month |\n| Load balancing and networking | $50-100/month |\n| Cloud Armor and security | $20-40/month |\n| **Total** | **~$800-1,400/month** |\n\n:::tip\nUse committed use discounts (CUDs) and sustained use discounts to reduce costs by 30-50%.\n:::\n\n## Installation & Setup\n\n### How long does installation take?\n\n**Typical Installation Timeline:**\n- Infrastructure provisioning (Terraform): 15-30 minutes\n- CrewAI Platform Helm deployment: 10-15 minutes\n- Post-installation configuration: 5-10 minutes\n- **Total: 30-55 minutes**\n\nFirst-time installations may take longer due to:\n- API enablement (5-10 minutes)\n- Initial image pulls from registry\n- DNS propagation for custom domains (up to 48 hours, typically 5-10 minutes)\n\n### Can I install CrewAI without using Terraform?\n\nWhile Terraform is the recommended approach for reproducible infrastructure, you can manually provision resources through the GCP Console:\n\n**Manual Setup Steps:**\n1. Create VPC network and subnets\n2. Provision GKE cluster\n3. Create Cloud SQL instance\n4. Create Cloud Storage buckets\n5. Configure IAM roles and service accounts\n6. Deploy CrewAI via Helm\n\n:::warning\nManual setup is **not recommended** for production environments due to:\n- Lack of version control and auditability\n- Higher risk of configuration drift\n- Difficulty reproducing environments\n- Complex rollback procedures\n:::\n\n### What authentication methods are supported?\n\nCrewAI Platform supports multiple authentication providers:\n\n| Provider | Description |\n|----------|-------------|\n| **WorkOS** | Recommended for enterprise SSO |\n| **Microsoft Entra ID** | Formerly Azure AD |\n| **Google Workspace** | Google OIDC authentication |\n| **Local authentication** | Development only |\n\nConfigure the provider in your `my-values.yaml`:\n```yaml\nenvVars:\n  AUTH_PROVIDER: \"workos\"  # or \"entra_id\", \"google\", \"local\"\n```\n\n:::note\nLocal authentication should only be used for development and testing. Production environments must use SSO-based authentication.\n:::\n\n### Do I need a custom domain?\n\n**Development:** Not required - you can use port-forwarding or a temporary LoadBalancer IP\n\n**Production:** **Strongly recommended** for:\n- Professional appearance\n- SSL/TLS certificate management\n- OAuth callback URL configuration\n- Stable endpoint for integrations\n\n**Setup Requirements:**\n1. Domain registered and managed (e.g., via Cloud DNS)\n2. DNS A record pointing to Load Balancer IP\n3. Managed SSL certificate provisioned\n4. Ingress configured with domain name\n\n## Configuration & Customization\n\n### How do I scale my deployment?\n\n**Horizontal Scaling (Recommended):**\n```yaml\n# In my-values.yaml\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 3  # Increase for more capacity\n\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 5  # Scale workers independently\n```\n\n**Auto-scaling with HPA:**\n```yaml\nweb:\n  autoscaling:\n    enabled: true\n    minReplicas: 3\n    maxReplicas: 10\n    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70\n```\n\nAfter updating values:\n```bash\nhelm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --values my-values.yaml\n```\n\n### Can I use an existing database?\n\n**Yes!** CrewAI supports using an external PostgreSQL database:\n\n```yaml\n# Disable bundled PostgreSQL\npostgres:\n  enabled: false\n\n# Configure external database\nenvVars:\n  DB_HOST: \"your-db-instance.example.com\"\n  DB_PORT: \"5432\"\n  DB_USER: \"crewai_user\"\n  DB_NAME: \"crewai_production\"\n\nsecrets:\n  DB_PASSWORD: \"your-secure-password\"\n```\n\n**Requirements:**\n- PostgreSQL 12 or higher\n- Network connectivity from GKE to database\n- Database created with UTF8 encoding\n\n### How do I configure custom storage?\n\nCrewAI uses S3-compatible storage. For GCP, configure Cloud Storage with HMAC keys:\n\n**Step 1: Create HMAC Keys**\n```bash\ngcloud storage hmac create SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL\n```\n\n**Step 2: Configure Storage**\n```yaml\nenvVars:\n  STORAGE_SERVICE: \"amazon\"\n  AWS_ENDPOINT: \"https://storage.googleapis.com\"\n  AWS_REGION: \"europe-west1\"\n  AWS_BUCKET: \"your-project-crewai-data\"\n  LOGS_BUCKET: \"your-project-crewai-logs\"\n\nsecrets:\n  AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID: \"GOOG1E...\"\n  AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY: \"your-secret-key\"\n```\n\n### Can I customize resource limits?\n\n**Yes!** Resource requests and limits can be customized per service:\n\n```yaml\nweb:\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: \"500m\"\n      memory: \"1Gi\"\n    limits:\n      cpu: \"2000m\"\n      memory: \"4Gi\"\n\nworker:\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: \"1000m\"\n      memory: \"2Gi\"\n    limits:\n      cpu: \"4000m\"\n      memory: \"8Gi\"\n```\n\n:::tip\nSet requests to typical usage and limits to peak capacity. Monitor actual usage with `kubectl top pods` and adjust accordingly.\n:::\n\n## Operations & Troubleshooting\n\n### How do I upgrade to a new version?\n\n**Step 1: Backup Configuration**\n```bash\ncp my-values.yaml my-values.yaml.backup.$(date +%Y%m%d)\n```\n\n**Step 2: Check Available Versions**\n```bash\nhelm search repo registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable --versions\n```\n\n**Step 3: Perform Upgrade**\n```bash\nhelm upgrade crewai-platform oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable/crewai-platform \\\n  --version NEW_VERSION \\\n  --values my-values.yaml\n```\n\n**Step 4: Monitor Rollout**\n```bash\nkubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-platform-web\nkubectl get pods -w\n```\n\nSee [Installation Guide - Step 8](install#step-8-upgrade-the-platform) for detailed upgrade instructions.\n\n### How do I back up my data?\n\n**Database Backups:**\n- Cloud SQL automated daily backups (enabled by default)\n- On-demand backup: `gcloud sql backups create --instance=INSTANCE_NAME`\n- Point-in-time recovery (PITR) available within 7 days\n\n**Configuration Backups:**\n```bash\n# Backup Helm values\nkubectl get secret -n default -o yaml > secrets-backup.yaml\n\n# Backup entire namespace\nkubectl get all -n default -o yaml > namespace-backup.yaml\n```\n\n**Storage Backups:**\n- Cloud Storage versioning enabled\n- Cross-region replication configured\n- Lifecycle policies for retention\n\n### What monitoring is available?\n\n**Built-in Monitoring:**\n- Cloud Monitoring dashboards (GKE and Cloud SQL)\n- Cloud Logging for centralized logs\n- Cloud Trace for distributed tracing\n- Uptime checks for availability\n\n**Access Metrics:**\n```bash\n# View pod metrics\nkubectl top pods\n\n# View logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform --tail=100\n\n# Check cluster status\nkubectl get nodes\nkubectl get pods\n```\n\n### How do I troubleshoot pod failures?\n\n**Step 1: Check Pod Status**\n```bash\nkubectl get pods\nkubectl describe pod POD_NAME\n```\n\n**Step 2: View Logs**\n```bash\nkubectl logs POD_NAME\nkubectl logs POD_NAME --previous  # For crashed pods\n```\n\n**Step 3: Check Events**\n```bash\nkubectl get events --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n```\n\n**Common Issues:**\n| Issue | Cause |\n|-------|-------|\n| **ImagePullBackOff** | Registry authentication failed |\n| **CrashLoopBackOff** | Application error - check logs |\n| **Pending** | Insufficient cluster resources |\n| **OOMKilled** | Out of memory - increase limits |\n\nSee [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) for detailed solutions.\n\n## Security & Compliance\n\n### Is CrewAI Platform secure?\n\n**Security Features:**\n- End-to-end encryption (TLS 1.2+)\n- Encryption at rest (Cloud SQL, Cloud Storage)\n- IAM-based access control\n- Network isolation via VPC\n- Cloud Armor DDoS protection\n- Identity-Aware Proxy (IAP) support\n- Regular security updates\n- Audit logging enabled\n\n**Best Practices:**\n- Use Workload Identity for service account authentication\n- Store secrets in Cloud Secret Manager\n- Enable binary authorization for container images\n- Implement least-privilege IAM roles\n- Regular vulnerability scanning\n\n### How do I manage secrets securely?\n\n**Recommended Approach: Cloud Secret Manager**\n\n**Step 1: Store Secret**\n```bash\necho -n \"your-secret-value\" | gcloud secrets create SECRET_NAME --data-file=-\n```\n\n**Step 2: Grant Access**\n```bash\ngcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding SECRET_NAME \\\n  --member=\"serviceAccount:SA_EMAIL\" \\\n  --role=\"roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor\"\n```\n\n:::warning\n**Never commit secrets to version control!** Always use Secret Manager or external secret management solutions.\n:::\n\n### Is CrewAI compliant with regulations?\n\nCrewAI Platform can be configured to meet various compliance requirements:\n\n**Supported Compliance Frameworks:**\n- **SOC 2 Type II**: With proper GCP organization policies\n- **GDPR**: Data residency and privacy controls available\n- **HIPAA**: Available with Business Associate Agreement (BAA)\n- **ISO 27001**: GCP infrastructure is certified\n\n**Compliance Configuration:**\n- Data residency: Deploy in specific GCP regions\n- Audit logging: Enabled by default\n- Data encryption: At rest and in transit\n- Access controls: IAM and RBAC\n\n:::note\nCompliance is a shared responsibility. While GCP and CrewAI provide compliant infrastructure, your implementation and operational practices must also meet regulatory requirements.\n:::\n\n## Integration & Development\n\n### How do I integrate with external services?\n\nCrewAI supports integrations through:\n\n**1. OAuth Configuration**\n```yaml\noauth:\n  enabled: true\n\nsecrets:\n  OAUTH_DB_HOST: \"10.29.0.2\"\n```\n\n**2. API Access**\n- RESTful API for programmatic access\n- Webhook support for event-driven workflows\n- SDK support (Python, Node.js)\n\n**3. Third-Party Integrations**\n- Vertex AI for ML models\n- 11Labs for voice synthesis\n- Custom integrations via API\n\nSee [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) for detailed guides.\n\n### Can I use custom AI models?\n\n**Yes!** CrewAI supports custom model integration:\n\n**Vertex AI Models:**\n- Deploy custom models to Vertex AI\n- Configure endpoint URLs in CrewAI\n- Use Workload Identity for authentication\n\n**External Models:**\n- OpenAI API\n- Anthropic Claude API\n- Custom inference endpoints\n\n### How do I access the API?\n\n**1. Port Forwarding (Development)**\n```bash\nkubectl port-forward svc/crewai-platform-web 8080:80\n# Access at http://localhost:8080\n```\n\n**2. LoadBalancer (Production)**\n```bash\nkubectl get svc crewai-platform-web\n# Access at LoadBalancer IP or configured domain\n```\n\n**3. API Authentication**\n- Obtain API key from CrewAI admin console\n- Include in request headers: `Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY`\n\n**Example API Call:**\n```bash\ncurl -X GET \"https://crewai.example.com/api/v1/agents\" \\\n  -H \"Authorization: Bearer YOUR_API_KEY\"\n```\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n| Resource | Link |\n|----------|------|\n| Installation Guide | [Installation Guide](install) |\n| Troubleshooting | [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) |\n| GCP Setup Guide | [GCP Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) |\n| Post-Installation | [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) |\n\n:::note Can't find your question?\nContact support at support@konecta.com or check the [CrewAI Enterprise Documentation](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com).\n:::\n"
}
```

---

## KIX-SERVICES-CREWAI Repository Exploration Summary

I've completed a thorough exploration of the **kix-services-crewai** repository, which manages CrewAI Enterprise deployment across three GCP environments. Here's a comprehensive breakdown of the deployment model:

---

## 1. REPOSITORY STRUCTURE

```
kix-services-crewai/
├── environments/
│   ├── dev/
│   │   ├── argocd/apps/
│   │   │   └── application.yaml          # Multi-source ArgoCD Application
│   │   ├── helm/crewai/
│   │   │   ├── values.yaml               # Gateway API & health check config
│   │   │   └── values-gke.yaml           # GKE-specific configuration
│   │   └── terraform/
│   │       ├── main.tf                   # Providers & data sources
│   │       ├── service-accounts.tf       # GCP SA & IAM bindings
│   │       ├── kubernetes.tf             # K8s namespaces, quotas, policies
│   │       ├── secrets.tf                # Secret Manager integration
│   │       ├── storage.tf                # GCS buckets & HMAC keys
│   │       ├── database.tf               # Cloud SQL configuration
│   │       ├── artifact-registry.tf      # Container image registries
│   │       ├── gateway-certificate.tf    # TLS certificates
│   │       └── backend.tf                # Remote state in GCS
│   ├── qa/                               # Same structure as dev
│   └── prod/                             # Same structure as dev
├── .github/workflows/                    # CI/CD pipelines
│   ├── validate-plan-apply.yml           # Main deployment workflow
│   ├── terraform-validate.yml            # Reusable validation
│   ├── terraform-plan.yml                # Reusable plan generation
│   ├── terraform-apply.yml               # Reusable apply execution
│   ├── terraform-drift.yml               # Drift detection
│   ├── nightly-quality.yml               # Scheduled quality checks
│   └── manual-validate-plan.yml          # Manual validation
├── docs/
│   ├── INDEX.md, SETUP.md, CICD.md
│   ├── runbooks/, changelog/, prps/
│   └── tasks/
├── CLAUDE.md                             # Repository standards
└── README.md                             # Main documentation
```

---

## 2. DEPLOYMENT MODEL ARCHITECTURE

### Multi-Layer Approach

The deployment uses three distinct layers working in concert:

#### **Layer 1: Infrastructure (Terraform)**
- **Per-environment Terraform configs** in `environments/{env}/terraform/`
- **Composes remote modules** from [kix-platform-modules](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules) repository
- **957 lines of HCL** distributed across 13 modular .tf files
- **State management**: Per-environment GCS buckets (`kd-tfstate-{env}`) with prefix `services/crewai/resources`

**Remote Modules Used:**
- `data-services/gcs` - GCS buckets for data + logs storage
- `secret-manager` - GCP Secret Manager secrets (single-secret-per-call pattern)
- `gke-config` - Kubernetes namespaces, resource quotas, network policies

**Inline Resources (not modularized):**
- Cloud SQL databases & users on existing platform instances
- GCP service accounts (inline to avoid circular dependencies with HMAC keys)
- HMAC keys for S3-compatible GCS access
- IAM bindings (Cloud SQL, GCS, Secret Manager, Workload Identity)
- Artifact Registry repositories
- Kubernetes secrets (populated from Secret Manager)
- Random passwords for databases and encryption keys

#### **Layer 2: Application Delivery (ArgoCD)**
- **Multi-source Applications** using ArgoCD's `sources` feature (3 sources per app):
  1. **OCI Helm Chart**: `oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable` (external CrewAI chart)
  2. **Local Helm Chart**: Git path for Gateway API, ESO, and policies
  3. **Git Values**: Separate repo source for Helm values referencing via `$values`

- **Auto-sync enabled** for dev/qa (production has manual sync for safety)
- **Prune + self-heal** enabled for drift correction
- **Ignores replicas** (managed by HPA) and secrets (managed by Terraform)

#### **Layer 3: Secrets Management (Dual System)**
- **GCP Secret Manager** (infrastructure layer - Terraform-managed):
  - Stores credentials securely
  - Database credentials (though IAM auth preferred)
  - GCS HMAC keys
  - OAuth configuration

- **Kubernetes Secrets** (application layer - Terraform-created):
  - Created by Terraform in the `crewai-{env}` namespace
  - Populated from Secret Manager values
  - Consumed by pods via `envFrom: secretRef`
  - Secret names: `crewai-credentials`, `crewai-database-config`, etc.

---

## 3. ENVIRONMENT CONFIGURATIONS

### Development
- **GKE Cluster**: `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke`
- **Domain**: `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com`
- **Replicas**: 1 for web/worker pods
- **CPU/Memory**: 1000m/2Gi requests, 2000m/4Gi limits
- **Auto-sync**: Enabled (immediate deployment)
- **Branch**: `develop` (controlled via workflow validation)

### QA
- **GKE Cluster**: `kd-ix-eur-qa-gke`
- **Domain**: `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com`
- **Replicas**: 2 for web/worker pods
- **CPU/Memory**: 1500m/4Gi requests, 3000m/8Gi limits
- **Auto-sync**: Enabled
- **Branch**: `main` (controlled via workflow validation)

### Production
- **GKE Cluster**: `kd-ix-eur-prod-gke`
- **Domain**: `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com`
- **Replicas**: 3+ with HPA (max 10)
- **CPU/Memory**: 2000m/6Gi requests, 4000m/12Gi limits
- **Pod Disruption Budget**: Min 2 available
- **Auto-sync**: Manual (safety control)
- **Deployment**: Git tags (`v*`) only, change window enforced Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC

---

## 4. CI/CD PIPELINE (GitHub Actions)

### Workflow Orchestration

**4 Active Workflows + 7 Reusable Workflows:**

| Workflow | Trigger | Purpose |
|----------|---------|---------|
| **validate-plan-apply** | Manual dispatch (`workflow_dispatch`) | Full pipeline: validate → plan → approval gate → apply |
| **manual-validate-plan** | Manual or called by nightly | Validate + plan only (no apply) |
| **nightly-quality** | Daily at 02:00 UTC | Validates develop→dev, main→qa, drift detection |
| **terraform-drift** | Called by nightly | Detects infrastructure drift, creates issues |

### Reusable Workflows (Called as Templates)

- **terraform-validate.yml**: Format check → init → validate → Trivy security scan
- **terraform-plan.yml**: Plan generation with statistics extraction
- **terraform-apply.yml**: Apply execution with pre/post validation
- **terraform-drift.yml**: Drift detection with GitHub issue notification

### Branch-Environment Protection Rules

```
Branch      → Allowed Environments
develop     → dev only
main        → dev, qa only
v* tags     → prod only
```

### Deployment Flow

```
PR to main/develop
        ↓
[Auto Validate on Changes]
  - Detects changed terraform/**
  - Runs fmt check, validate, Trivy scan
        ↓
Merge to branch
        ↓
[Manual Dispatch]
  - Choose environment (dev/qa/prod)
  - Branch validation (must match environment rules)
  - Change window check (prod only: Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC)
        ↓
[Validate] → [Plan] → [Review Gate] → [Apply]
        ↓
[Summary] - Post deployment report
```

### Security Scanning

- **Trivy IaC scanning** on all terraform directories
- **Severity levels**: CRITICAL, HIGH, MEDIUM, LOW
- **CRITICAL/HIGH findings** require manual review before proceeding
- **SARIF upload** to GitHub Code Scanning
- **Artifact retention**: 5 days

### Approval Gates

Three GitHub Environments with protection rules:
- `dev-approval` - Before dev apply
- `qa-approval` - Before qa apply
- `prod-approval` - Before prod apply (most restrictive)

---

## 5. TERRAFORM PATTERNS & DESIGN DECISIONS

### Module Composition Strategy

**Key Principle**: Compose remote modules for platform capabilities, keep service-specific resources inline.

**Remote Modules** (from kix-platform-modules `?ref=main`):
```hcl
module "gcs_data" {
  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
  # Provides bucket with versioning, lifecycle policies
}

module "secret_gcs_credentials" {
  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
  # Single-secret-per-call pattern (not multi-credential)
}
```

**Inline Resources** (service-specific):
```hcl
resource "google_sql_database" "crewai_production" {
  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
  name     = "crewai_plus_production"
}

resource "google_service_account" "crewai_platform" {
  # Inline to allow same-apply dependency with HMAC keys
}

resource "google_storage_hmac_key" "crewai" {
  service_account_email = google_service_account.crewai_platform.email
  # HMAC keys reference SA created in same apply
}
```

### Secret Management Architecture

**Dual-System Approach**:
1. **Terraform creates in Secret Manager**: GCS HMAC keys, database passwords
2. **Terraform creates K8s Secrets**: Synced from Secret Manager values
3. **Pods consume via envFrom**: `envFrom: - secretRef: name: crewai-credentials`

**Key Design Decision**: Terraform manages K8s service accounts (via gke-config module), so Helm values set `serviceAccount.create: false` to avoid conflict.

### Kubernetes Networking

- **Dual namespaces per environment**:
  - `crewai-{env}` - Platform services (web, worker, scheduler)
  - `crewai-crews-{env}` - Crew workload execution (isolated)

- **Network policies**: Default-deny-ingress with GCP health check IPs allowed
- **Resource quotas**: Per-namespace limits on CPU/memory
- **Pod Security Standards**: `baseline` mode (required for BuildKit capabilities)

---

## 6. HELM CHART CONFIGURATION

### Multi-Source Pattern

ArgoCD pulls from three sources:

**Source 1 - External CrewAI Chart (OCI)**:
```yaml
repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
chart: crewai-platform
targetRevision: 0.3.13  # Can be updated for version upgrades
```

**Source 2 - Local Gateway/ESO Helm Chart**:
```yaml
repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
path: environments/{env}/helm/crewai
```

**Source 3 - Values from Git**:
```yaml
repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
ref: values  # Special git ref for values-only source
```

### Helm Values Configuration

**Gateway API Setup** (from values.yaml):
- GKE L7 Global External Load Balancer configuration
- HTTPS listener with Certificate Manager
- HTTPRoute for traffic routing
- HTTP-to-HTTPS redirect
- GCPBackendPolicy for health checks & logging

**Application Values** (environment-specific):
- Replica counts (1/2/3 for dev/qa/prod)
- Resource requests/limits
- Pod security contexts
- Health probe configuration
- Service account configuration (`create: false`)

---

## 7. TERRAFORM PROVIDER CONFIGURATION

```hcl
# Required Versions
Terraform >= 1.5.0
Google ~> 5.0
Kubernetes ~> 2.23
Random ~> 3.5

# Multi-Project Setup
provider "google":
  project = var.gke_project_id       # k8s cluster project
  region  = var.region               # europe-west1

data "google_container_cluster" "gke":
  # Explicit GKE authentication (not reliant on KUBECONFIG)
  # Uses google client config token for k8s provider

# Kubernetes Provider
provider "kubernetes":
  host = "https://${data.google_container_cluster.gke.endpoint}"
  token = data.google_client_config.default.access_token
  cluster_ca_certificate = base64decode(cluster.master_auth.cluster_ca_certificate)
```

---

## 8. GITHUB SECRETS & ENVIRONMENT CONFIGURATION

### Repository-Level Secrets (All Environments)

| Secret | Usage | Description |
|--------|-------|-------------|
| `GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` | Terraform workflows | Workload Identity Federation provider |
| `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` | Terraform workflows | GitHub Actions service account email |
| `TERRAFORM_MODULES_APP_ID` | Module access | GitHub App ID for kix-platform-modules |
| `TERRAFORM_MODULES_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` | Module access | GitHub App PEM key for private modules |

### Terraform Variables (Sensitive - Not Yet Wired)

These need `TF_VAR_*` environment variables in workflows:
- `TF_VAR_oauth_client_id` - WorkOS OAuth
- `TF_VAR_oauth_api_key` - WorkOS OAuth
- `TF_VAR_oauth_domain` - WorkOS OAuth
- `TF_VAR_helm_registry_token` - CrewAI registry auth

**Status**: Currently not integrated into GitHub Actions workflows (noted in README as TODO).

---

## 9. KEY DESIGN PATTERNS & LESSONS LEARNED

### Patterns

1. **Single-Secret-Per-Call**: Secret Manager module uses individual calls per secret (vs. multi-credential pattern)
2. **Inline SA Creation**: Service accounts created inline to enable same-apply HMAC key dependencies
3. **Helm Values Via Git**: Uses ArgoCD's `$values` reference for clean separation of chart from values
4. **Multi-Source Applications**: Combines external OCI chart + local gateway/ESO helm + git values

### Mistakes & Corrections

From CLAUDE.md "Mistakes Learned":

1. **Secret-Manager Module Interface Mismatch**
   - Different interface on `feature/crewai-platform` branch vs `main`
   - **Fix**: Always use `?ref=main` and single-secret-per-call pattern

2. **Dual Service Account Creation**
   - Both Helm and Terraform can create same K8s SA
   - **Fix**: Set `serviceAccount.create: false` in Helm values

3. **Pod Security Standards vs BuildKit**
   - BuildKit needs Linux capabilities blocked by `restricted` PSS
   - **Fix**: Use `baseline` PSS level for all environments

---

## 10. DEPLOYMENT WORKFLOW SUMMARY

```
Developer Work
  ↓
PR to main/develop (optional Terraform changes)
  ↓
[Auto Validate + Plan] (if terraform/* changed)
  ├─ Format check
  ├─ Init (no backend)
  ├─ Validate
  └─ Trivy security scan
  ↓
Merge to develop/main
  ↓
Manual Dispatch: validate-plan-apply
  ├─ Input: environment (dev/qa/prod)
  ├─ Check branch-environment rules
  ├─ Check prod change window
  └─ Proceed to pipeline
  ↓
[Validate] → [Plan] → [Approval Gate] → [Apply]
  ├─ Terraform init (with backend)
  ├─ Terraform plan
  ├─ GitHub Environment approval required
  └─ Terraform apply
  ↓
[Post-Deployment Summary]
  ├─ Resource changes (add/change/destroy counts)
  ├─ Job status (validate/plan/review/apply)
  └─ Notification to GitHub

Nightly (02:00 UTC)
  ├─ Validate develop→dev (plan only)
  ├─ Validate main→qa (plan only)
  └─ Drift detection (all 3 environments)
     └─ Create GitHub issues on drift found
```

---

## SUMMARY

The **kix-services-crewai** repository implements a sophisticated, production-grade GitOps deployment model featuring:

- **Infrastructure as Code**: Terraform composing remote platform modules with environment-specific customization
- **GitOps Application Delivery**: ArgoCD multi-source applications pulling external OCI charts + local gateway config
- **Comprehensive CI/CD**: GitHub Actions with automated validation, security scanning, approval gates, and drift detection
- **Multi-Environment Safety**: Branch protection rules, change windows, and approval gates enforce safe promotion across dev→qa→prod
- **Secret Management**: Dual-layer approach using GCP Secret Manager + Kubernetes secrets with Workload Identity for secure access
- **Infrastructure Validation**: Nightly quality checks including drift detection and issue creation

This model provides strong separation of concerns (infrastructure, application delivery, secrets) while maintaining tight integration through Git-driven workflows and approval gates.

     7→
     8→# CrewAI Enterprise Platform
     9→
    10→This section provides comprehensive documentation for deploying and operating the **CrewAI Enterprise Platform** on Google Cloud Platform (GCP).
    11→
    12→## Overview
    13→
    14→CrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.
    15→
    16→## Documentation Structure
    17→
    18→| Document | Description |
    19→|----------|-------------|
    20→| [GCP Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) | Production-grade Terraform IaC blueprint for GCP infrastructure |
    21→| [Installation Guide](install) | Step-by-step CrewAI Platform installation using Helm |
    22→| [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) | Initial configuration and access setup after deployment |
    23→| [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) | Solutions for common issues during installation and operation |
    24→| [Third-Party Integrations](third-party) | Integration guides for WorkOS and other services |
    25→| [FAQ](faq) | Frequently asked questions about CrewAI Platform |
    26→
    27→## Quick Start
    28→
    29→### Prerequisites
    30→
    31→Before deploying CrewAI, ensure you have:
    32→
    33→- **GCP Organization** with billing enabled
    34→- **GKE Cluster** with sufficient resources
    35→- **Cloud SQL** PostgreSQL instance
    36→- **Cloud Storage** buckets for data and logs
    37→- **CrewAI Registry** credentials from the enterprise portal
    38→
    39→### Deployment Workflow
    40→
    41→1. **Provision Infrastructure** — Follow the [GCP Infrastructure Setup](gcp-setup) guide to deploy the required GCP resources using Terraform
    42→2. **Install CrewAI** — Use the [Installation Guide](install) to deploy CrewAI Platform via Helm
    43→3. **Configure Access** — Complete the [Post-Installation Setup](post-install) to initialize the platform and configure authentication
    44→4. **Verify Deployment** — Run verification steps and troubleshoot any issues using the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting)
    45→
    46→## Key Features
    47→
    48→- **Multi-agent Orchestration** — Coordinate multiple AI agents working together
    49→- **Role-based Specialization** — Define agent roles and responsibilities
    50→- **Task Delegation** — Automatic workflow management and task assignment
    51→- **Enterprise Security** — SSO, encryption, IAM, and compliance controls
    52→- **GCP-native Integration** — Optimized for Google Cloud Platform
    53→
    54→## Architecture Overview
    55→
    56→```
    57→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    58→│                    GCP Organization                              │
    59→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    60→│                                                                   │
    61→│  ┌──────────────────┐        ┌──────────────────────────────┐   │
    62→│  │  Folder Hierarchy│        │  GCP Project Infrastructure  │   │
    63→│  │  (Org Structure) │        │  ├─ APIs (50+)              │   │
    64→│  │  ├─ Root         │        │  ├─ Service Accounts        │   │
    65→│  │  ├─ Env          │        │  ├─ IAM Roles               │   │
    66→│  │  └─ Region       │        │  └─ Billing & Budget        │   │
    67→│  └──────────────────┘        └──────────────────────────────┘   │
    68→│           │                            │                         │
    69→│           └─────────────┬──────────────┘                         │
    70→│                         │                                        │
    71→│        ┌────────────────┴───────────────┐                       │
    72→│        │  CrewAI Platform Infrastructure │                       │
    73→│        │                                │                       │
    74→│  ┌─────┴──────────┐  ┌────────────────┐│ ┌──────────────────┐  │
    75→│  │   Networking   │  │   Storage      ││ │   Kubernetes     │  │
    76→│  │   ├─ VPC       │  │   ├─ Data      ││ │   ├─ Cluster     │  │
    77→│  │   ├─ Subnets   │  │   ├─ Logs      ││ │   ├─ Namespaces  │  │
    78→│  │   └─ NAT       │  │   └─ Backups   ││ │   └─ Add-ons     │  │
    79→│  └────────────────┘  └────────────────┘│ └──────────────────┘  │
    80→│                                ┌───────┴──────────┐             │
    81→│                                │  Database        │             │
    82→│                                │  ├─ Primary DB   │             │
    83→│                                │  ├─ Replicas     │             │
    84→│                                │  └─ Backups      │             │
    85→│                                └──────────────────┘             │
    86→│                                                                  │
    87→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    88→```
    89→
    90→## Support
    91→
    92→- **Internal Support**: Platform Engineering team via Slack #platform-engineering
    93→- **CrewAI Enterprise Portal**: [https://enterprise.crewai.com](https://enterprise.crewai.com)
    94→- **Official Documentation**: [https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com)
    95→
    96→---
    97→
    98→**Contact**: nathan.booth@konecta.com, mischa.vanoijen@konecta.com
    99→

    50→Before deploying CrewAI, ensure you have:
    51→
    52→- Access to the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository
    53→- GitHub Actions permissions to run workflows
    54→- GCP IAM access to the target environment project
    55→- **CrewAI Registry** credentials from the enterprise portal (stored in GCP Secret Manager)
    56→
    57→### Deployment Workflow
    58→
    59→```
    60→PR to main/develop
    61→        ↓
    62→[Auto Validate on Changes]
    63→  - Terraform fmt, validate, Trivy scan
    64→        ↓
    65→Merge to branch
    66→        ↓
    67→[Manual Dispatch: validate-plan-apply]
    68→  - Choose environment (dev/qa/prod)
    69→  - Branch validation enforced
    70→  - Change window check (prod only)
    71→        ↓
    72→[Validate] → [Plan] → [Approval Gate] → [Apply]
    73→        ↓
    74→ArgoCD syncs application automatically
    75→```
    76→
    77→1. **Make Changes** — Update Terraform or Helm values in `kix-services-crewai`
    78→2. **Create PR** — Changes are auto-validated on PR
    79→3. **Merge** — Merge to `develop` (dev) or `main` (qa)
    80→4. **Deploy** — Run the `validate-plan-apply` workflow in GitHub Actions
    81→5. **Verify** — ArgoCD syncs the application; verify in ArgoCD dashboard
    82→
    83→## Key Features
    84→
    85→- **Multi-agent Orchestration** — Coordinate multiple AI agents working together
    86→- **Role-based Specialization** — Define agent roles and responsibilities
    87→- **Task Delegation** — Automatic workflow management and task assignment
    88→- **Enterprise Security** — SSO (WorkOS), encryption, IAM, and compliance controls
    89→- **GCP-native Integration** — Optimized for Google Cloud Platform
    90→- **GitOps Deployment** — Infrastructure and application changes through Git
    91→
    92→## Architecture Overview
    93→
    94→```
    95→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    96→│                         kix-services-crewai Repository                       │
    97→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    98→│                                                                               │
    99→│  ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐    │
   100→│  │  environments/{dev,qa,prod}/                                         │    │
   101→│  │  ├── terraform/          # Infrastructure as Code                   │    │
   102→│  │  │   ├── service-accounts.tf   # GCP SA + IAM bindings             │    │
   103→│  │  │   ├── kubernetes.tf         # Namespaces, quotas, policies      │    │
   104→│  │  │   ├── secrets.tf            # Secret Manager integration        │    │
   105→│  │  │   ├── storage.tf            # GCS buckets + HMAC keys           │    │
   106→│  │  │   └── database.tf           # Cloud SQL databases               │    │
   107→│  │  ├── argocd/apps/        # ArgoCD Application manifests            │    │
   108→│  │  │   └── application.yaml      # Multi-source ArgoCD app           │    │
   109→│  │  └── helm/crewai/        # Helm values for Gateway API, ESO        │    │
   110→│  │      └── values.yaml           # GKE L7 LB, health checks          │    │
   111→│  └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘    │
   112→│                                                                               │
   113→│  ┌─────────────────────┐    ┌─────────────────────┐                         │
   114→│  │  GitHub Actions     │    │  ArgoCD             │                         │
   115→│  │  ├─ validate        │───▶│  Multi-source app:  │                         │
   116→│  │  ├─ plan            │    │  1. OCI Helm chart  │                         │
   117→│  │  ├─ approval gate   │    │  2. Local gateway   │                         │
   118→│  │  └─ apply           │    │  3. Git values      │                         │
   119→│  └─────────────────────┘    └─────────────────────┘                         │
   120→│                                                                               │
   121→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   122→                                      │
   123→                                      ▼
   124→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   125→│                           GCP Platform (per environment)                     │
   126→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
   127→│                                                                               │
   128→│  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐  ┌──────────────────┐          │
   129→│  │  GKE Cluster     │  │  Cloud SQL       │  │  Secret Manager  │          │
   130→│  │  ├─ crewai-{env} │  │  ├─ crewai_db    │  │  ├─ DB creds     │          │
   131→│  │  │   namespace   │  │  └─ crewai_oauth │  │  ├─ HMAC keys    │          │
   132→│  │  ├─ crewai-crews │  └──────────────────┘  │  └─ OAuth config │          │
   133→│  │  │   namespace   │                        └──────────────────┘          │
   134→│  │  └─ Gateway API  │  ┌──────────────────┐                                │
   135→│  │      L7 LB       │  │  GCS Buckets     │                                │
   136→│  └──────────────────┘  │  ├─ data bucket  │                                │
   137→│                        │  └─ logs bucket  │                                │
   138→│                        └──────────────────┘                                │
   139→│                                                                               │
   140→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
   141→```
   142→
   143→## Repository Links

  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/gcp-setup.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Infrastructure Setup\nsidebar_label: Infrastructure Setup\nsidebar_position: 2\ndescription: Terraform infrastructure configuration for CrewAI in kix-services-crewai\n---\n\n# CrewAI Infrastructure Setup\n\nCrewAI infrastructure is managed through Terraform in the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository. This guide explains the infrastructure components and how they're configured.\n\n## Repository Structure\n\n```\nkix-services-crewai/\n├── environments/\n│   ├── dev/\n│   │   ├── terraform/           # Infrastructure as Code\n│   │   │   ├── main.tf              # Providers & data sources\n│   │   │   ├── service-accounts.tf  # GCP SA & IAM bindings\n│   │   │   ├── kubernetes.tf        # K8s namespaces, quotas, policies\n│   │   │   ├── secrets.tf           # Secret Manager integration\n│   │   │   ├── storage.tf           # GCS buckets & HMAC keys\n│   │   │   ├── database.tf          # Cloud SQL configuration\n│   │   │   ├── artifact-registry.tf # Container image registries\n│   │   │   ├── gateway-certificate.tf # TLS certificates\n│   │   │   └── backend.tf           # Remote state in GCS\n│   │   ├── argocd/apps/\n│   │   │   └── application.yaml # ArgoCD Application manifest\n│   │   └── helm/crewai/\n│   │       ├── values.yaml      # Gateway API & health check config\n│   │       └── values-gke.yaml  # GKE-specific configuration\n│   ├── qa/                      # Same structure as dev\n│   └── prod/                    # Same structure as dev\n└── .github/workflows/           # CI/CD pipelines\n```\n\n## Infrastructure Components\n\n### What Terraform Creates\n\n| Component | Resource | Description |\n|-----------|----------|-------------|\n| **Service Accounts** | `google_service_account` | GCP SA for CrewAI workloads with Workload Identity |\n| **IAM Bindings** | `google_*_iam_member` | Cloud SQL, GCS, Secret Manager, Workload Identity |\n| **Namespaces** | `kubernetes_namespace` | `crewai-{env}` for platform, `crewai-crews-{env}` for workloads |\n| **Network Policies** | `kubernetes_network_policy` | Default-deny with GCP health check IPs allowed |\n| **Resource Quotas** | `kubernetes_resource_quota` | Per-namespace CPU/memory limits |\n| **GCS Buckets** | `google_storage_bucket` | Data bucket + logs bucket with versioning |\n| **HMAC Keys** | `google_storage_hmac_key` | S3-compatible access for CrewAI |\n| **Cloud SQL DB** | `google_sql_database` | Database on platform Cloud SQL instance |\n| **Cloud SQL User** | `google_sql_user` | Service account-based IAM authentication |\n| **Secrets** | `google_secret_manager_secret` | DB credentials, HMAC keys, OAuth config |\n| **K8s Secrets** | `kubernetes_secret` | Populated from Secret Manager for pod consumption |\n| **TLS Certificates** | `google_certificate_manager_*` | Managed SSL certificates for Gateway API |\n\n### Remote Modules Used\n\nTerraform composes modules from [`kix-platform-modules`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules):\n\n```hcl\n# GCS bucket with versioning and lifecycle policies\nmodule \"gcs_data\" {\n  source = \"github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main\"\n\n  project_id  = var.gke_project_id\n  bucket_name = \"${var.gke_project_id}-crewai-data\"\n  location    = var.region\n  # ...\n}\n\n# Single-secret-per-call pattern for Secret Manager\nmodule \"secret_db_password\" {\n  source = \"github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main\"\n\n  project_id  = var.gke_project_id\n  secret_id   = \"crewai-db-password\"\n  secret_data = random_password.db.result\n}\n\n# Kubernetes namespace with policies\nmodule \"gke_config\" {\n  source = \"github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/gke-config?ref=main\"\n\n  namespace_name = \"crewai-${var.environment}\"\n  # ...\n}\n```\n\n## Environment Configuration\n\n### Development\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` |\n| **Domain** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **Namespace** | `crewai-dev` |\n| **Replicas** | 1 web, 1 worker |\n| **Resources** | 1000m/2Gi requests, 2000m/4Gi limits |\n| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-dev` |\n| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n### QA\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-qa-gke` |\n| **Domain** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **Namespace** | `crewai-qa` |\n| **Replicas** | 2 web, 2 worker |\n| **Resources** | 1500m/4Gi requests, 3000m/8Gi limits |\n| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-qa` |\n| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n### Production\n\n| Setting | Value |\n|---------|-------|\n| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-prod-gke` |\n| **Domain** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **Namespace** | `crewai-prod` |\n| **Replicas** | 3+ web, 3+ worker (HPA max 10) |\n| **Resources** | 2000m/6Gi requests, 4000m/12Gi limits |\n| **PDB** | Min 2 available |\n| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-prod` |\n| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n## Secrets Management\n\nCrewAI uses a dual-layer secrets architecture:\n\n### Layer 1: GCP Secret Manager (Terraform-managed)\n\nTerraform creates secrets in GCP Secret Manager:\n\n```hcl\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret\" \"db_password\" {\n  secret_id = \"crewai-db-password-${var.environment}\"\n\n  replication {\n    auto {}\n  }\n}\n\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret_version\" \"db_password\" {\n  secret=[REDACTED].db_password.id\n  secret_data = random_password.db.result\n}\n```\n\n### Layer 2: Kubernetes Secrets (Terraform-created)\n\nTerraform creates K8s secrets populated from Secret Manager:\n\n```hcl\nresource \"kubernetes_secret\" \"crewai_credentials\" {\n  metadata {\n    name      = \"crewai-credentials\"\n    namespace = \"crewai-${var.environment}\"\n  }\n\n  data = {\n    AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id\n    AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret\n    DB_PASSWORD           = random_password.db.result\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Secrets Created\n\n| Secret Name | Contents | Used By |\n|-------------|----------|---------|\n| `crewai-credentials` | HMAC keys, DB password | Web, Worker pods |\n| `crewai-database-config` | DB host, port, name, user | Web, Worker pods |\n| `crewai-registry` | CrewAI OCI registry auth | BuildKit, image pulls |\n\n## Kubernetes Resources\n\n### Namespaces\n\nTwo namespaces per environment for isolation:\n\n| Namespace | Purpose |\n|-----------|---------|\n| `crewai-{env}` | Platform services (web, worker, scheduler) |\n| `crewai-crews-{env}` | Crew workload execution (isolated) |\n\n### Network Policies\n\nDefault-deny-ingress with specific allowances:\n\n```yaml\n# GCP health check IPs (mandatory for load balancer)\ningress:\n  - from:\n      - ipBlock:\n          cidr: 35.191.0.0/16\n      - ipBlock:\n          cidr: 130.211.0.0/22\n    ports:\n      - port: 8080\n        protocol: TCP\n```\n\n### Resource Quotas\n\nPer-namespace limits prevent resource exhaustion:\n\n```yaml\nspec:\n  hard:\n    requests.cpu: \"10\"\n    requests.memory: \"20Gi\"\n    limits.cpu: \"20\"\n    limits.memory: \"40Gi\"\n    pods: \"50\"\n```\n\n### Pod Security Standards\n\nCrewAI uses `baseline` Pod Security Standards (not `restricted`) to support BuildKit's Linux capabilities requirements.\n\n## Gateway API Configuration\n\nCrewAI uses GKE Gateway API (not Ingress) for traffic management:\n\n```yaml\n# Gateway (GKE L7 Global External Load Balancer)\napiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: Gateway\nmetadata:\n  name: crewai-gateway\nspec:\n  gatewayClassName: gke-l7-global-external-managed\n  listeners:\n    - name: https\n      protocol: HTTPS\n      port: 443\n      tls:\n        mode: Terminate\n        certificateRefs:\n          - name: crewai-certificate\n\n# HTTPRoute for traffic routing\napiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1\nkind: HTTPRoute\nmetadata:\n  name: crewai-route\nspec:\n  parentRefs:\n    - name: crewai-gateway\n  rules:\n    - backendRefs:\n        - name: crewai-web\n          port: 80\n```\n\n## Making Infrastructure Changes\n\n### 1. Clone the Repository\n\n```bash\ngit clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git\ncd kix-services-crewai\n```\n\n### 2. Make Changes\n\nEdit the appropriate files in `environments/{env}/terraform/`:\n\n```bash\n# Example: Update resource quotas for dev\nvim environments/dev/terraform/kubernetes.tf\n```\n\n### 3. Create a Pull Request\n\n```bash\ngit checkout -b feature/update-quotas\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"feat: increase dev resource quotas\"\ngit push origin feature/update-quotas\n```\n\n### 4. PR Validation\n\nGitHub Actions automatically runs on PR:\n- `terraform fmt` check\n- `terraform init` (no backend)\n- `terraform validate`\n- Trivy security scan\n\n### 5. Merge and Deploy\n\nAfter PR approval:\n1. Merge to `develop` (for dev) or `main` (for qa)\n2. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**\n3. Click **Run workflow**\n4. Select environment and branch\n5. Review plan output\n6. Approve deployment at the approval gate\n\n## Terraform State Management\n\n| Environment | State Bucket | Prefix |\n|-------------|--------------|--------|\n| dev | `kd-tfstate-dev` | `services/crewai/resources` |\n| qa | `kd-tfstate-qa` | `services/crewai/resources` |\n| prod | `kd-tfstate-prod` | `services/crewai/resources` |\n\n```hcl\n# backend.tf\nterraform {\n  backend \"gcs\" {\n    bucket = \"kd-tfstate-dev\"  # Environment-specific\n    prefix = \"services/crewai/resources\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Required Providers\n\n```hcl\nterraform {\n  required_version = \">= 1.5.0\"\n\n  required_providers {\n    google = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/google\"\n      version = \"~> 5.0\"\n    }\n    kubernetes = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/kubernetes\"\n      version = \"~> 2.23\"\n    }\n    random = {\n      source  = \"hashicorp/random\"\n      version = \"~> 3.5\"\n    }\n  }\n}\n```\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### Terraform Plan Fails\n\n```bash\n# Check formatting\nterraform fmt -check -recursive\n\n# Validate configuration\nterraform validate\n\n# Check for module version issues\nterraform init -upgrade\n```\n\n### Secret Manager Access Denied\n\nVerify the GitHub Actions service account has permissions:\n\n```bash\ngcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \\\n  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n```\n\n### Namespace Already Exists\n\nIf Terraform tries to create a namespace that exists:\n\n```bash\n# Import existing resource\nterraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)\n- [kix-platform-modules Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)\n- [Terraform GCP Provider Docs](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)\n- [GKE Gateway API Docs](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)\n"
}
