    64→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
    65→
    66→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    67→  bucket_name = "${var.gke_project_id}-crewai-data"
    68→  location    = var.region
    69→  # ...
    70→}
    71→
    72→# Single-secret-per-call pattern for Secret Manager
    73→module "secret_db_password" {
    74→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    75→
    76→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    77→  secret_id   = "crewai-db-password"
    78→  secret_data = random_password.db.result
    79→}
    80→
    81→# Kubernetes namespace with policies
    82→module "gke_config" {
    83→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/gke-config?ref=main"
    84→
    85→  namespace_name = "crewai-${var.environment}"
    86→  # ...
    87→}
    88→```
    89→
    90→## Environment Configuration
    91→
    92→### Development
    93→
    94→| Setting | Value |
    95→|---------|-------|
    96→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` |
    97→| **Domain** | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
    98→| **Namespace** | `crewai-dev` |
    99→| **Replicas** | 1 web, 1 worker |
   100→| **Resources** | 1000m/2Gi requests, 2000m/4Gi limits |
   101→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-dev` |
   102→| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |
   103→
   104→### QA
   105→
   106→| Setting | Value |
   107→|---------|-------|
   108→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-qa-gke` |
   109→| **Domain** | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   110→| **Namespace** | `crewai-qa` |
   111→| **Replicas** | 2 web, 2 worker |
   112→| **Resources** | 1500m/4Gi requests, 3000m/8Gi limits |
   113→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-qa` |
   114→| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |
   115→
   116→### Production
   117→
   118→| Setting | Value |
   119→|---------|-------|
   120→| **GKE Cluster** | `kd-ix-eur-prod-gke` |
   121→| **Domain** | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |
   122→| **Namespace** | `crewai-prod` |
   123→| **Replicas** | 3+ web, 3+ worker (HPA max 10) |
   124→| **Resources** | 2000m/6Gi requests, 4000m/12Gi limits |
   125→| **PDB** | Min 2 available |
   126→| **State Bucket** | `kd-tfstate-prod` |
   127→| **State Prefix** | `services/crewai/resources` |
   128→
   129→## Secrets Management
   130→
   131→CrewAI uses a dual-layer secrets architecture:
   132→
   133→### Layer 1: GCP Secret Manager (Terraform-managed)
   134→
   135→Terraform creates secrets in GCP Secret Manager:
   136→
   137→```hcl
   138→resource "google_secret_manager_secret" "db_password" {
   139→  secret_id = "crewai-db-password-${var.environment}"
   140→
   141→  replication {
   142→    auto {}
   143→  }
   144→}
   145→
   146→resource "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "db_password" {
   147→  secret=[REDACTED].db_password.id
   148→  secret_data = random_password.db.result
   149→}
   150→```
   151→
   152→### Layer 2: Kubernetes Secrets (Terraform-created)
   153→
   154→Terraform creates K8s secrets populated from Secret Manager:
   155→
   156→```hcl
   157→resource "kubernetes_secret" "crewai_credentials" {
   158→  metadata {
   159→    name      = "crewai-credentials"
   160→    namespace = "crewai-${var.environment}"
   161→  }
   162→
   163→  data = {
   164→    AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
   165→    AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
   166→    DB_PASSWORD           = random_password.db.result
   167→  }
   168→}
   169→```
   170→
   171→### Secrets Created
   172→
   173→| Secret Name | Contents | Used By |
   174→|-------------|----------|---------|
   175→| `crewai-credentials` | HMAC keys, DB password | Web, Worker pods |
   176→| `crewai-database-config` | DB host, port, name, user | Web, Worker pods |
   177→| `crewai-registry` | CrewAI OCI registry auth | BuildKit, image pulls |
   178→
   179→## Kubernetes Resources
   180→
   181→### Namespaces
   182→
   183→Two namespaces per environment for isolation:
   184→
   185→| Namespace | Purpose |
   186→|-----------|---------|
   187→| `crewai-{env}` | Platform services (web, worker, scheduler) |
   188→| `crewai-crews-{env}` | Crew workload execution (isolated) |
   189→
   190→### Network Policies
   191→
   192→Default-deny-ingress with specific allowances:
   193→
   194→```yaml
   195→# GCP health check IPs (mandatory for load balancer)
   196→ingress:
   197→  - from:
   198→      - ipBlock:
   199→          cidr: 35.191.0.0/16
   200→      - ipBlock:
   201→          cidr: 130.211.0.0/22
   202→    ports:
   203→      - port: 8080
   204→        protocol: TCP
   205→```
   206→
   207→### Resource Quotas
   208→
   209→Per-namespace limits prevent resource exhaustion:
   210→
   211→```yaml
   212→spec:
   213→  hard:
   214→    requests.cpu: "10"
   215→    requests.memory: "20Gi"
   216→    limits.cpu: "20"
   217→    limits.memory: "40Gi"
   218→    pods: "50"
   219→```
   220→
   221→### Pod Security Standards
   222→
   223→CrewAI uses `baseline` Pod Security Standards (not `restricted`) to support BuildKit's Linux capabilities requirements.
   224→
   225→## Gateway API Configuration
   226→
   227→CrewAI uses GKE Gateway API (not Ingress) for traffic management:
   228→
   229→```yaml
   230→# Gateway (GKE L7 Global External Load Balancer)
   231→apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
   232→kind: Gateway
   233→metadata:
   234→  name: crewai-gateway
   235→spec:
   236→  gatewayClassName: gke-l7-global-external-managed
   237→  listeners:
   238→    - name: https
   239→      protocol: HTTPS
   240→      port: 443
   241→      tls:
   242→        mode: Terminate
   243→        certificateRefs:
   244→          - name: crewai-certificate
   245→
   246→# HTTPRoute for traffic routing
   247→apiVersion: gateway.networking.k8s.io/v1
   248→kind: HTTPRoute
   249→metadata:
   250→  name: crewai-route
   251→spec:
   252→  parentRefs:
   253→    - name: crewai-gateway
   254→  rules:
   255→    - backendRefs:
   256→        - name: crewai-web
   257→          port: 80
   258→```
   259→
   260→## Making Infrastructure Changes
   261→
   262→### 1. Clone the Repository
   263→
   264→```bash
   265→git clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git
   266→cd kix-services-crewai
   267→```
   268→
   269→### 2. Make Changes
   270→
   271→Edit the appropriate files in `environments/{env}/terraform/`:
   272→
   273→```bash
   274→# Example: Update resource quotas for dev
   275→vim environments/dev/terraform/kubernetes.tf
   276→```
   277→
   278→### 3. Create a Pull Request
   279→
   280→```bash
   281→git checkout -b feature/update-quotas
   282→git add .
   283→git commit -m "feat: increase dev resource quotas"
   284→git push origin feature/update-quotas
   285→```
   286→
   287→### 4. PR Validation
   288→
   289→GitHub Actions automatically runs on PR:
   290→- `terraform fmt` check
   291→- `terraform init` (no backend)
   292→- `terraform validate`
   293→- Trivy security scan
   294→
   295→### 5. Merge and Deploy
   296→
   297→After PR approval:
   298→1. Merge to `develop` (for dev) or `main` (for qa)
   299→2. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**
   300→3. Click **Run workflow**
   301→4. Select environment and branch
   302→5. Review plan output
   303→6. Approve deployment at the approval gate
   304→
   305→## Terraform State Management
   306→
   307→| Environment | State Bucket | Prefix |
   308→|-------------|--------------|--------|
   309→| dev | `kd-tfstate-dev` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   310→| qa | `kd-tfstate-qa` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   311→| prod | `kd-tfstate-prod` | `services/crewai/resources` |
   312→
   313→```hcl
   314→# backend.tf
   315→terraform {
   316→  backend "gcs" {
   317→    bucket = "kd-tfstate-dev"  # Environment-specific
   318→    prefix = "services/crewai/resources"
   319→  }
   320→}
   321→```
   322→
   323→## Required Providers
   324→
   325→```hcl
   326→terraform {
   327→  required_version = ">= 1.5.0"
   328→
   329→  required_providers {
   330→    google = {
   331→      source  = "hashicorp/google"
   332→      version = "~> 5.0"
   333→    }
   334→    kubernetes = {
   335→      source  = "hashicorp/kubernetes"
   336→      version = "~> 2.23"
   337→    }
   338→    random = {
   339→      source  = "hashicorp/random"
   340→      version = "~> 3.5"
   341→    }
   342→  }
   343→}
   344→```
   345→
   346→## Troubleshooting
   347→
   348→### Terraform Plan Fails
   349→
   350→```bash
   351→# Check formatting
   352→terraform fmt -check -recursive
   353→
   354→# Validate configuration
   355→terraform validate
   356→
   357→# Check for module version issues
   358→terraform init -upgrade
   359→```
   360→
   361→### Secret Manager Access Denied
   362→
   363→Verify the GitHub Actions service account has permissions:
   364→
   365→```bash
   366→gcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \
   367→  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform
   368→```
   369→
   370→### Namespace Already Exists
   371→
   372→If Terraform tries to create a namespace that exists:
   373→
   374→```bash
   375→# Import existing resource
   376→terraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev
   377→```
   378→
   379→## Additional Resources
   380→
   381→- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)
   382→- [kix-platform-modules Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules)
   383→- [Terraform GCP Provider Docs](https://registry.terraform.io/providers/hashicorp/google/latest/docs)
   384→- [GKE Gateway API Docs](https://cloud.google.com/kubernetes-engine/docs/concepts/gateway-api)
   385→
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/install.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Deployment Guide\nsidebar_label: Deployment Guide\nsidebar_position: 3\ndescription: Deploy CrewAI Platform using ArgoCD and GitHub Actions\n---\n\n# CrewAI Platform Deployment\n\nCrewAI is deployed using **ArgoCD** for GitOps-based application delivery, with **GitHub Actions** managing infrastructure changes. This guide covers the deployment workflow.\n\n## Deployment Architecture\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    kix-services-crewai                           │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│                                                                   │\n│  GitHub Actions                    ArgoCD                        │\n│  ┌──────────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────────┐      │\n│  │ validate-plan-apply  │         │ Multi-source App     │      │\n│  │ ├─ terraform fmt     │         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │\n│  │ ├─ terraform init    │         │ │ Source 1: OCI    │ │      │\n│  │ ├─ terraform plan    │────────▶│ │ CrewAI Helm      │ │      │\n│  │ ├─ approval gate     │         │ │ chart v0.3.13    │ │      │\n│  │ └─ terraform apply   │         │ └──────────────────┘ │      │\n│  └──────────────────────┘         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │\n│                                   │ │ Source 2: Git    │ │      │\n│                                   │ │ Gateway API +    │ │      │\n│                                   │ │ local Helm       │ │      │\n│                                   │ └──────────────────┘ │      │\n│                                   │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │\n│                                   │ │ Source 3: Git    │ │      │\n│                                   │ │ values.yaml      │ │      │\n│                                   │ └──────────────────┘ │      │\n│                                   └──────────────────────┘      │\n│                                                                   │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n## Prerequisites\n\nBefore deploying CrewAI:\n\n| Requirement | Description |\n|-------------|-------------|\n| **Repository Access** | Write access to [kix-services-crewai](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |\n| **GitHub Actions** | Permissions to run workflows |\n| **GCP Access** | IAM roles for target environment project |\n| **ArgoCD Access** | Ability to view applications in ArgoCD dashboard |\n| **CrewAI Registry** | Credentials stored in GCP Secret Manager |\n\n## Environments\n\n| Environment | Branch | Auto-Sync | Change Window |\n|-------------|--------|-----------|---------------|\n| **dev** | `develop` | Yes | 24/7 |\n| **qa** | `main` | Yes | 24/7 |\n| **prod** | `v*` tags | Manual | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |\n\n### Branch-Environment Protection\n\nThe CI/CD pipeline enforces strict branch-to-environment mapping:\n\n```\nBranch      → Allowed Environments\ndevelop     → dev only\nmain        → dev, qa only\nv* tags     → prod only\n```\n\n## ArgoCD Application Configuration\n\nCrewAI uses ArgoCD's **multi-source** pattern, pulling from three sources:\n\n### Source 1: CrewAI OCI Helm Chart\n\n```yaml\n- repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable\n  chart: crewai-platform\n  targetRevision: 0.3.13\n  helm:\n    valueFiles:\n      - $values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values-gke.yaml\n```\n\n### Source 2: Local Gateway/ESO Helm Chart\n\n```yaml\n- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai\n  path: environments/dev/helm/crewai\n  targetRevision: develop\n```\n\n### Source 3: Values Reference\n\n```yaml\n- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai\n  targetRevision: develop\n  ref: values\n```\n\n## Deployment Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Make Changes\n\nClone the repository and make your changes:\n\n```bash\ngit clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git\ncd kix-services-crewai\n\n# Create a feature branch\ngit checkout -b feature/your-change\n```\n\n**Common changes:**\n\n| Change Type | File Location |\n|-------------|---------------|\n| Helm values | `environments/{env}/helm/crewai/values.yaml` |\n| ArgoCD app config | `environments/{env}/argocd/apps/application.yaml` |\n| Infrastructure | `environments/{env}/terraform/*.tf` |\n| CrewAI version | `application.yaml` → `targetRevision` |\n\n### Step 2: Create Pull Request\n\n```bash\ngit add .\ngit commit -m \"feat: description of change\"\ngit push origin feature/your-change\n```\n\nGitHub Actions automatically validates on PR:\n- Terraform format check\n- Terraform validate\n- Trivy security scan\n- SARIF upload to Code Scanning\n\n### Step 3: Merge to Target Branch\n\nAfter PR approval:\n- Merge to `develop` for dev deployments\n- Merge to `main` for qa deployments\n- Create `v*` tag for prod deployments\n\n### Step 4: Run Deployment Workflow\n\n1. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**\n2. Click **Run workflow**\n3. Select:\n   - **Environment**: dev, qa, or prod\n   - **Branch/Tag**: matches your target\n4. Click **Run workflow**\n\n### Step 5: Review and Approve\n\nThe workflow runs in stages:\n\n```\n[Validate] ──▶ [Plan] ──▶ [Approval Gate] ──▶ [Apply]\n```\n\n1. **Validate**: Format check, init, validate, Trivy scan\n2. **Plan**: Generate Terraform plan with resource counts\n3. **Approval Gate**: GitHub Environment protection (requires approval)\n4. **Apply**: Execute Terraform apply\n\n### Step 6: ArgoCD Sync\n\nAfter Terraform apply:\n- **dev/qa**: ArgoCD auto-syncs within 3 minutes\n- **prod**: Manual sync required in ArgoCD dashboard\n\n**Verify in ArgoCD:**\n\n```bash\n# Check application status\nargocd app get crewai-dev\n\n# Force sync if needed\nargocd app sync crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Helm Values Configuration\n\n### Key Configuration Files\n\n| File | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| `values.yaml` | Gateway API, health checks, GCP backend policies |\n| `values-gke.yaml` | GKE-specific: replicas, resources, service accounts |\n\n### Example values-gke.yaml\n\n```yaml\n# Application Configuration\nweb:\n  replicaCount: 1  # dev: 1, qa: 2, prod: 3\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: \"1000m\"\n      memory: \"2Gi\"\n    limits:\n      cpu: \"2000m\"\n      memory: \"4Gi\"\n\nworker:\n  replicaCount: 1\n  resources:\n    requests:\n      cpu: \"1000m\"\n      memory: \"2Gi\"\n    limits:\n      cpu: \"2000m\"\n      memory: \"4Gi\"\n\n# Service Account (Terraform-managed)\nserviceAccount:\n  create: false\n  name: crewai-platform-sa\n\n# External Database (Terraform-managed)\npostgres:\n  enabled: false\n\nenvVars:\n  DB_HOST: \"10.29.0.2\"  # Private IP from Terraform\n  DB_PORT: \"5432\"\n  DB_NAME: \"crewai_plus_production\"\n\n# External Storage (Terraform-managed)\nminio:\n  enabled: false\n\nenvVars:\n  STORAGE_SERVICE: \"amazon\"\n  AWS_ENDPOINT: \"https://storage.googleapis.com\"\n  AWS_REGION: \"europe-west1\"\n  AWS_BUCKET: \"kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data\"\n  LOGS_BUCKET: \"kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-logs\"\n\n# Secrets (from K8s secrets created by Terraform)\nexistingSecrets:\n  credentials: crewai-credentials\n  databaseConfig: crewai-database-config\n```\n\n:::warning Important\n- Set `serviceAccount.create: false` — Terraform creates the SA\n- Set `postgres.enabled: false` — Use external Cloud SQL\n- Set `minio.enabled: false` — Use external GCS buckets\n:::\n\n## Upgrading CrewAI Version\n\nTo upgrade the CrewAI Helm chart version:\n\n### 1. Update Application Manifest\n\nEdit `environments/{env}/argocd/apps/application.yaml`:\n\n```yaml\nsources:\n  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable\n    chart: crewai-platform\n    targetRevision: 0.3.14  # ← Update version\n```\n\n### 2. Create PR and Deploy\n\n```bash\ngit checkout -b feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14\ngit add environments/dev/argocd/apps/application.yaml\ngit commit -m \"feat: upgrade CrewAI to 0.3.14 in dev\"\ngit push origin feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14\n```\n\n### 3. Validate in Dev First\n\nAlways upgrade dev → qa → prod in sequence.\n\n## Monitoring Deployment\n\n### ArgoCD Dashboard\n\nAccess the ArgoCD UI to monitor:\n- Sync status\n- Resource health\n- Pod status\n- Events\n\n```bash\n# Port-forward to ArgoCD (if needed)\nkubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443\n\n# Or use CLI\nargocd app get crewai-dev\nargocd app logs crewai-dev\n```\n\n### Pod Status\n\n```bash\n# Check pods\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Describe pod for events\nkubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n### GitHub Actions\n\nMonitor workflow runs:\n1. Go to **Actions** tab\n2. Select **validate-plan-apply** workflow\n3. View job logs for each stage\n\n## Rollback Procedures\n\n### Option 1: Git Revert (Recommended)\n\n```bash\n# Revert the problematic commit\ngit revert HEAD\ngit push origin develop\n\n# Run validate-plan-apply workflow\n# ArgoCD will sync the reverted state\n```\n\n### Option 2: ArgoCD Rollback\n\n```bash\n# List revision history\nargocd app history crewai-dev\n\n# Rollback to previous revision\nargocd app rollback crewai-dev <revision-number>\n```\n\n### Option 3: Kubectl Rollback (Emergency)\n\n```bash\n# Rollback deployment directly (bypasses GitOps)\nkubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n\n# Note: This will be overwritten on next ArgoCD sync\n```\n\n## Nightly Quality Checks\n\nThe `nightly-quality.yml` workflow runs at 02:00 UTC daily:\n\n- Validates `develop` → `dev` configuration\n- Validates `main` → `qa` configuration\n- Runs drift detection for all environments\n- Creates GitHub issues if drift is found\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n### ArgoCD Sync Failed\n\n```bash\n# Check sync status\nargocd app get crewai-dev\n\n# View sync errors\nargocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run\n\n# Force hard refresh\nargocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh\n```\n\n### Pods Not Starting\n\n```bash\n# Check events\nkubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n\n# Check pod status\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n### ImagePullBackOff\n\nRegistry authentication issue:\n\n```bash\n# Check the registry secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Verify secret exists and is correct in Terraform\n```\n\n### Database Connection Failed\n\n```bash\n# Check database config secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Test connectivity from pod\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME\n```\n\n## Next Steps\n\nAfter successful deployment:\n\n1. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize organization and configure admin access\n2. **Verify Access** — Test the application URL\n3. **Configure Monitoring** — Set up alerts in Grafana\n\n## Additional Resources\n\n- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)\n- [ArgoCD Documentation](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/)\n- [CrewAI Enterprise Portal](https://enterprise.crewai.com)\n"
}

     5→description: Deploy CrewAI Platform using ArgoCD and GitHub Actions
     6→---
     7→
     8→# CrewAI Platform Deployment
     9→
    10→CrewAI is deployed using **ArgoCD** for GitOps-based application delivery, with **GitHub Actions** managing infrastructure changes. This guide covers the deployment workflow.
    11→
    12→## Deployment Architecture
    13→
    14→```
    15→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    16→│                    kix-services-crewai                           │
    17→├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
    18→│                                                                   │
    19→│  GitHub Actions                    ArgoCD                        │
    20→│  ┌──────────────────────┐         ┌──────────────────────┐      │
    21→│  │ validate-plan-apply  │         │ Multi-source App     │      │
    22→│  │ ├─ terraform fmt     │         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │
    23→│  │ ├─ terraform init    │         │ │ Source 1: OCI    │ │      │
    24→│  │ ├─ terraform plan    │────────▶│ │ CrewAI Helm      │ │      │
    25→│  │ ├─ approval gate     │         │ │ chart v0.3.13    │ │      │
    26→│  │ └─ terraform apply   │         │ └──────────────────┘ │      │
    27→│  └──────────────────────┘         │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │
    28→│                                   │ │ Source 2: Git    │ │      │
    29→│                                   │ │ Gateway API +    │ │      │
    30→│                                   │ │ local Helm       │ │      │
    31→│                                   │ └──────────────────┘ │      │
    32→│                                   │ ┌──────────────────┐ │      │
    33→│                                   │ │ Source 3: Git    │ │      │
    34→│                                   │ │ values.yaml      │ │      │
    35→│                                   │ └──────────────────┘ │      │
    36→│                                   └──────────────────────┘      │
    37→│                                                                   │
    38→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    39→```
    40→
    41→## Prerequisites
    42→
    43→Before deploying CrewAI:
    44→
    45→| Requirement | Description |
    46→|-------------|-------------|
    47→| **Repository Access** | Write access to [kix-services-crewai](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |
    48→| **GitHub Actions** | Permissions to run workflows |
    49→| **GCP Access** | IAM roles for target environment project |
    50→| **ArgoCD Access** | Ability to view applications in ArgoCD dashboard |
    51→| **CrewAI Registry** | Credentials stored in GCP Secret Manager |
    52→
    53→## Environments
    54→
    55→| Environment | Branch | Auto-Sync | Change Window |
    56→|-------------|--------|-----------|---------------|
    57→| **dev** | `develop` | Yes | 24/7 |
    58→| **qa** | `main` | Yes | 24/7 |
    59→| **prod** | `v*` tags | Manual | Tue-Thu 20:00-23:00 UTC |
    60→
    61→### Branch-Environment Protection
    62→
    63→The CI/CD pipeline enforces strict branch-to-environment mapping:
    64→
    65→```
    66→Branch      → Allowed Environments
    67→develop     → dev only
    68→main        → dev, qa only
    69→v* tags     → prod only
    70→```
    71→
    72→## ArgoCD Application Configuration
    73→
    74→CrewAI uses ArgoCD's **multi-source** pattern, pulling from three sources:
    75→
    76→### Source 1: CrewAI OCI Helm Chart
    77→
    78→```yaml
    79→- repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
    80→  chart: crewai-platform
    81→  targetRevision: 0.3.13
    82→  helm:
    83→    valueFiles:
    84→      - $values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values-gke.yaml
    85→```
    86→
    87→### Source 2: Local Gateway/ESO Helm Chart
    88→
    89→```yaml
    90→- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
    91→  path: environments/dev/helm/crewai
    92→  targetRevision: develop
    93→```
    94→
    95→### Source 3: Values Reference
    96→
    97→```yaml
    98→- repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai
    99→  targetRevision: develop
   100→  ref: values
   101→```
   102→
   103→## Deployment Workflow
   104→
   105→### Step 1: Make Changes
   106→
   107→Clone the repository and make your changes:
   108→
   109→```bash
   110→git clone git@github.com:konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai.git
   111→cd kix-services-crewai
   112→
   113→# Create a feature branch
   114→git checkout -b feature/your-change
   115→```
   116→
   117→**Common changes:**
   118→
   119→| Change Type | File Location |
   120→|-------------|---------------|
   121→| Helm values | `environments/{env}/helm/crewai/values.yaml` |
   122→| ArgoCD app config | `environments/{env}/argocd/apps/application.yaml` |
   123→| Infrastructure | `environments/{env}/terraform/*.tf` |
   124→| CrewAI version | `application.yaml` → `targetRevision` |
   125→
   126→### Step 2: Create Pull Request
   127→
   128→```bash
   129→git add .
   130→git commit -m "feat: description of change"
   131→git push origin feature/your-change
   132→```
   133→
   134→GitHub Actions automatically validates on PR:
   135→- Terraform format check
   136→- Terraform validate
   137→- Trivy security scan
   138→- SARIF upload to Code Scanning
   139→
   140→### Step 3: Merge to Target Branch
   141→
   142→After PR approval:
   143→- Merge to `develop` for dev deployments
   144→- Merge to `main` for qa deployments
   145→- Create `v*` tag for prod deployments
   146→
   147→### Step 4: Run Deployment Workflow
   148→
   149→1. Go to **Actions** → **validate-plan-apply**
   150→2. Click **Run workflow**
   151→3. Select:
   152→   - **Environment**: dev, qa, or prod
   153→   - **Branch/Tag**: matches your target
   154→4. Click **Run workflow**
   155→
   156→### Step 5: Review and Approve
   157→
   158→The workflow runs in stages:
   159→
   160→```
   161→[Validate] ──▶ [Plan] ──▶ [Approval Gate] ──▶ [Apply]
   162→```
   163→
   164→1. **Validate**: Format check, init, validate, Trivy scan
   165→2. **Plan**: Generate Terraform plan with resource counts
   166→3. **Approval Gate**: GitHub Environment protection (requires approval)
   167→4. **Apply**: Execute Terraform apply
   168→
   169→### Step 6: ArgoCD Sync
   170→
   171→After Terraform apply:
   172→- **dev/qa**: ArgoCD auto-syncs within 3 minutes
   173→- **prod**: Manual sync required in ArgoCD dashboard
   174→
   175→**Verify in ArgoCD:**
   176→
   177→```bash
   178→# Check application status
   179→argocd app get crewai-dev
   180→
   181→# Force sync if needed
   182→argocd app sync crewai-dev
   183→```
   184→
   185→## Helm Values Configuration
   186→
   187→### Key Configuration Files
   188→
   189→| File | Purpose |
   190→|------|---------|
   191→| `values.yaml` | Gateway API, health checks, GCP backend policies |
   192→| `values-gke.yaml` | GKE-specific: replicas, resources, service accounts |
   193→
   194→### Example values-gke.yaml
   195→
   196→```yaml
   197→# Application Configuration
   198→web:
   199→  replicaCount: 1  # dev: 1, qa: 2, prod: 3
   200→  resources:
   201→    requests:
   202→      cpu: "1000m"
   203→      memory: "2Gi"
   204→    limits:
   205→      cpu: "2000m"
   206→      memory: "4Gi"
   207→
   208→worker:
   209→  replicaCount: 1
   210→  resources:
   211→    requests:
   212→      cpu: "1000m"
   213→      memory: "2Gi"
   214→    limits:
   215→      cpu: "2000m"
   216→      memory: "4Gi"
   217→
   218→# Service Account (Terraform-managed)
   219→serviceAccount:
   220→  create: false
   221→  name: crewai-platform-sa
   222→
   223→# External Database (Terraform-managed)
   224→postgres:
   225→  enabled: false
   226→
   227→envVars:
   228→  DB_HOST: "10.29.0.2"  # Private IP from Terraform
   229→  DB_PORT: "5432"
   230→  DB_NAME: "crewai_plus_production"
   231→
   232→# External Storage (Terraform-managed)
   233→minio:
   234→  enabled: false
   235→
   236→envVars:
   237→  STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"
   238→  AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   239→  AWS_REGION: "europe-west1"
   240→  AWS_BUCKET: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data"
   241→  LOGS_BUCKET: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-logs"
   242→
   243→# Secrets (from K8s secrets created by Terraform)
   244→existingSecrets:
   245→  credentials: crewai-credentials
   246→  databaseConfig: crewai-database-config
   247→```
   248→
   249→:::warning Important
   250→- Set `serviceAccount.create: false` — Terraform creates the SA
   251→- Set `postgres.enabled: false` — Use external Cloud SQL
   252→- Set `minio.enabled: false` — Use external GCS buckets
   253→:::
   254→
   255→## Upgrading CrewAI Version
   256→
   257→To upgrade the CrewAI Helm chart version:
   258→
   259→### 1. Update Application Manifest
   260→
   261→Edit `environments/{env}/argocd/apps/application.yaml`:
   262→
   263→```yaml
   264→sources:
   265→  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
   266→    chart: crewai-platform
   267→    targetRevision: 0.3.14  # ← Update version
   268→```
   269→
   270→### 2. Create PR and Deploy
   271→
   272→```bash
   273→git checkout -b feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14
   274→git add environments/dev/argocd/apps/application.yaml
   275→git commit -m "feat: upgrade CrewAI to 0.3.14 in dev"
   276→git push origin feat/upgrade-crewai-0.3.14
   277→```
   278→
   279→### 3. Validate in Dev First
   280→
   281→Always upgrade dev → qa → prod in sequence.
   282→
   283→## Monitoring Deployment
   284→
   285→### ArgoCD Dashboard
   286→
   287→Access the ArgoCD UI to monitor:
   288→- Sync status
   289→- Resource health
   290→- Pod status
   291→- Events
   292→
   293→```bash
   294→# Port-forward to ArgoCD (if needed)
   295→kubectl port-forward svc/argocd-server -n argocd 8080:443
   296→
   297→# Or use CLI
   298→argocd app get crewai-dev
   299→argocd app logs crewai-dev
   300→```
   301→
   302→### Pod Status
   303→
   304→```bash
   305→# Check pods
   306→kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
   307→
   308→# Check logs
   309→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   310→
   311→# Describe pod for events
   312→kubectl describe pod <pod-name> -n crewai-dev
   313→```
   314→
   315→### GitHub Actions
   316→
   317→Monitor workflow runs:
   318→1. Go to **Actions** tab
   319→2. Select **validate-plan-apply** workflow
   320→3. View job logs for each stage
   321→
   322→## Rollback Procedures
   323→
   324→### Option 1: Git Revert (Recommended)
   325→
   326→```bash
   327→# Revert the problematic commit
   328→git revert HEAD
   329→git push origin develop
   330→
   331→# Run validate-plan-apply workflow
   332→# ArgoCD will sync the reverted state
   333→```
   334→
   335→### Option 2: ArgoCD Rollback
   336→
   337→```bash
   338→# List revision history
   339→argocd app history crewai-dev
   340→
   341→# Rollback to previous revision
   342→argocd app rollback crewai-dev <revision-number>
   343→```
   344→
   345→### Option 3: Kubectl Rollback (Emergency)
   346→
   347→```bash
   348→# Rollback deployment directly (bypasses GitOps)
   349→kubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   350→
   351→# Note: This will be overwritten on next ArgoCD sync
   352→```
   353→
   354→## Nightly Quality Checks
   355→
   356→The `nightly-quality.yml` workflow runs at 02:00 UTC daily:
   357→
   358→- Validates `develop` → `dev` configuration
   359→- Validates `main` → `qa` configuration
   360→- Runs drift detection for all environments
   361→- Creates GitHub issues if drift is found
   362→
   363→## Troubleshooting
   364→
   365→### ArgoCD Sync Failed
   366→
   367→```bash
   368→# Check sync status
   369→argocd app get crewai-dev
   370→
   371→# View sync errors
   372→argocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run
   373→
   374→# Force hard refresh
   375→argocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh
   376→```
   377→
   378→### Pods Not Starting
   379→
   380→```bash
   381→# Check events
   382→kubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   383→
   384→# Check pod status
   385→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   386→```
   387→
   388→### ImagePullBackOff
   389→
   390→Registry authentication issue:
   391→
   392→```bash
   393→# Check the registry secret
   394→kubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   395→
   396→# Verify secret exists and is correct in Terraform
   397→```
   398→
   399→### Database Connection Failed
   400→
   401→```bash
   402→# Check database config secret
   403→kubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml
   404→
   405→# Test connectivity from pod
   406→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \
   407→  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME
   408→```
   409→
   410→## Next Steps
   411→
   412→After successful deployment:
   413→
   414→1. **[Post-Installation Setup](post-install)** — Initialize organization and configure admin access
   415→2. **Verify Access** — Test the application URL
   416→3. **Configure Monitoring** — Set up alerts in Grafana
   417→
   418→## Additional Resources
   419→
   420→- [kix-services-crewai Repository](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai)
   421→- [ArgoCD Documentation](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/)
   422→- [CrewAI Enterprise Portal](https://enterprise.crewai.com)
   423→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Post-Installation Setup
     3→sidebar_label: Post-Installation Setup
     4→sidebar_position: 4
     5→description: Post installation steps for CrewAI Platform
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Post-Installation Setup for CrewAI Platform
     9→
    10→## Overview
    11→
    12→After successfully deploying the CrewAI Platform infrastructure, these initial setup steps are necessary to configure the core system, set up permissions, and prepare the environment for use.
    13→
    14→:::warning Important
    15→Complete these setup steps immediately after deployment. Skipping any steps may result in missing functionality or access issues.
    16→:::
    17→
    18→## Initial Setup Tasks
    19→
    20→These tasks must be run immediately after installation to configure the internal organization and administration access.
    21→
    22→### Step 1: Initialize Internal Organization
    23→
    24→Run the following command to initialize the required internal organization, typically named "Default."
    25→
    26→```bash
    27→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- rake studio:install_internal_organization
    28→```
    29→
    30→### Step 2: Set Up Default Permissions
    31→
    32→Configure the default permission set and assign the initial owner role. **Replace `admin@company.com` with your desired administrator's email address.**
    33→
    34→```bash
    35→# Replace with your admin email
    36→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -- \
    37→  rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults 'factory:add_owner[2,admin@company.com]'
    38→```
    39→
    40→### Step 3: Fix Missing 'Operate' Tab (Troubleshoot)
    41→
    42→If the **"Operate"** tab is missing in the Web UI, you may need to manually assign the `Owner` role to your user via the Rails console.
    43→
    44→**1. Shell into the `crewai-web` pod:**
    45→```bash
    46→kubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -- /bin/bash
    47→```
    48→
    49→**2. Start the Rails console:**
    50→```bash
    51→bin/rails c
    52→```
    53→
    54→**3. Define the role assignment function (paste this code block):**
    55→```ruby
    56→def assign_owner_role(user_email)
    57→  user = User.find_by(email: user_email)
    58→  organization = Organization.find_by(name: "Default")
    59→  owner_role = organization.roles.find_by(name: "Owner")
    60→  membership = organization.memberships.find_by(user_id: user.id)
    61→  membership.update!(role_id: owner_role.id)
    62→end
    63→```
    64→
    65→**4. Run the assignment function (replace with your user email):**
    66→```ruby
    67→assign_owner_role("nathan.booth@konecta.com")
    68→```
    69→
    70→**5. Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and refresh your browser.**
    71→
    72→## Enable Studio V2 (Optional)
    73→
    74→:::info What is Studio V2?
    75→Studio V2 is the next-generation interface and agent capability for CrewAI, providing enhanced features and improved user experience.
    76→:::
    77→
    78→### Step 1: Check LLM Connection and Delete Old Crews
    79→
    80→Ensure you have an LLM connection named **`studio-v2`** configured. Then, shell into the web-service pod and delete any old Studio crew deployments.
    81→
    82→**1. Shell into the web-service pod and run:**
    83→```bash
    84→bin/rails c
    85→```
    86→
    87→**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**
    88→```ruby
    89→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)
    90→Provisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)
    91→```
    92→
    93→### Step 2: Install the Studio Agent
    94→
    95→Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent. This process can take up to **~10 minutes**.
    96→
    97→```bash
    98→INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install
    99→```
   100→
   101→### Step 3: Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools
   102→
   103→Continuously check the agent status until it reports **"Crew is Online"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.
   104→
   105→```bash
   106→# Check status - wait until it says "Crew is Online"
   107→rake studio:agent:status
   108→
   109→# Sync and re-index tools
   110→rake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools
   111→rake studio:tools:reindex_tools
   112→```
   113→
   114→### Step 4: Install the Studio Runner
   115→
   116→Install the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for 10 minutes for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.
   117→
   118→```bash
   119→INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install
   120→```
   121→
   122→## Accessing the Application
   123→
   124→Use the appropriate method to access the CrewAI Web UI based on your deployment environment.
   125→
   126→### Development Access (Port Forwarding)
   127→
   128→Ideal for quick testing or development environments.
   129→
   130→```bash
   131→# Forward web service port (2603 is the external port, 80 is the internal service port)
   132→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web 2603:80
   133→
   134→# Access in browser
   135→open http://localhost:2603
   136→```
   137→
   138→### Production Access (Ingress / LoadBalancer)
   139→
   140→| Method | Command to Find Endpoint | Access URL Example |
   141→|--------|--------------------------|-------------------|
   142→| **Ingress** | `kubectl get ing crewai-ingress` | `https://crewai.company.com` |
   143→| **LoadBalancer** | `kubectl get svc crewai-web` | `http://<EXTERNAL-IP>:80` |
   144→
   145→## Verification Steps
   146→
   147→After completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:
   148→
   149→1. **Access the Web UI** using one of the methods above
   150→2. **Log in** with your configured authentication provider
   151→3. **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of administrative links/tabs
   152→4. **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project
   153→5. **Check background workers** for successful job processing:
   154→   ```bash
   155→   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker --tail=50
   156→   ```
   157→
   158→:::success Congratulations!
   159→Your CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!
   160→
   161→For more information, visit the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) or the [FAQ](faq).
   162→:::
   163→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/post-install.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Post-Installation Setup\nsidebar_label: Post-Installation Setup\nsidebar_position: 4\ndescription: Post installation steps for CrewAI Platform\n---\n\n# Post-Installation Setup for CrewAI Platform\n\n## Overview\n\nAfter successfully deploying the CrewAI Platform via ArgoCD, these initial setup steps are necessary to configure the core system, set up permissions, and prepare the environment for use.\n\n:::warning Important\nComplete these setup steps immediately after deployment. Skipping any steps may result in missing functionality or access issues.\n:::\n\n## Environment Namespaces\n\nCommands in this guide use environment-specific namespaces:\n\n| Environment | Namespace | Domain |\n|-------------|-----------|--------|\n| **dev** | `crewai-dev` | `crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **qa** | `crewai-qa` | `crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n| **prod** | `crewai-prod` | `crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com` |\n\nReplace `crewai-dev` with the appropriate namespace for your environment.\n\n## Initial Setup Tasks\n\nThese tasks must be run immediately after installation to configure the internal organization and administration access.\n\n### Step 1: Initialize Internal Organization\n\nRun the following command to initialize the required internal organization, typically named \"Default.\"\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  rake studio:install_internal_organization\n```\n\n### Step 2: Set Up Default Permissions\n\nConfigure the default permission set and assign the initial owner role. **Replace `admin@company.com` with your desired administrator's email address.**\n\n```bash\n# Replace with your admin email\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  rake factory:setup_permissions_defaults 'factory:add_owner[2,admin@company.com]'\n```\n\n### Step 3: Fix Missing 'Operate' Tab (Troubleshoot)\n\nIf the **\"Operate\"** tab is missing in the Web UI, you may need to manually assign the `Owner` role to your user via the Rails console.\n\n**1. Shell into the `crewai-web` pod:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash\n```\n\n**2. Start the Rails console:**\n```bash\nbin/rails c\n```\n\n**3. Define the role assignment function (paste this code block):**\n```ruby\ndef assign_owner_role(user_email)\n  user = User.find_by(email: user_email)\n  organization = Organization.find_by(name: \"Default\")\n  owner_role = organization.roles.find_by(name: \"Owner\")\n  membership = organization.memberships.find_by(user_id: user.id)\n  membership.update!(role_id: owner_role.id)\nend\n```\n\n**4. Run the assignment function (replace with your user email):**\n```ruby\nassign_owner_role(\"nathan.booth@konecta.com\")\n```\n\n**5. Exit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and refresh your browser.**\n\n## Enable Studio V2 (Optional)\n\n:::info What is Studio V2?\nStudio V2 is the next-generation interface and agent capability for CrewAI, providing enhanced features and improved user experience.\n:::\n\n### Step 1: Check LLM Connection and Delete Old Crews\n\nEnsure you have an LLM connection named **`studio-v2`** configured. Then, shell into the web-service pod and delete any old Studio crew deployments.\n\n**1. Shell into the web-service pod and run:**\n```bash\nkubectl exec --stdin --tty deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash\nbin/rails c\n```\n\n**2. Paste and run the following commands in the Rails console:**\n```ruby\nProvisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_V2_AGENT_SLUG).id)\nProvisioner::DeleteProvisionedCrewJob.perform_later(Deployment.find_by_slug(Studio::Install::Const::STUDIO_RUNNER_AGENT_SLUG).id)\n```\n\n### Step 2: Install the Studio Agent\n\nExit the Rails shell (Ctrl+D) and install the core Studio Agent. This process can take up to **~10 minutes**.\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  bash -c \"INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI ORGANIZATION_ID=1 rake studio:agent:install\"\n```\n\n### Step 3: Verify Agent Status and Sync Tools\n\nContinuously check the agent status until it reports **\"Crew is Online\"**. Then, sync and re-index the available tools.\n\n```bash\n# Check status - wait until it says \"Crew is Online\"\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:agent:status\n\n# Sync and re-index tools\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:tools:sync_crewai_tools\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- rake studio:tools:reindex_tools\n```\n\n### Step 4: Install the Studio Runner\n\nInstall the Studio Runner agent, which handles the execution environment. Wait for 10 minutes for the deployment process to fully complete before testing.\n\n```bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  bash -c \"INTERNAL_ORGANIZATION_NAME=CrewAI rake studio:runner:install\"\n```\n\n## Accessing the Application\n\n### Production URLs (Gateway API)\n\nAccess CrewAI using the configured domain:\n\n| Environment | URL |\n|-------------|-----|\n| **dev** | https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com |\n| **qa** | https://crewai.qa.ix.konecta-digital.com |\n| **prod** | https://crewai.ix.konecta-digital.com |\n\n### Development Access (Port Forwarding)\n\nFor local testing without going through the load balancer:\n\n```bash\n# Forward web service port\nkubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80\n\n# Access in browser\nopen http://localhost:2603\n```\n\n### Check Gateway Status\n\nVerify the Gateway API is configured correctly:\n\n```bash\n# Check Gateway status\nkubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check HTTPRoute\nkubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check backend health\nkubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Verification Steps\n\nAfter completing all setup tasks, verify the platform's readiness:\n\n1. **Access the Web UI** at the environment URL\n2. **Log in** with your configured authentication provider (WorkOS)\n3. **Verify admin access** by confirming the presence of the \"Operate\" tab\n4. **Test basic functionality** by creating a test crew or project\n5. **Check pods are healthy:**\n   ```bash\n   kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\n   ```\n6. **Check background workers** for successful job processing:\n   ```bash\n   kubectl logs -l app=crewai-worker -n crewai-dev --tail=50\n   ```\n\n## Verification Checklist\n\n```markdown\n## Post-Installation Verification\n\n### Core Setup\n- [ ] Internal organization initialized\n- [ ] Default permissions configured\n- [ ] Admin user has Owner role\n- [ ] \"Operate\" tab visible in Web UI\n\n### Application Access\n- [ ] Web UI accessible via domain URL\n- [ ] Authentication working (WorkOS login)\n- [ ] Can create test project/crew\n\n### Pods & Services\n- [ ] All pods in Running state\n- [ ] No CrashLoopBackOff errors\n- [ ] Worker pods processing jobs\n- [ ] Gateway API routing traffic correctly\n\n### Studio V2 (if enabled)\n- [ ] LLM connection \"studio-v2\" configured\n- [ ] Studio Agent status: \"Crew is Online\"\n- [ ] Tools synced and indexed\n- [ ] Studio Runner installed\n```\n\n:::success Congratulations!\nYour CrewAI platform is now fully installed and configured. Start creating and deploying crews!\n\nFor more information, visit the [Troubleshooting Guide](troubleshooting) or the [FAQ](faq).\n:::\n"
}
```

     1→---
     2→title: Troubleshooting Guide
     3→sidebar_label: Troubleshooting
     4→sidebar_position: 5
     5→description: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Troubleshooting Guide
     9→
    10→This guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI installation, configuration, and operation.
    11→
    12→:::tip
    13→Use the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands.
    14→:::
    15→
    16→## Installation & Deployment Issues
    17→
    18→### Helm Installation Failures
    19→
    20→#### Issue: Helm chart installation fails with 'values validation error'
    21→
    22→**Cause**: Missing or incorrectly formatted required values in `my-values.yaml`
    23→
    24→**Solution**:
    25→- Verify all required variables are present in your values file
    26→- Check YAML syntax for formatting errors (indentation, quotes)
    27→- Use `helm lint` to validate the chart before installation
    28→- Review [Prepare Configuration File guide](install#step-4-prepare-configuration-file-my-valuesyaml)
    29→
    30→#### Issue: 'ImagePullBackOff' errors after deployment
    31→
    32→**Cause**: Container registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist
    33→
    34→**Solution**:
    35→- Verify `image.registries[].password` is correctly base64-encoded
    36→- Confirm the service account has access to the container registry
    37→- Check that the image tag exists in the specified registry
    38→- Review Kubernetes events: `kubectl describe pod <pod-name>`
    39→
    40→### Database Connection Issues
    41→
    42→#### Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors
    43→
    44→**Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable
    45→
    46→**Solution**:
    47→- Verify `DB_PASSWORD` is correct and matches the database user
    48→- Confirm database host is reachable from the Kubernetes cluster
    49→- Check network policies allow traffic to the database
    50→- Test connection manually:
    51→  ```bash
    52→  kubectl exec -it <pod> -- psql -h <host> -U postgres
    53→  ```
    54→
    55→#### Issue: 'Database migration fails' on first deployment
    56→
    57→**Cause**: Database schema initialization incomplete
    58→
    59→**Solution**:
    60→- Ensure database is fully initialized before deploying application
    61→- Check database logs for migration errors
    62→- Verify user has proper privileges for schema creation
    63→- Run migrations manually if needed:
    64→  ```bash
    65→  kubectl exec -it <pod> -- rake db:migrate
    66→  ```
    67→
    68→## Authentication & Authorization Issues
    69→
    70→### WorkOS Authentication
    71→
    72→#### Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page
    73→
    74→**Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured
    75→
    76→**Solution**:
    77→- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value
    78→- Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)
    79→- Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets
    80→- Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled
    81→- Check browser console for authentication errors
    82→- Review application logs:
    83→  ```bash
    84→  kubectl logs -f <pod-name>
    85→  ```
    86→
    87→#### Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error
    88→
    89→**Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch
    90→
    91→:::warning IMPORTANT
    92→`WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes
    93→:::
    94→
    95→**Solution**:
    96→- Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)
    97→- **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)
    98→- Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long
    99→- Ensure the same password is used across all replicas
   100→- Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-docs/docs/crewai/troubleshooting.md",
  "content": "---\ntitle: Troubleshooting Guide\nsidebar_label: Troubleshooting\nsidebar_position: 5\ndescription: Solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation\n---\n\n# Troubleshooting Guide\n\nThis guide provides solutions for common issues encountered during CrewAI deployment and operation.\n\n:::tip\nUse the debug commands reference at the bottom of this page for quick troubleshooting commands. Remember to use the correct namespace: `crewai-dev`, `crewai-qa`, or `crewai-prod`.\n:::\n\n## ArgoCD & GitOps Issues\n\n### ArgoCD Sync Failed\n\n#### Issue: Application stuck in 'OutOfSync' or 'Degraded' state\n\n**Cause**: Resource conflict, invalid manifest, or dependency issue\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check sync status and errors\nargocd app get crewai-dev\n\n# View detailed sync errors\nargocd app sync crewai-dev --dry-run\n\n# Force hard refresh from Git\nargocd app get crewai-dev --hard-refresh\n\n# Check ArgoCD logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=argocd-application-controller -n argocd\n```\n\n#### Issue: 'ComparisonError' in ArgoCD\n\n**Cause**: Resource managed by both ArgoCD and another controller (e.g., Terraform)\n\n**Solution**:\n- Check `application.yaml` for proper `ignoreDifferences` configuration\n- Verify the resource isn't being modified by Terraform and ArgoCD simultaneously\n- Add ignored fields:\n  ```yaml\n  spec:\n    ignoreDifferences:\n      - group: \"\"\n        kind: Secret\n        jsonPointers:\n          - /data\n  ```\n\n### Terraform Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Resource already exists' error\n\n**Cause**: Resource created outside of Terraform or state mismatch\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Import existing resource\nterraform import 'kubernetes_namespace.crewai' crewai-dev\n\n# Or for module resources\nterraform import 'module.gke_config.kubernetes_namespace.main' crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: 'Secret Manager access denied'\n\n**Cause**: GitHub Actions service account lacks permissions\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Verify IAM policy\ngcloud secrets get-iam-policy crewai-db-password-dev \\\n  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n\n# Add missing binding\ngcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding crewai-db-password-dev \\\n  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform \\\n  --role=roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor \\\n  --member=\"serviceAccount:github-actions@kd-ix-eur-dev-platform.iam.gserviceaccount.com\"\n```\n\n## Deployment Issues\n\n### ImagePullBackOff\n\n#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'ImagePullBackOff' state\n\n**Cause**: Registry authentication failure or image doesn't exist\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check the registry secret exists\nkubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify secret has correct data\nkubectl get secret crewai-registry -n crewai-dev -o jsonpath='{.data.\\.dockerconfigjson}' | base64 -d\n\n# Check pod events for specific error\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify image exists in CrewAI registry (requires auth)\n```\n\n**Common causes**:\n- `crewai-registry` secret not created by Terraform\n- CrewAI registry credentials expired\n- Wrong image tag in ArgoCD application\n\n### Database Connection Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Unable to connect to database' errors\n\n**Cause**: Database credentials incorrect or database unreachable\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check database config secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Verify DB_HOST is correct (should be Cloud SQL private IP)\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev \\\n  -o jsonpath='{.data.DB_HOST}' | base64 -d\n\n# Test connection from pod\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  psql -h $DB_HOST -U $DB_USER -d $DB_NAME\n\n# Check Cloud SQL instance is running\ngcloud sql instances list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n```\n\n#### Issue: 'crewai-database-config' ConfigMap not found\n\n**Cause**: Terraform hasn't created the Kubernetes secret\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Verify secret exists\nkubectl get secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev\n\n# If missing, check Terraform state\ncd kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform\nterraform state list | grep secret\n\n# Re-run Terraform apply if needed\n# Use GitHub Actions validate-plan-apply workflow\n```\n\n## Authentication & Authorization Issues\n\n### WorkOS Authentication\n\n#### Issue: 'Authentication failed' or blank login page\n\n**Cause**: WorkOS credentials incorrect or misconfigured\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify `WORKOS_CLIENT_ID` matches the WorkOS dashboard value\n- Confirm `WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN` is correctly formatted (e.g., `your-domain.authkit.app`)\n- Check that `WORKOS_API_KEY` is properly stored in Kubernetes secrets\n- Verify WorkOS organization is active and AuthKit is enabled\n- Check browser console for authentication errors\n- Review application logs:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl logs -f deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Invalid or expired session' errors or 'key must be 32 bytes' error\n\n**Cause**: `WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` incorrect format or session key mismatch\n\n:::warning IMPORTANT\n`WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD` requires a **32-character string**, NOT 32 bytes\n:::\n\n**Solution**:\n- Generate correctly using: `openssl rand -hex 16` (generates 16 bytes as 32 hex characters)\n- **Do NOT use**: `openssl rand -base64 32` (generates 32 bytes, encodes to 44 characters - will fail)\n- Verify the password is exactly 32 characters long\n- Ensure the same password is used across all replicas\n- Check that secrets are properly synced from Cloud Secret Manager\n- Restart pods to pick up updated secrets:\n  ```bash\n  kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n  ```\n\n#### Issue: 'Magic Auth' not appearing as sign-in option\n\n**Cause**: AuthKit not configured with Magic Auth method\n\n**Solution**:\n- Enable Magic Auth in WorkOS AuthKit settings\n- Refresh browser cache or open in incognito mode\n- Verify AuthKit domain is correctly configured\n\n#### Issue: Google Workspace OIDC sign-in fails\n\n**Cause**: Google OAuth not properly configured on GCP Project\n\n**Solution**:\n- Verify Google OAuth consent screen is configured\n- Check OAuth 2.0 credentials are created and authorized for AuthKit domain\n- Confirm `your-domain.authkit.app` is added to authorized redirect URIs\n- Review GCP Console > APIs & Services > Credentials\n\n## Network & Gateway Issues\n\n### Gateway API Issues\n\n#### Issue: Application not accessible via domain\n\n**Cause**: Gateway, HTTPRoute, or certificate misconfiguration\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check Gateway status\nkubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check HTTPRoute\nkubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check GCPBackendPolicy for health check configuration\nkubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Check certificate status\nkubectl get managedcertificate -n crewai-dev\n\n# View Gateway events\nkubectl describe gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: 502 Bad Gateway errors\n\n**Cause**: Backend pods not healthy or health check failing\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check pod health\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check if pods are passing health checks\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify health check endpoint works\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- curl localhost:8080/health\n\n# Check GCPBackendPolicy health check configuration\nkubectl get gcpbackendpolicy -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n```\n\n### DNS Resolution\n\n#### Issue: Domain name not resolving\n\n**Cause**: DNS records not configured or propagation delay\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Test DNS resolution\nnslookup crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com\ndig crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com\n\n# Check Cloud DNS records\ngcloud dns record-sets list --zone=ix-konecta-digital-com\n\n# Verify Gateway external IP\nkubectl get gateway crewai-gateway -n crewai-dev -o jsonpath='{.status.addresses[0].value}'\n```\n\n## Storage Issues\n\n### GCS Access Issues\n\n#### Issue: 'Access Denied' when writing to storage bucket\n\n**Cause**: HMAC credentials invalid or service account lacks permissions\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check credentials secret\nkubectl get secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev -o yaml\n\n# Verify HMAC key is valid\ngcloud storage hmac list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-platform\n\n# Test access from pod\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- \\\n  aws s3 ls s3://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data/\n\n# Check bucket exists\ngcloud storage buckets describe gs://kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-crewai-data\n```\n\n## Kubernetes Pod Issues\n\n### Pods Not Starting\n\n#### Issue: Pods stuck in 'Pending' state\n\n**Cause**: Insufficient resources or node issues\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check node availability\nkubectl get nodes\n\n# Describe pod to see pending reason\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check resource requests vs available\nkubectl describe nodes | grep -A 5 \"Allocated resources\"\n\n# Check namespace quota\nkubectl get resourcequota -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n#### Issue: Pods crashing with 'CrashLoopBackOff'\n\n**Cause**: Application startup failure or configuration error\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# Check pod logs\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check previous logs (if restarted)\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev --previous\n\n# Check environment variables\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env | sort\n\n# Check events\nkubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n```\n\n### Secrets Issues\n\n#### Issue: Environment variables not populated from secrets\n\n**Cause**: Secret not created or not mounted correctly\n\n**Solution**:\n```bash\n# List secrets in namespace\nkubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev\n\n# Check specific secret exists and has data\nkubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev\n\n# Verify pod has secret mounted\nkubectl get pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev -o yaml | grep -A 10 envFrom\n\n# Restart pods to pick up secret changes\nkubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Debug Commands Reference\n\n:::tip Quick Reference\nQuick reference for common debugging commands\n:::\n\n```bash\n# ============== ArgoCD ==============\nargocd app get crewai-dev\nargocd app sync crewai-dev\nargocd app logs crewai-dev\nargocd app history crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Pods ==============\nkubectl get pods -n crewai-dev\nkubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\nkubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev --previous\nkubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Services ==============\nkubectl get svc -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev\nkubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Secrets ==============\nkubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev\nkubectl describe secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev\n\n# ============== Events ==============\nkubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'\n\n# ============== Exec into Pod ==============\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash\nkubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env\n\n# ============== Port Forward ==============\nkubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80\n\n# ============== Rollout ==============\nkubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\nkubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\nkubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev\n```\n\n## Support Resources\n\n| Resource | Link |\n|----------|------|\n| kix-services-crewai Repository | [GitHub](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |\n| Infrastructure Setup | [Infrastructure Guide](gcp-setup) |\n| Deployment Guide | [Deployment Guide](install) |\n| ArgoCD Documentation | [argo-cd.readthedocs.io](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/) |\n| kubectl Cheat Sheet | [Kubernetes Docs](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/cheatsheet/) |\n\n### Reporting Issues\n\nWhen reporting issues, include:\n- Complete error messages and stack traces\n- Output of relevant kubectl commands\n- Kubernetes events (`kubectl get events -n crewai-dev`)\n- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)\n- ArgoCD application status\n- Terraform plan output (if infrastructure-related)\n- CrewAI version and environment (dev/qa/prod)\n"
}

   358→
   359→# Restart pods to pick up secret changes
   360→kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   361→```
   362→
   363→## Debug Commands Reference
   364→
   365→:::tip Quick Reference
   366→Quick reference for common debugging commands
   367→:::
   368→
   369→```bash
   370→# ============== ArgoCD ==============
   371→argocd app get crewai-dev
   372→argocd app sync crewai-dev
   373→argocd app logs crewai-dev
   374→argocd app history crewai-dev
   375→
   376→# ============== Pods ==============
   377→kubectl get pods -n crewai-dev
   378→kubectl logs -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   379→kubectl logs deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev --previous
   380→kubectl describe pod -l app.kubernetes.io/name=crewai-platform -n crewai-dev
   381→
   382→# ============== Services ==============
   383→kubectl get svc -n crewai-dev
   384→kubectl get gateway -n crewai-dev
   385→kubectl get httproute -n crewai-dev
   386→
   387→# ============== Secrets ==============
   388→kubectl get secrets -n crewai-dev
   389→kubectl describe secret crewai-credentials -n crewai-dev
   390→kubectl describe secret crewai-database-config -n crewai-dev
   391→
   392→# ============== Events ==============
   393→kubectl get events -n crewai-dev --sort-by='.lastTimestamp'
   394→
   395→# ============== Exec into Pod ==============
   396→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- /bin/bash
   397→kubectl exec -it deploy/crewai-web -n crewai-dev -- env
   398→
   399→# ============== Port Forward ==============
   400→kubectl port-forward svc/crewai-web -n crewai-dev 2603:80
   401→
   402→# ============== Rollout ==============
   403→kubectl rollout status deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   404→kubectl rollout restart deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   405→kubectl rollout undo deployment/crewai-web -n crewai-dev
   406→```
   407→
   408→## Support Resources
   409→
   410→| Resource | Link |
   411→|----------|------|
   412→| kix-services-crewai Repository | [GitHub](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) |
   413→| Infrastructure Setup | [Infrastructure Guide](gcp-setup) |
   414→| Deployment Guide | [Deployment Guide](install) |
   415→| ArgoCD Documentation | [argo-cd.readthedocs.io](https://argo-cd.readthedocs.io/) |
   416→| kubectl Cheat Sheet | [Kubernetes Docs](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/kubectl/cheatsheet/) |
   417→
   418→### Reporting Issues
   419→
   420→When reporting issues, include:
   421→- Complete error messages and stack traces
   422→- Output of relevant kubectl commands
   423→- Kubernetes events (`kubectl get events -n crewai-dev`)
   424→- Application logs (sanitized for sensitive data)
   425→- ArgoCD application status
   426→- Terraform plan output (if infrastructure-related)
   427→- CrewAI version and environment (dev/qa/prod)
   428→
```

     1→---
     2→title: Frequently Asked Questions
     3→sidebar_label: FAQ
     4→sidebar_position: 7
     5→description: Common questions and answers about CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud
     6→---
     7→
     8→# Frequently Asked Questions
     9→
    10→:::info
    11→This FAQ covers common questions about installing, configuring, and operating CrewAI Platform on Google Cloud Platform.
    12→:::
    13→
    14→## General Questions
    15→
    16→### What is CrewAI Platform?
    17→
    18→CrewAI Platform is an enterprise-grade framework for orchestrating role-playing AI agents that work together to accomplish complex tasks. It provides a collaborative environment where autonomous AI agents can coordinate their efforts to solve sophisticated problems.
    19→
    20→**Key Features:**
    21→- Multi-agent orchestration and coordination
    22→- Role-based agent specialization
    23→- Task delegation and workflow management
    24→- Enterprise security and compliance
    25→- GCP-native integration
    26→
    27→### What are the system requirements?
    28→
    29→**Minimum Requirements for Development:**
    30→- GKE cluster with 3 nodes (e2-standard-2 or higher)
    31→- 8 vCPUs and 16 GB RAM total
    32→- 100 GB persistent storage
    33→- Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-f1-micro minimum)
    34→- 2 Cloud Storage buckets
    35→
    36→**Recommended Requirements for Production:**
    37→- GKE cluster with 6+ nodes (e2-standard-4 or higher)
    38→- 24+ vCPUs and 48+ GB RAM total
    39→- 500 GB persistent storage
    40→- Cloud SQL PostgreSQL instance (db-n1-standard-2 or higher)
    41→- Multi-regional Cloud Storage buckets
    42→- High availability configuration across 2+ regions
    43→
    44→### How much does it cost to run CrewAI on GCP?
    45→
    46→Costs vary based on environment and usage patterns:
    47→
    48→**Development Environment (estimated):**
    49→| Component | Cost |
    50→|-----------|------|
    51→| GKE cluster | $150-200/month |
    52→| Cloud SQL | $25-50/month |
    53→| Cloud Storage | $5-15/month |
    54→| Networking | $10-20/month |
    55→| **Total** | **~$190-285/month** |
    56→
    57→**Production Environment (estimated):**
    58→| Component | Cost |
    59→|-----------|------|
    60→| GKE cluster | $500-800/month |
    61→| Cloud SQL with HA | $200-400/month |
    62→| Cloud Storage multi-region | $30-60/month |
    63→| Load balancing and networking | $50-100/month |
    64→| Cloud Armor and security | $20-40/month |
    65→| **Total** | **~$800-1,400/month** |
    66→
    67→:::tip
    68→Use committed use discounts (CUDs) and sustained use discounts to reduce costs by 30-50%.
    69→:::
    70→
    71→## Installation & Setup
    72→
    73→### How long does installation take?
    74→
    75→**Typical Installation Timeline:**
    76→- Infrastructure provisioning (Terraform): 15-30 minutes
    77→- CrewAI Platform Helm deployment: 10-15 minutes
    78→- Post-installation configuration: 5-10 minutes
    79→- **Total: 30-55 minutes**
    80→

  "new_string": "## Deployment & Setup\n\n### How is CrewAI deployed?\n\nCrewAI uses a **GitOps deployment model** managed through the [`kix-services-crewai`](https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai) repository:\n\n| Layer | Technology | Description |\n|-------|------------|-------------|\n| **Infrastructure** | Terraform | GCS buckets, Cloud SQL, Secret Manager, K8s resources |\n| **Application** | ArgoCD | Multi-source apps pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry |\n| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Validation, security scanning, approval gates |\n\n### How long does deployment take?\n\n**Typical Deployment Timeline:**\n- Terraform apply (via GitHub Actions): 5-10 minutes\n- ArgoCD sync: 2-5 minutes\n- Post-installation configuration: 5-10 minutes\n- **Total: 15-25 minutes**"
}

    80→| **Application** | ArgoCD | Multi-source apps pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry |
    81→| **CI/CD** | GitHub Actions | Validation, security scanning, approval gates |
    82→
    83→### How long does deployment take?
    84→
    85→**Typical Deployment Timeline:**
    86→- Terraform apply (via GitHub Actions): 5-10 minutes
    87→- ArgoCD sync: 2-5 minutes
    88→- Post-installation configuration: 5-10 minutes
    89→- **Total: 15-25 minutes**
    90→
    91→First-time installations may take longer due to:
    92→- API enablement (5-10 minutes)
    93→- Initial image pulls from registry
    94→- DNS propagation for custom domains (up to 48 hours, typically 5-10 minutes)
    95→
    96→### Can I install CrewAI without using Terraform?
    97→
    98→While Terraform is the recommended approach for reproducible infrastructure, you can manually provision resources through the GCP Console:
    99→
   100→**Manual Setup Steps:**
   101→1. Create VPC network and subnets
   102→2. Provision GKE cluster
   103→3. Create Cloud SQL instance
   104→4. Create Cloud Storage buckets
   105→5. Configure IAM roles and service accounts
   106→6. Deploy CrewAI via Helm
   107→
   108→:::warning
   109→Manual setup is **not recommended** for production environments due to:
   110→- Lack of version control and auditability
   111→- Higher risk of configuration drift
   112→- Difficulty reproducing environments
   113→- Complex rollback procedures
   114→:::
   115→
   116→### What authentication methods are supported?
   117→
   118→CrewAI Platform supports multiple authentication providers:
   119→
   120→| Provider | Description |
   121→|----------|-------------|
   122→| **WorkOS** | Recommended for enterprise SSO |
   123→| **Microsoft Entra ID** | Formerly Azure AD |
   124→| **Google Workspace** | Google OIDC authentication |
   125→| **Local authentication** | Development only |
   126→
   127→Configure the provider in your `my-values.yaml`:
   128→```yaml
   129→envVars:
   130→  AUTH_PROVIDER: "workos"  # or "entra_id", "google", "local"
   131→```
   132→
   133→:::note
   134→Local authentication should only be used for development and testing. Production environments must use SSO-based authentication.
   135→:::
   136→
   137→### Do I need a custom domain?
   138→
   139→**Development:** Not required - you can use port-forwarding or a temporary LoadBalancer IP
   140→
   141→**Production:** **Strongly recommended** for:
   142→- Professional appearance
   143→- SSL/TLS certificate management
   144→- OAuth callback URL configuration
   145→- Stable endpoint for integrations
   146→
   147→**Setup Requirements:**
   148→1. Domain registered and managed (e.g., via Cloud DNS)
   149→2. DNS A record pointing to Load Balancer IP
   150→3. Managed SSL certificate provisioned
   151→4. Ingress configured with domain name
   152→
   153→## Configuration & Customization
   154→
   155→### How do I scale my deployment?
   156→
   157→**Horizontal Scaling (Recommended):**
   158→```yaml

> AGENT

Done. I've updated all the CrewAI documentation to reflect the new deployment model using `kix-services-crewai`. Here's a summary:

## Changes Made

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| [index.md](docs/crewai/index.md) | New GitOps architecture diagram, deployment model overview, environment table, repository links |
| [gcp-setup.md](docs/crewai/gcp-setup.md) | Now references Terraform in kix-services-crewai, shows module composition, secrets management |
| [install.md](docs/crewai/install.md) | Completely rewritten for ArgoCD/GitHub Actions workflow, multi-source app pattern |
| [post-install.md](docs/crewai/post-install.md) | Updated namespace references (`crewai-dev`), environment URLs, Gateway API verification |
| [troubleshooting.md](docs/crewai/troubleshooting.md) | Added ArgoCD/Terraform sections, Gateway API troubleshooting, updated namespace refs |
| [faq.md](docs/crewai/faq.md) | Updated deployment model Q&A, fixed broken anchor links |
| [third-party.md](docs/crewai/third-party.md) | Fixed broken anchor link (WorkOS content retained) |

## Key Documentation Updates

**New Deployment Model:**
- Infrastructure via Terraform in `kix-services-crewai/environments/{env}/terraform/`
- ArgoCD multi-source applications pulling CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry
- GitHub Actions `validate-plan-apply` workflow for deployments
- Gateway API instead of traditional Ingress

**Environment Naming:**
- Namespaces: `crewai-dev`, `crewai-qa`, `crewai-prod`
- Domains: `crewai.{env}.ix.konecta-digital.com`

**WorkOS Content:** Retained as requested for future integration

**Branch:** `feature/crewai-docs` pushed successfully

   147→### Layer 2: Kubernetes Secrets (Terraform-created)
   148→
   149→Terraform creates K8s secrets populated from Secret Manager:
   150→
   151→```hcl
   152→resource "kubernetes_secret" "crewai_credentials" {
   153→  metadata {
   154→    name      = "crewai-credentials"
   155→    namespace = "crewai-${var.environment}"
   156→  }
   157→
   158→  data = {
   159→    AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
   160→    AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
   161→    DB_PASSWORD           = random_password.db.result
   162→  }
   163→}
   164→```
   165→
   166→### Secrets Created
   167→
   168→| Secret Name | Contents | Used By |
   169→|-------------|----------|---------|
   170→| `crewai-credentials` | HMAC keys, DB password | Web, Worker pods |
   171→| `crewai-database-config` | DB host, port, name, user | Web, Worker pods |
   172→| `crewai-registry` | CrewAI OCI registry auth | BuildKit, image pulls |
   173→

> AGENT

Done. I've made two changes:

**1. Moved CrewAI Enterprise under Platform Services** in [sidebars.js](sidebars.js) - CrewAI is now a nested subcategory.

**2. Updated namespace references** across all CrewAI documentation to match the actual ArgoCD configuration:

| Environment | Namespace |
|-------------|-----------|
| dev | `crewai` |
| qa | `crewai-qa` |
| prod | `crewai-prod` |

**Files modified:**
- [sidebars.js](sidebars.js) - Sidebar restructure
- [gcp-setup.md](docs/crewai/gcp-setup.md) - Namespaces section, code examples
- [install.md](docs/crewai/install.md) - All kubectl command examples
- [post-install.md](docs/crewai/post-install.md) - Environment namespaces table, all commands
- [troubleshooting.md](docs/crewai/troubleshooting.md) - All debug commands and terraform import examples

Build passed and changes pushed to `feature/crewai-docs` branch.
